## LIME-18-fix — Fixed a script-halting crash this session's own static checks missed

- **Root cause, found by actually executing the real `app.js` against a real DOM** (installed `jsdom` into the session's scratchpad — outside the repo — since Chrome wasn't connected; this is the first LIME-18-family check that ran the real script instead of reasoning about it or testing an extracted function in isolation): LIME-18's new `thread.querySelectorAll('.lime-avatar[data-name]').forEach(paintAvatar);` line inside `renderThread` gets called during the very first synchronous pass through the page's main contacts/thread IIFE — which runs *before* the script reaches `const PALETTE_SIZE = 12;`, declared further down the file. `hashName` (called by `paintAvatar`) reads `PALETTE_SIZE`, so the very first avatar paint attempt threw `ReferenceError: Cannot access 'PALETTE_SIZE' before initialization`. In a browser, an uncaught error during one top-level statement's execution aborts every statement after it in that same script — so this one throw silently killed the reaction-picker wiring, the dropdown wiring, the mobile-nav wiring, and the standalone avatar-paint sweep, all of which sit later in the file. That's why *nothing* painted or clicked, not just the reply-summary avatars: the crash happened mid-render, after message/reply-summary HTML was already inserted (pure string building, unaffected) but before a single avatar got painted.
- Fix: moved the whole avatar-identity block (`PALETTE_SIZE`, `hashName`, `initialsFor`, `paintAvatar`) from its old position (after the contacts/thread IIFE) to directly above that IIFE, so it's fully initialized before anything can call it. Left the original one-time `document.querySelectorAll('.lime-avatar[data-name]').forEach(paintAvatar);` sweep in its old spot (after the IIFE) — it still needs to run *after* the initial render to catch the static Recent-row/sidebar avatars and the contact-list rows, none of which paint themselves individually.
- **Verification, this time by actually running the app**: installed `jsdom` in the session scratchpad (not the repo) and loaded the real `seed-data.js`/`data.js`/`app.js` as genuine `<script>` elements against the real `index.html` DOM (not `eval`, which has scoping quirks of its own — confirmed this the hard way while debugging). Bisected commit-by-commit (`573ac40`, `714eaec`, `f33f5b8`) to confirm the crash first appeared exactly at `f33f5b8` (LIME-18) and nowhere earlier. After the fix: zero thrown errors across repeated runs, 3 contacts and 8 thread messages render, avatars paint with real initials and color classes (`JC`/`lime-avatar--p9` for Jean Chung, `SR`/`lime-avatar--p1` for Shem Robinson), the reply summary's avatars paint too, and simulated clicks confirm both the notification dropdown and the reaction picker open correctly — the exact two categories of thing the user reported broken.
- `node --check public/js/app.js` passes. `git diff --stat` touches only `app.js`.
- **Process note for future sessions:** this session's own LIME-18 "verification" (Phase 3) extracted and tested `replyIndicatorHtml`/`formatLastReply` in isolation via Node, which correctly validated their *logic* but never actually loaded the file as a script — so it couldn't have caught a load-order crash like this one. Where Chrome isn't available, actually running the real file against a real DOM (jsdom, in the scratchpad) is a meaningfully stronger check than reasoning about the code or testing extracted fragments, and should be the default next time Chrome isn't connected for a JS-touching brief.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check — this time please confirm broadly: avatars everywhere (not just replies), and that reactions/dropdowns/the mobile menu are all clickable again, in addition to LIME-18's own reply-summary check.
- **Gate confirmed (2026-09-27): "works great."** Two small, separate visual bugs flagged via screenshot, same day: the reply panel's composer input is cut off/flush against the browser's right edge (needs the same gutter the main composer has), and the reaction pill and the reply-summary row under a message aren't left-aligned with each other. Both addressed below.


## LIME-18-fix2 — Reply composer inset + reaction/reply-summary alignment

- **Reply composer clipped against the right edge, root cause found by reading the positioning chain, not guessed:** `#replies-composer.lime-composer` is `position: absolute; left: 0; right: 0; bottom: 0`, so it anchors to its nearest *positioned* ancestor — but neither `.lime-replies-panel__composer` (its immediate parent) nor `.lime-replies-panel` has `position` set, so it skips both and anchors all the way up to `#right-panel`/`.seed-layout__right` (Seed's own component, `position: relative`). That ancestor's own `padding: var(--seed-space-4)` (16px) sits *inside* the containing block formed for an absolutely-positioned descendant — CSS's absolute-positioning containing block is the ancestor's padding box, so an inset:0 child can occupy that padding area rather than being pushed in by it. Net effect: the composer rendered flush against the panel's true outer edge instead of inset like the rest of the panel's content (the reply list above it gets its 16px inset "for free" from that same ancestor padding, since it's a normal, non-positioned flow child).
- Fix: added `position: relative;` to `.lime-replies-panel__composer` — the composer now anchors to it directly instead of escaping to `#right-panel`, mirroring exactly the role `.lime-chat-body` already plays for the *main* composer (`position: relative`, no padding of its own, already proven not to have this problem). No other property changed; `.lime-composer`'s own existing padding (`var(--seed-space-4)` sides) still provides the actual visual gutter, same as it already does for the main composer.
- **Reaction/reply-summary misalignment:** `.lime-message__reactions:not(:empty)` has `margin-left: var(--seed-space-3)` (12px); the reply summary's new `.lime-message__footer` (restored by LIME-18, from historical CSS this brief was explicitly told to reuse as-is and "raise adjustments at the gate instead") had no left margin at all, so it sat flush left while the reaction pill above it sat indented 12px. Added the matching `margin-left: var(--seed-space-3);` to `.lime-message__footer` — the two rows now share the same left edge.
- **Verification:** brace count in `lime.css` balanced (414/414, no change — pure additions, no rules removed). `git diff --stat` touches only `lime.css`. Both fixes are pure CSS positioning/spacing changes grounded in reading the actual containing-block/margin values, not measured live (Chrome wasn't connected this session) — the reply-composer fix in particular mirrors an already-proven pattern (`.lime-chat-body`) rather than a novel guess, but genuinely needs the user's eyes to confirm the pixel result.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: open a reply thread and confirm the "Reply..." input now has the same right-edge gutter as the main composer; on a message with both a reaction and a reply summary, confirm they now share the same left edge.
- **Gate feedback (2026-09-27), from the narrow-width "Thread" mobile panel view specifically:** the outer gutter fix held here too, but the *expanded* reply composer's formatting toolbar (B/I/U/S + 5 more tools + the "…" button) doesn't fit in the reply composer's narrower column at widths where the main composer's identical toolbar has plenty of room — the shared `@media (max-width: 480px)` breakpoint that collapses extra tools into the overflow menu is tuned for the main composer's wide column, not the reply composer's, which is always narrower regardless of window width. The row overflows and pushes the corner-anchored Send button off the edge. Also asked to shorten the reply composer's disclaimer text, which was truncating with an ellipsis. Addressed below.


## LIME-18-fix3 — Reply composer toolbar always collapses to overflow; shorter disclaimer text

- **Toolbar overflow, state-based rather than another viewport-width guess:** matched this codebase's own established convention for exactly this class of problem (see the comment already in `lime.css` for `#layout:not(.seed-layout--right-hidden) .lime-composer__tool`: "rather than guessing a viewport breakpoint, key directly off the actual state that causes it"). Added `#replies-composer .lime-composer__tool--overflow { display: none; }` / `#replies-composer .lime-composer__overflow { display: inline-flex; }`, unconditional — no media query. The reply composer's column is always narrow (the desktop right panel, or the full-width mobile "panel" view, itself still only as wide as that panel), unlike the main composer's, which only gets this narrow at the existing 480px breakpoint. This always shows just Bold/Italic/Underline/Strikethrough + the "…" overflow button for the reply composer, regardless of viewport width — matching the main composer's own narrow-width behavior, but without waiting for the viewport itself to narrow that far.
- **Shorter disclaimer text, not width-gated either, same reasoning:** LIME-12-fix4's placeholder-shortening fix explicitly skipped the reply composer ("already short, nothing to shorten there" — true for the placeholder, not the disclaimer) and gated the main composer's own fix to `window.innerWidth <= 480`. Added a new, separate, unconditional IIFE that rewrites `#replies-composer .lime-composer__privacy`'s text from "Your messages are always secure and encrypted, we never sell your data." to "Secure & encrypted" (keeping the shield icon). Unlike the placeholder (a `<textarea>`, no native truncation), this is a `<span>` with working CSS ellipsis, so it was already truncating cleanly — this shortens the underlying text itself so more of it stays legible, rather than just truncating more gracefully.
- Did **not** touch the outer composer-inset fix from `LIME-18-fix2` (`.lime-replies-panel__composer { position: relative }`) — confirmed by reading the mobile media query blocks that nothing there resets `.seed-layout__right`'s `position` or `padding` at ≤767px (only `min-width: 0`, an unrelated grid-sizing safety net), so that fix already covers this mobile "panel" view too; the remaining "cut off" symptom here was purely the toolbar row's own overflow, not a return of the outer-gutter bug.
- **Verification:** `node --check public/js/app.js` passes. Brace count in `lime.css` balanced (416/416). `git diff --stat` touches `app.js` and `lime.css` only. Actually ran the app (jsdom, in the session scratchpad, per the lesson from `LIME-18-fix`'s crash) rather than just reasoning about it: loads with zero errors, contacts/thread render as before (3/8), and the reply composer's privacy text confirmed live in the DOM as "Secure & encrypted". The toolbar-collapse CSS itself can't be verified this way (jsdom doesn't fetch/apply stylesheets), so it's grounded in the exact same selector/property pattern already proven at the `@media (max-width: 480px)` block just above it — genuinely needs the user's eyes on the actual layout result.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: in the narrow "Thread" mobile panel view, expand the reply composer's toolbar — only Bold/Italic/Underline/Strikethrough plus a "…" button should show, with room for the Send arrow; confirm the disclaimer line now reads "Secure & encrypted" without truncating.


## LIME-18-fix4 — Reply composer overflowing its panel; clamp fixed dropdowns to viewport

- Confirmed the survey's premise exactly: no global `box-sizing: border-box` reset exists anywhere in the actually-served app (Seed's own universal resets live only in its isolated component *demo* `.html` files, never loaded by `index.html`; `lime.css`/`layout.css` only set it on specific elements). `#replies-composer .lime-composer__box` (`width: 100%` + 12px left/right padding + 1px border, all content-box) was rendering 26px wider than its container; `#replies-composer .lime-composer__actions` (`width: 100%` + 4px each side) by 8px the same way. The main composer never hit this since its box is 75% wide, with room to spare.
- **Fix 1 (overflow):** added `box-sizing: border-box;` to both rules, scoped to `#replies-composer` only — the padding/border now come out of the declared 100% instead of adding to it, so the box's rendered width finally matches its container exactly, giving it the same even inset on both sides as everything else in the panel.
- **Fix 2 (clipped "…" menu):** `wireDropdownToggle`'s fixed-position branch always left-aligned a dropdown to its trigger with no check against the viewport's right edge — fine for the wide desktop dropdowns it was built for, but the reply composer's "…" menu near a narrow panel's right edge ran off-screen. Restructured the click handler to clamp *after* opening (not before — `offsetWidth` reads `0` until the dropdown is actually visible): `left = Math.max(8, Math.min(rect.left, innerWidth - offsetWidth - 8))`. The clamp only nudges left when there isn't room; dropdowns with plenty of space (every existing desktop use) land exactly where they always did, since `Math.min` is a no-op when `rect.left` already fits.
- **Verification:** `node --check public/js/app.js` passes. Brace count in `lime.css` balanced (416/416). `git diff --stat` touches `app.js` and `lime.css` only. Actually ran the real app in jsdom (per the standing rule from `LIME-18-fix`): zero errors, contacts/thread render as before (3/8), and both the notification dropdown and the reply composer's overflow dropdown open successfully via simulated clicks, running the new clamp code end-to-end with no exceptions. jsdom has no real layout engine (`getBoundingClientRect`/`offsetWidth` return zeros), so it can't confirm the actual pixel result of either fix — that genuinely needs the user's eyes, but the logic itself executes cleanly and matches the brief's exact specified algorithm.
- Left the noted "no global border-box reset" gap as its own open item, per the brief — not fixed here, logged in `PLOT.md` as a candidate later cleanup that needs a careful visual pass.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: in the narrow "Thread" mobile panel and in the desktop right panel, the reply composer should now sit evenly inset on both sides with nothing cut off; opening its "…" menu should show the whole menu on-screen; the notifications, profile and microphone dropdowns should still open in their usual spot.


## LIME-18-fix5 — Aligned the reply composer with the main composer and the right-panel gutter

- **Correction to LIME-18-fix2's own record, found while surveying this brief:** that entry's root cause ("`#replies-composer.lime-composer`, position:absolute") was wrong — checked the actual markup directly (`grep 'id="replies-composer"' public/index.html`), and the element only ever carried the class `lime-replies-panel__composer`, never `lime-composer`. Nothing inside it was ever absolutely positioned relative to it (the Send button anchors to `.lime-composer__box`, which has its own `position: relative`). Fix2's `position: relative` addition to `.lime-replies-panel__composer` was therefore an inert no-op — harmless, but not what actually fixed anything at the time (LIME-18-fix4's `box-sizing: border-box` was the real fix for the overflow). Removed the stale comment and the now-confirmed-pointless `position: relative` declaration as part of this pass.
- **Real root cause of the vertical/right-edge misalignment, computed from the CSS chain (Chrome not connected this session — these are computed, not measured, numbers, per the brief's own fallback):** `#right-panel`/`.seed-layout__right` carries `padding: var(--seed-space-4)` (16px) on *every* side, including bottom and right — a cost the main composer never pays, since it's `position: absolute; inset: 0` against `.lime-chat-body`, which has *no* padding of its own. `.seed-layout--right-flush` only zeroes the outer *grid's* `padding-right` (making the panel's own border sit flush with the viewport) — it does nothing to `.seed-layout__right`'s own internal 16px padding, and doesn't touch `padding-bottom` at all (that's shared by the center and right grid cells alike, since flush mode is horizontal-only).
- **Before (computed):**
  - Main composer's `.lime-composer__actions` row: 16px (grid's shared bottom padding) + 8px (`.lime-composer`'s own `--seed-space-2` bottom padding) = **24px** above the true viewport bottom.
  - Reply composer's `.lime-composer__actions` row: 16px (grid) + 16px (`#right-panel`'s own padding) + 12px (`.lime-replies-panel__composer`'s own `--seed-space-3` bottom padding) = **44px**. Reply sat **20px higher** than main — matches "slightly higher" exactly.
  - `#right-panel-toggle`'s button (position:absolute, `right: var(--seed-space-3)`, escaping `#right-panel`'s own padding the way only an absolutely-positioned element can): **12px** from the true viewport right edge. (The glyph itself sits a few sub-pixels further in, centered in the 36px button — that finer offset needs live measurement to nail exactly; the button's own edge is the computable proxy used here.)
  - Reply composer box's right edge: 16px (`#right-panel`'s own padding) + 12px (wrapper's own right padding) = **28px** from the true viewport right edge — **16px too far left** of the toggle.
  - "Thread" title's left edge: 16px (`#right-panel`'s own padding) + 16px (`.lime-replies-panel__header`'s own `--seed-space-4` padding) = **32px** from `#right-panel`'s left border.
  - Reply composer box's left edge: 16px (`#right-panel`) + 12px (wrapper's own `--seed-space-3` left padding) = **28px** — **4px too far left** of the title.
- **The change**, all on `.lime-replies-panel__composer` (the only rule touched):
  - `padding` split from a uniform `var(--seed-space-3)` into explicit longhands: top/right unchanged at `--seed-space-3` (12px), **bottom → `--seed-space-2`** (8px, matching the main composer's own bottom-padding token exactly), **left → `--seed-space-4`** (16px, matching the header's own left-padding token exactly, so the box lines up with the title above it).
  - `margin-right: calc(-1 * var(--seed-space-4))` and `margin-bottom: calc(-1 * var(--seed-space-4))` — cancels `#right-panel`'s own 16px padding on exactly those two sides (the ones the main composer never pays), without touching `#right-panel` itself (out of scope) or converting this element to absolute positioning (would have required also touching `.lime-replies-panel__list`, also out of scope, to compensate for the resulting overlap).
- **After (computed):**
  - Reply composer's actions row: (16 + 16 − 16 from the margin) + 8 (new bottom padding) = **24px** — matches main's 24px exactly.
  - Reply composer box's right edge: (16 − 16 from the margin) + 12 (unchanged right padding) = **12px** — matches the toggle's 12px exactly.
  - Reply composer box's left edge: 16 (`#right-panel`, untouched) + 16 (new left padding) = **32px** — matches the title's 32px exactly.
  - All three differences: **0px** by the arithmetic. Genuinely needs the user's eyes to confirm this holds in an actual render — sub-pixel rounding and the toggle's icon-glyph-vs-button-edge approximation noted above are the two places real numbers could differ slightly from computed ones.
- **Grows-upward check (reasoned, not measured):** `.lime-replies-panel__composer` is a normal (not absolutely positioned) `flex-shrink: 0` child, last in a `height: 100%` flex column whose only other child (`.lime-replies-panel__list`) is `flex: 1`. Expanding the composer's own content (the textarea growing, the toolbar appearing) grows *this element's* height, which the column accommodates by shrinking the list above it — the composer's own bottom edge (anchored by the column's fixed height, not by any property that changes on expand) stays fixed, so it can only grow upward. The new margin/padding values are constants that don't change on expand/collapse, so this property is unaffected by anything else in this fix.
- **Mobile check:** confirmed via the same media-query read as `LIME-18-fix4` — nothing at ≤767px touches `#right-panel`'s own padding (only `min-width: 0`, unrelated), so the same 16px cancellation holds at mobile width too. Not separately verified live.
- **Verification:** brace count in `lime.css` balanced (416/416, no change in count — pure edits, no rules added/removed). `git diff --stat` touches only `lime.css`.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: with a thread open on a wide window, compare the two composer boxes and the rows beneath them side by side — they should now sit at the same height. Compare the reply box's right edge to the panel-toggle icon above it — same distance from the window edge. Also worth a glance: expand the reply composer and confirm it grows upward only, and check mobile width still looks right (LIME-18-fix4's fix, unaffected in principle, but not independently re-verified here).


## LIME-18-fix6 — Reply summary underlines only on hover, never on avatars

- Root cause matched the brief exactly: `.lime-message__replies` set `text-decoration: underline` on the whole button, which — being an inherited, in-flow property — propagated to every descendant: the avatar initials, the bare "N replies" text node, and `.lime-replies__time`. The count had no element of its own to scope styling to.
- Wrapped the count in a new `<span class="lime-replies__count">` in `replyIndicatorHtml` (markup only, no other change). Set the button's own `text-decoration: none`, and added the underline back scoped to `:hover`/`:focus-visible` on `.lime-replies__count`/`.lime-replies__time` only — avatars are never targeted by either rule, so they can no longer inherit an underline regardless of hover/focus state.
- **Verification:** `node --check public/js/app.js` passes; brace count in `lime.css` balanced (418/418). `git diff --stat` touches `app.js` and `lime.css` only. Ran the real app in jsdom: zero errors, contacts/thread render as before (3/8), and the rendered reply summary contains a real `.lime-replies__count` element with the correct text ("3 replies") alongside `.lime-replies__time`. jsdom doesn't apply CSS, so the actual hover/rest `text-decoration` computed values need the user's eyes to confirm — the rule scoping itself is straightforward and matches the brief's exact selectors.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: at rest, nothing under a message should be underlined; hovering the reply summary should underline "N replies" and "Last reply …" but never the avatar letters. Also flagged per the brief: confirm this reading of "the avatar characters should have a hover underline" is right — if it meant avatars should underline too, that's a one-line follow-up.

## LIME-18-fix7 — Corrected fix5's wrong premise; lowered the main composer to match, measured not computed

- **Corrected fix5's actual premise, confirmed via `getComputedStyle(#right-panel).padding` → `"0px"`:** `lime.css` (~line 112) zeroes `.seed-layout__left`/`.seed-layout__right`'s padding outright — fix5's `margin-right`/`margin-bottom: -16px` on `.lime-replies-panel__composer` were "cancelling" a padding that never existed, pushing the box 16px past the panel's true right edge (clipped by `overflow-x: hidden`, reading as flush) and 16px past its natural bottom. Following the standing rule this session adopted after two wrong computed rounds ("pixel alignment must be measured, not measured — sorry, not computed"), every number below came from headless Chrome (`Google Chrome --headless=new --dump-dom`) via a temporary diagnostic script in `index.html`, removed before this commit (`git diff public/index.html` confirmed empty afterward).
- **Before (measured, 1567×818 viewport):** main composer's privacy row bottom `786.78`, reply's `802.78` (16px lower, matching "slightly higher" exactly). Toggle glyph's right gap `16.5px`, reply box's right gap `-4px` (actually past the viewport edge — clipped, reading as flush, matching plot's own description). Thread title's left `1283`, reply box's left `1283` (already exact — fix5's left-padding change happened to be correct despite its wrong premise elsewhere).
- **The user's direction:** keep the reply composer's current (lower) position — they like it — and bring the main composer down to match, rather than pulling the reply composer back up.
- **Right edge, corrected:** removed fix5's `margin-right: -16px` entirely (nothing to cancel) and changed the wrapper's own right padding from `--seed-space-3` (12px) to `--seed-space-4` (16px) — with zero panel padding, this alone lands the box within 1px of the toggle's own inset.
- **Vertical match, the real work:** measured that the main composer's absolutely-positioned box is *already* flush against `.seed-layout__center`'s hard `overflow: hidden` (confirmed: `chatBodyBottom` and `centerBottom` were both exactly `802`, zero slack) — it cannot move down at all without being clipped, unlike the reply panel, which Seed itself already loosens to `overflow-y: auto`. Flagged this exact stop condition to the user rather than guessing a third time; they chose to loosen the center panel's clipping rather than pull the reply composer back up.
  - Extended `.seed-layout__center` itself by `margin-bottom: calc(-1 * var(--seed-space-4))` — the same "extend into the grid's own reserved padding, not past its true outer edge" mechanism already proven safe on the reply side, just applied one level up (to the whole card, a normal grid item, rather than to an absolutely-positioned descendant). This grows the *entire card* 16px taller; `.lime-chat-body` (flex:1, chained down from it) and `.lime-composer` (bottom:0, unchanged) automatically follow — no separate change needed to either.
  - **A real mobile regression, found only because this pass measured rather than trusted the reasoning:** `.seed-layout { padding: 0; }` at `≤767px` zeroes the grid's own padding on every side for mobile — there's no 16px to extend into there. Measured directly: left unreset, the main composer's bottom edge rendered at `773` against an `innerHeight` of `757` — 16px past the actual mobile viewport, an overflow this specific measurement pass exists to catch. Added `.seed-layout__center { margin-bottom: 0; }` inside the existing `@media (max-width: 767px)` block to reset it there. Re-measured after the fix: `mainComposerBottom` (`757`) now matches `innerHeight` (`757`) exactly.
  - This means the two composers only match heights on **desktop** — at mobile width the main composer reverts to its original (already-safe) position, since the reply composer's own version of this trick still works there only because its panel tolerates it via `overflow-y: auto`, not a padding cushion. Not a regression from before this brief; just not a height match at that width, which the brief's own mobile check only ever required to be "fully on-screen with even side gaps," not equal heights.
- **After (measured, 1567×818):** privacy rows both `802.78125` — **exact match**. Right gaps `16.5` vs `16` — **0.5px**, within the ±1px target. Left edges unchanged, still exact. Message clearance (last message's bottom to the composer's top edge): **+27px**, confirmed positive. `hasHorizontalScroll`: false. Mobile (390×844, forced `data-mobile-view="thread"`): main composer's bottom now exactly matches the viewport height, no overflow.
- **Not separately measured:** the *expanded* composer state's clearance (would need simulating a real focus/auto-grow event in headless Chrome, not attempted this pass) — reasoned, not measured, that since the whole composer moved further from the messages (not closer), clearance there can only have improved, never worsened. Flagged for the user's own quick check.
- **Verification:** brace count in `lime.css` balanced (419/419). `git diff --stat` touches only `lime.css` — `public/index.html`'s diff is empty, confirming the temporary diagnostic script left no trace.

## LIME-20 — Menus open where they fit, and only one at a time

- Survey found 6 `fixed: true` calls (notif, user, voice, both composer-toolbar-overflow menus) plus the one non-fixed `more-menu` — 7 total, not the brief's expected "7 fixed plus more-menu" (8). A small headcount slip in the brief's drafting, not a sign anything's missing — grepped the whole file for every call site, so the list itself is complete. Proceeded rather than stopping, since it doesn't change what needed fixing.
- Confirmed the "several open at once" cause by reading the code: each toggle's click handler calls `e.stopPropagation()`, so switching between two open menus never lets that click reach the document-level "close on outside click" listener that would otherwise close the first one.
- **One at a time:** added a module-level `registeredDropdowns` Set. Opening any registered dropdown now closes every *other* one in the Set first. Hit the exact same temporal-dead-zone crash as `LIME-18-fix` while wiring this up — the reply-composer's own `wireDropdownToggle` calls run during the script's first pass, before a `const` declared next to the function itself would be initialized. Caught it by actually running the app in jsdom before committing (not by re-reasoning through it), and fixed it the same way as before: moved `const registeredDropdowns` up near the other early-needed top-level declarations (`PALETTE_SIZE` etc.), well above every IIFE that calls `wireDropdownToggle`.
- **Flip plus clamp:** fixed-mode menus now measure their own `offsetHeight`/`offsetWidth` after opening (has to be visible first) and pick whichever side of the trigger actually fits — below by default, above if there's no room below, and whichever side has more room if neither fits cleanly. The horizontal clamp from `LIME-18-fix4` is unchanged. Explicitly clears the unused `top`/`bottom` property each time, so a menu that flipped one way on a previous opening doesn't carry a stale value into the next.
- **Escape:** one shared `keydown` listener (not one per menu) closes whichever registered dropdown is currently open.
- **`more-menu` tried, then reverted:** switched it to `fixed: true` per the brief's instruction to share one positioning path — checked live with headless Chrome, and it rendered at `left: 2896` in a 1567px-wide viewport, badly off-screen. Root cause: `.lime-dropdown` (its actual CSS) is still `position: absolute`, not `fixed` — inline `top`/`left` values computed for a viewport-relative fixed element land somewhere else entirely against an absolute element's own (different) containing block. Reverted to non-fixed per the brief's own explicit fallback for exactly this case. It still gets the one-at-a-time and Escape behavior (neither is gated by `fixed`), just keeps governing its own position via its existing, already-correct CSS. `LIME-21` gives it the shared fixed-position CSS, at which point it can switch.
- **Verification:** `node --check` passes. Real app loads in jsdom with zero errors; opening notifications then the user menu leaves only the user menu open, and Escape closes it — exactly Phase 3's required check. Measured every other menu live with headless Chrome (temporary diagnostic script, removed before this commit — `git diff public/index.html` confirmed empty): notifications opens below and fully on-screen; the user menu and voice menu both open above, fully on-screen; the header "…" (now non-fixed again) still opens below, fully on-screen — matching the brief's expectations for all four. Could not get the reply composer's own overflow menu to a directly-testable state in headless Chrome — `--dump-dom` mode doesn't reliably honor a scripted `.focus()` call needed to expand the composer's toolbar first, a known headless limitation, not a code issue. It shares the exact same code path as the voice menu (confirmed correct), so I'm confident in it without a direct measurement, but flagging the gap rather than papering over it.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: notifications should open downward, fully visible; opening any menu should close whichever one was already open; Escape should close an open menu; no menu should ever run off the screen near an edge, including the header "…" and both formatting "…" menus this pass couldn't directly measure.
- **Gate feedback (2026-09-27):** opening below the bell covered the nav items (Link, Jam) underneath it — asked for it to open beside the bell instead. Addressed below, same day.


## LIME-20-fix — Notifications menu opens beside the bell

- Added a `placement` option to `wireDropdownToggle` (default `'vertical'`, the existing flip-and-clamp behavior, unchanged for every other menu). `placement: 'right'` opens the menu to the right of the trigger instead of above/below it, top-aligned with the trigger and clamped vertically so it can't run off the top or bottom edge. Passed only for `notif-btn`, per scope — the user menu keeps its existing flip-above behavior.
- **Fallback, not a separate code path:** if there isn't room on the right (`rect.right + gap + w > innerWidth - 8`), it falls straight through to the same vertical flip-and-clamp logic every other fixed menu already uses — no new positioning math for that case, just reusing what's already proven.
- **Verification:** `node --check` passes; real app loads in jsdom with zero errors, existing one-at-a-time/Escape behavior unaffected. Measured live with headless Chrome (temporary diagnostic script, removed before this commit — `git diff public/index.html` confirmed empty) at 1567×905: with the sidebar **expanded**, the dropdown's left edge (196) sits right at the bell's right edge (188) + the 8px gap, its top (140) matches the bell's top (140) exactly, fully on-screen. **Collapsed**, same result: left (128) vs bell's right (120) + gap, top (152) matches (152) exactly. Both `openedRight` and `topWithin1px` true.
- **Fallback not empirically triggered:** tried a 300×700 window to force the "no room on the right" case, but headless Chrome's `--window-size` clamps to a ~500px-wide minimum regardless of the requested value (confirmed: `innerWidth` reported `500`, not `300`) — even at that clamped width there was still enough room for the dropdown to open right. Verified the fallback by reading the code instead: the condition and the vertical path it falls back to are unchanged from LIME-20's own already-measured-correct logic, just gated by a simple boolean check. Flagging the gap rather than claiming a live check that didn't actually happen.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: open notifications with the sidebar expanded, then collapsed — it should open beside the bell both times, with Link and Jam still visible underneath.


## LIME-21 — All menus use Seed's dropdown styles, with Lime's selected lime

- Survey listed 7 real menus using the old classes (notifications, user, the header "…", both composer toolbar-overflow menus, both voice menus) — not 8 as the brief's own prose says in a couple of places (small headcount slip, same category as LIME-20's 7-vs-6; every listing I could find — the gate's own menu list, the scope items — independently lines up with 7). Proceeded; doesn't change what needed converting.
- **Markup, all 7 menus:** container → `class="seed-dropdown__menu lime-menu"` (kept the same id); items → `seed-dropdown__item`; the old `--active`/`--danger` modifiers carried over unchanged (Seed already defines both). Notifications and both voice menus also get `seed-dropdown__menu--wide`. Added Seed's `dropdown.css` `<link>` after `nav.css`.
  - **User menu:** email/phone become one `.lime-menu__header` (two stacked `<div>`s, no extra markup needed for that — block-level children already stack). Sign Out becomes `seed-dropdown__item seed-dropdown__item--danger` (Seed's own danger style, already red text + red hover, used as-is).
  - **Voice menus (both):** every row now gets a `.lime-menu__check-slot` wrapping a `dew-check` icon — previously only the active row had *any* icon span, which is exactly why its text sat indented relative to the others (the bug the brief named). CSS hides the slot by default, shows it only inside `.seed-dropdown__item--active`, so all four device names now share the same left edge regardless of which is chosen.
  - **Notifications:** rows keep `class="seed-dropdown__item lime-notif"` — `.lime-notif` is now a bare marker class (the `data-contact` JS hook still reads it, confirmed unaffected), no layout properties of its own; `.seed-dropdown__item` supplies display/padding/gap/hover uniformly, matching every other menu.
- **`lime.css`:** replaced the old `.lime-nav-dropdown*` block with one `.lime-menu` override block: `position: fixed` (Seed's own rule is absolute; JS still sets top/left, LIME-20's code untouched), `.lime-menu.is-open { display: block }` (plot's own leaned approach — Lime's JS keeps toggling `is-open`, never touches Seed's `--open` modifier), `.seed-dropdown__item { box-sizing: border-box }` (the actual bug — Seed's own item is `width: 100%` + padding with no border-box, so every item was rendering wider than its menu; same root cause as LIME-18-fix4, flagged again for upstreaming), the lime `--active` background/text colors (LIME-14's tokens, not Seed's stock mint), `.lime-menu__header`'s muted/no-hover/default-cursor styling, `#notif-dropdown { max-width: 320px }`, and the check-slot rules. Kept the literal `z-index: 1000` (already proven correct; no Seed z-token reaches that high, and nothing else in the app does either) rather than inventing a new one.
- **Deleted:** `.lime-nav-dropdown*` (all of it), `.lime-dropdown*`, and `.lime-more-menu`'s own CSS rule (`position: relative`, needed only for the old absolute-positioned dropdown it anchored — moot now that more-menu is fixed too). Left the `.lime-more-menu` *wrapper div* in the markup untouched — still groups the trigger and menu, just carries no styling of its own anymore.
- **Amendment applied:** switched `more-menu` to `{ fixed: true }` (removed LIME-20's own comment explaining why it *couldn't* yet, replaced with one explaining why it now can) and confirmed `placement: 'right'` (LIME-20-fix) still works after the class rewrite — measured live, unaffected, both notify the identical result as before the class changes.
- **A real mistake, caught and fully recovered before anything was committed:** after the first successful full measurement pass, I removed the temporary diagnostic script with `git checkout -- public/index.html` — the same cleanup command used safely in every prior alignment brief. Those briefs only ever had the diagnostic script in `index.html`; this one *also* had this brief's real, substantive markup changes in the same file, and `git checkout` reverts the whole file to HEAD, not just the lines I'd just added. It silently wiped every real edit back to the old `lime-nav-dropdown` classes. Caught it immediately by grepping for the new classes right after (found zero), redid all 7 markup edits from scratch against the exact same source, and re-verified from there — this time removing the diagnostic script with a targeted `Edit` instead. **Lesson for future briefs that mix permanent changes with a temporary diagnostic script in the same file: never use `git checkout` to clean up the diagnostic — it discards everything else in that file too, not just the temporary part.**
- **Verification:** `grep -rn 'lime-nav-dropdown\|lime-dropdown' public/` returns exactly 3 lines — all three are comments explaining the *old* class names for historical context (e.g. "same reason the old .lime-nav-dropdown needed…"), not live selectors or markup; reported the real grep output rather than claiming it returns nothing, since literally it doesn't. Brace count in `lime.css` balanced (412/412). `node --check` passes. Real app loads in jsdom with zero errors; all 7 menus exist, open on click, and close on a second click. LIME-20's one-at-a-time/Escape behavior re-tested against this exact code and still intact.
  - Measured live with headless Chrome (temporary script, this time actually removed via a targeted `Edit` — `git diff public/index.html` shows only the real, permanent changes): **item overflow is 0px** on every menu I could open non-interactively (notifications, user, more-menu, voice) — the `box-sizing` fix confirmed working, not just theorized. Active voice item's computed background is `rgb(228, 249, 190)` (`#E4F9BE`, `--seed-lime-100`, exactly `--selected-bg-subtle-default`) and text `rgb(5, 92, 46)` (`--seed-lime-700`) — real lime, not Seed's stock mint. Check-slot visibility: `visible` on the active row, `hidden` on every other row. Sign Out's color (`rgb(139, 8, 54)`) and the header's muted color/`default` cursor both read as expected. `more-menu` now opens below its button and fully on-screen — the amendment's specific ask.
  - **Not independently re-measured:** the reply-composer's own overflow and voice menus (`replies-composer-toolbar-overflow-dropdown`, `replies-voice-mode-dropdown`) — same headless `.focus()` limitation noted in LIME-20 prevented expanding the reply composer non-interactively. They share byte-for-byte identical classes/markup patterns with their already-measured main-composer counterparts, so I'm confident without a direct measurement, but flagging the gap rather than claiming a check that didn't happen. Hover background (`--calm-bg-subtle-hover`) wasn't captured via a live `:hover` screenshot either — headless `--dump-dom` can't force real `:hover` state — confirmed instead by reading Seed's own `dropdown.css` rule directly (`.seed-dropdown__item:hover { background: var(--calm-bg-subtle-hover); }`, stock, unmodified).
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: open every menu (notifications, profile, header "…", both microphone menus, both formatting "…" menus) — same background/spacing/hover grey everywhere; the chosen microphone should read lime with every device name lined up at the same left edge; no highlight should ever stick out past a menu's edge. Also: your call on Seed's edge-to-edge rows vs the old rounded inset style — happy to add a follow-up override if you'd rather have the old look back.
- **Gate feedback (2026-09-27):** the user saw Seed's edge-to-edge rows and asked for the search modal's own rounded-row pattern instead, applied everywhere, with lime pulled back to just nav and primary actions. Redrafted as LIME-21b, replacing this brief's approach entirely.


## LIME-21b — One shared menu style (search modal pattern), lime reserved for nav and primary actions

- **Correction to the brief's own premise:** it opens by saying LIME-21 "was never executed" — it was; landed as `2db5912` and gated ("looks good" wasn't said explicitly, but the user moved straight to reacting to *how it looked*, i.e. they saw it running). This brief replaces that committed work, not an unstarted one — worth the record being accurate about what actually shipped.
- **Root cause of the search modal never overflowing, confirmed by reading the actual markup:** its result rows are real `<button>` elements, which the browser defaults to `box-sizing: border-box`. Every broken menu row across this whole saga (LIME-18-fix4, LIME-20's discovery, LIME-21's Seed items) has been a `<div>` with `width: 100%` plus padding in content-box. Converting the remaining `<div>` rows to real `<button type="button">`s here fixes the same class of bug at its actual source, not just via an explicit `box-sizing` override (kept anyway, for the `<a>`-based notification rows, which don't get the button default).
- **One shared system, replacing LIME-21's Seed-dropdown-based one entirely:** `.lime-menu`/`.lime-menu__item`/`--selected`/`__label`/`__divider`/`__header`/`__check`, built directly from the search modal's own values (confirmed field-for-field before writing anything: container `--soil-bg-elevated`/`--seed-radius-lg`, item `--seed-space-2` padding/`--seed-space-3` gap/`--seed-radius-md`/`--seed-text-sm`, icon 14px `--soil-text-muted`, label `--seed-text-xs` medium). Seed's `dropdown.css` is no longer referenced anywhere in this app — not unlinked yet, since the search modal's own markup never used Seed classes, but nothing in `lime.css` reads from it either now.
- **The search modal itself folded into the shared system:** its own `.lime-search-modal__item`/`__label` rules deleted (fully superseded), its markup renamed to `.lime-menu__item`/`.lime-menu__label`. Found one JS reference this required updating beyond the brief's own explicitly-listed scope (`wireDropdownToggle` for `more-menu` only): `app.js`'s search-modal IIFE queried `.lime-search-modal__item` to wire "click a result closes the modal" — renaming the class without updating this selector would have silently broken that behavior. Updated it, since leaving it would have broken working code as a direct, unavoidable consequence of the explicitly-authorized rename, not a scope expansion.
- **All 7 menus converted:** container → `lime-menu` (+ `lime-menu--wide` for notifications and both voice menus, replacing LIME-21's Seed-token-driven `min-width`). Every `<div class="seed-dropdown__item">` row became a real `<button type="button" class="lime-menu__item">` — toolbar-overflow menus (both), voice menus (both), and the user menu's "Settings" row. Notification rows stayed `<a class="lime-menu__item lime-notif">`, per the brief.
  - **Sign Out lost its red/danger styling** — the brief explicitly wants it as "a normal item, no red" now (a real, deliberate change from LIME-21, not an oversight). Confirmed live: its computed text color is `rgb(19, 27, 23)`, `--soil-text`, not any `--bad-*` red.
  - **Voice menus' check icon:** rebuilt per the brief's more literal instruction — every row gets an empty `<span class="lime-menu__check">` (reserving the 14px slot so names still align), but the `dew-check` icon *inside* it only exists in the selected row's own markup, not present-but-hidden in the others the way LIME-21 did it. Simpler CSS as a result (no visibility toggle needed — confirmed live: the selected row's check is present, every other row's `.lime-menu__check` is genuinely empty).
- **Reactions (neutral):** `.lime-reaction--active` → `--calm-bg-subtle-active` background / `--calm-border-normal-default` border (was Seed's lime `--selected-*` scale). Its own hover has nowhere further to step within the subtle-background tier, so per the brief the *border* deepens instead (`--calm-border-bold-default`) — still a visible change, just via a different channel.
- **Panel dividers (neutral), both the outer left/right handles and the center handle:** hover → `--calm-border-normal-default`, dragging → `--calm-border-bold-default` (were the lime `--selected-bg-subtle-hover`/`-active`). Rewrote the long explanatory comment above the outer handles, which had specifically documented *why* lime was chosen there (to match the nav) — now documents why it's neutral instead, rather than leaving stale reasoning in place.
- **Other lime found, not touched — reporting as questions, per the brief's own instruction not to change anything the user didn't name (menus, reactions, dividers):**
  - `--lime-badge-light` (a separate token, not `--selected-*`-based) — the "Soon" badge on Jam. Sits inside the nav, so arguably already covered by "nav is allowed lime," but flagging since it wasn't explicitly named.
  - `.lime-recent__item--unread`'s `box-shadow` ring, using `--selected-border-bold-default` — an unread-indicator ring around a Recent-row avatar. Not a menu, reaction, or divider; left alone.
  - `.lime-voice__play` (the voice-message play button), using `--selected-bg-bold-default`/`--selected-text-bold-default` (the bold tier, untouched by this brief's subtle/border-tier changes anyway) — a persistent, prominent green button. Also not one of the three named categories; left alone.
- **A near-repeat of the LIME-21 `git checkout` mistake, avoided this time:** after the first successful measurement pass, cleaned up the temporary diagnostic script with a *targeted* `sed` deletion of its exact line range, confirmed immediately afterward that all 30 `.lime-menu__item` occurrences and zero old-class references survived, rather than risking `git checkout` wiping the real changes again the same way it did last time.
- **Verification:** `grep -rn 'lime-nav-dropdown\|lime-dropdown\b\|lime-search-modal__item\|lime-search-modal__label' public/` returns only 4 lines, all comments (three historical, one describing the new shared item's relationship to the old one) — no functional leftovers. Brace count in `lime.css` balanced (412/412). `node --check` passes. Real app loads in jsdom with zero errors; all 7 menus open/close; one-at-a-time and Escape both re-confirmed intact; the search modal opens, and clicking a result correctly closes it (confirming the renamed selector works).
  - **Headless Chrome, live:** item overflow **0px** on every menu I could open non-interactively (notifications, user, more-menu, voice) — confirmed, not just theorized. Every item's `border-radius` computed to `8px`, matching the search modal's own item exactly. The selected voice row's background is `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)` — fully transparent, genuinely no lime — with the checkmark present only in that row's own markup. Simulated an actual reaction click (no reaction is `--active` at page load; that state is session-only, tracked in JS, empty until you react) and confirmed the resulting active chip's background (`rgb(236, 234, 228)`) and border (`rgb(216, 211, 200)`) are both neutral soil tones, nowhere near `#E4F9BE`. The nav's active button and the Send button's active state both still compute `rgb(228, 249, 190)` — confirmed lime survives exactly where the user wants it kept. `more-menu` opens below its trigger, fully on-screen (the amendment's ask). Notifications still opens beside the bell (LIME-20-fix's `placement: 'right'`, unaffected by the class rewrite).
  - **Not independently re-measured:** item hover background (`--calm-bg-subtle-hover`) — headless `--dump-dom` can't force real `:hover` state; confirmed by reading the rule directly instead, same limitation and same workaround as LIME-21. The reply-composer's own overflow and voice menus weren't separately measured (same `.focus()` limitation as LIME-20/21) — they share byte-for-byte identical markup patterns with their already-measured main-composer counterparts.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` in Firefox for the user's check: open every menu — notifications, profile, header "…", both microphone menus, both formatting "…" menus — they should now look like the search modal (rounded rows, grey hover, nothing sticking out). The chosen microphone should show a checkmark, not lime, with names lined up. Reactions should read grey/neutral. Panel dividers should go grey on hover, not lime. Lime itself should still show up on the nav and the Send arrow. Also flagged above as open questions: the "Soon" badge, the unread-avatar ring, and the voice-message play button — let me know if any of those should also lose their lime, or if they're fine as nav-adjacent/primary-action-adjacent exceptions.

## LIME-22 — Close button on the search modal

- Confirmed `dew-close` exists (`grep -o 'dew-close\|dew-x' vendor/seed/icons/dew/dist/dew.css` → `dew-close` only) before writing any markup, matching plot's own pre-check.
- Added the button exactly as specified, last child of `.lime-search-modal__field`, wired to the same `close()` the backdrop and Escape already use. No change needed to the existing focus trap's `focusable()` — it already selects any non-disabled, visible `button`, so a real `<button>` here is picked up automatically; confirmed this directly rather than assuming (see verification).
- **CSS, measured not guessed, per the brief's own instruction:** headless Chrome showed the close glyph's right gap from the modal's edge at `22px` against the search icon's own `16px` left gap — a `6px` difference, not the `8px` (`--seed-space-2`) the brief offered as a *candidate* value. Applying `--seed-space-2` would have landed at `14px`, 2px short of the target and outside this session's usual ±1px tolerance. Used the literal measured `-6px` instead, with a comment explaining why no token fits: the icon-button's own internal centering (36px box around a smaller glyph) adds inset a bare `.dew` glyph never has, which is also why the search icon itself didn't need any adjustment at all.
- **Verification:** `node --check` passes; real app loads in jsdom with zero errors. Clicking the close button closes the modal and returns focus to the trigger — the exact same behavior Escape already has, confirmed by comparing `document.activeElement` before and after. Confirmed the close button is genuinely present in the focus trap's own computed `focusable()` list (not just assumed from reading the selector). Headless Chrome, live: after the `-6px` fix, the close glyph's right gap is exactly `16px`, matching the search icon's left gap with **0px** of difference. The button sits in the field's top row (vertically centered with the search icon and input, same as the rest of that row), satisfying "top right of the modal."

## LIME-23 — Legible neutral "Soon" badge

## LIME-19b — Merged "Messages" list: DMs and groups, live and recency-sorted

- **Survey confirmed every fact the brief assumed**, no discrepancies: the listener-constraint line order (`.lime-contact` rows built once inside the main list IIFE, both later once-at-load click bindings — recent-highlight and the mobile `setView('thread')` router — run after that IIFE completes, so new rows built the same way stay covered); the old `data-scope="teachers"/"groups"` references (index.html tabs/panels, one `app.js` comment, one `lime.css` comment); and the profile panel's behavior on a header-avatar click (`open-profile-avatars` just calls `setRightPanelOpen(true)` — it never sets `data-panel`, so it always shows whichever panel is already active, defaulting to the static "profile" markup regardless of DM or group). Left that panel's contents untouched, per scope — noting here, as instructed, that it is not group-aware.
- **Seed data:** added `conv-011` ("PS 113 Staff Room", 10 participants: teacher-002 + 001/010/013/014/015/016/018/022/024) to `seed-data/conversations.json`, `seed-data/messages.json` (6 new messages, `msg-110`–`msg-115`, from 5 distinct senders including the current user, spanning 2025-07-07/08, last one at 11:32 timestamped later than every other Messages-scope conversation's own latest message so it sorts to the top), and the embedded `public/js/seed-data.js` copy. All three confirmed byte-identical via a Python parse-and-compare, not just eyeballed. Inserted as targeted text edits (matching each file's own existing formatting — compact inline `participants` arrays + `\u`-escaped unicode in the two `seed-data/*.json` files, expanded arrays + literal unicode in `seed-data.js`, exactly as those files already did before this brief) rather than a wholesale `json.dumps` regeneration, which would have reformatted every pre-existing record's `participants` array along with it.
- **`data.js`:** added `getMessageConversations` (direct + group, current user), `otherParticipants` (participants minus self, in seed order), `firstName`/`joinNames` (for the unnamed-group title fallback — not exercised by any of today's seed data, every group so far has an explicit `name`, but implemented per spec), `getConversationTitle`, `getLatestActivity` (last item of `getMessagesByConversation`, replies included, or `null`).
- **`app.js`, the list IIFE:** replaced the old `getDirectConversations()`-only render with one over `getMessageConversations()`, sorted newest-activity-first (no-activity conversations last, alphabetical among themselves) — `sortConversations()`. Added `orderedOthers()` (most-recent-speaker-first, ties broken by seed participant order) used by both the list's avatar cluster and the thread header. Added the group avatar cluster (`avatarClusterHtml`/`avatarClusterMemberHtml`) and a `directAvatarHtml` split out from the old inline markup. `conversationRowHtml`/`rowPreviewHtml`/`conversationSearchText` build each row; `rowPreviewHtml` splices a "Sender: "/"You: " prefix into `previewFor`'s existing `.lime-contact__preview-text` span for groups, rather than duplicating its voice/location/text branching. `renderThread` no longer takes a `teacher` param — resolves each message's sender via `getTeacherById(m.sender_id)`, falling back to a placeholder `{ display_name: 'Unknown', status: 'offline' }` if a sender id doesn't resolve (untested by today's data, every sender id is valid). `selectConversation` no longer takes `teacher` either — sets the breadcrumb via `getConversationTitle` and rebuilds `#open-profile-avatars`'s contents on every call via `conversationHeaderAvatarsHtml` (DM: unchanged two-avatar markup; group: up to 5 most-recent-speaker-first faces, then a neutral "+N" tile if more, then a muted "N members" label) — this button's contents used to be static "Shem R"/"Jean Chung" markup that never updated regardless of which conversation was open; it now actually reflects the open conversation for the first time.
- **Live updates:** `handleSend` and `submitReply` (the latter resolving the parent message's `conversation_id` before calling `sendReply`) each dispatch `document.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('lime:activity', { detail: { conversationId } }))`. The list IIFE's own listener updates that row's preview/time in place and re-sorts by moving existing `<li>` nodes (`list.appendChild` on an already-attached node relocates it) — never recreates, per the load-time-listener constraint.
- **Tabs/markup:** `data-scope="teachers"` → `"messages"` (label "Teachers" → "Messages"), `data-scope="communities"` label "Community" → "Communities", Group Chat tab and its static panel deleted outright, "All Teachers" heading → "All", `#crumb-teachers`'s initial text → "Messages" (id kept, per the brief — nothing depends on the string, and the existing code comment already documents that the id no longer matches its label), segmented-pill width `/3` → `/2`.
- **A real bug found and fixed, not in the brief's own list:** the group thread header's new "N members" label was rendering as "**0** members" for the 10-person group — the "1" glyph was genuinely being painted over by the rightmost overlapping avatar, confirmed by comparing `.lime-topbar__member-count`'s DOM `textContent` (correctly "10 members") against its live rendered position versus the avatar-group's own children's `getBoundingClientRect()`s. Root cause: `.seed-avatar-group`'s negative-margin overlap stacking (used for the "cascade of circles" look) makes its children's actual painted extent bigger than the flex layout believes the group's box to be, so a fixed `gap`/`margin-left` calculated from the group's own layout box under-shoots the real edge. Fixed empirically (measured, not computed, per this project's standing rule) by giving `.lime-topbar__member-count` a `margin-left: 20px` sized to clear the worst case — and this component's worst case is fixed and known: `HEADER_AVATAR_CAP = 5` means at most 6 avatar-group children ever exist (5 faces + one "+N" tile), regardless of how large a group's real membership is, so this fixed value covers every group, not just the 10-person one it was tuned against. Re-measured after the fix: 4px of genuine clear space between the rightmost avatar and the label, confirmed both via `getBoundingClientRect()` and a cropped screenshot.
- **Verification:** `node --check` on both `app.js`/`data.js`; `lime.css` brace count balanced (434/434 after the new cluster CSS); `grep -rn 'data-scope="groups"\|Group Chat' public/` returns nothing. Real app loads in jsdom with zero errors. Confirmed live: "All" list renders exactly 7 rows, newest-activity-first (PS 113 Staff Room → Jean → Alexi → PS 113 7th Grade Team → Jean/Mary/Jimin & Me → Math Teachers NYC → Kai last, the one conversation with zero messages). Opening "PS 113 7th Grade Team" renders the correct per-message sender for all 3 real senders in that thread (Jean, Shem/me, Priya) and sets the breadcrumb to the group name. Sending in Alexi's DM moves it to the top of the list with the new text; replying in Jean's DM updates her row's preview and moves her conversation back to the top. Clicking a row still sets `data-mobile-view="thread"` (the load-time listener constraint held). Confirmed a group message sent by the current user prefixes its row preview with "You: ", not a name.
  - **Headless Chrome, live, both breakpoints (1567px and 767px):** two tabs split the segmented pill exactly evenly. The 3-member triangle cluster (conv-006) measures exactly as designed — 22px circles at (9,0)/(0,18)/(18,18) inside the 40px frame, flush to its edges, `x`-aligned with the plain DM avatar frames in the same list. The 2×2 grid cluster (conv-011, 9 "others") shows 20px circles at (0,0)/(19,0)/(0,19)/(19,19) with a "+6" tile in the last slot (9 others − 3 shown faces = 6, correct). conv-011's thread header shows 5 faces + "+4" + "10 members" (post-fix, legible) at both widths, with **0px** overlap against the "…" more-menu button at either width and no horizontal page overflow (`scrollWidth === innerWidth` at both 767 and 1567). No cluster tuning beyond the header-label fix above was needed — the brief's own suggested starting offsets measured correct on the first pass.
  - **Not independently re-measured:** the unnamed-group title fallback (`joinNames`) and the `renderThread` unknown-sender placeholder — neither is exercised by any conversation in today's seed data (every group has an explicit name; every message's `sender_id` resolves). Implemented per spec, confirmed by code reading, not by a live trigger.
- **Flagging per the brief's own gate instruction:** the thread header caps group avatars at 5 faces before switching to "+N" — the brief itself flagged this as something the user might want raised, given it needs to share a row with the breadcrumb and the "…" button even at mobile width. Measured comfortably fits at both 1567px and 767px with room to spare (see above), so there's slack if 6 or 7 is preferred instead.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` for the user's check: the left list should now be one "All" section covering both 1:1s and groups (Recent/Starred above it are still the old static placeholders — Starred is LIME-19c's job), sorted with the most recently active conversation on top; group rows should show a small avatar cluster plus a member count after the name; opening "PS 113 Staff Room" or "PS 113 7th Grade Team" should show each message under its real sender and the group's name in both the breadcrumb and the top-right avatar cluster ("10 members"/"4 members"); sending or replying anywhere should immediately re-sort that conversation to the top of the list. Two tabs now: Messages, Communities.


## LIME-19b-fix — Group header avatars readable and ordered, list member count removed

**Survey confirmed all four of the brief's root causes exactly as diagnosed**, against the actual post-19b code:
- `.lime-topbar__avatars` was fixed at 36px wide with `margin-left: -20px` on each 28px avatar — sized for a DM's fixed 2 avatars. A group's 5–6 avatars each showed only a sliver, and the row overflowed that 36px box to the left.
- The hover highlight only ever covered that fixed 36px box, not the avatars spilling out past it — reading as "broken" because the highlighted area and the visible avatars didn't line up.
- `conversationHeaderAvatarsHtml` appended the "+N" tile last in DOM, and `.seed-avatar-group` is `row-reverse` (first DOM child renders rightmost) — so "+N" landed leftmost, the opposite end from where it reads naturally next to "N members".
- `.lime-topbar__member-count`'s `margin-left: 20px` was a fixed value tuned against one group's overflow amount, which differs by avatar count — so the gap before "N members" was inconsistent between group sizes.

**The change, exactly per the brief:**
- **List:** removed the member-count span and the now-purposeless `.lime-contact__name-row` flex wrapper it required — the wrapper's *only* job was holding name+count on one line, so with the count gone it's dead structure, not something the brief needed to separately call out. `.lime-contact__name` reverts to being `.lime-contact__body`'s sole first child; its own ellipsis/truncation properties (added in 19b) still apply exactly the same, wrapper or not. Deleted `.lime-contact__name-row` and `.lime-contact__count` from `lime.css` together, since both existed for the same now-removed feature.
- **Group header markup:** `conversationHeaderAvatarsHtml`'s group branch now builds its DOM children backwards — the "+N" tile (if any) first, then the shown avatars reversed (5th-most-recent-speaker down to most-recent) — so `row-reverse` renders them left to right in the intended reading order: most recent speaker … 5th, then "+N" at the right end. Added a `lime-topbar__avatars--group` class alongside the existing `lime-topbar__avatars` one (kept, not replaced, so the DM's own untouched rules still apply where relevant, e.g. the mobile `data-mobile-view="contacts"` rule at `lime.css` ~3345 that targets `.lime-topbar__avatars .seed-avatar` generically).
- **Group header CSS:** `.lime-topbar__avatars--group { width: auto; margin-right: 0; }` lets the box size to its real content instead of the DM's fixed 36px. `.lime-topbar__avatars--group .seed-avatar { width: 28px; height: 28px; margin-left: -8px; border-color: var(--lime-cluster-ring, var(--soil-bg-canvas)); }` — Seed's own `:last-child` rule (3-selector specificity vs. this rule's 2) still wins and zeroes that one avatar's margin, same reasoning already documented on the DM rule just above it. `.lime-avatar-trigger:hover` now also sets `--lime-cluster-ring: var(--calm-bg-subtle-hover)`, so the overlap rings blend into the hover pill instead of showing the old fixed `--soil-bg-surface` against it. **One necessary deviation from the brief's literal selector:** wrote it as the chained `.lime-topbar__avatars.lime-topbar__avatars--group` (two classes) rather than the brief's bare `.lime-topbar__avatars--group` (one class) — the 1024px media query's own `.lime-topbar__avatars { width: 32px }` rule has equal specificity to a single-class selector and sits later in the file, so a single-class override would have lost the cascade at narrower widths and the group header would've reverted to a fixed, squeezed box exactly at the width where "fits at 767px" matters most. Chaining both classes raises this rule's specificity above the media query's, so it wins everywhere. Confirmed by measuring at 767px (see below) rather than assuming.
- **Spacing:** removed `.lime-topbar__member-count`'s `margin-left: 20px` hack entirely; `.lime-avatar-trigger`'s `gap` changed from `--seed-space-1` to `--seed-space-2`, now the label's only spacing. This works now — where it didn't in 19b — because the avatar-group's layout box used to be artificially clamped to a fixed 36px regardless of its real (overlap-adjusted) content width, which is what made the flex `gap` calculation wrong; with `width: auto` the box's layout edge now actually matches its visual edge, so a plain `gap` is sufficient and no group-size-dependent margin is needed.
- Comments on `.lime-avatar-trigger` and `.lime-topbar__member-count` rewritten to describe the new reasoning rather than leaving the old (now-wrong) workaround explanation in place.

**Verification:** `node --check` passes on `app.js`; `lime.css` brace count balanced (434/434). Real app loads in jsdom with zero errors; list rows contain no `.lime-contact__count` anywhere (`grep -c` returns 0 in `app.js`, `lime.css`, and `index.html`); all of LIME-19b's own jsdom checks re-run clean (7-row sort order, per-message senders, live reorder on send/reply, mobile-view listener) — no regression.
- **Headless Chrome, live, both breakpoints (1567px and 767px), using the exact x/y rects of every avatar tile, not just their own box sizes:**
  - **conv-011 (10 members, 9 others):** left-to-right DOM/visual order is Jean, Grace, Priya, Aisha, Marcus, "+4" — exactly most-recent-speaker-first (matches the seed messages' actual send order) with "+4" rightmost. Computed each tile's *actually visible* (unoccluded) width from its neighbors' overlap, not just its own 32px box (28px + Seed's 2px border ×2, confirmed by content-box math, same on both DM and group tiles): the frontmost (Jean) is fully visible at 32px: every other tile is 24px visible — comfortably clear of the brief's 18px floor.
  - **conv-006 (4 members, 3 others):** same order (Jean, Marcus, Priya), same 24px-visible pattern for the two non-frontmost tiles.
  - **Gap to "N members":** exactly 8px for both conv-011 and conv-006 — identical, not just close, satisfying "the same for conv-011 and conv-006" more strictly than the brief's own ±1px tolerance required.
  - **Containment:** every avatar tile and the "N members" label sit fully inside `#open-profile-avatars`'s own rect for all three cases (conv-011, conv-006, Jean's DM) — no overflow past the trigger's own box, at either width.
  - **767px:** conv-011's header right edge sits 4px clear of the "…" more-menu button (719 vs. 723) — no overlap, confirming the chained-class specificity fix actually holds at the width it was written for, not just assumed.
  - **DM unchanged:** Jean's DM header still orders Shem-then-Jean with the base (non-`--group`) rules only — no `--group` class present, `--seed-space-1`→`--seed-space-2` gap change has no visual effect on a single-child flex container, confirmed by direct comparison against the same measurement taken during LIME-19b.
- Opened `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/index.html` for the user's check: list rows show no numbers after group names anymore. Opening "PS 113 Staff Room" should show five readable faces (not slivers), then "+4", then "10 members", all clearly legible; opening "PS 113 7th Grade Team" should show the same spacing before "4 members". Hovering the header avatar area should highlight the whole avatar-and-label row, not just part of it. DMs should look unchanged.
- **Gate feedback (2026-09-27): "looks good, only the dm changes are missing the same updates as there."** Right — the brief explicitly scoped the DM header as "unchanged," but that instruction predates seeing the group fix; once the group looked right, the old DM-only rules read as the same bug in a milder form (2 avatars fit inside their fixed 36px box exactly, so no overflow, but the ring was still the old hardcoded `--soil-bg-surface`, not tracking hover, and the -20px overlap left the non-frontmost avatar showing less of itself than the group's now does). **Direct user instruction, not a plot brief — applying it now as a same-day follow-up rather than waiting for a redraft, since it's a small, low-risk, fully reversible CSS/markup unification of a system this same brief just built.**
  - Unified `.lime-topbar__avatars`/`.lime-topbar__avatars .seed-avatar` to the group's own approach (`width: auto`, `margin-left: -8px`, ring tracks `--lime-cluster-ring`) and deleted the now-redundant `--group`-suffixed rules and the `lime-topbar__avatars--group` class in `app.js` — one shared system for DM and group alike, not two tuned-separately ones.
  - Removed the 767px media query's own `.lime-topbar__avatars { width: 32px; height: 32px }` override, which would otherwise have reintroduced the exact same fixed-box clamp this brief just removed, at exactly the width where it matters most. Updated the comment above it (previously described shrinking "the icon buttons + avatar group slightly" — now only the icon buttons do).
  - **Verified live, not assumed:** `node --check` passes; `lime.css` brace count balanced (431/431, down from 434 — net rules removed, not added). `grep -c 'lime-topbar__avatars--group'` returns 0 in both `app.js` and `lime.css`. Real app loads in jsdom with zero errors; all of LIME-19b's own checks re-run clean. Headless Chrome, live: Jean's DM header now shows the exact same overlap pattern as the groups (Jean fully visible at 32px as the frontmost avatar, Shem 24px visible behind her — previously both were squeezed into the fixed 36px box with much less of Shem showing), and `containsAll` (every avatar inside the trigger's own rect) still holds for the DM, conv-006, and conv-011 alike. A cropped screenshot of the DM header confirms both initials ("JC", "SR") are now fully legible, not partially hidden.
- **One correction to PLOT.md's own continuation note:** it says the `67a4107` follow-up commit "has no `Brief:` trailer... left as is, not rewritten." Checked via `git log -1 --format=%B 67a4107` — it does carry `Brief: LIME-19b-fix`. Noting the correction here rather than repeating the wrong claim, same as the LIME-21/21b precedent earlier in this file.


## LIME-24a — Data model and Supabase schema draft (docs only)

Docs-only brief: no app code touched, nothing to `node --check` or load in jsdom. Verification here means careful reading, cross-checked against the actual source files the brief's shared context was built from, not command output.

- **Survey, against the real files (not just the brief's summary of them):** read `seed-data/seed.ts`, `seed-data/useMockData.ts`, `public/login.html`, and `README.md` directly.
  - Confirmed the demo-session shape (`{email, displayName}` in `localStorage['lime-demo-session']`, set by `login.html` after checking one credential from the gitignored `public/js/demo-config.local.js`) exactly as the brief stated.
  - Confirmed `useMockData.ts`'s `Message.type` union is `'text' | 'voice' | 'location' | 'image'` — `image` isn't exercised by any seed record today, but it's part of the real contract, so `messages.type`'s check constraint in `schema.sql` includes it even though nothing currently produces it.
  - **Found and flagged two things the brief's own shared context didn't mention, per its instruction to flag anything inconsistent with the schema, rather than silently resolving or silently ignoring them:**
    1. **`seed-data/seed.ts`'s reactor-selection algorithm doesn't dedupe senders before slicing**, and would violate `message_reactions`' own `(message_id, user_id, emoji)` primary key if run against this schema as written. Traced a concrete, real example rather than reasoning abstractly: `conv-001`'s `msg-006` carries a `{emoji: "😍", count: 5}` reaction; the first 5 messages in `conv-001` (raw file order, confirmed by reading `messages.json` directly rather than through `getMessagesByConversation`'s sorted view) are sent by teacher-002, teacher-001, teacher-002, teacher-001, teacher-002 — only 2 distinct senders among the 5 slots `seed.ts` would try to insert. Documented in full in `schema.sql`, right above the table it breaks, with a suggested fix (`[...new Set(reactors)].slice(0, count)`) and a note that LIME-24b's local adapter — which production-ready principle 4 requires to match `seed.ts`'s "exact" algorithm — should match a *corrected* version of it, not reproduce the same bug locally.
    2. **Community-type conversations in `seed-data/conversations.json` carry two fields the proposed schema doesn't account for:** `avatar_color` (a hex string) and `member_count` (a plain number, e.g. 847). Neither fits cleanly: `avatar_color` is presentational and unique to communities, and `member_count` directly contradicts the schema's own principle 2 (a real count should come from `conversation_members`, not a stored duplicate) — confirmed none of the 9 seed communities actually lists that many participant ids, so the stored numbers are display-mockup placeholders, not real membership data. Left unresolved and flagged in both `schema.sql` and `data-model.md`, deferred to LIME-28 (live Communities) rather than guessed at now, since Communities are still a static mockup and nothing today depends on this being decided.
- **`docs/schema.sql`:** the 5 tables (`profiles`, `conversations`, `conversation_members`, `messages`, `message_reactions`) with the brief's exact added columns (`conversations.deleted_at`, the three `conversation_members` per-user columns, `profiles.email unique`, `message_reactions`' composite uniqueness — implemented as its primary key rather than a separate unique constraint plus a surrogate id, since the brief's own contract never calls for reactions to be referenced by their own id anywhere), the two requested indexes, and an RLS section written entirely as commented-out `create policy` statements covering all five listed cases (member read, member insert, owner-only rename/delete, self-only membership update, public community read) — not enabled, per the brief.
- **`docs/data-model.md`:** the 8 principles restated in the user's and plot's own language (not copy-pasted verbatim, but nothing reworded away from its meaning); the entity diagram as a text list; the UI-concept-to-table mapping table; the full store API from the brief's own item 3 (reads, writes, events, lifecycle), copied faithfully; the auth seam section; and the switch checklist, including a step explicitly calling back to the reactor-dedup fix as a prerequisite for running `seed.ts` for real.
- **No SQL execution available to verify `schema.sql` against** (no `psql`, no local Postgres, no `docker` in this environment — confirmed by checking, not assumed) — matches the brief's own "no code to run" capability assumption. Verified instead by a careful line-by-line re-read after writing (checking `references`/`on delete` targets exist, `check` constraint value lists match the real seed data's actual observed values, parens balance) and by cross-referencing every claimed column against the actual JSON shapes read during survey.
- Nothing else was touched — `git status` shows only the two new files under `docs/` plus this `TEND.md` entry.

**Gate:** the user skims `docs/data-model.md` — mainly the principles and the switch checklist — to confirm it matches their intent, and plot reviews the full contract (including the two flagged inconsistencies above) before LIME-24b is sent.


## LIME-24a-fix — Auth-linked profiles, recursion-safe RLS, DM uniqueness, reactor rule (docs only)

Plot's review of `9e5506f` found five issues; all five addressed, docs only, no app code or seed data touched.

1. **Auth-linked profiles.** Added `profiles.auth_user_id uuid unique references auth.users(id)` (nullable). Added `current_profile_id()` (`stable security definer`, resolves `auth.uid()` → `profiles.id`) and rewrote every policy to compare against it instead of `auth.uid()` directly — the original draft's policies compared a uuid to a text column and would never have matched anything. Documented the two onboarding paths in `data-model.md`'s auth seam section (new signup → profile row created with `auth_user_id` set; existing seed teacher → linked by email on first login) and added both the helper-creation and onboarding-wiring steps to the switch checklist.
2. **Recursion-safe RLS, completed.** Added `is_member(conv_id)` and `is_owner(conv_id)` (same `security definer` pattern) so no policy on `conversation_members` queries `conversation_members` from inside itself — Postgres RLS policies recurse infinitely when they do.
