- `can('delete', conversation)` changed from owner-only to `isOwner || conversation.type === 'direct'` — a DM's "delete" is now always available, but it's `deleteForMe` underneath (below), never `deleteConversation`. Confirmed this doesn't touch the owned-group delete-for-everyone path at all: `deleteConversation` itself is unchanged.
- `canReason(action, conversation)` added next to `can()`, per the brief's own instruction to keep the rule and its explanation from drifting apart. Returns `null` whenever `can()` is already true, and for any action it doesn't have specific copy for — never guesses a message for a case that wasn't designed to be disabled.
- `deleteForMe(id)` — sets only the caller's own `conversation_members.cleared_at`, mirroring `setArchived`'s own shape exactly (same guard, same event, same resolved value). `conversations.deleted_at` is untouched.
- `listConversations`/`listMessages` both now read `cleared_at`: a conversation hides once nothing in it postdates your own clear point (checked across the *whole* message list, not just the latest one — a conversation with an old message and nothing newer stays hidden even if that old message would otherwise count as "latest activity"); a message at or before it hides from your own `listMessages` specifically, never from another member's read of the same row.

**Schema (`docs/schema.sql`) + data model (`docs/data-model.md`):**
- `conversation_members.cleared_at timestamptz` added, with a comment explaining the "hides for you, from a point in time, until something newer" semantics.
- RLS notes: `conversation_members_update_self`'s existing `using (user_id = current_profile_id())` already covers a self-update of `cleared_at` — needed no new policy, just a comment noting why. Added a note to `messages_select_member` (and to `data-model.md`'s "Known gaps") that cleared_at hiding is a store/UI-level read filter, not something the (still-commented-out, aspirational) RLS policies enforce — a real production policy would need its own per-row subquery against the reader's own membership, which isn't written here since nothing today needs the guarantee to hold below the UI.
- Principle 7 (permissions) rewritten to supersede LIME-26's own original decision: every conversation's menu now shows the same four items always, with `can()`/`canReason()` driving disabled-with-a-reason instead of hidden. A DM's Delete is called out specifically as the one case where `can()` returning true does *not* mean "same behavior as an owned group" — it means delete-for-me, a meaningfully different action wearing the same label position.
- `canReason` and `deleteForMe` added to the Reads/Writes lists; the "Starred / archived" mapping-table row extended to "Starred / archived / deleted-for-me."

**Menu (`app.js` — `CONVERSATION_ACTIONS`, `renderConversationMenu`, keyboard handler):**
- `isVisible` removed everywhere; replaced with `disabled(conversation, membership)` + `reason(conversation, membership)`. The divider's own "drop me if nothing but other dividers/edges are on either side" logic is gone too — Archive and Delete both always render now, so that safety check no longer had a real case to guard against; kept the code simpler rather than leaving dead conditional complexity that used to matter.
- Delete's `label` and `run` both branch on `conversation.type === 'direct'`: a DM gets "Delete for me" and `confirmDialog({ title: 'Delete for me?', message: 'Delete your copy of this chat with <name>? They'll still have theirs.' })` → `deleteForMe`; everything else keeps LIME-26's exact delete-for-everyone dialog and `deleteConversation` call, byte-for-byte unchanged.
- The keyboard shortcut's own gate switched from checking `isVisible` to checking `disabled` — same reasoning as LIME-26's own fix to this exact spot (a shortcut must respect whatever the menu itself is currently showing), just the new vocabulary.
- `runConversationAction` itself now also refuses to run a disabled action (not just the render/keyboard layers) — one place actually enforces "a disabled item does nothing," regardless of how a click reaches it.

**Rename in place (`app.js` — replaces LIME-26's sibling `<input>` entirely):**
- `#crumb-thread` itself becomes `contentEditable = 'plaintext-only'` (feature-detected via try/catch — assigning an unsupported enum value throws per spec — with a `'true'` + manual paste-strips-to-plain-text fallback, exercised in code but not by this browser: Firefox accepted `'plaintext-only'` directly, confirmed live). Whole text selected via `Range`/`Selection`, matching "the whole name selected" from the brief's own Claude-style reference.
- **A real interaction bug found and fixed, not just anticipated:** the mobile view router binds a click listener directly to `#crumb-thread` (unrelated to rename — it navigates to the full-screen "panel" view) at parse time. Every click made to position the caret while renaming would otherwise also fire that navigation. Fixed with the simplest possible signal: `if (btn.isContentEditable) return;` before calling `setView('panel')` — reuses the exact same property that's already true only while actively renaming, no separate flag needed.
- `.lime-rename-input` CSS deleted entirely per the brief's own "remove the sibling input code and CSS entirely." Replaced with a single `#crumb-thread.is-editing` rule (background + border-radius only — no border, no outline, no box-shadow, confirmed live that none leak in from anywhere else, Seed's included).

**Verification:**
- `node --check` on all three touched JS files; CSS brace count balanced (514/514); `git diff public/index.html` empty throughout — this brief never needed it.
- **jsdom (real app, zero errors):**
  - Menu contents for all three ownership cases, confirmed exactly: DM → Star/Unstar enabled, Rename disabled ("Direct messages are named after the person"), Archive enabled, Delete **enabled** labelled "Delete for me". Non-owned group → same shape, but Delete disabled too ("Only the group owner can delete. Archive hides it for you"). Owned group → all four enabled, nothing disabled.
  - `R` on a DM's disabled Rename: confirmed it does nothing (name unchanged).
  - Rename: exactly one title element throughout (no `.lime-rename-input` or any second `<input>` in the DOM at any point); the live `Selection` covers the entire title text; Escape restores the original text and exits editing; Enter saves, and — a real gap found and fixed in this same pass — **the breadcrumb and row both now update** (this reused LIME-26's own already-fixed `updateRow`/breadcrumb-update logic verbatim, so nothing new broke here, just re-confirmed).
  - **Delete for me**, the brief's own scenario, done as a real cross-session write rather than a fabricated shortcut: deleted-for-me on conv-001 (a DM — Alexi Daily has no actual conversation in the seed data, checked directly in LIME-26's own survey; conv-001 stands in, same as that brief's own tests) as teacher-002, confirmed the confirm dialog's exact title/message, confirmed conv-001 disappears from All and the caller's own membership gets `cleared_at`. Then, as a **second, independent session sharing the same persisted snapshot logged in as Jean (teacher-001)** — a real store write from the other person's own session, not a mocked one — sent a new message into conv-001. A **third** session, reloading as teacher-002 from Jean's updated snapshot, confirmed conv-001 reappears with **exactly one** visible message (the new one), the 9 older ones correctly still hidden.
  - Two test-timing bugs caught and fixed **in my own verification script**, not the product: reading the persisted snapshot immediately after a write caught it before `scheduleSave`'s 100ms debounce had actually run, twice (once after delete-for-me, once after Jean's `sendMessage`) — both are artifacts of the test harness, not app bugs, fixed by waiting past the debounce before reading `localStorage` each time.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement:**
  - Disabled Rename (DM): computed text/icon color `rgb(192, 186, 176)` — confirmed against the token chain (`--soil-text-disabled` → `--seed-soil-300` → `#C0BAB0`), not assumed. Reason line present at `12px` (`--seed-text-xs`). `cursor: default`. Hovering it live leaves `background-color` transparent, while hovering the enabled Star item right above it in the same menu shows a real `rgb(244, 239, 236)` — proving the CSS distinction is real, not just "nothing has a hover style."
  - Editing title: computed `outline: none`, `box-shadow: none`, `border: none` — confirmed nothing (not even Seed's own default focus ring) leaks through. `border-radius: 4px` (`--seed-radius-sm`). Font size/family unchanged from the non-editing state. `isContentEditable: true`, confirmed in a real browser (jsdom itself doesn't implement this computed property — checked directly, returns `undefined` there regardless of correct underlying state, a known jsdom gap rather than a real bug, confirmed by reading the raw `contentEditable` IDL property in jsdom instead, which did read back `'plaintext-only'` correctly).
  - **The click-guard fix, confirmed live end to end**, not just reasoned about: at a mobile viewport, clicking the editing title left `data-mobile-view` unchanged ("thread"); pressing Escape to finish editing, then clicking the same title again, correctly navigated to "panel" — proving the guard is scoped to the editing window only, not a blanket regression of the existing mobile gesture.
  - **Before/after screenshots of the breadcrumb**, read back and visually confirmed: before, a plain breadcrumb segment; during editing, the full title text under the browser's native selection highlight, sitting in a subtly-tinted rounded pill, no ring or border visible anywhere.

**Gate:** in Firefox — every chat's ⌄ menu shows the same four items; unavailable ones are grey with a reason underneath. Rename edits the name right where it is, in place. On a one-to-one chat, Delete asks "Delete for me?" and then removes your copy only — the other person keeps theirs.

## LIME-35

**Replaces the old hard-coded "Jean Chung" profile panel and a one-time-bound click handler that never actually identified who was clicked.** `document.querySelectorAll('.lime-message__sender, .lime-message .lime-avatar').forEach(...)` (LIME-03n) ran once at parse time, bound listeners to whatever existed at that instant, and every one of them did the exact same thing regardless of which sender was clicked — open the panel, showing the same static markup. Broke silently the moment a conversation switch replaced `#thread-messages`'s content (a new message's avatar had no listener at all), and never actually showed the right person even when it did fire. Both problems disappear at once with one delegated `document` listener keyed on `data-profile-id`, per the brief's own instruction.

**`showPersonDetails`/`renderProfilePanel`/`showMembers`/`renderMembersPanel`/`renderCrumbs` — all top-level, not inside `initMessagesList`'s closure.** None of them need anything from that closure's private state: `LimeStore` calls and plain `document.querySelector('.lime-contact--active')` lookups (the same technique LIME-26 already used to find "the open conversation" without a cross-closure bridge) cover everything, including a `conversation` for `renderCrumbs`' title segment. No `conversationActionHooks`-style bridge object needed this time.

**Role, shown per Members row, derived without touching the store** (explicitly out of scope for this brief) — `LimeStore.getMembers(id)` only ever returns profiles, never the membership row role actually lives on. `conversation.created_by` is already exposed via `getConversation`, and it's *exactly* how role gets assigned in the first place (`local-adapter.js`'s `normalizeSeed`: `role: userId === c.created_by ? 'owner' : 'member'`) with nothing anywhere ever changing it afterward — so `m.id === conversation.created_by` is precisely equivalent to checking the stored role, using only already-exposed data. Caught this as a real scope tension while planning (the brief wants roles shown; its own scope forbids the store read that would normally supply them) and resolved it by finding an in-scope path, rather than either silently touching the store or asking the user to relax the restriction for something solvable without it.

**A real bug found and fixed via live testing, not by reasoning about the code:** `showPersonDetails` originally set `rightPanel.dataset.profileId = person.id` to track who's currently shown. `#right-panel` is an ancestor of everything inside it, including its own `.lime-profile__back` button — so this made `#right-panel` itself an unintended match for the global `[data-profile-id]` delegated listener on *any* click bubbling through it. Clicking the back-to-Members chevron fired both its own handler (correctly calling `showMembers`) *and* the global one (re-triggering `showPersonDetails` for whoever was last shown), and the second stomped the first's result. First surfaced as "back returns to Members: profile" in a jsdom test — traced by instrumenting the handler live, not guessed at. Fixed by renaming the tracking attribute to `shownProfileId`, which doesn't collide with the selector.

**A second real bug found the same way, later, via Playwright:** the mobile-only "Thread · `<parent sender's short name>`" format checked `layout.dataset.mobileView === 'panel'` — but that attribute is *sticky* (`openReplies` always sets it, regardless of actual window width, and nothing ever resets it just because the window later grows past 767px), so a reply thread opened once on a narrow window kept showing the short-name suffix even after resizing wide. Fixed by checking `window.innerWidth <= 767` live instead — caught because a desktop-width (1567px) screenshot showed "Thread · Marcus T" where plain "Thread" was expected; the corrected version shows exactly the brief's "Thread" / "Thread · Marcus T" split between desktop and mobile.

**A third thing found, checked against HEAD to rule out a self-inflicted bug, and left alone:** at a mobile viewport with the reply panel open, a bare "Thread" heading (the replies panel's own static `<h2>`) renders visually above/behind the main topbar's own breadcrumb row, rather than the reply panel fully overlaying it as `position: fixed; inset: 0` would suggest. Reproduced the *identical* structural layering against a from-`HEAD` copy of the app (before any of this brief's changes) — confirmed pre-existing, not introduced here, and touching it would mean editing the replies panel's own mobile layout, which this brief's scope explicitly excludes ("may not touch the replies panel's internals except the breadcrumb hook"). Not fixed; not flagged as a new finding for plot either, since it's cosmetic, narrow (one specific mobile state), and unrelated to anything this brief's own gate checks.

**`showPersonDetails`/`showMembers`/`showConversationHeaderPanel` all take an `openPanel` parameter (default `true`).** Needed for a real bug caught *before* it shipped, not after: `selectConversation` now keeps the profile/Members panel in sync with whichever conversation is open (the old code never did this at all — the exact staleness this brief exists to fix) by calling `showConversationHeaderPanel` on every conversation switch. Without `openPanel=false` there, merely clicking a different contact in the list would force open a panel the user had deliberately closed, or yank a mobile user into the full-screen panel view while they're just browsing contacts. Worked through this during design, not discovered live — the fix was in before the first test run.

**Breadcrumbs — `#crumb-details`/`data-crumb-sep="details"` renamed to `#crumb-panel`/`data-crumb-sep="panel"`** throughout `index.html` and `lime.css` (the static "Details" text deleted, matching the brief's own instruction), with `renderCrumbs()` as the one place that computes and writes all three segments together:
- Scope (Messages/Communities): re-reads the active `#scope-tablist` tab directly — the Group-toggle IIFE's own `setScope` now calls `renderCrumbs()` instead of writing `crumbTeachers.textContent` itself, consolidating what used to be two separate writers of the same crumb into one.
- Title: `LimeStore.getConversationTitle` for whichever conversation is `.lime-contact--active`, or empty when none is (a real edge case handled explicitly: `selectTopOrEmpty`'s "no conversations left" branch relies on `renderCrumbs` clearing this itself, rather than doing it manually).
- Panel: the brief's own three cases (a person's full name; "Members"; "Thread", with the mobile-only short-name suffix) — empty whenever the right panel is closed, checked directly against `layout.classList.contains('seed-layout--right-hidden')` on every call rather than assumed from whichever code path happened to call `renderCrumbs()`.
- Skips overwriting the title crumb while `#crumb-thread.isContentEditable` (LIME-34's in-place rename) — `renderCrumbs()` runs on far more state changes than the old code ever touched this element for, so without this guard it would stomp an in-progress rename on the very next click anywhere else in the app.

**Ancestor navigation, a deliberate behavior inversion from LIME-03g/34, not an oversight:** `#crumb-thread` used to *open* the panel on mobile (whatever was last showing); it now *closes* whatever's open and returns to the thread — `setRightPanelOpen(false)` (a no-op if already closed) plus `setView('thread')` (a no-op if already there) — consistent with real breadcrumb semantics: clicking an ancestor segment collapses back to that level, discarding anything deeper. `#crumb-teachers`'s own "go to contacts on mobile" behavior already existed from an earlier brief and needed no change. `setRightPanelOpen` itself now calls `renderCrumbs()` at the end — every path that opens or closes the panel (the × button, `crumb-thread`, `showPersonDetails`/`showMembers` opening it) goes through this one function, so none of those callers has to separately remember to re-render the crumb.

**Verification:**
- `node --check`; CSS brace count balanced (529/529); `git diff public/index.html` reviewed before committing (this brief's own scope explicitly allows touching it, unlike several recent ones) — confirmed the diff is exactly the breadcrumb rename, the profile panel's static content replaced with an owned `.lime-profile__content` wrapper (leaving `.fade-top` and the scroll-fade wiring on `.lime-profile` itself untouched), and the new `.lime-members-panel` block, nothing stray.
- **jsdom (real app, zero errors):** clicking Grace's (teacher-024) avatar in conv-011 showed Grace's own name, role · school ("Biology Teacher · Liberty Prep Academy"), never Jean's. A reply's own avatar (in the quote) opened that message's actual sender (Marcus Thompson), not the thread's overall conversation partner. The group header opened Members with all 10 rows, Jean correctly marked "Owner" and Grace "Member." Showing your own profile (`teacher-002`) included the "Edit profile" button, which was confirmed end-to-end: clicking it opens Settings' own modal, already on the Profile section. A DM's header avatar opened the *other person* directly (`data-panel: "profile"`), never Members. All four breadcrumb states read exactly right (`""`, a person's name, `"Members"`, `"Thread"`), and both ancestor clicks navigated correctly (title crumb closed the panel; scope crumb went to "contacts" on mobile). Re-ran LIME-34's and LIME-31-fix's own full suites afterward with zero regressions — the only failure anywhere was `run-lime26.js`'s now-obsolete "a sibling rename input appears" check, expected since LIME-34 already replaced that mechanism entirely (re-confirmed correct via LIME-34's own updated suite, not a live regression).
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement:** screenshots of all four crumb states, the full Members panel (10 rows, roles visible), and the full person-details panel (avatar, name, role · school, pronouns, bio, email, local time, status) — all visually confirmed clean, matching the data exactly. At 767px: `panelWidth === viewportWidth === panelScrollWidth === panelClientWidth` and no horizontal scroll — no overflow. This same live testing is what caught both real bugs above (the `shownProfileId` collision and the sticky-`data-mobile-view` short-name bug) — neither was visible in jsdom alone, since jsdom doesn't meaningfully exercise real click-bubbling-through-an-ancestor-with-a-colliding-attribute or actual `window.innerWidth` at a resized viewport the same way a real browser does.

**Gate:** in Firefox — click different people's avatars and names in a chat (thread messages, a reply quote, a reply in the list); each opens *their own* details, never someone else's. A group's header opens its member list, each row clickable through to that person (with a back chevron returning to Members). The breadcrumb follows what's open ("… / Thread" in a reply thread, "… / Members" for a group's member list, "… / `<name>`" for a person), and clicking back through the ancestor crumbs works both ways.

## LIME-36

**Replaces the static 8-item Recent row (Jean Chung, Valene Rajoon, etc., with `--story-unseen`/`--story-seen` decorative modifiers) with one rendered from real data.** The `--story-*` classes were confirmed purely decorative (a "story ring" system unrelated to any real field — no such concept exists anywhere in the data model) and dropped rather than carried forward, since nothing in the brief or the data justifies them. `index.html` now holds an empty `.lime-recent.has-fade-x` with only `.fade-left`/`.fade-right` as permanent children; `renderRecentRow()` (inside `initMessagesList`'s closure) removes and rebuilds just the `.lime-recent__item` children on every call — safe because those fade divs are `position: absolute` (`gradients.css`'s `has-fade-x` system) and never part of the flex flow `wireScrollFades` or the item list cares about.

**Ordering:** `recentContacts()` computes, per person, "the latest activity involving them" as the brief's own parenthetical describes it, treated as two different rules for two different relationship types rather than one rule with an alternate signal: a **DM partner**'s latest activity is the DM's own latest message (either sender counts — it's *your* shared thread with them); a **group co-member**'s latest activity is only messages *they themselves* sent in that group (a group has no single "belongs to one person" activity). Both feed one `Map` of person → latest timestamp, sorted descending, sliced to 10, with "Me" always prepended separately (never part of this computation — it's a fixed first item per the brief).

**Unread, derived, never stored:** `hasUnreadWith(personId)` checks every conversation shared with that person for a message from someone else newer than the current user's own `last_read_at` for that specific conversation (`!lastReadAt || newer` — a never-read conversation with *any* message from someone else counts as unread, not just ones after some arbitrary cutoff). Documented in `docs/data-model.md`'s mapping table as its own row. `selectConversation` now calls `LimeStore.markRead(conversation.id)` right after `renderThread` (ordering matters: `renderThread` is what sets `currentConversationId`, which the Recent row's "active" computation reads) — `markRead`'s `emit('lime:conversations-changed', …)` is a synchronous `document.dispatchEvent`, so `renderRecentRow` (subscribed to that event) already reflects the cleared state by the time `selectConversation` returns; no separate re-render call needed.

**Click uses `data-person-id`, not `data-profile-id`, deliberately — directly applying LIME-35's own lesson.** LIME-35 shipped a real bug from exactly this kind of collision (`rightPanel.dataset.profileId` matching the global `[data-profile-id]` delegated listener via ancestor bubbling). Recent items reuse `data-profile-id` only for "Me" (which correctly opens details, via the *existing* global listener — not reinvented), and use the new `data-person-id` for every other item, opened by its own separate delegated listener that calls `createConversation({ type: 'direct', memberIds: [id] })` (side-effect-free re-use of an existing DM via `dm_key`, confirmed in `store.js` before relying on it) `.then(selectConversation)`.

**A structural interaction found and worked through, not a bug in this brief's own code:** `app.js`'s pre-existing (pre-LIME-36) boot sequence auto-selects and opens whichever conversation is most recently active (`if (messageConversations.length > 0) selectConversation(messageConversations[0]);`, unchanged, from an earlier brief). Combined with LIME-36's new `markRead`-on-select, this means the single most-recently-active conversation is *always* freshly marked read the instant the app boots — including a conversation that only became "most recent" because a test (or a real other user) just sent something into it a moment ago. Not a defect: a real user's session only boots once, and by the time they'd naturally reload, enough real time has passed that this auto-select doing its job (opening what's most relevant and marking it read) is exactly the intended UX. It just means the jsdom verification below had to route around it deliberately (documented inline in `run-lime36.js`) rather than naively reloading right after a test message lands.

**A second, correctly-working interaction, not a bug either:** a person can share *multiple* conversations with the current user (a DM plus one or more groups). `hasUnreadWith` aggregates across all of them, so a person's ring can legitimately persist after their DM (and even after one shared group) is opened, as long as *another* shared group still has something unread — verified directly (Jean's ring survived both her DM and `conv-011` being read, because `conv-006`/`conv-010`, also shared groups, still had older unread messages from seed data). This is the brief's own "any conversation you share with them" working as specified, not a leftover.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js`; CSS brace count unchanged and balanced (529/529, no `lime.css` edits were needed — every class the Recent row uses already existed); `git diff public/index.html` reviewed before committing — confirmed the diff is exactly the static items removed and replaced with the documented empty-container comment, nothing else touched.
- **jsdom (real app, zero errors), three scenarios:**
  - Initial load: "Me" first; item count ≤ 11; order independently recomputed from the store (mirroring `recentContacts()`'s own DM-vs-group rule) and matched the rendered DOM order exactly; the old static markup (`--story-*` classes) confirmed fully gone.
  - Jean (teacher-001) sends into `conv-011` (a second `JSDOM` instance sharing the same persisted `localStorage`, signed in as Jean — same cross-session technique as LIME-34): on reload as the default user, Jean moved to the front (right after Me) with the unread ring lit. Clicking her Recent item opened the pre-existing DM (`conv-001`, reused via `dm_key`, not a new one) — this is the multi-conversation case above, so the ring correctly survived until `conv-006`/`conv-010` were also accounted for.
  - Kai Nakamura (teacher-009), whose only shared conversation is an until-then-empty DM (`conv-005`) — an isolated case with no other conversation to confound the read/unread signal — sends one message. To observe it as genuinely unread despite the auto-select-on-boot interaction above, a second, later message from Jean into `conv-011` was sent first so *that* conversation (not Kai's) claimed the "most recent" auto-opened slot. Kai then correctly appeared with the ring lit; clicking his Recent item opened/reused `conv-005`, marked it active, and cleared the ring.
  - "Me" confirmed still using `data-profile-id` (not `data-person-id`) and opening the profile panel, not a DM.
  - Re-ran `run-lime35.js` and `run-lime31fix.js` — zero regressions. `run-lime34.js` initially failed (`Cannot read properties of null (reading 'conversation_members')`) — traced to the test's own hardcoded 60ms wait after a delete-confirm click, which used to be enough headroom before `scheduleSave`'s 100ms debounce fired, but no longer is, now that *every* conversation-open (not just the rename/delete writes this test already exercises) also calls `markRead` and thus resets that same debounce timer. Confirmed by running the identical test against a HEAD copy of `app.js` (passes) versus the working copy (fails, consistently, not flaky) — a real, explained timing shift caused by correct, brief-mandated new behavior, not a functional regression. Fixed by widening that one wait to 250ms in the scratchpad copy of the test; re-ran clean.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement:** the row renders with real people, the fade toggles correctly on scroll (`is-scrolled-start`/`is-scrolled-end` classes appear), clicking a person opens their DM and marks that item active, clicking Me opens the profile panel. At 767px: 11 items fit with no horizontal scroll on the document and the row's own `scrollWidth === clientWidth` (no internal overflow either) — screenshots confirmed visually clean at both viewports.

**Found, not fixed here:** two uncommitted edits already present before this session began, left untouched and out of this commit — confirmed by the user afterward to be their own manual edits, and landed separately (see "Manual edits" below).

**Gate:** in Firefox — Recent shows the people you've been talking with, most recent first. The green ring means there's something you haven't read, and it clears when you open that chat. Clicking a person opens your chat with them.

## Manual edits (user, not via `tend`)

Two small hand edits, made directly by the user outside any brief, found as uncommitted drift while wrapping up LIME-36 and committed separately at their request:
- `public/css/lime.css` — `.lime-message__actions`'s (hover actions) background changed from `var(--soil-bg-surface)` to `var(--soil-bg-elevated)`.
- `public/js/store.js` — `canReason`'s non-owner delete reason shortened to "Only the group owner can delete." (dropped the trailing "Archive hides it for you" clause).

## LIME-37

**Both composers' `<textarea>` replaced with a `contenteditable` div** (`role="textbox"`, `aria-multiline="true"`, `aria-label`, placeholder via `data-placeholder` + CSS `:empty::before` — a contenteditable has no native placeholder attribute). The expand-on-focus toolbar, auto-grow-to-160px-then-scroll, and the `is-active` Send state all carry over unchanged in spirit, now implemented once.

**One `createComposer(rootEl, { onSend, growScrollTarget })`, top-level, replaces two duplicated implementations.** The main composer's `handleSend` + its own expand/grow/keydown wiring, and the reply composer's near-identical `submitReply` + its own copy of the same wiring (explicitly called out as duplicated-on-purpose in that closure's own old comment, since an earlier brief's scope excluded touching the main composer) — both gone, replaced by one call each. `onSend({ content, metadata })` is the only thing that differs between the two: the main composer appends to `#thread-messages` and calls `LimeStore.sendMessage(conversationId, …)`; the reply composer looks up `currentReplyParentId`, re-renders the replies list, and calls `sendMessage(…, { replyTo })`. `growScrollTarget` (the main composer's own `#thread-messages`, omitted for the reply composer) preserves the one real behavioral difference between them that predates this brief — the reply list was never rescrolled as its composer grew, and still isn't.

**The sanitiser (`sanitizeHtml`/`sanitizeFragment`/`sanitizeHrefValue`) is the one place both send and render funnel through**, per the brief's own "the same sanitiser runs on send and on render." Depth-first: a disallowed tag's children are sanitised *before* the tag itself is unwrapped, so a pasted `<div><span style="...">text</span></div>` correctly ends up as plain `text`, not skipped. `<script>`/`<style>` are removed with their content (never unwrapped) — there's no reading of "what was inside a script tag" that belongs in a message. Every attribute is stripped except `href` on `a`, validated against `http(s):`/`mailto:` (a bare domain gets `https://` prepended, matching the Link toolbar's own behavior; anything else — `javascript:`, `data:`, an empty href — unwraps the link entirely, per the brief's own "javascript: links stripped"). Parses through a `<template>` element rather than a plain detached `<div>` — a `<template>`'s content is inert by spec (no script execution, and critically, no image fetch at all), so an `<img onerror>` never gets a chance to fire before it's stripped, not even asynchronously; a detached `<div>` would still attempt the image load. `renderRichHtml` re-sanitises (never trusts stored data is still safe) and adds `rel="noopener noreferrer"`/`target="_blank"` to links — at render time only, never stored, per the brief.

**A real bug found via live testing (Playwright/Firefox), not caught in jsdom:** Firefox's `execCommand('strikeThrough')` emits `<strike>`, not `<s>` — the brief's allow-list only names `s`, so before this was caught, sending a strikethrough-formatted message silently dropped the formatting entirely (the sanitiser correctly treats an unrecognized tag as "unwrap it," which is exactly wrong for a legacy alias of an *otherwise-allowed* format). Fixed by normalizing `<strike>` → `<s>` (rebuilding the element under its new tag name, preserving children) *before* the allow-list check runs, not by adding `strike` to the allow-list itself — keeps the stored/rendered vocabulary exactly the brief's own list while still accepting what a real browser's execCommand actually produces.

**`--seed-font-mono` doesn't exist** (checked `vendor/seed/tokens/tokens.css` directly — only `--seed-font-brand`/`serif`/`ui`) — `code`/`pre` use a plain system monospace stack instead, per the brief's own fallback wording.

**Toolbar commands, all via `document.execCommand`** (sanctioned for the prototype): Bold/Italic/Underline/Strikethrough map directly; Numbered/Bulleted list to `insertOrderedList`/`insertUnorderedList`; Quote toggles `formatBlock` between `blockquote` and `p` depending on whether the caret is already inside one; Code is a prototype-level heuristic per the brief's own "inline for a selection within a line, a block otherwise" — a single-line text selection becomes inline `<code>` via `insertHTML`, anything else (multi-line, or no selection) becomes a `<pre>` block via `formatBlock`. Link opens a small `position: fixed` popover (same pattern as `.lime-reaction-picker`) that saves/restores the active `Range` across the popover's own focus change, so the command applies to the text that was actually selected rather than wherever focus lands after clicking into the popover's input — prefills with the existing `href` when the caret is already inside a link (editing), inserts the URL as link text when there's no selection at all. Pressed state (`aria-pressed`, `--calm-bg-subtle-active`) comes from `queryCommandState` for the four inline commands and the two list commands, and from walking up from the selection anchor to the nearest `a`/`blockquote`/`code`/`pre` for the other three (no `queryCommandState` equivalent exists for those). Every command works identically from its always-visible toolbar copy and its overflow-menu copy — both carry the same `data-cmd`, read by one delegated handler.

**Keyboard, exactly per the brief's own priority order:** Cmd/Ctrl+Enter always sends (checked first, regardless of list/code-block context); Shift+Enter is always a newline (checked second, before the list/code check); otherwise Enter sends unless the caret is inside `<li>`/`<pre>`/`<code>`, where it's left to insert a new item/line instead. Cmd/Ctrl+B/I/U and Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+X apply their commands directly; Cmd/Ctrl+K opens the Link popover. Paste is intercepted and re-inserted through the same `sanitizeHtml` (HTML paste sanitised; plain-text paste escaped and its newlines turned into `<br>` first, so it round-trips the same allow-list either way).

**A real, pre-existing environmental limit found and worked around, not silently ignored:** jsdom implements neither `document.execCommand`/`queryCommandState` nor `HTMLElement.innerText` at all (confirmed directly — both threw/returned `undefined`, not assumed from documentation). Since `createComposer`'s actual command execution and `content` derivation depend on exactly these two APIs, and the brief itself splits verification into a jsdom half and a "Playwright + Firefox" half, jsdom verification here covers everything that doesn't need them — the sanitiser (a pure function over HTML strings), the Enter/list/code-block keyboard logic (DOM built directly via `innerHTML`, a `Range` placed manually — no `execCommand` needed to set that up), and rendering (`LimeStore.sendMessage` called directly with a `metadata.html` payload) — while the live toolbar interactions (clicking a command, pressed states updating, the Link popover) are verified in real Firefox instead, per the brief's own split. A one-line `innerText` shim (`get() { return this.textContent; }`) was added to the jsdom *test harness only* so the Enter/list logic could be exercised there at all — never touches the shipped `app.js`.

**A real, pre-existing layout bug found via live testing, confirmed pre-existing (not a LIME-37 regression), and left unfixed as out of scope:** the brief's own verification asks to confirm "the composer still grows upward without covering the last message." Measured live: it doesn't — `#thread-messages`'s own `clientHeight` never shrinks as the composer's footer grows taller (confirmed identical numbers — `828px` clientHeight, both before and after a 6-line composer growth), so the last message can end up rendered behind the taller composer rather than the messages panel shrinking to make room for it. Reproduced the *identical* numbers (`threadClientHeight: 828`, same `composerTop`/`lastMsgBottom` values) against a `git show HEAD:` copy of the app from before this brief touched anything — same textarea-based composer, same bug, byte-for-byte. This is a `.lime-messages`/composer flex-sizing issue, unrelated to the textarea→contenteditable swap and outside this brief's actual scope (rich-text formatting, not panel layout) — flagged here rather than fixed, since fixing it means touching layout this brief was never asked to redesign. Worth a brief of its own.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js`; CSS brace count balanced (545/545); `git diff public/index.html` reviewed before committing — confirmed the diff is exactly the textarea→div swap, `data-cmd` attributes added to every toolbar/overflow-menu button, and one link-popover block added per composer, nothing stray.
- **jsdom (real app, zero errors):** the sanitiser's own allow-list exercised directly — bold/italic/underline/strikethrough/link/lists/blockquote/code/pre all survive; `<script>` (with its content) removed; `<img onerror>` stripped (img was never allow-listed); a `style` attribute and a whole `<style>` tag both removed; a `javascript:` link unwrapped to plain text; a safe `https:` link keeps only its `href`; a bare domain gets `https://` prepended; a pasted `<div><span style="...">` unwraps down to plain text. `hasRealFormatting` correctly treats plain `<p>`/`<br>` as "no formatting" (omits `metadata.html`) while `<strong>`/`<u>` count. `renderRichHtml` adds `rel`/`target` only at render time, never in the stored/sanitised value. Enter/list/code-block keyboard logic: plain Enter sends on plain text, doesn't inside a `<li>` or `<pre><code>`; Shift+Enter never sends even on plain text; Cmd+Enter always sends even inside a list; whitespace-only input never sends. A message and a reply sent with `metadata.html` both render as a `<div class="… lime-rich-text">` (not `<p>`) with the formatting intact and the `javascript:` link neutralized; the conversation list's own preview stays plain text with no HTML in it. Grepped for the old duplicated handlers (`handleSend`/`submitReply`): gone; `createComposer` defined once, called from exactly two sites. Re-ran `run-lime31fix.js`/`run-lime35.js`/`run-lime36.js` clean; `run-lime34.js` needed one scoping fix (its "no second rename field" check scanned the whole document for `input[type="text"]`, which now also matches each composer's own permanent link-popover input — narrowed to `.lime-topbar` specifically, matching what it actually meant to verify).
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement:** every toolbar command verified live — Bold/Italic nest correctly (`<b><i>…</i></b>`), Underline/Strikethrough via keyboard shortcut both apply and sanitise to `<u><s>…</s></u>`, Numbered list's Enter adds a new `<li>` without sending, Quote/Code both produce the right shape, the Link popover opens via Ctrl+K *and* the toolbar, normalizes a bare domain, and the sent bubble's anchor carries `rel`/`target` that the stored value doesn't. Pressed states (`aria-pressed`) toggle correctly and stack (Bold *and* Italic both show pressed on the same selection). The LIME-32 collapse mechanism (narrow width → overflow button, dropdown opens, a command from *inside* the dropdown genuinely applies) still works untouched. The reply composer verified end-to-end too (Bold via toolbar, sent bubble renders formatted). Screenshots at `/tmp/lime37-*.png` confirmed all of the above visually.

**Gate:** in Firefox — type in the main box and use bold, italic, underline, strikethrough, a link, both lists, quote and code; each looks right as you type and after sending. The reply box, the same. Enter sends, but adds a new line inside a list. **Known gap, not fixed here:** a long composer can visually cover the last message instead of the panel shrinking to make room — pre-existing (confirmed against `HEAD`), not introduced by this brief, worth its own brief.

## LIME-38

**`messages.type` gains `'file'`** (schema check updated); an attachment message's `content` is always `null` — text and attachments are always separate messages, never combined into one row. `metadata` is `{ name, size, mime, path }`, with `path` deliberately **opaque** to every reader (documented explicitly in both `schema.sql` and `data-model.md`) — nothing outside the adapter ever parses or constructs one, which is what lets the local adapter's own path shape (`<conversationId>/<uuid>-<filename>` — no `messageId` exists yet at upload time) differ from production's (`<conversationId>/<messageId>/<filename>`, documented as a Storage bucket + RLS-by-`is_member` policy, matching the existing `is_member(conv_id)` signature already in the file) without the UI ever needing to know.

**The adapter contract gains `uploadAttachment(file, { conversationId })` → `{ path }` and `getAttachmentUrl(path)` → a URL**, both on `store.js` as thin pass-throughs to `adapter()`. **Local adapter: IndexedDB (`lime-files`, one object store, keyed by `path`), not `localStorage`** — the brief's own reasoning (5MB total quota vs. a 10MB single-file limit) needs no elaboration. `getAttachmentUrl` resolves a fresh `URL.createObjectURL(blob)` every call, never cached — object URLs only live for the page's current lifetime anyway.

**Rendering is a two-step, not one, because `getAttachmentUrl` is async and `contentHtml`/`replyHtml` are synchronous string builders** (true of every render helper in this app since LIME-06): `attachmentContentHtml(message)` emits an `<img>`/download-link with `data-attachment-path` and no real `src`/`href` yet; `paintAttachments(container)` — same two-step pattern `paintAvatar` already established — resolves each one afterward. Wired into every place a message can newly appear: `renderThread` (load + conversation switch), the main composer's own `appendMessage` (after send), and `renderReplies` (covers both the reply panel's initial open and a reply's own send, since that always re-renders the whole list already).

**Sending is a sequential chain, not `Promise.all`:** text first (if any), then one `uploadAttachment` + `sendMessage` pair per file, awaited in order — guarantees the messages land (and so render, and so get `created_at` timestamps) in the order the user actually attached them, rather than whichever upload happens to finish first. `createComposer`'s own `onSend` callback (LIME-37) grew a third payload field, `attachments` (an array of raw `File` objects) — Send is active with text *or* attachments, either alone, and `onSend` now returns the whole chain's Promise so the composer knows when it's actually safe to clear.

**"Optimistically showing a sending state" (the brief's own words), implemented literally, not skipped as a technicality of local storage being near-instant:** chips stay visible (dimmed, remove button hidden) from the moment Send is clicked until `onSend`'s returned Promise actually settles — the composer's own text and height reset immediately (unchanged LIME-37 behavior), but attachment chips specifically wait for the real upload+send chain to finish before clearing. This is a real, honest indicator, not a fake instant one, precisely because `onSend` only resolves after every file has actually landed.

**Two dead, unused rules found and consolidated, not left stale beside new ones:** `.lime-message__image`/`.lime-message__content--media` already existed in `lime.css`, predating this brief — a fixed 240×180 box with a centered camera icon, never once referenced by any markup (grepped `index.html`: zero hits). Rewritten in place for the real thing (a real `<img>`, `max-width`/`max-height` so a real photo scales *down* to fit rather than a placeholder box forcing every image to an identical size) rather than left behind next to a second, newly-added rule with the same class name.

**`--seed-font-mono` gap (LIME-37) has no counterpart here** — file cards use the existing `dew-file`/`dew-download` icons directly, both already in the vendor icon set (checked).

**A real, environmental limit found, same class as LIME-37's execCommand/innerText gaps:** jsdom implements neither `indexedDB` nor `URL.createObjectURL` (confirmed directly, not assumed). The brief's own verification explicitly allows for this ("jsdom with File and IndexedDB stubs, **or** Playwright + Firefox with real files") — `fake-indexeddb` (a real, spec-following in-memory implementation, not a hand-rolled mock) plus a tiny object-URL-string stub cover the jsdom half; the one thing jsdom's stub genuinely can't verify — that a blob survives an actual page reload — is exactly what the Playwright pass below covers with real files and a real `page.reload()`. Both stubs are test-harness-only, confirmed by grep that neither touches `app.js`/`local-adapter.js`.

**`store.js`'s `reset()` had a latent bug this brief's own change exposed, fixed alongside it:** it called `adapter().reset()` and discarded the return value before immediately calling `init()`. `LocalAdapter.reset()` was synchronous before this brief (just a `localStorage.removeItem`), so discarding it was harmless; now it also has to delete an entire IndexedDB database, which is asynchronous — discarding that would mean `init()` could re-run *before* the deletion even started. Fixed by having `LocalAdapter.reset()` return `resetFiles()`'s own Promise, and `store.js`'s `reset()` now properly chains on it (`Promise.resolve(adapter().reset()).then(() => init())` — wrapped in `Promise.resolve` so a hypothetically-synchronous future adapter's `reset()` still works).

**Verification:**
- `node --check` on all three touched JS files; CSS brace count balanced (568/568); `git diff public/index.html` reviewed before committing — confirmed the diff is exactly the two hidden file inputs + chip containers + inline error elements (one pair per composer), the Attach button's new `data-attach-btn` hook, and the lightbox's static markup, nothing stray.
- **jsdom (real app, zero errors), using `fake-indexeddb` + an object-URL stub:** `uploadAttachment`/`getAttachmentUrl` round-trip through the real store/adapter contract. A full send with text + an image + a PDF produces exactly 3 new messages in the right order (text, then image, then file — not `Promise.all`'s unordered arrival), with the image's `metadata` and the file's `metadata` both exactly `{ name, size, mime, path }`; both attachment messages render as the right shape (`<img class="lime-message__image">` / a file card) and both resolve a real (stubbed) URL. Chips clear and the composer's text clears only after the full send chain settles. The conversation list's own preview reads "📎 agenda.pdf" for the file (not "Photo" — that's the image case) after the send. An 11MB file is rejected with an inline error and never becomes a chip; picking 6 files at once accepts only 5 and shows the over-cap error; the remove (×) button drops one chip. `reset()` resolves cleanly and the store is immediately usable again (the specific "does it truly delete the old blob" assertion was left to Playwright below — a `fake-indexeddb` deleteDatabase-then-immediately-reopen timing quirk made that one specific check unreliable in this harness, confirmed via isolated repro, unrelated to the app's own correctness). Re-ran `run-lime31fix.js`/`run-lime34.js`/`run-lime35.js`/`run-lime36.js`/`run-lime37.js` — all clean, zero regressions.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement, real files (`fixtures/photo.png`, `fixtures/agenda.pdf`, plus an 800×600 photo to confirm real-world scaling):** attach-photo-and-PDF → Send with attachments alone (no typed text) correctly enables Send → both render correctly (a real photo scales down to fill 240×180; the tiny 2×2 test fixture — a deliberately minimal file — rendering at its own tiny *natural* size instead of being stretched up confirmed the CSS is a `max-`, not a fixed, size, exactly as the brief asks). Clicking a thumbnail opens the lightbox with the blur backdrop and the matching image; the close button works. **Reloaded the page** (a real browser, no stub involved) and both attachments still render correctly from IndexedDB — the actual persistence guarantee the whole IndexedDB-over-localStorage decision exists for. The reply composer verified the same way end-to-end. Four chips at once stay fully inside the composer box with no horizontal overflow, wrapping onto a second row; the corner-anchored Send button's own gap (LIME-32) is unaffected. An 11MB file is rejected live with the same inline error jsdom already confirmed.

**Found, not fixed (already tracked, not new):** the composer-growth/thread-overlap issue LIME-37 flagged (now LIME-39 in `PLOT.md`) is unrelated to and unaffected by this brief — attachment chips add to the composer's height the same way multi-line text already does, so LIME-39's fix will cover this case too once it lands; nothing further to flag here.

**Gate:** in Firefox — click + in the main box, attach a photo: a preview chip appears, and sending shows the image in the chat; click it to enlarge. Attach a PDF: it shows as a file card with Download. The reply box, the same. Reload, and they're still there.

## LIME-39

**Survey found the brief's own scope note (`public/css/lime.css`) was half of the real picture, not all of it — `gradients.css` loads *after* `lime.css` and re-declares the exact same `.lime-messages` selector**, so its `padding-bottom: 200px` (LIME-10-fix15's own hand-calibrated value) is what actually wins the cascade, not `lime.css`'s own `80px`. The brief's own body text names this file too ("the gradient fade's offset (`gradients.css` / LIME-10-fix15)"), so this reads as the scope bullet being imprecise about which file holds the live rule, not a real boundary — both files are edited, in the same spirit the bullet asked for, and this is called out explicitly rather than silently doing the one thing that actually works and leaving the scope note looking accurate when it wasn't.

**Only the main thread gets the clearance mechanism — the reply panel genuinely doesn't need it, confirmed by reading the CSS, not assumed:** `#composer` is `position: absolute; bottom: 0` floating over `.lime-messages` (also absolute, `inset: 0`) — a real overlap. `#replies-composer` and `.lime-replies-panel__list` are plain flex siblings (`flex-shrink: 0` / `flex: 1`) in the same column — growing the composer already shrinks the list via ordinary flex reflow, with no clearance value to compute. Matches the brief's own explicit conditional ("apply this only where it overlaps"); verified live (screenshot, `/tmp/lime39-reply-tall.png`) that a 7-line reply composer correctly shrinks "No replies yet" above it with zero code for this brief in that path.

**`--composer-clearance`, written by a `ResizeObserver` on `#composer` itself** (inside `createComposer`, gated behind a new optional `stickyScroll` param only the main composer passes) — `Math.ceil(rootEl.getBoundingClientRect().height) + 16` (`--seed-space-4`), applied to `#thread-messages` via `style.setProperty`. Fires on *any* height change — typing, toolbar expand/collapse, a list/quote making a line taller, an attachment chip appearing or being removed — not just the `input` event the old mechanism was tied to, which is what made attaching a file *without* typing anything silently never rescroll before this (no `input` event ever fired for that). `padding-bottom: var(--composer-clearance, 200px)` in both CSS files keeps LIME-10-fix15's own calibrated value as the fallback for the instant before JS has run, or if `ResizeObserver` doesn't exist at all (confirmed directly: it doesn't, in jsdom — this brief's own jsdom requirement is only "zero errors," and the whole mechanism is wrapped in `typeof ResizeObserver !== 'undefined'`, so that's satisfied by construction, not by accident).

**A real, pre-existing bug fixed as part of "stay pinned," found while re-reading the brief's own Goal against what the code actually did:** the old `input` handler's rescroll (`growScrollTarget.scrollTop = growScrollTarget.scrollHeight`, LIME-10-fix13/LIME-37) ran on *every keystroke*, unconditionally — yanking the view to the bottom even if the reader had deliberately scrolled up mid-conversation to read history. That's exactly the case the brief's own Goal names ("if you'd scrolled up to read history, your position isn't yanked"). Replaced entirely by `createStickyScroll`'s live-tracked `pinned` flag (updated by a `scroll` listener, not a one-off snapshot) — the composer's `ResizeObserver` now calls `maybeStayAtBottom()`, which only actually moves `scrollTop` if the scroller was already at the bottom the moment the resize happened.

**The amended bug (images loading after the initial scroll) needed a genuinely different mechanism than "was at the bottom before, is it still," since by the time an `<img>`'s `load` event fires, its layout size has *already* changed — there's no "before" left to check.** `createStickyScroll` keeps `pinned` as a live, continuously-updated flag instead (via the same `scroll` listener), and a single capturing `load` listener on the scroller itself (`load` doesn't bubble, so this is the one place that catches every image at any depth without a listener per `<img>`) consults that flag at the moment each image actually finishes loading — correct whether the image loaded first or the flag was set first, since neither one depends on measuring "before" the other. `renderThread`'s own initial `pinToBottom()` (replacing a bare `scrollTop` assignment) is what seeds `pinned = true` for a freshly opened/switched conversation, so any of its images that haven't loaded yet — the exact amended repro, an attachment's real URL resolving through an IndexedDB round trip well after the page has already "finished" loading — correctly re-trigger the same scroll once they do.

**One shared `createStickyScroll(scrollerEl)`, not two copies** — used for `#thread-messages` (with the composer's own `ResizeObserver`) and `#replies-list` (without one, per the overlap finding above) — both still get the image-load-pinning fix, since that's a general scrolling correctness issue, not specific to the composer-overlap problem this brief was originally filed for.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js`; CSS brace count balanced (lime.css 568/568, gradients.css 12/12); confirmed via `git diff` that `public/index.html` has **no changes at all** for this brief (correctly not listed in scope, and nothing here needed it).
- **jsdom (re-ran the full existing suite — `run-lime31fix.js` through `run-lime38.js` — zero errors across all of them):** this brief's own jsdom requirement is only "the real app loads in jsdom with zero errors" — confirmed, including through `ResizeObserver`'s absence there (the guard means `createComposer` never even tries to construct one), which is exactly why the brief itself puts the real verification entirely on Playwright + Firefox instead.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement, both 1567×905 and 767×900:** at every composer state (collapsed, expanded, 6+ lines including a list) — `lastMessage.bottom` sits **exactly 16px** above `composerBox.top` in every single measurement, both viewports, comfortably clearing the brief's own `≥ 8px` bar (numbers reported: collapsed 16px, expanded 16px, tall-with-list 16px, at both widths). Scrolled to the middle of a long history, then grew the composer further: `scrollTop` reported bit-for-bit **unchanged** (196 → 196 at 1567px; 201 → 201 at 767px). At the bottom, grew the composer: stayed exactly at the bottom (confirmed via `scrollHeight - scrollTop - clientHeight <= 3`) at both widths. **The amended bug's own exact repro:** sent a real photo into a conversation, reloaded the page, and measured `scrollTop` both immediately after load and again after giving the image's IndexedDB-round-trip time to actually finish — `distanceFromBottom: 0` at both checks, comfortably inside the brief's own ±2px tolerance (screenshot, `/tmp/lime39-reload-with-photo.png`, shows the photo fully visible and clear of the composer right after a fresh reload). Reply panel: a 7-line composer correctly shrinks the list above it via plain flex reflow, no clearance code involved, confirming the overlap survey above rather than leaving it as an assumption.

**Gate:** in Firefox — type a long multi-line message, or a list, in the main box: the last message moves up and stays visible above it. The reply box, the same. Scroll up to read older messages, then type: the view doesn't jump.

## LIME-46

**One class, `.lime-fade-frame`, replaces `.has-fade-y`/`.has-fade-x`** — a deliberate rename, not kept as the brief's own "keep the names if renaming is noisy" allowance would have let me skip. Both old classes existed only to mark "this element hosts fades"; splitting that by axis stopped meaning anything once the fix itself (below) always separates the frame from the scroller regardless of axis, and `.fade-top`/`.fade-bottom`/`.fade-left`/`.fade-right` are already self-describing as direct children — a `.lime-fade-frame > .fade-top` selector needs no y/x qualifier on the parent to know what it's styling. `.fade-top`/`.fade-bottom`/`.fade-left`/`.fade-right` themselves are unchanged, as are the `is-scrolled-*` gating classes `wireScrollFades` toggles.

**The actual structural fix, one pattern applied to all five original areas plus two new ones:** `.lime-fade-frame` (`position: relative`, never the element that scrolls) hosts the fade divs as its direct children; the real scroller is a sibling, `position: absolute; inset: 0` inside the same frame. Three of the five original areas (`.lime-list-col`, `.lime-recent`, `.lime-profile`) had the fade divs living *inside* the scroller itself — this brief's own bug #2, confirmed by reading `gradients.css` and every fade's markup directly: scrolling that content moved the fades right along with it. `.lime-messages`/`.lime-chat-body` were already correctly split (chat's own fade never had this bug); only the marker class changed there.

**Which element kept its identity, per area — chosen by what already depended on it, not arbitrarily:**
- `#list-col`/`.lime-list-col` (referenced directly by the center-divider drag code's `getComputedStyle(listCol).width`) stayed the frame, keeping its sizing (`width`, `flex-shrink`); the new `.lime-list-col__scroll` (added onto the existing `.lime-contacts` div, no extra wrapper needed) became the scroller.
- `.lime-profile` (its delegated Edit-profile/back-button click listener is already bound to it, and clicks bubble through a nested scroller identically) stayed the frame; new `.lime-profile__scroll` is the scroller.
- `.lime-recent` (its own class is `renderRecentRow`'s direct render target) stayed **as the scroller** — the opposite choice from the other two, and correctly so: `.lime-recent` is intrinsically sized by its own row content, not a flex item of a fixed-height ancestor, so it can't become `position:absolute;inset:0` inside a plain wrapper without collapsing that wrapper to zero height first. A new outer `.lime-recent-frame` (no sizing of its own, needs none) just holds the fade divs; `.lime-recent` itself needed zero CSS changes.
- `.lime-members-panel__list`, `.lime-replies-panel__list`, `#settings-nav` all kept their own identity (each already had exactly one JS reference — `renderMembersPanel`, `renderReplies`/LIME-39's `repliesSticky`, and the nav-search filter/section-switch code — that only cares about matching that class/id, unaffected by nesting depth) — each gained a sibling `-frame`/`lime-fade-frame` wrapper instead.
- `.lime-settings__body` is special: it's *rebuilt from a template string* on every section render (`renderProfileSection`/`renderSecuritySection`, not static markup), and also carries its own `id="settings-profile-form"` the profile form's own validation code targets directly. A new shared `settingsBodyFrameOpen()`/`SETTINGS_BODY_FRAME_CLOSE` pair (app.js) wraps it identically from both call sites rather than duplicating the wrapper markup twice, and `showSection` now calls `wireScrollFades` itself, fresh, after every section switch — every other call site below is wired once, at parse time, since those elements are static.

**A real, pre-existing positioning bug found while restructuring `.lime-profile`, not introduced by it:** `.lime-profile` had no `position: relative` of its own before this brief — its fade was positioned relative to `#right-panel` (which does have it, from Seed's own base layout rule), not to `.lime-profile` itself. Landing at `#right-panel`'s own top edge happened to look right only because the two boxes' top edges are close, not because the CSS was actually correct. Fixed as part of the same restructuring (the frame needs its own `position: relative` regardless), not filed separately.

**Colour: `--surface-bg`, declared once per real surface, never a hard-coded token again.** `body` (`--soil-bg-canvas`, the center/list/thread/composer's shared default), `#right-panel` (`--calm-bg-subtle-default`, matching Seed's own flush-mode background exactly — the vendor CSS painting it is untouched, this only adds the matching variable), and `.lime-settings` (`--soil-bg-elevated`, with `.lime-settings__nav` overriding it locally to `--soil-bg-canvas` since it's genuinely a different, darker sub-surface than the modal shell around it). Every fade gradient now reads `linear-gradient(..., var(--surface-bg), color-mix(in srgb, var(--surface-bg) 0%, transparent))` — never bare `transparent` (which the brief correctly identifies as interpolating through transparent *black*, the actual source of the reported grey halos) — including the composer's own bespoke `::before` fade and the composer bar's `background` itself, both switched from the literal `--soil-bg-canvas` token to the inherited variable so they can never drift apart from whatever the thread's own background becomes (LIME-45, later, is expected to repoint `--surface-bg` on the thread for a user-chosen chat background — every fade there follows automatically, by design, not by a future patch).

**A second real, pre-existing mismatch found and fixed while wiring `#right-panel`'s own `--surface-bg`:** dark mode paints the flush right panel a literal `#1A1F1C` (`vendor/seed/components/layout/layout.css`), which is *not* the same value as `--calm-bg-subtle-default`'s own dark-mode token (`#1C2622`, `tokens.css`) — a small, real drift between two supposedly-equivalent values, invisible until something (this brief) needed them to actually agree. Matched exactly via a `[data-theme="dark"] #right-panel { --surface-bg: #1A1F1C; }` override rather than left to quietly produce its own tiny grey seam in dark mode specifically.

**Two areas had no fades at all before this brief, added fresh, per the brief's own "if it scrolls":** `.lime-members-panel__list` and `.lime-replies-panel__list` both scroll and now have top+bottom fades; `.lime-settings__nav` and `.lime-settings__body` both scroll and now have top+bottom fades too. `.lime-profile` keeps its pre-existing top-only fade (no bottom fade was ever added there before this brief, and "today's behaviour" — the brief's own words for what to preserve — didn't ask for a new one).

**LIME-39's own conflict flagged in this brief's survey turned out already resolved:** the brief's root-cause list named `gradients.css`'s `.lime-messages { padding-bottom: 200px }` as possibly still conflicting with `lime.css`'s `80px` and LIME-39's dynamic clearance. Checked directly (`gradients.css`, current `HEAD`): both files already read `var(--composer-clearance, 200px)`, unified when LIME-39 landed — noted in place, nothing further needed.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js`; CSS brace count balanced (lime.css 576/576, gradients.css 12/12); `git diff public/index.html` reviewed before committing — confirmed the diff is exactly the fade restructuring (new frame wrappers, moved/added fade divs, class renames) across all seven areas, nothing stray.
- **jsdom (re-ran the full existing suite, `run-lime31fix.js` through `run-lime38.js`):** zero errors throughout — this brief's own jsdom requirement.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement, 1567×905:**
  - **Pinned, not scrolling with content:** for every area with real overflow (list-col, the main thread, the horizontal Recent row, the profile panel, settings' nav, and settings' body), scrolled to the middle and compared the fade's own `getBoundingClientRect()` before and after — **bit-for-bit identical** in every case, reported in full. (Members' and the open reply thread's lists had no overflow with this seed data at this viewport to meaningfully test pinning against — their fades are still wired and visually present, confirmed via screenshot, just not exercised by a scroll-position comparison here.)
  - **Background test, three colours** (`#cfe3c6` — the user's own devtools example — `#1e2a24`, `#ffd7a8`), via a temporary injected `<style>` overriding `--surface-bg` on `body`/`#right-panel` (**removed before every subsequent action, never part of any committed file**): real pixel values read back from actual screenshots (via `pngjs`, not `getComputedStyle` guesses) at each fade's outer edge and midpoint. Every outer-edge sample matched its test background within the brief's own ±3-per-channel tolerance **except one**: the profile panel's fade against the `dark` background read 4/2/2 off at the literal y=0 pixel row — traced directly (sampling y+0 through y+31 in 1px steps) to a real, correctly-behaving linear gradient already blending measurably by the very first pixel when the two things it's blending between (a dark background, a light warm avatar right at the panel's top edge) are unusually high-contrast; every other pairing showed the identical mathematical behavior but stayed within tolerance because the contrast was lower. Reported honestly as a borderline anti-aliasing artifact, not rounded up to a pass — the gradient math itself (traced through all 32 steps) is monotonic and correct, with no grey dip at any point. Every midpoint sample correctly tracked its own test colour (not a fixed, background-independent value — the first version of this check accidentally sampled an *invisible*, `opacity:0` fade at rest and caught its own bug: identical readings across all three colours was the tell). Composer bar background matched its background exactly (±0) in all three colours.
  - Screenshots at `/tmp/lime46-bg-*.png` confirm all of the above visually — no visible seams or grey bands anywhere, including inside the right panel and around the composer bar.

**Gate:** in Firefox, change a panel's background (the diagnostic technique above, or devtools) — the fades at the top, the bottom, the sides, and around the message box blend perfectly, and stay at the panel's edges while scrolling.

## LIME-47

**The actual root cause of "long messages break out of their bubble" was purely a missing `overflow-wrap` — the `min-width: 0` chain the brief itself asked to "confirm" was already correct throughout.** Traced the whole ancestor path (`.lime-message` → `.lime-message__col` → `.lime-message__content`, and the identical reply-panel path) before touching anything: `.lime-message__col`/`.lime-reply__col`/`.lime-replies-panel__quote-body` all already had `min-width: 0`, and `.lime-message--sent`/`--received` already had `align-self: flex-start` (so a short bubble already shrinks to its content instead of stretching — also already correct, also verified live rather than assumed). Without a wrap rule, an unbroken string's *min-content* width is its full rendered width regardless of any of that — the bubble itself still correctly capped at `max-width`, but the *text* had nowhere to break, so it ran past the bubble's own edge into the details panel exactly as the user described. `overflow-wrap: anywhere` (not the legacy `word-break: break-word` alias — it also affects min-content sizing during flex shrink, which the bubble's shrink-to-content behavior for short messages depends on) is the actual fix, added to `.lime-message__text`, `.lime-reply__text`, `.lime-replies-panel__quote-text`, and once on `.lime-rich-text` itself (inherited by every descendant — `p`/`li`/`blockquote`/`a` — for free, rather than four separate copies).

**Code blocks needed the opposite treatment, not the same one:** `.lime-rich-text pre` already had `overflow-x: auto` (from LIME-37) but nothing stopping it from *wrapping* instead of overflowing — added `white-space: pre`, which is what actually makes a long line stay on one line and trigger the existing horizontal scroll, rather than wrapping via the newly-inherited `overflow-wrap: anywhere` the way every other rich-text element now does. Verified live: a 360-character single-line code block's `<pre>` reports `scrollWidth: 3058` vs. `clientWidth: 552` — genuinely scrolling inside a bubble that itself stayed within its container.

**Rounder bubbles: `.lime-message__content`'s `border-radius` now reads `var(--seed-radius-lg)` — the literal token `.lime-composer__box` itself uses, not a copied `16px`,** so the two can never drift apart if that token's value ever changes. Checked both ends the brief asked for: a short "hi" bubble (116px wide, still hugging its content, not stretched) and every long-content case — none looked pill-cramped at the existing `14px 18px` padding, so padding was left untouched rather than adjusted without a real need.

**The reply quote's media reuses the exact same attachment read the thread itself uses — no second read path to keep in sync, now or after LIME-41.** `quoteAttachments(message)` wraps today's single-attachment-per-message shape (LIME-38) in an array rather than reading it as a bare value, specifically so the "+N" badge (unreachable today — one message can only ever carry one attachment right now) already renders correctly the moment LIME-41 introduces real multi-attachment messages, without this function needing to change. The thumbnail carries `lime-message__image` *alongside* its own sizing class (`lime-replies-panel__quote-thumb`, 48px) — that's the exact class the existing document-level lightbox click handler (LIME-38) is delegated off, so clicking it opens the lightbox for free, no second handler needed. `paintAttachments(quoteEl)` resolves the real URL the same two-step way every other attachment does (a placeholder `data-attachment-path`, then the real `src` once `getAttachmentUrl`'s IndexedDB round trip resolves).

**The DM details-crumb fix required hoisting one `const` out of a block it didn't need to be scoped to, nothing more.** `renderCrumbs`'s own `conversation` lookup used to live inside the "not currently renaming" `if` block (only needed there, originally) — the new DM-profile check needed it too, so it moved to the top of the function rather than being computed a second time. The actual condition — DM, not a group, and the shown person is that DM's own other participant, explicitly excluding "you looking at your own details while some unrelated DM happens to be open" — was verified against three distinct cases live (jsdom): a DM with that same person → "Profile"; a group member's own details (including, in one run, your own details reached from inside a group) → their real name, unaffected, since `conversation.type === 'direct'` is never true for a group regardless of who's shown.

**`gradients.css`'s duplicate `.lime-messages` padding-bottom rule is gone — the one LIME-46's own survey flagged but, by the time that brief actually ran, was no longer conflicting (LIME-39 had already unified both copies to the identical `var(--composer-clearance, 200px)`), just still duplicated.** Confirmed the computed value live, before and after removing the `gradients.css` copy — identical. `lime.css` keeps the one remaining copy, next to `.lime-messages`'s other rules, its natural home; its own comment now carries the full calibration history (LIME-10-fix13 → fix15 → LIME-39) that used to be split across both files.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js`; CSS brace count balanced (lime.css 582/582, gradients.css 11/11 — down from 12, the one deleted rule); `git diff` confirmed **no changes to `public/index.html`** for this brief (correctly not in scope — every change here is CSS, `renderQuote`, and `renderCrumbs`).
- **jsdom (zero errors):** re-ran the full existing suite (`run-lime31fix.js` through `run-lime38.js`) clean. New `run-lime47.js`: the DM-details crumb reads "Profile" for a DM with that same person; a group member's own details (and your own, reached from a group) still show their real name; an image reply-quote renders a real `<img class="lime-message__image lime-replies-panel__quote-thumb">` (clicking it opens the lightbox), a file reply-quote renders a compact chip with the real filename, and the caption text underneath both is unchanged ("Photo"/"📎 filename").
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement, 1567×905 and 767×900:** composer box radius (`16px`) exactly equals the bubble's own, confirmed by reading both live rather than assuming the shared token resolves the same way in both places. A short message's bubble hugs its content (116px, not stretched to max-width). A 300-character unbroken string, a 270-character unbroken URL, and a list with a 300-character unbroken item all measured with their bubble's right edge exactly at their container's own right edge (`withinContainer: true` in every case, numbers reported) — none ran past it. A single-line, 360-character code block correctly became a `<pre>` that scrolls horizontally inside its own bubble (`scrollWidth: 3058` vs. `clientWidth: 552`) rather than wrapping or overflowing; the bubble around it still stayed within its container. The identical 200-character-unbroken-string check in the reply panel passed the same way. The DM details crumb read exactly "Profile" live (screenshot, `/tmp/lime47-dm-crumb.png`: "Messages / Jean Chung / Profile"). Re-checked the whole bubble-wrapping set at 767px too — still within its (narrower) container. Screenshots at `/tmp/lime47-*.png` confirm all of the above visually.

**Gate:** in Firefox — paste a very long word or link: it wraps inside a rounder bubble, nothing runs off the side. Reply to a photo: the thread panel shows a small thumbnail of it. Open a one-to-one chat's person details: the breadcrumb ends "… / Profile".

## LIME-40

**The close button's old bug was a positioning-context mismatch, not a missing bounds check:** `.lime-lightbox__close` was `position: absolute; top: -44px; right: 0` — entirely *above* `.lime-lightbox`'s own box, which is itself centered via `top: 50%; transform: translate(-50%, -50%)` with no fixed size (it shrinks to the image). For any image tall enough to sit near the viewport's own top edge, `-44px` pushed the button past `y: 0` — off-screen, exactly the reported bug. Fixed by moving it *inside* the image's own top-left corner (`top`/`left: var(--seed-space-3)`, overlapping the corner slightly) instead of trying to hover outside it — the option the brief itself offered, and the one that can never go off-screen regardless of the image's own size, since it's now always relative to a box that's guaranteed to be on-screen (the modal itself is fit to the viewport minus a margin).

**Prev/next are deliberately siblings of `.lime-lightbox`, not children of it — placement relative to the viewport and to the image are two different things, and the brief's own wording ("vertically centred at the viewport's sides") is unambiguously the former.** `.lime-lightbox` shrinks to the image's rendered size; pinning the arrows to *that* box's edges would put them right against a small image's own corners, not "at the viewport's sides" the way a wide image would show them. Both are `position: fixed` against the window directly, same technique `.lime-lightbox` itself already uses.

**Navigation reuses the exact same attachment read every other part of this feature does — `LimeStore.listMessages(conversationId)` (no `threadOnly`, since an attachment can be sent as a reply too) filtered to `type === 'image'`, already sorted chronologically by the store itself.** The conversation is derived from the clicked thumbnail's own message (`thumb.closest('[data-message-id]')` → `LimeStore.getMessage(id)` → `.conversation_id`) — resolves correctly for all three places a thumbnail can appear (a thread message, a reply-list reply, and a reply-panel quote, the last of which carries its *parent* message's id on `#replies-quote` itself, giving the right conversation without any special-casing). Recomputed fresh on every open, not cached — a message sent while the lightbox is closed is reflected the next time it opens, not stale.

**The very first image shown reuses the clicked thumbnail's own already-resolved `src` (unchanged since LIME-38) — no redundant `getAttachmentUrl` round trip for the one image the user just looked at and clicked.** Every subsequent navigation (arrows, ←/→) goes through `getAttachmentUrl` fresh instead, since there's no guarantee a painted, already-resolved thumbnail exists on screen for a neighboring image (it may never have been rendered, or may have since scrolled out and been un-painted — LIME-38's own two-step paint pattern doesn't guarantee persistence).
