**No wrap, verified as actual behavior, not just hidden arrows:** the arrow buttons' own `[hidden]` state and the keyboard handler's bounds check (`lightboxIndex > 0` / `< lightboxImages.length - 1`) are two independent guards — confirmed live that pressing →  past the last image is a genuine no-op (the counter reads identically before and after), not merely an invisible-but-still-functional button.

**A real specificity gotcha found and fixed while wiring the arrows' visibility:** the native `[hidden]` UA-stylesheet rule (`display: none`) loses to `.lime-icon-btn`'s own author-stylesheet `display: flex` when both apply to the same element — author styles always outrank UA defaults regardless of selector order. A bare reliance on the `hidden` attribute alone would have left both arrows visibly showing (just inert-looking, still `display:flex`) even when JS correctly set the attribute. Fixed with an explicit `.lime-lightbox__nav[hidden] { display: none; }` rule, high enough specificity to actually win.

**Closing needed one extra step precisely because prev/next/counter are siblings, not children, of `.lime-lightbox`:** they don't inherit its own `display:none`-when-closed the way children would, so `onBeforeClose` now explicitly hides all three (and clears `lightboxImages`) alongside its existing cleanup, rather than only re-gating them lazily on the next open.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js`; CSS brace count balanced (590/590); `git diff public/index.html` reviewed before committing — confirmed the diff is exactly the lightbox's own new markup (a counter span, two nav buttons), nothing stray.
- **jsdom:** re-ran the full existing suite (`run-lime31fix.js` through `run-lime47.js`) — zero errors throughout, confirming the lightbox restructuring didn't disturb anything else.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement, 1567×905 and a 500×500 stress case:** sent 3 photos into a conversation; opening the first showed `1 / 3`, prev hidden (no wrap at the start), next visible. Close button's own rect: fully inside the viewport in both sizes (explicitly checked all four edges against the viewport bounds, not assumed), and within 40px of the image's own top-left corner in both. Clicking next and pressing → both advanced correctly (counter `1/3` → `2/3` → `3/3`), next correctly hid at the last image, and one more → past the end left the counter unchanged (genuine no-wrap, not just a hidden button). ← correctly went back. Escape closed the lightbox *and* hid the (sibling) arrows/counter — checked explicitly, not assumed from the lightbox's own closed state. A single-image conversation showed neither arrows nor a counter. Screenshots at `/tmp/lime40-*.png` confirm all of the above visually, including the 500×500 case where the old `-44px` positioning would have put the close button off-screen entirely.

**Gate:** in Firefox — click a photo. The × sits at the photo's top-left, fully on screen; arrows (or ←/→) move between that chat's other photos, with a counter and no wrap at either end.

## LIME-41

**The data model change:** a new `message_attachments` table (`id`, `message_id`, `path`, `name`, `size`, `mime`, `width`, `height`, `position`, `created_at`, indexed on `(message_id, position)`) supersedes LIME-38's "one message per file, `metadata.path` on the message itself." Any message — including one with real `content` — can now carry 0..n attachments; typed text becomes that same message's own caption, not a separate message. `sendMessage`'s contract gains `attachments: [{ path, name, size, mime, width, height }]`; a new `getAttachments(messageId)` read returns them sorted by `position`. No data migration: an old `'image'`/`'file'` message with `metadata.path` carries no `message_attachments` rows at all — `getAttachments` synthesizes an equivalent single-row result from its own metadata when it finds none, so every reader (album rendering, previews, the lightbox) calls `getAttachments` and never branches on `message.type` itself, covering both eras through one code path. Documented in full, including the exact synthesized shape, in `docs/data-model.md`'s new "Multi-attachment messages" section; `docs/schema.sql` gets the real table plus RLS policy comments mirroring `message_reactions`'s own `is_member`-via-the-parent-message pattern.

**Sending changed from N+1 messages to exactly one.** LIME-38's composer send flow — a text message first, then one message per attached file, sequentially — is replaced: every file uploads (`LimeStore.uploadAttachment`, unchanged) and has its dimensions read (`readImageDimensions`, new — decodes via a plain `Image()`, resolves `{ width, height }` or `null` for a non-image/undecodable file) via `Promise.all` (order-preserving regardless of which upload actually finishes first), then a single `sendMessage` carries the caption plus the whole attachments array. Both composers (main thread and reply) got the identical rewrite.

**Width/height are recorded at upload time, not deferred to whenever the `<img>` first paints** — the brief's own "after a reload, the album layout doesn't shift" requirement. Confirmed live: sent a 7-image album, read the grid's rect before a full page reload and after re-selecting the same conversation — identical width and height both times.

**Real conflict found and flagged rather than silently resolved: LIME-38's existing 5-file-per-message cap directly contradicted this brief's own verification checklist ("sending 7 photos … creates one message with 7 attachments").** Stopped and asked rather than guessing; the user chose to remove the count cap entirely (only the 10MB-per-file size limit remains) — an album is the whole point of this brief, and a hard 5-file ceiling doesn't fit a WhatsApp/Apple-style album feature. `MAX_ATTACHMENTS_PER_MESSAGE` and its enforcement in `addFiles` are gone; `docs/data-model.md`'s LIME-38 section and `run-lime38.js`'s own regression test updated to match (that file's "6 files → only 5 accepted" assertion no longer holds and was rewritten, not just deleted, to assert "7 files → all 7 accepted, no cap" instead).

**Album rendering, shared by the main thread and the (compact, un-bubbled) reply list via one `albumHtml(message, attachments, { wrap })` function:** 1 image keeps the exact existing 240×180 single thumbnail (no grid wrapper at all — confirmed structurally, `.lime-album` is absent for this case); 2 side-by-side; 3 one large (spanning both grid rows) plus two stacked; 4+ a 2×2 grid, the 4th tile carrying a `+N` overlay (`images.length − 4`) once there are more than 4. 5+ also renders an "N photos" button that opens the gallery modal. Non-image files always render as file cards below the album (or standalone if there are no images at all), and a caption — if the message has real `content` — renders below that. `wrap: true` (main thread) puts the media/file blocks in the usual `.lime-message__content` bubble, matching LIME-38's own bubble treatment exactly; `wrap: false` (replies) renders them bare, matching how the reply list already used `attachmentContentHtml`'s raw output before this brief.

**Lightbox scoping is genuinely two different behaviors, chosen per click, not one unified rule:** a message with 2+ images of its own (a real album) scopes the lightbox to just that message's images — the brief's own explicit requirement. A message with 0 or 1 image of its own — a legacy single-attachment message, or a new message that just happens to carry one — keeps LIME-40's original whole-conversation scope unchanged, now flattened across every message's own `getAttachments` result (so an album sitting alongside single-image sends in the same conversation still contributes all of its own images to that wider scope, not a single placeholder entry). Which image within an album was actually clicked is read from a new `data-attachment-index` attribute on each tile (the image's index within that message's own image-only list), not inferred from DOM position.

**A real emergent bug found and fixed live, not anticipated during design: opening the lightbox from inside the gallery modal (two `createModal` instances open at once) meant a single Escape press closed both** — each instance's own `open()` installs its own `document`-level `keydown` listener, and both fired off the one keypress. Not a real stack: fixed by having the lightbox's own click handler close the gallery modal first as a hand-off (`galleryModal.close()`, a harmless no-op when it wasn't open) rather than trying to make two simultaneously-open modals coexist. Verified live: the gallery modal is confirmed closed the instant the lightbox opens on top of it, and Escape then closes only the lightbox, cleanly.

**Also fixed (brief's own "seen in the user's screenshots" bug):** the composer's attachment error banner (e.g. "… is over the 10MB limit") used to stay visible after a successful send or after removing the offending chip — only the *next* file pick ever cleared it (`hideAttachmentError()` was only ever called at the top of `addFiles`). Now also called at the start of `send()` and at the start of `removeAttachment()`. Verified live in Firefox: forced the banner visible, removed a chip — cleared; forced it visible again, sent — cleared.

**List previews:** `previewFor`/`plainPreviewFor` now check `message.content` before anything attachment-related — a caption always wins, matching the brief's own "the caption if there is one" ordering — and fall back to a shared `attachmentSummaryPlain` helper otherwise: "Photo" / "📎 filename" for the single-attachment case (identical wording to LIME-38, legacy or not), "📷 N photos" / "📎 N files" / both joined for a real multi-attachment album, correctly pluralized (`1 photo`, not `1 photos`) for a mixed one-image-one-file case.

**`quoteMediaHtml` (LIME-47) needed no design change, only a data-source swap** — it was already written to return an array in anticipation of exactly this (documented at the time as "already renders correctly, +N included, whenever LIME-41 adds real multi-attachment messages"). Confirmed: it now reads `LimeStore.getAttachments(message.id)` instead of the old message-type-only `quoteAttachments`, and the "+N" thumbnail overlay — never exercised by real data before this brief — renders correctly against a genuine multi-image album.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js public/js/store.js public/js/local-adapter.js`; all three clean.
- **jsdom (zero errors), full regression suite re-run:** `run-lime31fix.js`, `run-lime34.js`, `run-lime35.js`, `run-lime36.js`, `run-lime37.js`, `run-lime47.js` all still pass unchanged. `run-lime38.js` updated in place for LIME-41's new send model and the removed file-count cap (details above) — passes against the new behavior. New `run-lime41.js`: sending 7 photos + a caption produces exactly one message with 7 attachments, correct order/names/positions, width/height recorded via a test-harness `Image` stand-in (jsdom never fires `load`/`error` for a blob: stub URL — confirmed directly with a standalone probe, a real environmental gap, shimmed only in the test harness, never in `app.js`); the 2×2 grid renders exactly 4 tiles with a "+3" overlay on the 4th only; the "7 photos" label opens the gallery modal showing all 7; a tile click (both from the thread and from inside the gallery) opens the lightbox correctly scoped and indexed; 2- and 3-image albums use their own grid classes; a mixed 2-photo-plus-1-file album renders both an album and a separate file card under one caption; a single image keeps the old no-grid thumbnail path; a synthesized legacy `type: 'image'` message (no real `message_attachments` row) still renders and still previews as "Photo".
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement, 1567×905:** screenshots at `/tmp/lime41-album-grid.png` (2×2 grid, "+3" overlay, "7 photos" label, caption, exactly matching the brief's own WhatsApp/Apple references), `/tmp/lime41-lightbox-album.png` (lightbox open at "1 / 7", prev hidden/next visible), `/tmp/lime41-gallery-modal.png` (all 7 tiles in a responsive grid over the blur backdrop). Reported tile rects for the 4-tile grid (all four uniformly 119×119), the 2-tile layout (same top, offset left — true side-by-side), and the 3-tile layout (first tile 180px tall vs. 89px for the other two — genuinely spanning both rows, not just visually similar). Reload test: the same album's grid rect measured identically before and after a full page reload plus conversation re-select. Reply composer exercised separately (`measure-lime41-reply.js`): a 3-image album sent as a reply renders with `.lime-album--3`, no bubble wrap (matching the reply list's existing compact style), and its own tile opens the lightbox scoped to just those 3. Attachment-error persistence fix confirmed live (`measure-lime41-error.js`): clears on chip removal and on send.

**Gate:** in Firefox — select several photos, add a caption, and send. One bubble shows a grid with "+N". Click a tile to open it large, with arrows. "N photos" opens the full grid.

## LIME-40-fix

**The design: a real toolbar bar, not a repositioned overlay.** `.lime-lightbox` became a `display: flex; flex-direction: column` (from `display: block`), with a new `.lime-lightbox__bar` (close button left, counter right, `justify-content: space-between`) as an in-flow sibling *above* `.lime-lightbox__img`, not a child positioned over it. `align-items: stretch` (the flex column default) is what keeps the bar's own width exactly matching the image's rendered width with zero extra JS or measurement — the column's cross-axis size is set by its widest child (the image, up to its max-width), and the bar stretches to match automatically. This rules out both LIME-40's original off-screen bug and this fix's own predecessor problem (a translucent control sitting on the image, nearly invisible) by construction: the bar can never overlap the image, and its controls never sit on unpredictable photo content.

**The image's own `max-height` reserves exactly the bar's footprint, not a guess.** `.lime-lightbox__bar` is a fixed `height: 40px` (the close button's own height, vertically centering the counter pill inside it); `.lime-lightbox__img`'s `max-height` became `calc(100vh - 96px - 40px - var(--seed-space-2))` — the original 96px outer margin, minus the bar's 40px, minus the column's 8px gap — so bar + gap + image can never together exceed `.lime-lightbox`'s own outer `max-height: calc(100vh - 96px)` bound. The bar is mathematically guaranteed on-screen, not just usually.

**A real accessibility bug found via measurement, not assumption: the brief's own mandated `--soil-bg-elevated` fill fails its own 3:1 contrast requirement against this app's backdrop.** Sampling actual rendered pixels (not tokens — a token comparison wouldn't reflect the backdrop's own blur+tint) gave the button's white fill only **1.37:1** against the blurred backdrop's own near-white tint (`rgb(220,220,217)`) — nowhere near 3:1. Rather than deviate from the brief's own explicit `--soil-bg-elevated` spec for the fill, added a `1.5px solid var(--calm-border-bold-default)` border (with `box-sizing: border-box` so the button's own footprint stays exactly 40px, not 43px) — WCAG 1.4.11 non-text contrast is satisfied by a control's visible *boundary* against adjacent colors, not necessarily its interior fill, when the two are otherwise similar. The border alone measured **6.53:1** in light mode and **6.23:1** in dark mode (`data-theme="dark"`), both comfortably clearing 3:1 — verified with real pixel samples in both themes, not assumed to carry over. Precisely locating the border pixel itself took a row-by-row scan (`debug-border-pixels.js`): one pixel row *inside* the 1.5px border already reads as anti-aliased fill, not the border's own solid color — the first naive sample point (offset 1px in) silently measured the wrong surface and would have reported a false pass.

**The gallery modal's close button (LIME-41) does *not* have the same problem, checked rather than assumed exempt.** It sits on `.lime-gallery-modal`'s own fixed, solid `--soil-bg-elevated` panel — not on unpredictable photo content — and reveals itself via its icon glyph's own color (`--soil-text-muted`) against that fixed panel, the same pattern every other icon-button-on-a-solid-panel in this app already uses (search modal, settings modal). Measured: the icon glyph alone gives **4.87:1** against the panel, already well past 3:1. Left unchanged, per the brief's own conditional ("gets the same treatment, **if** it has the same problem").

**Hover and focus are genuinely distinct treatments, verified separately — and the focus ring surfaced a real, worth-documenting :focus-visible nuance.** Hover (`--calm-bg-subtle-hover`) measured as a real, different computed background from resting (`rgb(244,239,236)` vs `rgb(255,255,255)`). The `:focus-visible` outline rule (`2px solid var(--calm-border-bold-default)`, `outline-offset: 2px` — unlike every other `:focus-visible` rule already in this file, which only swaps a background) was verified correct in complete isolation (a minimal two-button page, a genuine A→B keyboard focus change): it does show a real, visible ring. But it will rarely be *seen* to trigger from inside the live lightbox itself, for two pre-existing, out-of-scope reasons, both confirmed by direct probing rather than assumed: (1) thumbnails have no keyboard affordance at all — only a mouse click opens the lightbox, so there is no keyboard path *into* it to begin with; (2) the shared `createModal` focus trap (also used by every other modal in this app, unchanged here) holds exactly one focusable item in the lightbox (the close button, auto-focused via `onOpen` the instant a mouse click opens it) — pressing Tab re-focuses that *same* element, and Firefox's own `:focus-visible` heuristic does not re-trigger on a `.focus()` call that produces no actual focus change, regardless of the keydown that caused it (confirmed with a minimal repro: `probe-focusvisible3.js`). Neither is this brief's to fix (its scope is the close/counter/arrows bar, not thumbnail keyboard access or the shared modal trap) — noted honestly as a verified, explained caveat rather than silently marked a full pass.

**Arrows restyled to the identical solid/opaque treatment (40px, `--soil-bg-elevated` fill, the same `--calm-border-bold-default` border, same hover/focus rules) for visual consistency — confirmed live, not just declared:** the resting backgrounds of the close button and the next-arrow measured byte-for-byte identical (`rgb(255,255,255)` in both). `.lime-lightbox__nav[hidden] { display: none; }` (LIME-40's own specificity fix for `.lime-icon-btn`'s author-stylesheet `display: flex` beating the UA `[hidden]` rule) is no longer strictly load-bearing — these buttons no longer carry `.lime-icon-btn` at all, so there's no competing `display` declaration to lose to — but kept explicit anyway rather than relying on the UA stylesheet's own default for something this visible, the same reasoning LIME-40 gave it in the first place.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js`; CSS brace count balanced (611/611, up from HEAD's 606 — the new `.lime-lightbox__bar`, `.dew` icon-sizing, and `:focus-visible` rules); no `app.js` changes were needed at all — every id (`lightbox-close`, `lightbox-counter`, `lightbox-prev`, `lightbox-next`) is unchanged, and the JS already selected purely by id, never by class, so the markup/CSS restructuring didn't touch it.
- **jsdom (zero errors):** re-ran the full regression suite (`run-lime31fix.js` through `run-lime47.js`, plus LIME-41's own `run-lime41.js`) — all still pass unchanged; the lightbox's own counter/nav assertions in `run-lime41.js` (built on ids, not classes or layout) needed no updates.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement, 1567×905, a 500×500 stress case, and dark mode:** bar rect measured entirely above the image (`bar.bottom <= img.top`), left/right edges aligned to the image's own edges within 2px, fully inside the viewport in both sizes. Close button and prev/next arrows all exactly 40×40. Contrast: border vs. backdrop 6.53:1 (light) / 6.23:1 (dark), both ≥3:1; fill vs. backdrop reported honestly at 1.37:1 (why the border exists) rather than omitted. Hover background genuinely differs from resting; the `:focus-visible` rule verified correct in isolation, with the live-lightbox caveat above documented rather than glossed over. Escape, ←/→ keys, and the arrow buttons all still work (`1/3` → `2/3` → `3/3` → back to `2/3`, Escape closes). Screenshots at `/tmp/lime40fix-bar.png` (1567×905), `/tmp/lime40fix-small-viewport.png` (500×500), `/tmp/lime40fix-dark.png` (dark mode) confirm all of the above visually. Gallery modal close button checked and confirmed exempt (4.87:1 already, unrelated failure mode) — left unchanged.

**Gate:** in Firefox — open a photo. The × sits in a bar just above the photo's top-left, clearly visible on any photo, and highlights on hover. The counter is at the top-right of the same bar.

## LIME-42

**Real audio playback, not the decorative `'voice'` message type.** A `mime`-starting-`audio/` attachment now gets its own category (`isAudioAttachment`, split out of "files" the same way images already are) and renders a genuine inline player — visible play/pause, a waveform-styled bar, a duration label — backed by a real hidden `<audio>` element, styled to match the existing (LIME-10, still purely decorative, still unwired) `'voice'` message bubble exactly, per the brief's own "matches the existing voice-message bubble." Deliberately built under fresh class names (`.lime-audio-attachment*`, not `.lime-message__voice`/`.lime-voice__*`) so the two features — one real, one still a placeholder — can never collide or be mistaken for each other; `.lime-voice__play` stays exactly as inert as it always was.

**`duration_seconds` recorded at upload time, mirroring LIME-41's own `width`/`height` reasoning exactly.** A new `readAudioDuration(file)` (a hidden `Audio()`, `preload: 'metadata'`, resolves `.duration` once `loadedmetadata` fires) runs alongside `readImageDimensions` in both composers' `onSend`. New `message_attachments.duration_seconds` column (schema.sql), threaded through `sendMessage`'s attachment push and `getAttachments`'s legacy-synthesis fallback (`null` there — no pre-LIME-42 message ever had one). Verified live: the duration label reads a real decoded value from an actual 3-second WAV tone (not a placeholder), and is unchanged after a full page reload — proving it's read from stored metadata, not re-decoded from the file every render.

**Playback: only one attachment plays at a time**, and the button's icon/`aria-label` track the real `<audio>` element's own `paused`/`ended` state rather than just the click that triggered it — `play`/`pause`/`ended` don't bubble (spec), so the listener has to run on the capturing phase, the same technique `createStickyScroll`'s own `load` listener already established for an identical reason. Verified live: `.paused` genuinely flips on click (not simulated), and starting a second player's audio genuinely pauses the first's.

**No `dew-pause` icon exists in this project's icon set** (checked `vendor/seed/icons/dew/dist/dew.css` directly, same kind of check LIME-40 already did for `dew-pause`'s sibling gap). A small inline SVG (two bars) stands in while playing, matching the brief's own "an inline SVG if a double-check icon doesn't exist" allowance from a later brief in this same file — the same fallback pattern, applied here first.

**Typed file cards: extension badges, keyed off the filename (not the mime alone), all four tints measured for real contrast before being trusted.** `FILE_CATEGORY_BY_EXT` maps pdf→bad, doc(x)→calm, xls(x)/csv→good, ppt(x)→warn, zip/rar/7z/tar/gz and txt/md/log→neutral; anything else falls back to the neutral tile with a generic file glyph — today's exact pre-LIME-42 look, not a guessed color for an extension with no real convention. All four `-text-bold-default`-on-`-bg-subtle-default` pairings measured at 6.4:1 contrast or better in both light and dark mode (`probe-tints.js`) — comfortably clear of the brief's own "only if it passes contrast, otherwise neutral" bar, so none needed the fallback.

**Name truncation: the base shrinks with an ellipsis; the extension is a separate, never-shrinking sibling after it** — the brief's own "ellipsis-truncated in the middle, keeping the extension," implemented as two flex children rather than attempting true geometric middle-truncation (which needs canvas text measurement to do properly, overkill for what this brief actually asks for). `min-width: 0` on both the row and its shrinking child, the same flex-ellipsis gotcha `.lime-message__file-meta` already had to work around once before.

**A real, multi-step bug found and fixed live, not caught by structural jsdom checks alone: "PDFs and images open in a new tab from the card" needed two separate corrections before it actually worked in Firefox.**
1. First attempt: `window.open()` called inside `getAttachmentUrl`'s own `.then()` — silently failed. Real browsers only allow `window.open()` to succeed as a direct result of a synchronous user gesture; calling it after an awaited IndexedDB round trip is exactly the pattern popup blockers exist to block. **Fix, attempt 2:** open a blank tab synchronously in the click handler, then point that same tab's `location.href` at the real URL once it resolves — the standard "open now, navigate later" workaround. This required dropping `noopener` (which was on the very first version): `noopener` makes `window.open()` itself return `null` per spec, silently defeating the one handle the whole workaround needs.
2. Attempt 2 still failed. Root-caused with three isolated, app-free probes (`debug-open-tab5/6/7.js`) rather than guessed at: a fresh `text/plain` blob opened and stayed open in a new tab; an identical-shape `application/pdf` blob's tab opened and immediately closed; a `image/png` blob opened and stayed open. This isolated the real cause to this specific headless Firefox test profile's own PDF-viewer availability, not the app's `window.open()` mechanism at all — a genuine environmental gap, not a code defect (documented explicitly, not silently retried further).
3. **Fix, attempt 3 (final):** replaced the JS `window.open()` approach entirely with a real `<a target="_blank" rel="noopener" data-attachment-path="...">` for the badge+meta area, resolved by the exact same `paintAttachments` pass every other attachment already goes through — no custom click handler needed at all. This is the same mechanism the Download link (a sibling, not nested — HTML doesn't allow nesting interactive content) already proves works for a blob URL, and sidesteps the async-popup-blocker problem entirely since a real anchor's own native "open in new tab" behavior doesn't have that restriction. Verified structurally in Playwright (real `<a>`, correct `target`/`rel`, `href` resolved to a real `blob:` URL, Download unaffected) — full end-to-end "a PDF actually renders in the new tab" stays unverifiable in this specific test profile for the reason found in step 2, noted as a test-environment limitation rather than claimed as a pass.

**Album ordering, unchanged from LIME-41 except for where audio slots in:** image grid (+ gallery label) → caption → audio players → file cards. Verified live with a real mixed message (WAV + PDF, with a caption): all three sections render in one bubble, in that order.

**List previews:** a single audio attachment (no caption) reads "🎵 Audio" (the brief's own exact wording, no count — matching "Photo"'s own no-count form); a real mixed multi-attachment message extends the existing pattern with "🎵 N audio" ("audio" doesn't pluralize).

**A pre-existing, out-of-scope CSS bug found while surveying the old file-card markup, flagged rather than fixed:** a dead, unreferenced `.lime-message__file` block further down `lime.css` (no `-card`, nothing in `app.js` has ever rendered it) redeclares the bare `.lime-message__file-name`/`-download` class names the *real* card also used, and — since neither selector is scoped to a parent — silently wins the cascade for both, meaning the real Download button had been rendering at 28×28 rather than its own, earlier rule's stated 32×32, since LIME-38. LIME-42's new file-card markup uses fresh `.lime-filecard__*` names specifically to sidestep this rather than inherit it, but the dead block and the collision it already caused are both still sitting in `lime.css`, untouched — documented in `docs/data-model.md` and here, not silently cleaned up, since removing dead code wasn't this brief's own scope.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js`; CSS brace count balanced (632/632, up from HEAD-of-this-brief's 611 — new badge/filecard/audio-attachment rules).
- **jsdom (zero errors), full regression suite:** `run-lime31fix.js` through `run-lime47.js` (including LIME-41's own suite) all still pass. New `run-lime42.js`, with test-harness-only stand-ins for two more real, confirmed jsdom gaps beyond LIME-41's own `Image` shim — a `win.Audio` constructor (jsdom never fires `loadedmetadata`/`error` for a blob: stub URL, the same class of gap as `Image`) and an `HTMLMediaElement.prototype.play`/`.pause`/`paused` override (jsdom's real `<audio>` elements implement `.play()` as "Not implemented," returning `undefined` rather than a Promise, and `paused` is a getter-only accessor with no working setter) — covers: one audio message records the real duration and renders a real player; play/pause genuinely toggles `.paused` and the icon/aria-label; a second player starting pauses the first; all seven badge categories (PDF/DOC/XLS/PPT/ZIP/TXT/unrecognized) render the right label and tint class; a long filename keeps its extension in a separate element; the PDF open anchor's full structural contract (`<a>`, `target="_blank"`, `rel="noopener"`, resolved `href`, Download as a separate un-nested element); list previews for a single and a mixed audio attachment.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement:** a genuine 3-second WAV tone (synthesized locally, `fixtures/tone.wav` — no mp3 encoder available in this environment, and the app only cares about `mime.startsWith('audio/')`, not codec) plays and pauses for real, duration label reads `0:03` before **and** after a full page reload. PDF badge renders "PDF" with the `--bad` tint class live. Base-name truncation CSS confirmed present and active (`text-overflow: ellipsis`, `white-space: nowrap`, `scrollWidth > clientWidth` for a deliberately long name) with the extension always shown intact. The three-attempt `window.open()` → deferred-navigation → real-anchor debugging arc above, plus the isolated PDF-viewer-availability root cause, all confirmed via dedicated probe scripts (`debug-open-tab2.js` through `7.js`) before the final structural verification. A real mixed message (audio + PDF + caption) renders all three in one bubble, correctly ordered. Screenshots at `/tmp/lime42-audio-player.png`, `/tmp/lime42-filecard.png`, `/tmp/lime42-mixed.png`.

**Gate:** attach an audio file, and it plays in the chat. Attach a PDF, a Word file and a zip, and each shows a clear card.

## LIME-41-fix

**The caption bug was purely a rendering gap — the send path was already correct.** Verified directly (`probe-caption-bug.js`) before touching anything: sending a bold-formatted caption already stored `metadata.html` correctly (the composer computes `metadata` once and passes it through regardless of whether attachments are present). The actual bug was `albumHtml`'s caption line — a hand-rolled `'<p class="lime-message__caption">' + escapeHtml(message.content) + '</p>'` that never looked at `metadata.html` at all. Fixed by sharing `messageBodyHtml` with every text message, per the brief's own "share one caption renderer" — the brief's own suspicion ("probably plain `content` used for captions") was right about the symptom, wrong about needing a send-path fix; only the render path needed one. Wrapped in a real `.lime-message__content` bubble (`wrap: true`) matching a text message's own bubble exactly, so LIME-47's width rules apply for free — it's the identical markup shape, not a lookalike.

**"5 photos" floating centred was a UA-default button style meeting a flex-stretch container, not a positioning bug.** `.lime-album__gallery-label` is a `<button>` (UA default `text-align: center`) inside `.lime-message__col`, a flex column whose default `align-items: stretch` made the button's own box stretch to the column's full width — the button's *own left edge* was already correctly aligned with the grid; the text inside it just centred within that invisible, oversized box, reading as "floating with a gap." One declaration, `align-self: flex-start`, fixes both the stretch and the appearance of centring — no `text-align` override needed, since a shrink-to-content button's own default centring is invisible once the box itself isn't wider than its label.

**The viewer's controls moved from a shared full-width bar (LIME-40-fix's own design) to three independently-positioned controls outside the image** — close at the top-right corner, a new "back to all photos" mirroring it at the top-left, the counter centred below — per the user's own later, more specific QA. `.lime-lightbox__img`'s `max-height` now reserves two separate budgets (52px above for the close/back row, 36px below for the counter) instead of LIME-40-fix's single one, so all three controls stay guaranteed on-screen regardless of image size, the same "reserve the space, don't just hope" principle LIME-40-fix itself established.

**Borders removed, replaced with `--seed-shadow-md` — and the *right* contrast question changed, not just the answer.** LIME-40-fix's border existed because the button's own near-white fill measured a real ~1.4:1 against the blurred backdrop. Removing the border doesn't fix that fill-vs-backdrop number at all — it makes it irrelevant, because the border was never what made the *icon* legible in the first place. Measured live: the icon glyph (`--soil-text`, near-black) against the button's own fill (`--soil-bg-elevated`, near-white) comes out to **17.55:1** — nowhere near the 3:1 floor, confirming the user's own plot-supplied reasoning ("a dark icon on a white fill does" clear 3:1) rather than assuming it.

**A real, load-bearing bug found and fixed, not just a markup tidy: the shared backdrop's DOM position silently blocked every click on the wall underneath it.** `#photo-wall` was placed before `#lightbox-backdrop` in the document; since both share the same `--seed-z-modal` and neither the wall nor the backdrop set an explicit stacking order beyond that, the backdrop — later in DOM order — painted **on top of** the wall's own masonry tiles despite being visually mostly-transparent, making them entirely unclickable. Caught live by Playwright's own actionability check ("`#lightbox-backdrop` intercepts pointer events"), not by visual inspection, which wouldn't have shown anything obviously wrong. Fixed by reordering: backdrop, then wall, then viewer — each later element now correctly stacks above the one before it, matching the real two-level relationship (viewer above wall above backdrop).

**The wall+viewer pair is deliberately *not* built on the shared `createModal` helper every other modal in this app uses.** Two independent `createModal` instances stacked (LIME-41's own gallery-card approach) each install their own `document` keydown listener; a single Escape press fired both, which LIME-41 worked around with a hand-off (close the gallery first, no real stack). This brief's own two-level "Escape steps back exactly one level, opened-from-wall only" behavior can't be expressed as a hand-off — it needs state (is the viewer's current session anchored to the wall or not) and exactly one Escape owner. Built with bespoke `openLightbox`/`openPhotoWall`/`backToWall`/`closeStack` functions and a single top-level `keydown` listener instead, sharing one backdrop element between the two. `backToWall()` never touches the wall's own DOM at all (its masonry is never rebuilt or hidden mid-session) — confirmed live that scroll position survives a full round trip (open wall → scroll → open viewer → back to wall) with zero bookkeeping needed, simply because nothing ever moved it.

**The "+N" overlay tile now opens the wall, not the viewer at that one photo** — `data-gallery-message-id` added to that tile alongside the existing "N photos" label, read by the same delegated click handler. This meant an existing test assumption broke (a bare `[data-gallery-message-id]` selector was no longer unique within one bubble) — updated the regression test to scope to `.lime-album__gallery-label` specifically rather than leaving it silently matching the wrong element.

**Masonry via CSS `columns`, not `grid`** — a real Pinterest wall (content flows down one column before starting the next) needs that specific layout algorithm; `grid`'s row-based placement can't produce it without an extra JS pass. Column count verified live at all three of the brief's own checkpoints (5 at 1567px, 3 at 900px, 2 at 500px) via real viewport resizes, not just declared and assumed. Tile aspect ratios verified against 7 real PNGs built locally with genuinely different natural dimensions (400×250 landscape, 250×500 portrait, 300×300 square, 200×600 tall, 600×200 wide) — every rendered tile's own width/height ratio matched its natural ratio within measurement tolerance, confirming no forced square crop.

**Two Playwright test-methodology bugs, self-caught and fixed rather than reported as false app failures:** (1) 7 tiles across 5 wide columns didn't actually overflow a 905px viewport (`scrollHeight === clientHeight`, confirmed directly) — the first scroll-preservation check was trivially "true" because there was nothing to scroll; fixed by sending a real 16-photo album specifically for that check, confirming genuine overflow first. (2) `page.click()`'s own actionability check silently scrolled the wall's scroller to bring the target tile into view before clicking, corrupting the very scroll-position-preservation test it was part of (a scrollTop of 60 became 493 from Playwright's own automation, not the app) — fixed by dispatching the click directly in-page instead.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js`; CSS brace count balanced (641/641).
- **jsdom (zero errors):** full regression suite (`run-lime31fix.js` through LIME-42's own `run-lime42.js`) all still pass; `run-lime41.js` updated in place for the "+N" tile's new `data-gallery-message-id` and the gallery-card→wall rename (its own selectors, not its assertions, changed), plus new coverage added directly into it: wall opens with all N images, a tile opens the viewer scoped to the album with "back to all photos" visible, back-button returns without closing the wall, Escape from a wall-opened viewer steps back one level (wall stays open), a second Escape then closes the wall, and the "+N" tile opens the wall rather than the viewer. New `run-lime41fix.js`: a bold word plus a two-item list survive through the caption (both the thread's wrapped bubble and a reply's own unwrapped, compact caption).
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement, 1567×905/900×905/500×905/500×500:** formatted caption sent through the *real* toolbar (Ctrl+B, not a simulated `innerHTML` set) keeps its `<b>` tags; "5 photos" measured within 8px under the grid, left-aligned within 1px. Viewer: close sits entirely above the image, right-aligned to its edge within 2px; counter entirely below, centred within 2px; both fully inside the viewport; `border-width: 0px` confirmed computed, `box-shadow` confirmed present; icon-vs-fill contrast 17.55:1. Wall: full-screen (exact viewport dimensions), close pinned to the viewport's own top-right, column counts of 5/3/2 confirmed at the three specified widths, every one of 7 mixed-ratio tiles kept its own natural aspect ratio. A full open→scroll→viewer→back-to-wall round trip preserved scroll position exactly; the two-level Escape stack (step back, then close) confirmed live, matching the jsdom result. 500×500 stress case: every control (wall close, viewer close, viewer counter) stayed fully on-screen. Screenshots at `/tmp/lime41fix-viewer.png`, `/tmp/lime41fix-wall.png`, `/tmp/lime41fix-wall-500.png`, `/tmp/lime41fix-small-viewport.png`.

**Ask (per the brief's own gate):** the controls were built sitting *outside* the photo (close above-right, counter below-centre, wall close at the viewport's own corner) — plot's reading of the user's QA. If the intent was actually for them to sit *overlaid on* the photo instead, that's a one-line positioning change (swap the `bottom`/`top` offsets from outside the image's box to inside it) — flagging per the brief's own instruction rather than guessing which the user meant.

**Gate:** in Firefox — **Caption:** send photos with a formatted caption. It keeps its formatting, in a bubble under the grid, with "5 photos" right under the grid. **Viewer:** open a photo. The × is outside its top right, the counter is centred below it, and there are no borders. **Wall:** "5 photos" opens a full-screen, Pinterest-style wall. Choose a photo to see it alone, then "back to all photos".

## LIME-43

**No new columns, exactly as the brief demanded — every piece already existed.** `LimeStore.getReceiptStatus(messageId)` is a pure derivation: "delivered" needs no state at all (existing in the store *is* delivered); "viewed by X" reads `conversation_members.last_read_at` (LIME-36's `markRead`, already persisted) against the message's own `created_at` for every *other* member of that conversation, excluding the sender. A DM's ✓✓ threshold is "that one other person"; a group's is "every other member, unanimous" — both fall out of the same `notYet.length === 0` check once the sender is excluded from the membership scan.

**The tooltip always carries the full breakdown, not just whatever the tick's own binary state implies.** A group can sit at a plain ✓ while some — not all — members have read it; that partial state (who's viewed, who hasn't) is exactly what hovering or focusing the tick reveals, matching the brief's own two literal examples verbatim: `"Viewed by Jean · 2:20 PM"` for a DM's single viewer, `"Viewed by Jean, Priya\nNot yet: Marcus"` for a group mid-way through. Names use `firstName`, not the sender-label `shortName` ("First L.") convention used everywhere else in this app for message senders — checked against the brief's own literal wording first (both examples read "Jean," not "Jean C"), rather than defaulting to the existing convention without checking whether it actually matched what was asked for.

**The tooltip itself is a plain native `title` attribute, not a custom component** — matches this app's own existing convention (every other tooltip in this codebase, Close/Download/etc., already uses `title=` alone), and satisfies "hovering or focusing" for free: native `title` tooltips trigger on both mouse hover and keyboard focus in Firefox without any extra JS, confirmed live rather than assumed.

**A real layout bug found live, caused directly by this brief's own new element, not pre-existing:** `.lime-message__actions` (the hover-revealed react/reply/more bar, `position: absolute; top: -16px; right: 8px` relative to the whole message) genuinely overlaps a receipt tick's own position at the end of the meta line for a short sender name/time — confirmed by reading both elements' real bounding rects while hovering, not assumed from the CSS alone. Without its own stacking context, the newly-added receipt lost every pointer event in that overlap region to `.lime-message__actions` (later in DOM order) — making the tick, the exact thing a user would hover to see who's viewed a message, literally un-hoverable at that point. First caught by Playwright's own hover action failing with "actions bar intercepts pointer events," not by visual inspection. Fixed with `position: relative; z-index: 1` on `.lime-message__receipt` alone — doesn't touch `.lime-message__actions`' own existing position, timing, or behavior at all. Re-verified with `document.elementFromPoint` at the overlap's exact centre: resolves to the receipt's own child glyph, not the actions bar, after the fix.

**Live updates patch in place, not a full re-render.** `markRead` already emitted `lime:conversations-changed` with `kind: 'read'` (LIME-36) — the open thread now listens for that event, scoped to the currently-open conversation, and replaces just the affected `.lime-message__receipt` elements' `outerHTML`. Verified live (not just asserted) that a different message's own DOM node is provably untouched across the update — a custom marker attribute set on it before the event survives after, which a `thread.innerHTML` full-replace would have wiped out.

**No live cross-tab/cross-session sync exists in this prototype to piggyback on** (no `storage` event listener, and `localStorage` writes don't fire in the writing tab anyway) — verified instead with the exact same "a real store write from the other person's own session" test hook LIME-34 already established: separate `JSDOM` windows, sharing the same persisted snapshot, logged in as each other member in turn (Jean, then Priya, then Marcus for the group case), each calling `markRead` for real. A production `SupabaseAdapter` with realtime subscriptions would push these live across sessions for free — noted in `data-model.md`, not built, since this app has no realtime layer at all yet. The privacy-setting-to-disable-receipts and "sign in as someone else"/demo-toggle mentions in the brief's own gate are both noted as future work, not built — neither was asked for as this brief's own deliverable, only flagged as options for *verifying* it.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js public/js/store.js`; CSS brace count balanced (644/644).
- **jsdom (zero errors):** full regression suite (`run-lime31fix.js` through LIME-41-fix's own `run-lime41fix.js`, LIME-42, LIME-43) all still pass. New `run-lime43.js`: a DM message starts ✓ (aria-label "Delivered", tooltip "Delivered", exactly one check glyph, `tabindex="0"`); a received message never shows a receipt at all; Jean reading it flips it to ✓✓ with the exact tooltip wording the brief itself gives; a 4-person group (`conv-006`) message starts ✓, stays ✓ after only Jean reads (tooltip shows the real partial breakdown, "Viewed by Jean" / "Not yet: Priya, Marcus"), and only reaches ✓✓ once Priya and Marcus have also read it (tooltip lists all three, no "Not yet" line); a live in-window update (merging a second session's snapshot back in and dispatching the real event) flips an already-rendered ✓ to ✓✓ without touching an unrelated message's own DOM node.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement:** a sent message's receipt renders at a real, non-zero size in the neutral-grey color the brief specifies. The `.lime-message__actions` overlap bug found, confirmed via real rects and `elementFromPoint`, and fixed — re-verified that a genuine Playwright `hover()` on the receipt itself (previously timing out) now succeeds, and that keyboard `.focus()` correctly lands on it. Screenshots at `/tmp/lime43-delivered.png`, `/tmp/lime43-hover-fixed.png`.

**Gate:** send a message and see ✓. Sign in as the other person (a second-session test hook, the same one LIME-34 established — no demo toggle built, since it wasn't asked for as part of this brief's own deliverable), and it becomes ✓✓. Hover or focus shows who.

## LIME-43-revert

**A mechanical revert, verified as exactly that rather than assumed.** `git revert --no-commit c1516a6` (LIME-43's own commit) cleanly undid every code change with no conflicts, since plot had already confirmed LIME-43 was purely additive (no deletions across any file). `git diff a67d834 -- public/` — the commit immediately before LIME-43 — came back **empty**, confirming byte-for-byte that `public/` is exactly what it was before the receipts feature existed, not just "close" or "equivalent."

**History and design were deliberately kept, not reverted along with the code.** `git checkout HEAD -- TEND.md docs/data-model.md` restored both files to their post-LIME-43 versions after the revert would otherwise have rolled them back too — the LIME-43 record in `TEND.md` and the full receipts derivation design in `docs/data-model.md` (the `last_read_at`-based rule, the DM-vs-group ✓✓ threshold, the tooltip format) both stay exactly as written, with a new note at the top of the `data-model.md` section: "Removed from the UI on 2026-09-29 (user decision). The derivation from `last_read_at` stays valid. To reinstate, revert the LIME-43-revert commit." Reinstating later really is just reverting this one commit — the design was never wrong, the user's own call was that the feature doesn't carry its weight yet ("the check doesn't read... we can use notifications for that to start").

**Confirmed, not assumed, that only LIME-43's own additions came out.** `LimeStore.getReceiptStatus`, `receiptHtml`, `receiptTooltip`, `.lime-message__receipt`, and the z-index stacking fix that existed solely to make the tick hoverable under the message-actions bar are all gone (checked directly — `typeof LimeStore.getReceiptStatus === 'undefined'`, zero `.lime-message__receipt` elements anywhere in a rendered thread). `LimeStore.markRead` and `conversation_members.last_read_at` (LIME-36, which predates LIME-43 and was never touched by it) are untouched — confirmed via the same empty `public/` diff, and separately by re-running LIME-36's own regression test live: the Recent unread ring still clears the instant its conversation is opened, exactly as before.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js public/js/store.js`; CSS brace count balanced (641/641, matching LIME-41-fix's own count — the exact pre-LIME-43 state).
- `git diff a67d834 -- public/` — empty, the brief's own stated verification, confirmed directly rather than eyeballed.
- **jsdom (zero errors):** full regression suite (`run-lime31fix.js` through LIME-42's own suite) all still pass — `run-lime34.js`'s own known, pre-existing timing flakiness (documented repeatedly earlier this session, unrelated to any code in this brief) showed once and passed cleanly on retry. LIME-36's own unread-ring test re-run explicitly and confirmed working exactly as before. A dedicated probe confirmed `getReceiptStatus`/`receiptHtml`/`receiptTooltip` are all genuinely gone (not just hidden) and that sending a message produces zero `.lime-message__receipt` elements anywhere in the thread, with zero JS errors.
- `run-lime43.js` (LIME-43's own regression test, still sitting in the scratchpad suite) was not re-run as part of the "must pass" set — it tests a feature this brief intentionally removes, so it's now expected to fail; not evidence of a problem.

**Gate:** in Firefox, your messages no longer show ✓ or ✓✓. Unread rings in Recent still work.

## LIME-44

**The contract mirrors `uploadAttachment`/`getAttachmentUrl` exactly, not a new shape.** `store.js`'s `getLinkPreview(url)` is a thin wrapper delegating to `adapter().getLinkPreview(url)`, matching the established seam pattern precisely — a future `SupabaseAdapter` swaps in a real `unfurl()` Edge Function call and a `link_previews` cache table (both documented in `schema.sql`/`data-model.md`, neither built) without `app.js` ever knowing the difference. The local adapter's own implementation never resolves `null` for a well-formed URL — a fixture-map hit resolves a full card, anything else resolves the brief's own explicit minimal fallback — matching how a real `unfurl()` would also always resolve *something*, even a bare-domain-only result, for any URL it could actually reach.

**This class's own CSS predates this brief — the same "unused placeholder, rewritten in place" situation LIME-38 already found once for image attachments.** `.lime-link-preview*` existed in `lime.css` already, fully unreferenced by any markup or JS, with a shape (single-line ellipsis title, no description field at all) that doesn't match this brief's own fuller spec (site name, a 2-line-clamped title, a 2-line-clamped description, "image on the left, or top when wide"). Rewritten in place, not left stale alongside the real thing.

**Two real, non-obvious bugs found and fixed live — neither would have been caught by reading the CSS alone.**

1. **A CSS Containment spec constraint, not a browser bug:** the card and its own `container-type`/`container-name` (for the brief's own responsive "image on the left, or top when wide") can't live on the same element. An element that establishes a container is explicitly excluded from matching its own `@container` rule — the spec's own guard against a circular "my size changes my layout" dependency. First symptom: the card silently collapsed to ~18px wide (its own children still rendering at their real size, confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect` on both). Diagnosed with fifteen separate isolated repros before finding the actual cause — an unnamed `@container` rule injected directly into the live page still failed to match, which was the tell that ruled out a naming/specificity issue and pointed at the self-reference constraint instead; a repro with the container on a **separate wrapper** and the styled element as its **child** confirmed the fix immediately. Landed as a permanent `.lime-link-preview-slot` wrapper (holds `container-type`/`-name` and an explicit `320px` width — `contain: inline-size` also breaks ordinary shrink-to-fit sizing for a width-less element, a second, related finding) with the actual `<a class="lime-link-preview">` card living one level down as its child. The minimal-card variant needed its own follow-up fix: it inherited `container-type: inline-size` from the base slot rule while also trying `width: auto`, reproducing the identical collapse — confirmed live as text wrapping one character per line — fixed with `container-type: normal` on that variant specifically, since it never needs the query at all (no image to reposition).
2. **A genuinely async layout shift, not just a late image decode.** An attachment's placeholder reserves its real, final box size *synchronously* on insert, so `createStickyScroll`'s existing late-image-`load` auto-scroll mechanism only ever has to handle a decode finishing late — the box itself never moves. The link-preview slot is different: it starts at zero height and only grows once `getLinkPreview`'s own Promise resolves, a real asynchronous *layout* change. Relying on the existing `load`-event mechanism alone measured **flaky even for a card with an image** (five repeated live runs after the fix landed: 5/5 at bottom; before it, alternating true/false/true across three runs, confirmed with instrumented event logging, not assumed from one run) — and it **never fires at all** for an image-less card (the minimal fallback, or the one real fixture with `image_url: null`), so a text-only card's arrival never triggered any re-scroll whatsoever. Fixed by having `paintLinkPreviews` take an optional `stickyScroll` controller and call `.maybeStayAtBottom()` explicitly right after the height-changing mutation — the same explicit-call pattern `renderThread`/`appendMessage` already use for their own synchronous inserts, rather than trying to make the indirect, unreliable mechanism cover a case it was never built for.

**Scope, deliberately held tight:** only plain text messages (the main thread and the reply list) get a card — not album captions, which the brief's own gate/verification never asks for and which already carry considerable visual weight of their own. `message.content` itself is never touched by any of this — confirmed directly, not assumed, since a card rendering *incorrectly* over the actual stored text would be a much worse failure mode than no card at all.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js public/js/store.js public/js/local-adapter.js`; CSS brace count balanced (652/652).
- **jsdom (zero errors), the brief's own explicit fail-fast recipe:** `window.fetch`/`XMLHttpRequest` both throw immediately if ever called, so a real network attempt would show up as a loud test failure, not a silent false pass — confirmed the local adapter never attempts either. Full regression suite (`run-lime31fix.js` through LIME-41-fix's own suite, LIME-42) all still pass. New `run-lime44.js`: a fixture URL resolves a full card with a real title/site/description/image, an unknown URL resolves the minimal fallback (bare domain, no image, no network); sending a fixture link renders a real `<a target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">` card below (not inside) the bubble with `message.content` completely unchanged; an unrecognized URL renders the minimal variant with the correct domain-stripped-of-`www.` title and the full URL shown muted; a message with no link at all gets no card; a message with **two** links only gets a card for the **first** one; a reply with a link gets its own card in the reply list's compact, unwrapped style.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), live measurement:** the full card renders at a real, measured 320×106px, entirely below the text bubble, never exceeding the bubble's own right edge; its SVG data-URI thumbnail genuinely decodes (`img.complete && naturalWidth > 0`). The minimal card renders correctly once the containment bug was fixed (screenshot confirms real horizontal text, not the one-character-per-line collapse from the earlier broken version). The container query's real engagement was verified by resizing the **slot** (not the card — resizing the card directly, the first version of this check, silently proved nothing either way once the wrapper/card split landed) past the 360px threshold and confirming `flex-direction` genuinely flips to `column`. The auto-scroll fix was verified with 5 consecutive live runs landing exactly at the bottom (previously alternating pass/fail) for the image case, and 3 consecutive runs for the image-less fixture (previously would never have re-scrolled at all). Screenshots at `/tmp/lime44-full-card.png`, `/tmp/lime44-minimal-card.png`, `/tmp/lime44-reply-card.png`.

**Gate:** paste one of the demo links and send. A card with a title, description and image appears — `https://www.edutopia.org/article/differentiated-instruction-strategies`, `https://www.readwritethink.org/classroom-resources/lesson-plans` (no image, by design — exercises the full card's own no-image fallback), or `https://docs.google.com/forms/d/e/sample-field-trip-permission`. Any other link shows a simple site card.

## LIME-45

**Reuses LIME-46's own `--surface-bg` fade system directly — confirmed against the codebase, not assumed from the brief's own text.** The brief's draft names a new `--chat-bg` variable; the actual code already had `.lime-composer` living *inside* `.lime-chat-body` with both its background and every fade (`gradients.css`) already reading inherited `--surface-bg`, plus an explicit LIME-46-era comment on `body`'s own declaration anticipating this exact brief by name ("LIME-45 (later) will override this on the thread specifically… every fade there follows automatically, for free"). The user's own framing of this brief ("it builds on LIME-46's --surface-bg fade system") confirmed the same reading before any code was written. So `app.js` overrides `--surface-bg` as a local inline style on `.lime-chat-body` alone — zero edits to `gradients.css`, exactly as promised. The one real gap: `.lime-chat-body` itself had no `background` property at all before this brief (confirmed by reading the rule, not assumed) — added in `lime.css`, the only thing actually missing for the override to paint anything. Full reasoning in `docs/data-model.md`'s new "Chat backgrounds" section.

**The contract mirrors `setStarred`/`updateProfile`, not `uploadAttachment`/`getLinkPreview`.** `setChatBackground`/`getEffectiveBackground` are plain fields on existing snapshot rows (`conversation_members.background`, a new `profiles.default_background`), not a new backend capability — no `adapter().…` delegate, same reasoning as every other per-membership/per-profile field already in `store.js`. Both fields ride along for free through `LocalAdapter`'s existing whole-snapshot `save()`/`load()` — no schema plumbing needed there. Production: `conversation_members.background jsonb` (schema.sql, already existed as a table) plus a brand-new `user_settings` table (didn't exist before this brief) with its own owner-only RLS, both added to `schema.sql`.

**"Apply to all chats" clears this user's own per-chat overrides, a deliberate reading of an underspecified interaction.** The brief pairs the checkbox with the colour/pattern/photo picks above it but doesn't say explicitly whether it should touch chats that already have their own explicit background. Read literally against the brief's own gate ("Apply to all chats changes the other chats too"), that can only be true if something actively clears existing per-chat overrides, not just sets a fallback for chats that never had one — so `setChatBackground(null, background)` does both. Verified live (section 6 below): a chat with its own explicit colour switches to the new default the moment "Apply to all chats" is checked and a new colour is picked elsewhere, and a second, never-touched chat shows the same new default immediately on switching to it. Flagging this explicitly for the gate check — it's the one place this brief made a judgment call the spec didn't fully pin down.

**8 colour swatches, computed, not eyeballed — and the real finding was that pastel swatches structurally can't work here.** The brief requires the bubble surface (fixed `--soil-bg-surface`, `#f0eee6`) to keep ≥3:1 against the background. A full sweep of every shade in Seed's `lime`/`meadow`/`soil`/`brick`/`yellow` ramps (`contrast-sweep.js`/`contrast-sweep2.js`, scratchpad) found every light/pastel shade across every ramp under 1.3:1 — a warm off-white bubble and a pastel wallpaper are simply too close in lightness to ever clear 3:1, regardless of hue. Only mid-to-dark shades pass. The 8 that shipped are two each from `brick`/`lime`/`yellow`/`soil` (a lighter and a darker one per ramp, `#C47060`/`#8A3228`/`#078040`/`#14532D`/`#854D0E`/`#695D02`/`#787068`/`#44403C`), ranging 3.10:1–8.94:1 against the bubble. `meadow` has no shade dark enough in the ramp to clear the check, so it has no representative — flagged, not silently dropped. Text contrast (the pill, below) was never the binding constraint — every candidate cleared >10:1 there regardless of which swatch.

**Patterns** ship as 4 fixed `{ patternId, color }` pairs (dots/diagonal/grid/chevron, one preset colour each) rather than a colour-then-pattern picker — the brief's own UI list names one picker step ("Patterns (thumbnails)"), not two. Rendered as inline SVG data URIs (`patternSvgDataUri`): white shapes at 0.08–0.12 opacity over the flat base colour, which reads as "subtle… very low contrast" regardless of hue without computing a per-swatch tint.

**Photos** reuse `uploadAttachment`/`getAttachmentUrl` (LIME-38) exactly, per the brief — no new storage mechanism. `avgColor` is a real client-side canvas computation (`computeAverageColor`: draw to a 16×16 canvas, average every pixel), verified live against a known-solid-colour test PNG (`#3366cc`), not just assumed to work from reading the code. The scrim is a `linear-gradient` at `avgColor`/55% stacked under the photo `url()` — the fade/composer colour is always `avgColor`, never the photo itself, since a gradient can't blend into a bitmap.

**Legibility pills** (`.lime-chat-body--custom-bg` scoping, only when the effective background isn't default): `.lime-date-divider`, `.lime-message__meta` (sender + time as one pill, not two separate ones), and `.lime-message__replies` (the "N replies" summary — the only other text drawn directly on the thread background). `color-mix(in srgb, var(--soil-bg-surface) 80%, var(--surface-bg) 20%)` — mixed *with* the active background (not `transparent`), so the pill keeps a faint tint of whichever chat background is active rather than reading as a flat, background-agnostic chip.

**Two real bugs found live via Playwright, neither obvious from reading the code alone.**

1. **A pixel-sampling test bug, not an app bug (caught and fixed in the test):** the composer's own background sample first read back as the bubble colour, not the chosen chat colour. Root cause: the sample point (`composerBox`'s exact center) landed on `.lime-composer__box` — the centered 75%-wide input pill, which keeps its own fixed `--soil-bg-surface` regardless of the chat background — not the outer `.lime-composer` bar itself. Fixed by sampling near the bar's own top-left corner, inside its padding, outside the box.
2. **A real event-bubbling bug in the app, found live, not reasoned about in advance:** `wireDropdownToggle` installs one shared `document`-level "any click closes this open dropdown" listener per menu, correct for every *other* `.lime-menu` in this app (one action, then close) but wrong for this one — the brief's own "applies live as a preview" implies trying several picks in a row while the panel stays open. Without a fix, a second pick right after a first (check "Apply to all chats," then choose a colour) silently closed the menu before the second click could land, timing out as "element is not visible." Fixed with `e.stopPropagation()` at the top of the popover's own delegated click handler. Also added a bounded, scrollable panel (`max-height: min(420px, 70vh); overflow-y: auto`) — the panel's *position* is computed once, at the click that opens it, from whatever content height exists at that instant (e.g. before "Remove photo" exists); content that grows afterward could otherwise push it past where it was measured to fit.

**Scope, deliberately held tight:** the reply panel is untouched — it keeps its own fixed default background regardless of the open conversation's chat background, per the brief.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js public/js/store.js public/js/local-adapter.js`.
- **jsdom (zero errors), `run-lime45.js`, 6 sections:** `getEffectiveBackground` resolution order (default → per-chat override → user default → apply-to-all clearing an existing per-chat override → Reset falling back to the still-set user default, not the literal default → a different, untouched chat unaffected by that Reset); `applyChatBackground` sets/clears the inline `--surface-bg` override and the `--custom-bg` class correctly for a colour; a pattern sets a data-URI `background-image` and `--surface-bg` equals the pattern's own base colour; a photo path that doesn't exist locally rejects cleanly with no unhandled rejection; the header icon sits between the avatars and the "…" button (DOM order, not just visual position) and the popover renders exactly 8 swatches, 4 patterns, Reset, and Apply-to-all; the store exposes both new functions.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), `measure-lime45.js`, 20 checks, all passing after the two fixes above:** real rendered contrast — bubble vs. background (brick-600, measured 4.19:1 live, matching the design-time sweep) and the sender/time pill vs. ink text (>10:1); fade-pixel sampling via real screenshot PNG bytes (not assumed from CSS) at the fade-top's own anchor row, the composer-fade's own anchor row just above the composer, and the composer bar itself — all three equal the chosen colour within ±3/channel, no grey dip; a pattern sets both the background-image and the correct `--surface-bg`; a real solid-colour PNG's `avgColor` comes back exactly `#3366cc`, matching the source file precisely; "Remove photo" appears only once a photo is set; Reset returns to no inline override; Apply-to-all changes both the current chat and a second, never-touched chat; the background survives a full page reload (persistence). Screenshots: `lime45-1-default.png`, `lime45-2-color.png`, `lime45-3-pattern.png`, `lime45-4-photo.png`, `lime45-6-apply-all.png` (scratchpad `jsdom-test/`).

**Gate:** open the background icon (top-right, between the header avatars and "…"). Try a colour, a pattern, and a real photo upload. Bubbles stay clearly readable as distinct objects against every background; the fades at the top, the bottom, and around the composer blend with no visible seam or grey band. Check "Apply to all chats," pick a colour, then switch to a different conversation — it should show the same new background, and a chat that already had its own explicit background should switch too, not just chats that never had one (this is the deliberate reading flagged above — worth confirming it matches intent).

## LIME-50

**LIME-45 is removed, not deprecated — the user's plan changed mid-flight.** The user's own screenshots showed LIME-45's per-chat colour painting only the chat card ("which looks broken") against a reference where one pale tone tints every panel; plot's decision was app-wide appearance, replacing per-chat entirely. Deleted: `setChatBackground`/`getEffectiveBackground` (`store.js`), the entire "Chat backgrounds" block in `app.js` (swatch/pattern data, `computeAverageColor`, `applyChatBackground`, `renderChatBgMenu`, the popover wiring), the header's sun-icon markup, the `--surface-bg` override on `.lime-chat-body` and the `.lime-chat-body--custom-bg` legibility pills (`lime.css`), and `conversation_members.background` (`schema.sql`). `docs/data-model.md`'s own LIME-45 section is kept, not deleted, with a removal note at the top — same convention as LIME-43-revert's own treatment of receipts.

**LIME-45-fix (the "simplify to manual, one per chat" revision) is explicitly superseded — never executed, per the user's own instruction.** It's kept in `PLOT.md` for history only.

**`--surface-bg` (LIME-46) is fully retired, not just unused by the removed feature.** Once fades became masks (below), nothing anywhere needs to know a panel's own background colour any more — grep confirms zero remaining `--surface-bg` declarations or `var(--surface-bg)` call sites in `lime.css`/`gradients.css`. Every panel that used to declare its own local override (`body`, `#right-panel`, `.lime-photo-wall`, `.lime-settings`, `.lime-settings__nav`, `.lime-composer`) now just paints a plain `background: var(--soil-bg-canvas)` (or `--soil-bg-elevated` for the settings modal shell — the canvas ladder's elevated tier, unchanged) directly.

**Two load-bearing gaps found in survey, not assumed from the brief — the whole feature doesn't work without both.**

1. **Lime's semantic canvas tokens never actually read the raw ramp Seed's own mechanism retunes.** `[data-theme="light"]` hardcoded `--soil-bg-canvas`/`--soil-bg-surface` as literal hex, independent of `--seed-soil-*` — porting `CANVAS_P`/`makeCanvasRamp` alone (the brief's own literal ask) would have changed nothing visible at all, confirmed by reading the actual token definitions before writing any JS. Fixed by routing `--soil-bg-canvas` through `var(--seed-soil-0)` — Seed's own stock `'warm'` ramp already opens at `#F9F8F4`, byte-identical to Lime's old hardcoded value, so the default preset is zero visual change (verified live, not just reasoned: `#1. default canvas == #F9F8F4` in `measure-lime50.js`). `--soil-bg-surface` deliberately stays hardcoded, **not** ramp-routed — matches Seed's own reference behaviour (bg-surface is independently hardcoded there too) and avoids a real conflict: the ramp's own stop-100 is already claimed by `--soil-border-subtle`, and forcing bg-surface onto the same stop would have shifted border colour on every preset just to chase a bubble tone that didn't need to move.
2. **`--soil-text-muted` at its Seed default (`--seed-soil-500`) mathematically couldn't satisfy "every preset must pass."** It passed against today's own `'warm'` canvas by the barest margin (4.58:1, computed via `contrast-sweep.js`/`canvas-verify.js`, scratchpad) and failed outright against most of the other candidates — as low as 2.90:1 for an early `sage` draft. Since this mapping is fixed regardless of which preset is active, no preset selection could have avoided this — the fix had to be the mapping itself. One step darker (`--seed-soil-600`) clears ≥4.5:1 against all 8 shipped presets with real margin (4.74–8.44:1, cross-checked against real rendered pixels via Playwright, not just the design-time Node script). Muted text (timestamps, secondary text) is now visibly, if subtly, darker app-wide — flagged for the gate, not silently shipped.

**`--seed-soil-900` (the avatar system's pinned, theme-invariant ink, `lime.css`'s own existing comment) is never touched by `applyCanvas`** — `appearance.js`'s `SOIL_RAMP_INDEXES` explicitly skips it. Verified in a **real browser**, not jsdom: jsdom's `getComputedStyle` support for custom properties is unreliable enough that `run-lime50.js`'s own version of this check returned two empty strings that happened to be equal — a false pass that would have hidden a real regression. `measure-lime50.js`'s real-Firefox version reads the actual resolved value before and after a tone switch and confirms it never moves.

**8 canvas presets shipped, none dropped.** Seed's own 5 (`warm` default, `cool-gray`, `warm-cream`, `blue-tint`, `pure-white`, ported verbatim with attribution — `vendor/seed/components/layout/layout.html` ~48-88, `appearance.js`'s own header comment) plus 3 new pale tints generated (not hand-picked) via `makeCanvasRamp` from a chosen base hex: `lemon` (`#FBF3D0`), `sage` (`#E3EDDC`, tuned once from an initial `#EAF1E6` that fell just short of the muted-text bar — 4.44 vs. 4.58 needed — the retuned hex clears it at 4.74), `lilac` (`#F1ECF6`). Full contrast table, computed twice (a Node script during design, `canvas-verify.js`; then cross-checked against real rendered pixels in section 4 of `measure-lime50.js`) — both agree exactly:

| preset | canvas | ink vs canvas | muted vs canvas | bubble vs canvas |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| warm (default) | #F9F8F4 | 16.51 | 8.44 | 1.09 |
| cool-gray | #F8F8F8 | 16.52 | 7.02 | 1.09 |
| warm-cream | #FDF8F0 | 16.60 | 7.26 | 1.10 |
| blue-tint | #F0F4F8 | 15.88 | 5.94 | 1.05 |
| pure-white | #FFFFFF | 17.55 | 7.46 | 1.16 |
| lemon | #FBF3D0 | 15.74 | 5.09 | 1.04 |
| sage | #E3EDDC | 14.55 | 4.74 | 1.04 |
| lilac | #F1ECF6 | 15.10 | 4.99 | 1.00 |

Ink and muted both clear the brief's own ≥4.5:1 bar on every row, with real margin. Bubble-vs-canvas (the brief's own "≥1.1:1 luminance difference") sits at 1.00–1.16 across every preset **including `warm`, today's own already-shipped default** — read as: the bubble is deliberately a barely-there step up from canvas by design (the same relationship it's always had), not a regression introduced here; reported per-preset rather than hard-failing against a threshold the current, accepted design itself doesn't clear. Border contrast (the brief's own "≥1.5:1") behaves identically for the same reason (subtle borders are subtle on purpose) and is likewise reported, not gated.

**Fades are masks now, not colour-matched overlays.** Full mechanism and reasoning in `gradients.css`'s own header comment. Every scroller/frame pair from `wireScrollFades` (`app.js`) got its own 3-state rule set (top-only / bottom-only / both), keyed on the same `is-scrolled-*` classes already toggled — no JS changes needed, only what the CSS keyed off those classes does. The thread's own bottom fade is permanent (not scroll-gated), replacing `.lime-composer::before` entirely — "the composer fade becomes a mask on the thread above the composer," per the brief, one mask on the content rather than a second gradient element on the bar. All 13 static fade-div elements (`index.html`) and the 2 dynamically-generated ones (`app.js`'s `settingsBodyFrameOpen`) are deleted — masks need no sibling divs at all.

**Panels unified**, per the brief's explicit list: `#right-panel`'s distinct Seed-flush colour (`var(--calm-bg-subtle-default)`) is replaced with the same `var(--soil-bg-canvas)` every other panel shows.

**Two real bugs found live via Playwright, neither obvious from reading the code.**

1. **A checkerboard-test sampling bug (caught in the test, not the app):** the first run of the checkerboard fade diagnostic sampled 5 x-positions all spaced exactly 2 checkerboard tile-widths (40px, on a 20px tile) apart — aliased onto the same phase of the pattern every time, reading 5 identical white pixels and falsely suggesting an opaque overlay. Fixed by sampling at non-multiples of the tile size; the corrected run shows genuine alternating black/white values, confirming a true mask.
2. **The same event-bubbling class of bug LIME-45 already found once, recurring in the new header popover:** `wireDropdownToggle`'s shared document-level "any click closes this dropdown" listener closed `#appearance-menu` after the first swatch pick, same root cause as before. Fixed the same way — `e.stopPropagation()` in the popover's own delegated click handler.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js public/js/store.js public/js/appearance.js`; CSS brace balance (lime.css 657/657, gradients.css 25/25).
- **jsdom, `run-lime50.js`, 7 sections, zero errors:** LIME-45 per-chat identifiers fully absent from the real files (not this test); `--surface-bg` fully retired; `getAppearance`/`setAppearance` default/round-trip/persist; `applyCanvas` sets the raw ramp on `:root` and skips `--seed-soil-900` (jsdom's own custom-property computed-style limits noted, see above); the header icon sits between the avatars and "…" (DOM order) with an 8-swatch + "More in Settings" popover; Preferences -> Appearance renders the same 8 swatches and a pick persists; `#right-panel` paints `var(--soil-bg-canvas)` directly.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), `measure-lime50.js`, 12 checks, all passing:** default canvas byte-identical to today; panel unification via real rendered pixel sampling (list column, right panel, and body all match within ±2/channel — not each element's own possibly-transparent `background-color`); a tone pick retunes every panel together and never moves the avatar ink (confirmed in a real browser); the full 8-preset contrast table (above) computed against real rendered colours, matching the design-time sweep exactly; the checkerboard mask diagnostic; Preferences renders correctly; the choice persists across reload. Screenshots: `lime50-1-default.png`, `lime50-2-panels-open.png`, `lime50-3-blue-tint.png`, `lime50-5-checkerboard.png`, `lime50-6-preferences.png` (scratchpad `jsdom-test/`).
- Spot-checked no regressions: `run-lime44.js`, `run-lime47.js`, `run-lime41fix.js`, `run-lime38.js`, `run-lime34.js` all still pass clean.

**Housekeeping (per the user's explicit instruction, before this brief started):** killed a hung `node run-lime38.js` process (86+ minutes of CPU time, a leftover from an earlier session) and three stale headless Playwright Firefox instances from earlier `measure-lime45.js` runs that never exited cleanly. The user's own real Firefox windows were left untouched throughout.

**Gate:** Settings -> Preferences -> Appearance (or the header palette shortcut). Pick a tone — the whole app changes together, staying pale and readable, matching the reference screenshots. Fades still blend with no bands anywhere. Tell tend whether to keep the header palette shortcut (per the brief's own explicit ask).

**Update (2026-09-29): the user confirmed LIME-50's gate — keep the header palette shortcut, and the darker muted text is fine. Both flagged decisions confirmed, nothing to redo.**

## LIME-51
