**Survey conclusion, confirmed before writing any CSS: Seed's own `[data-theme="dark"]` token block already does most of the work.** `tokens.css` ships real dark values for `--soil-*`, `--calm-*`, `--good-*`/`--warn-*`/`--bad-*`, and `--selected-*` — and a full grep of `lime.css`/`gradients.css` found only ~20 raw colour literals in the entire app, most already justified exceptions (see below). Setting `data-theme="dark"` on `<html>` alone already made the large majority of the app look reasonable; the brief's own "audit and fix" turned out to be narrow and specific, not a ground-up dark palette — a genuinely different shape of work than the brief's own framing implied, worth stating plainly rather than performing a bigger rewrite than the evidence called for.

**The mode mechanism:** Light/Dark/System via `.seed-tabs--pill` (Seed's own tab component, reused rather than inventing a new segmented control), shared between the header popover and Settings → Preferences → Appearance through one top-level `modeTabsHtml()` (`app.js`, not IIFE-private — same reasoning `escapeHtml` already is). `LimeAppearance.applyTheme(mode)` resolves `'system'` via `matchMedia` and sets `data-theme`; a single `matchMedia` change listener (installed once) re-reads the *stored* mode fresh on every fire rather than closing over whatever mode was active at install time, so switching into or out of System later still works correctly.

**No flash of light theme — the brief's own hard requirement, verified live, not assumed from reading the inline script.** A tiny `<script>` in `<head>`, before any stylesheet link, reads a raw `lime-theme` localStorage key and sets `data-theme="dark"` immediately if needed — `LimeStore` itself loads asynchronously and can't be consulted this early. Identical block duplicated in `index.html`/`login.html`/`signup.html` (the brief's own explicit ask, not an oversight — a shared external file would itself be a render-blocking request). Replaces, not adds to, the old `localStorage.getItem('lime-theme')` read that used to sit unconditionally at the top of `app.js` — confirmed via grep that nothing had ever written that key, so it was already fully inert.

**A real, load-bearing bug found live via Playwright, invisible from reading the code alone:** `body` never set its own `color` at all. Every element without an explicit colour (the message bubble text among them) fell through to the browser's own UA default — black — not `--soil-text`. This was completely masked in light mode, where `--soil-text` resolves to `#131b17`, visually indistinguishable from pure black at normal sizes; only exposed once dark mode gave `--soil-text` an actually different value to diverge from. Measured live: **1.31:1** (bubble text on a dark bubble) before the fix, a real WCAG failure that would have shipped invisibly if verification had stopped at "does the app boot in dark mode without errors." Fixed with one line, `color: var(--soil-text)` on `body` — **14.50:1** after, re-verified against real rendered pixels, not just re-read from the CSS.

**Avatar pastels: 12 real dark variants, computed, not eyeballed.** Moved from a theme-invariant `:root` block into `[data-theme="light"]`/`[data-theme="dark"]` pairs. Each dark value is its light counterpart's own HSL with saturation reduced 15 points (floored at 20) and lightness reduced 10 points (floored at 55) — deep enough to read as a dark-mode surface, never dropping below the point where the one ink colour that still never moves (`--seed-soil-900`, pinned globally for the avatar identity system) stops passing AA. Verified: 9.58:1 to 12.97:1 across all 12, nowhere close to the 4.5:1 floor.

| hue | light | dark | ink contrast |
|---|---|---|---|
| blush | #F9D8DA | #EBB3B6 | 9.74 |
| peach | #FCDCC5 | #F1C09D | 10.67 |
| butter | #FBEFB8 | #EFDE91 | 12.97 |
| mint | #D3F0DE | #B5DBC3 | 11.60 |
| sky | #D2E9F7 | #AED2E8 | 11.02 |
| periwinkle | #DBDEF7 | #B8BDE7 | 9.58 |
| lavender | #E8DCF5 | #CEBCE2 | 9.96 |
| lilac | #F0D9EE | #DABCD7 | 10.16 |
| sand | #F0E6D2 | #DBCEB4 | 11.28 |
| teal | #CFEEEA | #B2D8D3 | 11.41 |
| apricot | #FBE0B0 | #EECA8A | 11.24 |
| rose | #F5DCE2 | #E2BCC5 | 10.22 |

**The only other hardcoded-colour gap the audit found:** `.lime-recent`/`.lime-messages`'s own visible scrollbar thumbs (`rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.15)` — every other scroller in the app hides its thumb entirely, `scrollbar-width: none`, so this pair is the only place a thumb color is even visible). A black-based translucent thumb is nearly invisible on a near-black dark canvas. Wrapped in a new `--lime-scrollbar-thumb` token (light: the original value; dark: `rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.18)`) rather than left as a bare literal now that there are two values to hold.

**Nav/primary-button lime accent needed no Lime-specific dark override — checked computationally, not assumed.** Seed's own dark `--selected-*` tokens are already lime-hued by default (unlike Seed's "mint" default that Lime's own light-mode override retunes) and measured 5.58:1 (primary button text on its own background) and 11.09:1/8.06:1 (nav "selected" text/icon) — all clearing the brief's own 4.5:1/3:1 bars before any change. Confirmed live via Playwright too (section 4 below), not just from reading `tokens.css`.

**Canvas tones are light-mode-only, per the brief's own explicit ask** (matching Seed's own convention) — the swatch grid renders `disabled` (not hidden) in dark mode, with "Tones apply in light mode" printed inline, in both the header popover and Preferences.

**Everything else audited and left alone, with reasoning, not silently:** the confirm-dialog's danger button (`color: #fff` on a bold red background), the album/reply-quote "+N" photo overlays (`rgba(19, 27, 23, ...)` scrims with white text over arbitrary photo content), and the modal backdrop's own blur tint are all genuinely theme-invariant by design — a bold-coloured surface or a scrim over a photo, not a themed panel, so converting them to tokens would just create a second value with nothing different to hold. The `#14b8a6` decorative "story unseen" avatar-ring gradient endpoint is a fixed accent, checked via screenshot in both themes, not assumed to be fine. `gradients.css`'s own `#000` values are every one inside a `mask-image` gradient — the mask's own alpha channel, never a painted colour — grep-visible but not a real hit; noted so a future audit doesn't re-flag them.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js public/js/store.js public/js/appearance.js`; CSS brace balance (lime.css 663/663).
- **jsdom, `run-lime51.js`, 9 sections, zero errors:** all three HTML entry points carry the pre-paint theme script; the old unconditional `lime-theme` read is gone from `app.js`; `applyTheme` resolves light/dark/system correctly against both a light-OS and dark-OS `matchMedia` stub and persists the raw key; `setAppearance({theme})` never clobbers a previously-set canvas (or vice versa); the header popover and Preferences both render 3 mode tabs and all 8 canvas swatches `disabled` in dark, with the "Tones apply in light mode" note present; picking Dark from Preferences applies live and persists; the avatar palette's light/dark split exists in the stylesheet (jsdom's own custom-property computed-style support inside attribute-selector blocks is unreliable, cross-checked for real in Firefox instead); a fresh boot with a simulated dark OS preference produces zero errors.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), `measure-lime51.js`, all passing after the two fixes above:** no-flash confirmed by setting the theme through the real store (matching actual usage, not just the raw mirror key) and reloading — `data-theme="dark"` immediately, before the app has rendered anything, and the body's rendered canvas colour confirmed dark, not a light-then-dark swap; System mode resolving light under a simulated light OS preference and then live-following a simulated change to dark (`page.emulateMedia`); a dark-mode contrast table (contact names, sender names, timestamps, bubble text, the primary button) sampled against real rendered pixels, all clearing 4.5:1; the final grep-count of remaining raw colour literals (39 in `lime.css`, all accounted for above; 66 in `gradients.css`, all mask-alpha `#000`, none painted). Screenshots across the brief's own view list, both themes where applicable: `lime51-1-dark-main.png`, `lime51-3-light-main.png`, `lime51-3-dark-main.png`, `lime51-3-dark-replies.png`, `lime51-3-dark-profile-panel.png`, `lime51-3-dark-menu.png`, `lime51-3-dark-search-modal.png`, `lime51-3-dark-confirm-dialog.png`, `lime51-3-dark-link-preview.png`, `lime51-3-dark-image-message.png`, `lime51-3-dark-settings-profile.png`, `lime51-3-dark-settings-security.png`, `lime51-3-dark-settings-preferences.png`, `lime51-3-dark-login.png` (scratchpad `jsdom-test/`). Toasts are not screenshotted — grepped and confirmed no toast mechanism exists anywhere in this app yet (LIME-27, which would add one, hasn't landed), so there's nothing to verify there, not an oversight.
