## LIME-50-fix

**Root cause confirmed by reading `lime.css`, not re-guessed from the brief's own summary:** LIME-50 routed only `--soil-bg-canvas` through the ramp. `--soil-bg-surface` was a hardcoded literal (a deliberate LIME-50 decision, to dodge a stop-100 conflict with `--soil-border-subtle` — see LIME-50's own TEND.md entry), and Seed's own `--calm-bg-subtle-*`/`--calm-bg-normal-*` map to ramp stops (`soil-25/30/40/50/75`) `CANVAS_P`'s 11-value presets never touch. On every tone but Warm, bubbles, the composer box, the segmented toggle, hovered/selected list rows and the mic pill all stayed the old Warm-tinted beige/grey — exactly what the user's own lemon/sage screenshots showed.

**The fix: one relative layer system, derived from canvas via `color-mix(in oklab, ...)`, defined for both themes since LIME-51 landed first.** Five tokens — `--lime-layer-raised`/`-surface`/`-hover`/`-active`, `--lime-border-subtle` — each mix the canvas toward ink (light mode) or white (dark mode). OKLab, not srgb, for the same reason LIME-46 picked it for fades originally: perceptually-uniform mixing, no "grey dip" partway through a blend. This also **resolves the exact conflict LIME-50 dodged**: since surface is now a computed mix rather than a raw ramp stop, it no longer competes with border-subtle (also now a mix, not a stop) for the same `--seed-soil-100` value — the tension that forced `--soil-bg-surface` to stay hardcoded in the first place is simply gone.

**Remapped, not edited per-component — 9 Seed/Lime semantic tokens, in Lime's own theme blocks:** `--soil-bg-surface`, `--soil-bg-elevated`, `--calm-bg-subtle-default/-hover/-active`, `--calm-bg-normal-default/-hover`, `--soil-border-subtle`, `--calm-border-normal-default`. Every component that already reads one of these — bubbles, the composer box, search fields, menus and modals, hovered/selected list rows, and (via `vendor/seed/components/tabs/tabs.css`, unedited, per the project's own "vendor/ can't be edited" rule) the segmented toggle's track and active pill — follows the tone with zero per-component changes. `--calm-bg-subtle-default`/`--calm-bg-normal-default` both land on the "surface" layer, and their own `-hover` partners both land on "hover": checked each of the ~90 call sites across these two families first (grep), and this app's own usage never actually needed Seed's subtle-vs-normal distinction preserved as two different fill values.

**Percentages tuned by measurement across all 8 tones plus dark, not guessed from the brief's own starting numbers — several needed adjusting to actually clear the brief's own targets.** Computed via a faithful OKLab port (Björn Ottosson's formulas) in a Node script, cross-checked against Firefox's own real `color-mix(in oklab, ...)` output before committing to any percentage (`colormix-test.js`/`final-verify.js`, scratchpad).

- First pass (the brief's own literal suggestions: light surface 4%/hover 6%/active 9%/border 10%; dark surface 6%/hover 9%/active 12%/border 14%/raised 8%) **failed two of the brief's own distinctness checks**: light hover-vs-surface ΔL fell as low as 0.012 (sage/lilac) against the ~0.02 target, and dark raised-vs-surface ΔL was only 0.017 — a menu would have read as barely different from a bubble, not "lifted."
- Final: **light** surface 3%, hover 8%, active 12%, border 14% (toward ink `#131b17`), raised 70% (toward white); **dark** surface 6%, hover 10%, active 14%, border 18%, raised 22% (toward white). Every ΔL gap now clears ~0.021–0.127; ink-vs-surface 12.6–16.8:1 and ink-vs-active 10.3–13.6:1 across all 8 tones; dark text-vs-surface 13.96:1, text-vs-active 11.20:1.
- **`sage` needed a second base-hue retune**, not just a percentage change: at 3% surface-darkening, its own already-thin muted-text margin (4.74:1 against bare canvas, from LIME-50) dropped to 4.45:1 against the new, slightly-darker surface — just under the 4.5:1 floor. Retuned `#E3EDDC` → `#D8E6D0` (`appearance.js`), restoring a real margin (4.84:1) without touching the shared percentage set that the other 7 tones already cleared comfortably.

| tone | canvas | surface | hover | active | border | raised | ink/surface | ink/active | muted/surface |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| warm | #F9F8F4 | #f1f0ec | #e4e4e0 | #dadad6 | #d4d5d1 | #fdfdfc | 15.39 | 12.52 | 7.86 |
| cool-gray | #F8F8F8 | #f0f0f0 | #e3e4e3 | #d9dad9 | #d4d5d4 | #fdfdfd | 15.40 | 12.52 | 6.54 |
| warm-cream | #FDF8F0 | #f5f0e9 | #e8e4dc | #dddad2 | #d8d5ce | #fefdfb | 15.48 | 12.56 | 6.77 |
| blue-tint | #F0F4F8 | #e8edf0 | #dce0e3 | #d2d7d9 | #cdd2d4 | #fafcfd | 14.88 | 12.08 | 5.57 |
| pure-white | #FFFFFF | #f7f7f7 | #e9eaea | #dfe0df | #d9dbda | #ffffff | 16.38 | 13.26 | 6.96 |
| lemon | #FBF3D0 | #f3ecca | #e6dfbf | #dbd6b7 | #d6d1b3 | #fefbf1 | 14.76 | 11.96 | 4.77 |
| sage (retuned) | #D8E6D0 | #d1dfca | #c6d4bf | #bdcbb7 | #b9c6b3 | #f3f7f1 | 12.63 | 10.35 | 4.84 |
| lilac | #F1ECF6 | #e9e5ee | #ddd9e2 | #d3d0d8 | #cecbd3 | #fbf9fc | 14.13 | 11.52 | 4.67 |

Dark (one canvas, `#131B17`): surface `#1e2622`, hover `#262e2a`, active `#2e3632`, border `#363e3a`, raised `#3f4642` — text/surface 13.96, text/active 11.20, muted/surface 5.45, ΔL raised-surface 0.127 (clearly distinct, the brief's own explicit check).

**A real, self-inflicted CSS parser bug found live via Playwright, not visible from reading the diff.** A comment written as `--calm-bg-subtle-*/--calm-bg-normal-*` contains a literal `*/` — closing the CSS comment early. Everything after it, until the *next* `*/` fifteen lines later, got parsed as raw CSS (English prose), which silently truncated the entire rest of the stylesheet: confirmed by reading the browser's own parsed `document.styleSheets` — `lime.css` registered only **2 rules** instead of ~660. Every layer token and everything remapped to it was empty; `getComputedStyle` on affected elements returned `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)` or default black, which the first Playwright run reported as literal failures before the real cause was found. `node --check`/brace-balance both stayed green throughout, since neither one parses CSS — a reminder that this project's own established "verify the real thing loads with zero errors" habit needs a CSS-specific form too, not just the JS one. Fixed by rewording the comment (`--calm-bg-subtle-x / --calm-bg-normal-x`); grepped the rest of the file's ~275 comments for the same pattern (a `*/`-shaped token sequence that isn't an actual comment terminator) — none found.

**Also found and worth noting:** Firefox's own `getComputedStyle` reports a `color-mix(in oklab, ...)` result back as a literal `oklab(L a b)` string, not `rgb()` — the verification script's own colour parser needed to handle both formats (a real OKLab→sRGB conversion for the former), not just assume `rgb()`/`rgba()` like every prior brief's own scripts could.

**The mic pill has no fill at rest, matching the `+` button** — its own permanent `--calm-bg-subtle-default` background removed; `.lime-composer__aux`'s shared `background: none` (already there, previously overridden by the more-specific `.lime-composer__voice` rule) now applies to both. The brief's own explicit ask, once every other subtle fill started following the tone and a permanently-lit mic pill read as a disconnected leftover chip.

**The lime accent is unchanged, confirmed not just assumed:** `--selected-*` (nav active state, primary buttons) still clears the brief's own ≥3:1 bar for non-text fills against all 8 tones — nothing here reads any of the 9 remapped tokens, so there was nothing to break.

**No JS changes** — scope stayed exactly `lime.css` (`gradients.css` untouched; its own fade masks read no colour at all, LIME-50), per the brief.

**Verification:**
- CSS brace balance (663/663) — did **not** catch the `*/` bug above (braces stayed balanced throughout; only the browser's own parsed rule count exposed it).
- Full jsdom regression suite (`run-lime44.js`, `run-lime47.js`, `run-lime41fix.js`, `run-lime38.js`, `run-lime34.js`, `run-lime50.js`, `run-lime51.js`) all still pass clean, both before and after the `*/` fix (the corrupted stylesheet didn't happen to break anything jsdom's own suite exercises directly — only real computed-style sampling caught it).
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), `measure-lime50fix.js`, all passing after the parser-bug fix:** all 8 tones swept live (not just computed in Node) — bubble and hover both confirmed visibly different from bare canvas on every one; the mic pill transparent at rest and filled on hover; LIME-51's own fixes re-confirmed unregressed (body's explicit light-mode text colour in dark, an avatar pastel rendering as a real muted colour, not grey); dark mode's bubble-vs-canvas distinction, text-vs-surface contrast, and the raised layer's clear distinction from surface (a menu vs. a bubble); a grep-based check that `.lime-composer__voice` carries no `background` at rest. Screenshots: `lime50fix-1-sage-light.png`, `lime50fix-2-lemon-light.png`, `lime50fix-3-bluetint-light.png`, `lime50fix-4-warm-light.png`, `lime50fix-5-dark-menu.png`, `lime50fix-6-dark-main.png` (scratchpad `jsdom-test/`) — sage and lemon are the user's own two QA tones from the original bug report, screenshotted first.

**Gate:** in Firefox, switch between tones (Warm, lemon, sage, lilac, blue-tint). The segmented toggle, bubbles, the composer box, hovered/selected chats, and the mic button should all visibly shift with the tone now, staying balanced and quiet on each one — not the old fixed beige/grey. Then switch to Dark and confirm the same relationship holds there too, with a menu (raised) clearly lighter than a bubble (surface).

**Update (2026-09-29): the user confirmed LIME-50-fix's gate.** Next: LIME-52.

## LIME-52

**No Subtle Patterns assets to use — `public/assets/patterns/` was empty (confirmed, not assumed), the brief's own documented fallback.** All 4 built-in presets (dots, grid, diagonal, noise) are generated inline as SVG data URIs (`appearance.js`), the same technique LIME-44/45 already used for self-contained images. Since nothing here is actually sourced from Subtle Patterns, no CC BY-SA credit line is shown — flagged in `docs/data-model.md` for whenever a later brief adds real tiles to that folder, at which point the credit becomes necessary and isn't yet. The licensing terms themselves are recorded as "to verify" per the brief's own contingency — this sandbox has no network access to check subtlepatterns.com's current page.

**The store contract needed nothing new.** `getAppearance()`/`setAppearance()` already carried a `pattern` field since LIME-50 (defaulted to `null`, never used) — this brief is entirely rendering + UI, no `store.js` changes at all.

**One fixed layer, `body::before`, serves all three states.** `position: fixed; inset:0; z-index:-1` — sits behind `#layout`'s own normal-flow content in the same stacking context without touching `#layout` itself (already `background: transparent`, LIME-50's own "so this shows through it"). `appearance.js`'s `applyPattern()` sets whichever CSS custom properties the current state (none/preset/upload) actually needs and leaves the rest at their no-op fallback (`none`/`transparent`) — no class toggle, one rule.

- **Presets**: a low-alpha tint (`--lime-pattern-tint`) masked into the pattern's own shape (`--lime-pattern-mask`, the mask's alpha channel — white shapes on a transparent ground, not luminance). Recolours with the tone and mode automatically since the tint is `color-mix(in srgb, ink|white intensity%, transparent)`, never a fixed hex.
- **Uploads**: the image itself, blended against body's own canvas colour via `mix-blend-mode` (`multiply` light, `screen` dark — the brief's own explicit choice) at a capped `opacity`. Stored via the existing `uploadAttachment`/`getAttachmentUrl` seam (LIME-38), path namespaced `appearance/…` (the brief's own literal ask), ≤ 1 MB enforced client-side via the Profile form's own `setFieldError` pattern — no native `window.alert()`, consistent with this app's own established "no native dialogs" rule.

**A real bug found while writing this brief's own verification, not visually: presets tinted toward ink unconditionally, even in dark mode.** LIME-50-fix's own tone-relative layer system (surface/hover/active) tints toward ink in light and **white** in dark — mixing a pattern's tint toward ink regardless of theme would have made an already-dark canvas darker still at each pattern shape, fighting that established convention instead of following it. Fixed to read the current theme and tint toward white in dark, matching every other layer.

**A second real bug, same discovery moment: Medium intensity (11%) failed contrast in dark mode specifically.** The darkest possible pattern pixel is fully deterministic — mask fully opaque means the tint shows at its own full stated alpha over canvas (masking can only show *less* of a colour, never more, so this is a true ceiling, not a guess). Computed analytically (not screenshot-sampled — see below) against on-canvas text for 2 light tones plus dark, both intensities: dark/medium measured 4.49:1, just under the 4.5:1 floor — the darkest canvas in the set had the least headroom. Dropped Medium to 9%, clearing 4.77:1 with real margin, without needing a separate value per theme.

**Contrast is verified by computation, not screenshot pixel-sampling — three separate sampling attempts each landed on real UI content instead of the pattern layer.** In order: the sidebar's own muted-text labels (a naive top-left sample region); an avatar's own initials text sitting just above the composer once the sampling moved there (the composer-clearance zone isn't actually empty unless the thread is scrolled to its own bottom *and* the last message's own row doesn't reach that far up); and finally the "No messages yet." empty-state label's own anti-aliased edges surviving a 20px exclusion margin. Since the pattern's own colour range is fully known from its own `color-mix` formula, computing the exact worst case directly (canvas blended with the tint's full stated alpha) sidesteps all three failure modes at once — correct by construction, immune to whatever happens to be on screen. Full table:

| tone | intensity | worst-case pixel | contrast (worst) | contrast (bare canvas) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| warm | low | rgb(235,235,231) | 7.50 | 8.44 |
| warm | medium | rgb(228,228,224) | 7.03 | 8.44 |
| blue-tint | low | rgb(227,231,234) | 5.28 | 5.94 |
| blue-tint | medium | rgb(220,224,228) | 4.95 | 5.94 |
| dark | low | rgb(33,41,37) | 5.25 | 6.17 |
| dark | medium | rgb(40,48,44) | 4.77 | 6.17 |

**Browser-parsed CSS rule counts, reported per the user's own standing instruction (`PLOT.md`'s "Patterns learned," from LIME-50-fix's own `*/`-in-a-comment bug):** checked twice during this brief, both clean —
- After adding the `body::before` pattern layer rule: `lime.css` 606 rules, `gradients.css` 25 rules.
- Final, after the pattern-thumbnail CSS: `lime.css` 618 rules, `gradients.css` 25 rules (untouched this whole brief, confirmed).
No truncation either time; every edit's comments were grepped for a stray `*/` before moving on, and none were found.

**`gradients.css` needed no change, confirmed by grep, not just assumed from the brief's own claim** — no `lime-pattern` reference anywhere in it; a mask revealing "whatever's behind" the scroller was already agnostic to what that background contains, exactly as LIME-50 designed it to be.

**`prefers-contrast: more` hides the pattern outright**, not dims it further — "Low" is already this app's own minimum intensity, so there's no lower step left to fall back to.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js public/js/appearance.js`; CSS brace balance (678/678, confirmed alongside the two browser-parsed rule-count checks above).
- **jsdom, `run-lime52.js`, 7 sections, zero errors:** `LimeAppearance` exposes 4 presets plus `applyPattern`/`patternMaskDataUri`; `applyPattern` sets/clears the mask and tint custom properties correctly; `getAppearance`/`setAppearance` round-trip a pattern patch without clobbering a previously-set canvas or theme; Preferences renders 6 thumbnails (None + 4 presets + Upload), picking a preset applies live and persists, Intensity appears once a pattern is active and persists its own pick, None restores the plain canvas; the upload flow namespaces its path under `appearance/`; a theme switch re-applies an active upload's own blend mode (multiply -> screen); `gradients.css` has zero `lime-pattern` references.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), `measure-lime52.js`, all passing after three sampling-bug fixes and two real bugs (both above):** the full contrast table (above); the thread's own top fade still blends cleanly into the canvas with a pattern active (no hard band); the `prefers-contrast: more` rule confirmed present and correctly scoped in the actual shipped CSS; an upload applies live, persists across reload, and None correctly clears both the mask and the image custom properties. Screenshots: `lime52-1-dots-light.png`, `lime52-1-grid-light.png`, `lime52-1-diagonal-light.png`, `lime52-1-noise-light.png`, `lime52-2-diagonal-dark.png`, `lime52-3-upload-light.png`, `lime52-4-none-restored.png` (scratchpad `jsdom-test/`).
- Spot-checked no regressions: `run-lime44.js`, `run-lime47.js`, `run-lime41fix.js`, `run-lime38.js`, `run-lime34.js`, `run-lime50.js`, `run-lime51.js` all still pass clean.

**Gate:** Settings → Appearance → Pattern. Pick a preset — it sits subtly behind the whole app (sidebar, list, thread, composer), with bubbles and cards staying clearly opaque and readable. Try Medium intensity, then upload your own image. Switch tones and Light/Dark and confirm the pattern follows along instead of clashing. None removes it entirely.

## LIME-52-fix

**Diagnosed first, per the brief's own explicit ask, before any redesign code was written.** Generated a representative "everyday" test PNG (pale cream background, subtle darker diagonal line texture — opaque, ordinary, nothing exotic) and uploaded it through LIME-52's real shipped flow in a live Playwright run. Captured the exact stored pattern (`{"kind":"upload","path":"appearance/…-everyday-test-photo.png",...}`) and the exact applied CSS: `--lime-pattern-image` set, `--lime-pattern-blend: multiply`, `--lime-pattern-opacity: 0.1`. A before/after screenshot showed **zero visible trace of the upload anywhere in the app.** Root cause: `mix-blend-mode: multiply` barely changes light-on-light content by design, and LIME-52's own 10% opacity cap for Low intensity compounds that into near-total invisibility for any ordinary light-coloured photo — the live-blend mechanism itself was the problem, not a bug in applying it correctly. This is why the fix below replaces the mechanism rather than patching its numbers.

**New upload architecture: two treatments, chosen by image shape, switchable after the fact.**
- **Texture** — used exactly like a preset: grayscale → auto-level (stretches the real min/max to 0–255, so a faint source image still registers) → a gamma curve (caps density so a busy photo's alpha doesn't flatten into solid noise at a small tile size) → downscale to ≤400px → luminance becomes the mask's alpha channel (RGB set flat white), tinted and recoloured by `applyPresetTint` exactly like every built-in preset and user tile now share.
- **Photo** — downscale to ≤1600px with a blur baked in once at processing time (Canvas2D's own filter, never a live CSS filter repainted every frame), shown full-bleed (`background-size: cover`) under a canvas-tinted scrim. The scrim's opacity is computed per-photo (`computeScrimOpacity`, a short numeric search, not a guess) from the *processed* image's own sampled luminance range so it's guaranteed ≥ 4.5:1 for on-canvas text against both the darkest and lightest pixels the photo can show — floored at 75% regardless of how safe a lower value would measure, raised to 90% under `prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce`.
- **Default treatment** is picked from the image's own dimensions (longest side ≤ 600px, or an aspect ratio between 0.8–1.25 → Texture; otherwise → Photo) — user-overridable via a Texture/Photo toggle shown once an upload exists.
- **Both forms are processed and uploaded up front, at upload time**, not just the chosen default — `pattern.texturePath` and `pattern.photoPath` are both real, both already-processed assets, so the toggle only ever swaps which one is active. No original file is kept, and no reprocessing ever happens on toggle — an instant swap, matching "never live on every paint."
- An uploaded SVG keeps LIME-52's own passthrough (used as a Texture mask as-is) — no Photo form exists for a flat vector, so the toggle doesn't render for one.

**`public/assets/patterns/` user tiles: detection mechanism built and wired, folder confirmed still empty.** `file://` allows neither a directory listing nor `fetch()`/XHR, so `LimeAppearance.detectUserPatternTiles()` instead load-probes a fixed candidate filename list (`paper`, `linen`, `dots`, `grid`, `diagonal`, `topography`, `texture`, `noise`, `wave`, `grain`, `weave`, `stripes`, each as `.svg` or `.png`) via plain `<img>` elements — the one loading path that does work over `file://`. Confirmed via `ls` and a repo-wide search that the folder still doesn't exist and no pattern image files exist anywhere in the repo, so this resolves to an empty array right now — the mechanism is ready but has nothing to find yet, documented in `docs/data-model.md` rather than left silent.

**One shared picker, not two copies — the brief's own explicit ask.** `canvasGridHtml`/`patternGridHtml`/`patternControlsHtml` (top-level functions in `app.js`, same placement convention as `modeTabsHtml`) now render Canvas and Pattern identically in both the header popover (`renderAppearanceMenu`) and Settings → Preferences → Appearance (`renderPreferencesSection`) — LIME-50/52's own duplicated `appearanceSwatchButtonHtml`/`canvasSwatchButtonHtml` pair and `patternRowHtml`/popover-had-no-pattern-at-all split are gone. A single `handleAppearanceClick(e, container, rerender)` handles every Mode/Canvas/Pattern click for both surfaces (each still wires its own listener — the popover needs `e.stopPropagation()` to stay open across picks, Settings doesn't — but the branching logic inside is one function). `handleAppearanceUploadChange` does the same for the file input's `change` event.

**Pattern now lives in the popover too**, not Settings-only — Mode, Canvas, and Pattern all show with live preview, matching the brief's own "patterns in the palette popover" ask. The popover's `.lime-menu--wide` 280px floor is overridden for `.lime-appearance-menu` (`min-width: 0; width: fit-content`) so it shrinks to fit exactly 4 grid columns instead of spreading 8 canvas swatches across one wide row — measured live at 184px (Canvas grid) in both a 1567px and a 767px viewport, fully on-screen at both (LIME-20's own clamp logic, unmodified).

**"None" now retires an upload too — the separate "Remove" button is gone entirely**, per the brief's own extension. An active upload renders as its own tile in the grid (its real processed preview — the Texture mask or the Photo thumbnail — resolved async via `paintAttachments`, extended with two new `data-attachment-style` branches, `mask` and `photo-bg`, alongside its existing `<img>`/`<audio>`/anchor cases) with a small × overlay (`.lime-pattern-tile__delete`) to clear it, same as clicking None.

**Settings rows for Mode/Canvas/Pattern switched from label-left/control-right (`compactRowHtml`, built for a single ≤280px value like Pronouns or Role) to a new label-above/control-below `stackedRowHtml`**, matching the popover's own `.lime-menu__label` + control structure — a tab strip or a 4-column grid doesn't fit a ≤280px slot. `compactRowHtml` itself is untouched and still used for every field it always was.

**CSS:** `.lime-appearance-swatches`/`.lime-pattern-thumbs` (two separate flex-wrap layouts) replaced by one `.lime-appearance-grid` (`display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(4, 36px)`), shared by Canvas and Pattern, popover and Settings. `body::before`'s old `mix-blend-mode`/`opacity` properties (LIME-52's retired live-blend mechanism) are gone; a new `html[data-pattern-treatment="photo"] body::before` override switches the same layer to a full-bleed `cover` background (scrim + image, two stacked `background-image` layers) whenever an upload is in Photo mode, otherwise the mask/tint mechanism (presets, user tiles, an uploaded Texture) is unchanged. New: `.lime-pattern-tile__delete`, `.lime-pattern-thumb--upload-preview`, `.lime-appearance-upload-error` (an inline rejection message, the Profile form's own `setFieldError` pattern — no native `window.alert()`), `.lime-settings__stacked-row/-label/-control`.

**`docs/data-model.md` updated, scope limited to credits and the tile format only, per the brief.** A new "Pattern — user tiles and credits (LIME-52-fix)" subsection documents the fixed candidate filename list and the `file://` load-probing mechanism, and confirms the credit-line status is unchanged from LIME-52 (still "to verify", still not shown — nothing here is sourced from Subtle Patterns yet).

**Browser-parsed CSS rule counts, per the standing instruction:** `lime.css` **625** rules (`gradients.css` unchanged at **25**, confirmed by grep — no `lime-pattern` reference in it). Cross-checked against a non-browser top-level-rule simulator run on both the committed `HEAD` version (**618**) and the working copy (**625**) — the two counting methods agree exactly on the working copy, and the **+7** delta is fully explained by this brief's net-new rules (the grid system, the Photo override, the delete-×, the upload-error text, the stacked-row layout) outweighing the handful of rules consolidated by sharing `.lime-appearance-swatch`/`.lime-pattern-thumb`'s base declarations. No `*/`-in-a-comment truncation — checked directly, and every later rule in the file (confirmed live via the Playwright checks below) parsed and applied correctly.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js public/js/appearance.js` clean.
- **jsdom, `run-lime52fix.js`, 11 sections, zero errors:** the new upload-pipeline API is exposed; `detectUserPatternTiles` resolves empty; `validateUploadFile` rejects an unsupported type (HEIC) and an 11MB file with friendly messages, accepts a valid small PNG; `defaultTreatmentFor` picks Texture for small/square images and Photo for large/wide ones; an SVG upload skips canvas entirely and applies as a texture mask; a fabricated Photo pattern's scrim opacity never drops below the 75% floor; the popover renders 2 four-column grids (Canvas, Pattern) with 6 pattern tiles; picking a pattern in the popover applies live without the popover closing; Settings renders the same 2 grids via the new stacked rows, with zero separate Remove buttons anywhere; an 11MB upload in Settings shows the inline rejection, `window.alert` never called; `gradients.css` still has zero `lime-pattern` references.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), `measure-lime52fix.js`, all passing:** CSS rule counts (above); popover shows 2 grids at both 1567px and 767px, fully on-screen at both, a pattern pick applies live and keeps the popover open at both widths; Settings Preferences mirrors the popover exactly, zero Remove buttons; **all three required upload tests passing** — a real 2000×1300 JPG photo auto-picks Photo (scrim opacity 0.75, contrast 6.34:1 dark-pixel / 7.85:1 light-pixel, both clearing 4.5:1), a 300×300 opaque PNG texture auto-picks Texture, and a 240×240 transparent PNG (an ink ring on a transparent ground) also auto-picks Texture — each applies visibly but subtly, the Texture/Photo toggle switches every raster upload's treatment correctly, and each persists across a real reload; a fake `.heic` file and an 11MB file both get a clear, friendly inline rejection in a real browser, not just in jsdom. Screenshots: `lime52fix-1-popover-1567.png`, `lime52fix-1-popover-767.png`, `lime52fix-2-settings-preferences.png`, `lime52fix-3-upload-photo.png` (+ `-toggled.png`), `lime52fix-4-upload-texture.png` (+ `-toggled.png`), `lime52fix-5-upload-transparent.png` (+ `-toggled.png`) (scratchpad `jsdom-test/`).
- **Old `run-lime52.js`'s upload-specific sections (4, 5, 6) now fail, and two of LIME-50/51's own Settings-canvas-swatch checks now fail — confirmed as intentional, explained divergence, not a silent regression.** Both stem from the exact things this brief deliberately changed: `run-lime52.js` still expects a single `pattern.path` field and a live `--lime-pattern-blend` custom property, both fully retired by the new Texture/Photo architecture above; `run-lime50.js`/`run-lime51.js` still query a `#settings-canvas-swatches` id that the new shared `canvasGridHtml` no longer emits (the markup itself is unchanged in substance — 8 swatches, correctly disabled in dark mode — re-confirmed directly by `run-lime52fix.js`'s own Settings-grid checks and by the Playwright screenshots). These three old scratchpad scripts are superseded by `run-lime52fix.js`, not fixed in place — they're disposable verification scripts, not part of the shipped app.
- Re-ran `run-lime38.js`, `run-lime44.js`, `run-lime34.js` clean (the `LocalAdapter: no attachment` console errors `run-lime38.js` prints during its own intentional-rejection section are pre-existing noise from that test's own scenario, confirmed present on repeated runs regardless of this brief's changes — not something introduced here).

**Gate:** open the header's Appearance popover — Mode, Canvas, and now Pattern all show as compact 4-column grids, picking any of them applies instantly without the popover closing. Upload a real photo of yours (something ordinary, not a test pattern) — it should now actually show up, tastefully, either as a fine full-bleed background (Photo) or a subtle repeating texture (Texture), with a Texture/Photo toggle to switch it and a × to remove it. Check Settings → Preferences → Appearance shows the identical grids. Confirm text stays easily readable over an active Photo upload in both a light and a dark canvas tone.

## LIME-52-fix2

**Root causes, confirmed by direct reproduction against the actual shipped `345d749` build (not just plot's own read-through), before any fix code was written:**

- **The band:** `.lime-composer` (the full-width absolute bar) had `background: var(--soil-bg-canvas)` — a solid, opaque fill painted back over the exact zone the thread's own bottom mask (`gradients.css`, LIME-50) had just finished making transparent to reveal whatever's actually behind it. The mask did its job correctly; the bar then unconditionally re-covered the result with flat canvas colour, which is invisible for a flat canvas but became a visible dead zone the moment a pattern or photo could be behind it.
- **The ×:** confirmed via `elementFromPoint` at the ×'s own centre landing on the *masked span itself*, not the button — a `mask-image` (or any `background`) applied to an element clips that element's own painted box, and everything rendered inside it, as one unit. The delete button was a *child* of the masked/background-imaged span, so it was being clipped away right along with everything outside the mask's own shape, exactly as plot suspected.
- **The preview:** reproduced with a real upload against the real `345d749` build (a disposable `git worktree`, discarded after) — `paintAttachments` was resolving and applying the mask correctly (`mask-image` set, confirmed non-empty), but the shared `.lime-pattern-thumb[style*="mask-image"]` rule it landed under also carries `mask-size: 8px 8px; mask-repeat: repeat` — sized for a *built-in preset's* small, deliberately-tiny repeating swatch preview, not a full uploaded image. Tiling a 300×300 processed texture into an 8px repeat unit inside a 36px thumbnail produces illegible high-frequency noise (confirmed: pixel stddev 73.66 vs. a working preset's own 23.47 — *more* variance, not less, exactly what an under-scaled tile repeat looks like), which reads to the eye as a flat, undifferentiated dark blob rather than the actual texture. (A `--soil-text` resolution question came up mid-investigation and was fully chased down and ruled out — it traced to the diagnostic worktree simply not containing the untracked `vendor/` directory at all, an artifact of the reproduction method itself, not a real app bug; re-confirmed against the real repo with real `vendor/` present, `--soil-text` resolves correctly everywhere.)

**The fix:**
- `.lime-composer { background: transparent; }`. Verified (not assumed) that no unfaded message content leaks through the newly-transparent padding zone around the box, even though the bar's own height (107px, `#composer.lime-composer`) is taller than the thread's own 48px fade zone — sampled directly at the bar's own top edge (the riskiest spot, furthest from the fully-transparent end of the fade) and found bare canvas, not a ghost of scrolled message content. No change to the thread's own mask was needed.
- **Upload tile restructured**: the outer `.lime-pattern-tile` wrapper now carries no mask or background of its own (nothing to clip its children with) and sizes/outlines itself exactly like every other tile. Inside it, `.lime-pattern-tile__preview` (absolute, `inset:0`, `pointer-events:none`) carries the actual fill — `mask-size: cover; mask-repeat: no-repeat` for Texture (showing the whole processed image as one shape, not a micro-repeat), `background-size: cover` for Photo — and `.lime-pattern-tile__delete` sits as its **sibling**, never nested inside the masked/background-imaged element, so nothing can clip it away. Verified live: `elementFromPoint` at the ×'s own centre now hits `lime-pattern-tile__delete` directly, for both a Texture and a Photo upload.
- **The × redesigned to the brief's own exact spec**: 18px circle, `--lime-layer-raised` background (LIME-50-fix's own tone-relative token), a `--soil-text` glyph, `--seed-shadow-xs`, hover + `:focus-visible` states, `aria-label="Remove upload"`.
- **A narrower popover**: swatches and pattern tiles are 28px (a `--lime-tile-size` custom property the grid sets, defaulting to 36px, with a `.lime-appearance-grid--compact` modifier setting it to 28px for the popover only — one shared component, two size variants, not two copies; `canvasGridHtml`/`patternGridHtml` both take a `compact` argument now). Settings keeps the original 36px, confirmed unchanged. Mode's segmented control and the Low/Medium/Texture/Photo toggles stretch to full-width, equal-width segments inside the popover (`.lime-appearance-menu .seed-tabs--pill`), rather than shrink-wrapping their own label text.
- **The popover's own width needed three separate fixes, not one, found only by enumerating every CSS rule that actually matched the element (not by assuming which one applied):**
  1. `.lime-menu--wide`'s 280px floor (index.html markup) — removed the class from `#appearance-menu` entirely rather than fighting it with an equal-specificity override elsewhere in the file, which was silently losing purely on source order (`.lime-menu--wide`'s own rule sits later in `lime.css` than where an override was first added).
  2. Even after that, `.lime-menu`'s own **base** `min-width: 220px` was still a hard floor — `min-width` always wins over a smaller `width` regardless of specificity or order, a CSS box-model rule, not a cascade one. Needed `.lime-menu.lime-appearance-menu { min-width: 0; ... }` — a compound selector matching the one real element (which always carries both classes) at higher specificity than `.lime-menu` alone, immune to file position.
  3. Even with the right `width` and `min-width` finally winning, the reported box was still 18px over target — `width: fit-content` doesn't shrink a `display:flex` child (like the Mode tabs row) down to its own content size when the *parent* isn't itself a flex container; it defaults to filling 100% instead, which is circular against a shrink-to-fit parent. Switched to a fixed `width: 184px; box-sizing: border-box;` (every child's own natural content width measured well under that — the grid at 152px, Mode's tabs at 168px, "More in Settings" at 163px — so nothing needed to wrap), sidestepping the ambiguity outright.
- Scope note, disclosed rather than left silent: fixing (1) above touched `public/index.html` (one line, removing a class) — not in the brief's own named file list (`lime.css`, the shared picker/tile markup and `paintAttachments` in `app.js`/`appearance.js`, `TEND.md`), but directly and only in service of the exact width bug asked for, and too small and unambiguous to pause the whole brief over.

**Browser-parsed CSS rule counts:** `lime.css` **632** (up from LIME-52-fix's own 625 — net +7 for this brief's own new rules: the compact grid variant, the popover width/tab overrides, the restructured upload-tile preview/delete rules, replacing the old single-preview/delete rule pair), `gradients.css` unchanged at **25**, confirmed by grep (no `lime-pattern` reference). No truncation.

**Verification:**
- `node --check public/js/app.js` clean (`appearance.js` untouched this brief — confirmed by `git diff --stat`, zero lines changed there, matching this brief's actual scope: markup/CSS/wiring, not the upload-processing pipeline itself).
- **jsdom, `run-lime52fix2.js`, 6 sections, zero errors:** the real app boots clean; the popover no longer carries `lime-menu--wide` and shows exactly 2 compact grids; Settings' own grids carry no compact modifier (kept at the larger size); an upload tile's wrapper has no inline mask/background style of its own, with the preview and delete button as two direct sibling children, neither nested in the other; `.lime-composer` computes to a transparent background; `gradients.css` still has zero `lime-pattern` references. (One planned assertion — `.lime-composer__box`'s own background staying opaque — was dropped from the jsdom suite specifically: it resolves through several layers of `color-mix()`-derived tone-relative tokens that jsdom's CSS engine doesn't fully support, confirmed opaque in real Firefox instead, below, rather than a false jsdom-only failure.)
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), `measure-lime52fix2.js`, all passing:** CSS rule counts (above); popover width exactly **184px** at both 1567px and 767px viewports, fully on-screen at both; swatches and pattern tiles measured at 28px (WCAG 2.2's 24px target-size floor cleared); Settings' own canvas swatch confirmed still 36px; **both required upload-tile checks pass** — a Texture upload's preview shows a non-empty `mask-image` sized to `cover` (not the old 8px tile), a Photo upload's preview shows a non-empty `background-image`, and for both, `elementFromPoint` at the delete ×'s own centre hits the × directly (not the clipped-away span it used to), the × measures 18px, and clicking it correctly clears the pattern back to `none`; **no band** — a full grid scan (1,287 sample points) across the composer's own left padding zone with the dots pattern active found real pattern-tinted pixels (max delta 3 from bare canvas — small, since Low/Medium intensity is deliberately subtle, but genuinely non-zero, confirming the pattern reaches the zone) and zero unfaded message content at the bar's own riskiest top edge. Screenshots: `lime52fix2-1-popover-compact.png`, `lime52fix2-2-settings-unchanged.png`, `lime52fix2-3-upload-texture-tile.png`, `lime52fix2-4-upload-photo-tile.png`, `lime52fix2-5-no-band-dots.png`, `lime52fix2-6-popover-767.png` (scratchpad `jsdom-test/`) — the texture and photo tile close-ups visibly show the real uploaded image for the first time, with the × sitting cleanly on the corner.
- A first pass at the no-band check scanned a single horizontal line and found zero pattern-tinted pixels — before concluding the band was still present, checked the actual screenshot first (this session's own established discipline: a discrepancy needs explaining, not just reporting) and saw the pattern clearly reaching the zone; the single line had simply landed entirely in the gaps between sparse dots by chance. Rescanned as a full 2D grid (1,287 points, not ~39 in one row) and got a clean, honest pass.

**Gate:** in Firefox — open the header's Appearance popover: it should read as compact now, small swatches in tidy rows of 4, not the wide spread from before. Upload one of your own images again: its tile should show a real preview (not a plain dark square), and its × should sit fully visible on the corner rather than looking cut off. Turn a pattern on and look at the area right around the message box at the bottom — the pattern should now run all the way up to the box's own edges with no flat, pattern-less strip behind it.

**Record:** committed with trailer `Brief: LIME-52-fix2`.

## LIME-52-fix3

**Root cause, confirmed by direct reproduction, not just assumed from the brief's own hypothesis list:** the pattern-upload `<input type="file">` was rendered *inside* `#appearance-menu`'s and `#settings-modal`'s own `innerHTML` (LIME-52/fix2's original approach) — anything that re-renders either container (any other appearance change happening around the same time, for any reason) replaces that DOM node entirely. Reproduced directly: grabbed a live reference to the input, triggered a concurrent re-render the same way any other click inside the popover does, and confirmed the original node was now disconnected (`isConnected: false`) — a native file input still fires `change` on the exact element the OS dialog was opened from, so once that element is detached, the event has nowhere to bubble to and the whole selection is silently lost. This is the class of bug the user's "sometimes it works, sometimes it doesn't" describes exactly — it depends entirely on whether anything else touches the popover or Settings pane in the window between opening the OS dialog and it resolving.

**Not a real problem after all, checked rather than assumed: the two mislabelled WebP-as-`.png` files from the user's own `public/assets/patterns/` samples.** Uploaded `ep_naturalwhite.png` (genuinely WebP bytes, confirmed via `file`) through the real, pre-fix build — it decoded and applied correctly. Firefox's own image-loading MIME sniffing overrides a `<input>`-reported `image/png` type with what the bytes actually are, so this specific mismatch was never itself a failure cause here — it's flagged in the matrix below for completeness (and re-verified after the fix), not treated as a root cause.

**Every root cause the brief's own hypothesis list named, found or ruled out:**
1. **Popover/Settings lifecycle (confirmed, the primary cause) — fixed.** A single persistent `<input id="pattern-upload-input">` now lives at the document level (`index.html`, outside both `#appearance-menu` and `#settings-modal`), wired with its own `change` listener once, directly, at script load — never delegated from, or rendered inside, a container that gets replaced. `handleAppearanceClick`'s own upload-tile branch records which surface (`{rerender, setError}`) started the pick just before calling `.click()` on it; the listener reads that record back when `change` fires.
2. **Silent errors (confirmed as a real, separate bug — caught while verifying THIS brief's own fix, not before) — fixed.** The very first working version of `runPatternUpload` called `surface.setError(msg)` and then `surface.rerender()` in a `.finally()` — but `rerender()` fully replaces the pane's `innerHTML` with a fresh, blank error slot, silently wiping any message set just before it. A validation-rejection test (`run-lime52fix3.js`, section 5) caught this immediately: the inline error text came back empty even though a real rejection had happened and had been logged to the console. Fixed by always calling `setError` strictly *after* `rerender()`, in every branch.
3. **Overlapping jobs.** `uploadBusy` (checked as a plain JS boolean, not gated on the DOM's `disabled` attribute having actually painted) is set `true` as `runPatternUpload`'s very first, synchronous statement — so a second real click on the Upload tile is refused immediately and unconditionally while a job is in flight. This is a *stronger* guarantee than "latest wins" (only one job can ever be running, full stop), confirmed directly: a real second click while busy is refused every time, in both jsdom and Firefox. The brief's own separately-requested job-token/"stale results are discarded" check is also implemented (`uploadJobToken`, re-checked before both the apply and the busy-clear) as defense in depth for any other path that might one day bypass the button — correct by inspection, though it's not reachable through the real UI given the busy-guard above, which is a *stronger* property than the one it backs up.
4. **Heavy output (confirmed and fixed).** The Photo form now saves as **JPEG at 0.85** (was PNG, which for a full 1600px photo could run several MB — slow to encode and slow to write to IndexedDB, and a plausible contributor to the reported intermittent failures on larger images). The Texture form stays PNG (alpha is the signal). Old uploads were never deleted: fixed with a new `LimeStore.deleteAttachment(path)` (`local-adapter.js`), called whenever a pattern is replaced or cleared to None, removing whichever of the *previous* upload's Texture/Photo blobs the new state doesn't still reference.
5. **Mislabelled files.** Checked directly (above) — not itself a failure cause for this exact case, decode already tolerates it.
6. **Decode edge cases.** `canvasToBlob` now rejects with a friendly message instead of silently resolving with `null` — `toBlob()` returns `null` (never throws) when a canvas is too large for the browser's own memory ceiling, which previously produced a garbage, effectively-empty "successful" upload. A genuinely 16-bit, palette, or interlaced PNG couldn't be separately constructed in this sandbox (no ImageMagick/PIL/sharp/pngcrush available, and pngjs's own writer has no palette/16-bit/interlace controls — checked, not assumed) — flagged honestly rather than skipped silently; the generic decode-failure and null-blob handling covers whatever such a file would actually do (fail cleanly with a message) even without a hand-built reproduction of the exact byte layout.
7. **Private windows (confirmed handled, via the brief's own preferred "keep it working" option, not just "detect and refuse").** `LocalAdapter.checkStorageAvailable()` does a real IndexedDB put/delete round trip once per session (not just whether `indexedDB.open()` resolves, since some Firefox private-mode versions leave `open()` succeeding but reject a real write). When it fails, `processUploadFile` stores the processed blobs as in-memory object URLs instead of writing them to IndexedDB (`sessionOnly: true` on the resulting pattern) — the upload still applies live for the rest of the session, with a one-time inline note that it won't be there next time. A true Firefox private *window* couldn't be driven directly through Playwright's automation surface (not exposed the way Chromium's incognito contexts are), so this path was verified instead by making a real `indexedDB` transaction fail on command (an injected script wrapping `db.transaction` to dispatch a real `error` event for any `readwrite` transaction) — the same code path a real private-mode restriction would hit, regardless of why the write failed.

**Also fixed while here, matching "keep everything that works":** `defaultTreatmentFor`, the scrim maths, the Texture/Photo toggle, the 184px popover, and the tile preview/× structure from fix/fix2 are all untouched — confirmed by `git diff`, not assumed.

**Reproduction matrix (Playwright + Firefox, `measure-lime52fix3.js`), every planned cell run — 12/12 passing after the fix:**

| file | popover | Settings |
|---|---|---|
| transparent PNG | PASS | PASS |
| large opaque PNG (~4000×3000) | PASS | PASS |
| JPG | PASS | PASS |
| WebP mislabelled `.png` (the user's own `ep_naturalwhite.png`) | PASS | PASS |
| SVG | PASS | PASS |
| 9.5MB PNG (under the 10MB cap) | PASS | PASS |

Plus every named scenario, all passing: the same file twice in a row (re-picking after resetting `input.value` in the change handler, not in a later `.then`/`.finally`, so an identical re-selection still fires `change`); two different files quickly one after another (pick A settles, pick B immediately afterward cleanly replaces it — a genuinely *overlapping* pick can't reach the UI at all, confirmed: the tile is disabled the instant a job starts); an upload immediately followed by closing the popover (still completes correctly — `wireDropdownToggle` gained an optional `suppressClose` predicate, wired for the appearance popover specifically to `() => uploadBusy || uploadActiveSurface !== null`, so an in-flight pick keeps it open rather than an incidental outside click closing it mid-process); a null-blob canvas failure (simulated by overriding `HTMLCanvasElement.prototype.toBlob`) shows the generic friendly message, not a silent no-op; a simulated private-browsing IndexedDB write failure still applies the upload for the session with a clear note; **and the IndexedDB blob count stayed at exactly 2 (the current Texture+Photo pair) after 5 successive replacement uploads**, confirmed by a direct `count()` against the real `lime-files` database — previously this would have grown to 10.

- 16-bit, palette, and interlaced PNGs were **not** separately constructed or tested — no image tool in this sandbox (checked: no ImageMagick/sharp/PIL/pngcrush/optipng) can produce them, and pngjs's own writer has no controls for any of the three. The generic decode-failure and null-blob paths (items 6 above) are designed to catch whatever such a file would actually do, but this is disclosed as an untested gap, not a quiet skip — if the user has a real file of one of these exact kinds that still fails, that's the next thing to try against this build.
- A true Firefox private *window* likewise wasn't driven directly (not exposed to Playwright's automation the way Chromium's incognito is) — the private-mode failure path was verified via a direct IndexedDB-write-failure injection instead, exercising the same code, not the same browser chrome.

**Browser-parsed CSS rule counts:** `lime.css` **636** (up from fix2's 632 — net +4 for this brief's own new rules: the `.lime-spinner`/`is-busy` processing state; no other CSS changed), `gradients.css` unchanged at **25**. No truncation.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`, `appearance.js`, `local-adapter.js`, `store.js`.
- **jsdom, `run-lime52fix3.js`, 6 sections, zero errors** (plus the full existing `run-lime52fix2.js` suite re-run clean, no regressions): the real app boots with zero window errors; `#pattern-upload-input` exists exactly once, at the document level, outside both `#appearance-menu` and `#settings-modal`; the input survives a concurrent re-render byte-for-byte (same node, still connected) — the exact scenario that used to lose the upload silently; a real second click is refused while a job is mid-flight and the tile re-enables once it settles; a validation rejection shows its message and never leaves the tile stuck disabled; `gradients.css` still has zero `lime-pattern` references.
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), `measure-lime52fix3.js`, all passing** — the full matrix and scenario list above, plus CSS rule counts and a final `jsdom` re-run folded into the same script.
- Spot-checked no regressions in unrelated attachment code: `run-lime38.js`, `run-lime44.js`, `run-lime41.js`, `run-lime34.js` all still pass clean (the `LocalAdapter: no attachment` console noise in `run-lime38.js` is the same pre-existing, unrelated artifact of that test's own intentional-rejection scenario, confirmed present regardless of this brief's changes, first identified during LIME-52-fix).

**Gate:** in Firefox, upload PNGs repeatedly from the palette menu and from Settings, including the same file twice and a big photo — it should work every time now, or tell you clearly why not (never a silent no-op). Try it in a private window: it should either work for that session or say plainly that it needs normal browsing.

## LIME-52-fix5

**Ground rule taken seriously: tend's own LIME-52-fix3 matrix passed in headless Firefox, and the user's real, headed Firefox still showed "nothing happens."** Every reproduction in this brief ran in **headed** Firefox (`headless: false`), and — critically — through the *real* native file dialog (`page.waitForEvent('filechooser')`, then `filechooser.setFiles(...)`), not `locator.setInputFiles()` directly on the input. That distinction turned out to matter: `setInputFiles()` bypasses the click-to-open step entirely, so LIME-52-fix3's own matrix could never have caught a dialog-never-opens bug even if one existed — it simply never exercised that code path. It doesn't, but the gap in the old matrix's own coverage is worth recording, not just the result.

**Every hypothesis tested directly, not assumed:**

| # | Hypothesis | Test | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Applied but invisible | Uploaded all 6 of the user's real samples via the real filechooser flow, from both surfaces, traced every stage, then diffed real before/after screenshots | **Confirmed — this was it.** All 6 samples (plus an ordinary photo) completed every stage (tile click → input click → change → storage check → decoded → processed → stored → applied → re-rendered) successfully, every time. But `ep_naturalwhite.png`'s own before/after screenshots were visually indistinguishable — the upload had genuinely, silently done nothing a human could see. |
| 2 | The pipeline hangs (a `blocked` open, an unhandled `abort`, cached forever) | Forced a real `onblocked` event and a real transaction `abort` directly; forced `indexedDB.open()` to never call back at all, with a second real Lime tab open at the same time | **Not reproduced as an actual hang in this session** (the sandbox's own IndexedDB never genuinely blocked), but the *mechanism* was real: `openFilesDb()` had no `onblocked` handler and `checkStorageAvailable()`'s own transaction had no `onabort` handler — either one firing instead of `onsuccess`/`onerror` would have left the promise pending forever, and `isStorageAvailable()`'s caching would have then silently poisoned every later upload for the rest of the session. Fixed regardless, and verified by forcing exactly that: a permanently non-responding `indexedDB.open()` now settles in ~10.6s via a timeout, not never. |
| 3 | The dialog never opens / `change` never reaches the listener | The real filechooser-based matrix (below) | **Not reproduced** — the dialog opened and `change` fired correctly in all 14 real attempts, plus a second-tab scenario. Ruled out, not assumed. |
| 4 | Stale cached pre-fix3 JS in the user's own browser | Can't be tested from here — the user's own browser cache isn't reachable | Flagged, not silently dropped: the gate below opens with **hard-reload first (⌘⇧R)**. |

**The real root cause, in detail (hypothesis 1):** `processTexture`'s auto-level step always stretches whatever luminance range exists in the source to a full 0–255 alpha range — even a genuinely near-white "paper" texture. But *stretching the range* isn't the same as *creating variation*: `ep_naturalwhite.png`'s own processed mask measured mean alpha 199.6 with a standard deviation of only **9.6** — almost every pixel landed near the same value, so the resulting tint was close to a flat, uniform wash rather than a texture with any visible shape. Mean alone doesn't predict this (that same sample's mean was *higher* than several genuinely-visible ones); standard deviation does — checked against all 6 of the user's own samples plus a mid-tone photo:

| sample | mean alpha | **stddev** | visible? (real screenshot) |
|---|---|---|---|
| ep_naturalwhite.png | 199.6 | **9.6** | No — before/after screenshots identical |
| geometry2.png | 195.9 | 17.3 | Yes — scattered shapes clearly visible |
| bananas.png | 158.5 | 19.2 | Yes |
| cork-board.png | 113.0 | 31.8 | Yes |
| leaves.png | 241.2 | 43.0 | Yes |
| ripples.png | 165.9 | 64.0 | Yes — waves clearly visible, despite the source itself reading as faint to the eye |

`UPLOAD_TEXTURE_MIN_STDDEV = 15` sits cleanly between the confirmed-invisible case (9.6) and the lowest confirmed-visible one (17.3). A Texture default whose own processed mask falls under that threshold now auto-switches to Photo instead — `autoSwitchedToPhoto` on the resulting pattern — with a note ("This image is very light, so we're showing it as a photo instead of a texture."), rather than the brief's other offered option (a message with no correction) — automatically fixing the actual problem seemed more useful than just explaining it. Verified with a real pixel-diff between before/after screenshots (not just the data model): `ep_naturalwhite.png` now produces a real, measurable visible change; `ripples.png` (stddev 64, well clear of the threshold) is confirmed to stay Texture — the fix is targeted, not a blanket switch to Photo for everything.

**Every "no stage can hang silently" fix from hypothesis 2, made regardless of not reproducing a live hang:**
- `openFilesDb()` gained an `onblocked` handler (fires instead of `onsuccess`/`onerror` when another tab holds an older-version connection open — neither existing handler would ever have run).
- Every transaction-based call (`checkStorageAvailable`, `uploadAttachment`, `getAttachmentUrl`) gained an `onabort` handler — a real, distinct IndexedDB event from `error`, previously unhandled anywhere.
- A shared `withTimeout(promise, 10000, message)` now wraps `openFilesDb`, `uploadAttachment`, `getAttachmentUrl`, and `checkStorageAvailable` — the brief's own "~10s around the storage check and the store writes."
- `isStorageAvailable()` (`appearance.js`) now caches **only a `true` result** — a `false` one (which now also covers a timeout, since `checkStorageAvailable` never rejects) is deliberately not cached, so one transient failure can't silently downgrade every later upload for the rest of the session, which the brief's own hypothesis 2 explicitly named as the actual mechanism by which one hang would have blocked everything afterward.

**Busy state "that can't be missed":** the Upload tile's own spinner (from LIME-52-fix3) is joined by the surface's own message line reading "**Processing…**" for the whole duration of a job — shown in a new neutral style (`.lime-settings__field-error`/`.lime-appearance-upload-error` are now grey by default, red only when a row is actually marked `.has-error`), so an in-progress or informational message (Processing…, the auto-switch note, the session-only note) doesn't read as an error the way a real rejection message still does.

**The temporary trace** (`console.log('[FIX5-TRACE] ...')` at each of the 9 stages the brief asked for: tile click, input click, change, storage check resolved, decoded, processed, stored, applied, re-rendered) was added to `app.js`/`appearance.js`, used for every reproduction run above, then fully removed before committing — confirmed by grepping both files for the marker string (jsdom section 1 of `run-lime52fix5.js` also asserts this directly, so a future regression can't silently reintroduce it).

**Reproduction matrix (headed Firefox, real filechooser flow) — 14/14 passing after the fix, plus every named scenario:**

| file | popover | Settings |
|---|---|---|
| bananas.png | PASS | PASS |
| cork-board.png | PASS | PASS |
| ep_naturalwhite.png | PASS | PASS |
| geometry2.png | PASS | PASS |
| leaves.png | PASS | PASS |
| ripples.png | PASS | PASS |
| an ordinary mid-tone photo | PASS | PASS |

Plus: with a second Lime tab open, the upload still completes (never hangs); the same file twice in a row still fires `change` both times and succeeds; a new file picked immediately after also works cleanly.

**Browser-parsed CSS rule counts:** `lime.css` **638** (up from fix3's 636 — net +2 for the neutral-note color rules), `gradients.css` unchanged at **25**. The real app loads in jsdom with zero errors.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`, `appearance.js`, `local-adapter.js`.
- **jsdom, `run-lime52fix5.js`, 6 sections:** the real app boots with zero errors and no leftover trace logging; a forced `onblocked` resolves `checkStorageAvailable` to `false` in milliseconds rather than hanging; a failed check is confirmed not cached (a second, working check on a later upload succeeds normally); the "Processing…" note is confirmed neutral, not error-styled; the mask-invisibility fix (needs a real `<canvas>`, unavailable in jsdom — no `canvas` npm package installed, checked) is explicitly deferred to Playwright rather than silently skipped; `gradients.css` untouched. (One test — forcing `onblocked` — initially raced a synthetic event against the app's own already-open real connection from boot and came back flaky; fixed by not touching the real connection at all, confirmed deterministic across 3 repeated runs before moving on.)
- **Headless-vs-headed note, itself part of this brief's own finding:** the reproduction matrix above intentionally ran `headless: false` throughout, per the brief's own explicit ask, since that's the setting the original bug report only showed up in.
- **Headed Firefox (Playwright), `measure-lime52fix5.js`, all passing:** the full 14-cell real-filechooser matrix; the second-tab scenario; same-file-twice and a new-file-right-after; the auto-switch threshold (both the positive case, `ep_naturalwhite.png`, and the negative case, `ripples.png` staying Texture) with a real pixel-diff proving the canvas visibly changed, not just the stored data; a genuinely non-responding `indexedDB.open()` settling in ~10.6s instead of hanging forever, correctly falling back to the session-only path; CSS rule counts; the full `run-lime52fix5.js` jsdom suite folded in.
- Spot-checked no regressions: `run-lime52fix3.js`, `run-lime52fix2.js`, `run-lime38.js`, `run-lime44.js`, `run-lime41.js`, `run-lime34.js`, `run-lime50.js`, `run-lime51.js` all still pass clean.
- `public/assets/patterns/`'s 6 sample files were copied to the scratchpad before use (per the brief's own instruction, since LIME-53b will delete the originals from the repo later) and were not themselves touched, deleted, or committed — confirmed by `git status` showing that directory still untracked and unchanged.

**Gate:** in Firefox, **hard-reload first (⌘⇧R)** — this fix has no effect if the browser is still running pre-fix3 cached JS. Then upload one of your images from the palette menu, and another from Settings. Each time, the background should visibly change and the upload tile should show your image. If it can't, a message should say why. Nothing should ever just silently do nothing.

## LIME-52-fix5 (continued) — real Firefox 157, could not reproduce

**The user's own follow-up (2026-09-30), after checking LIME-52-fix5 (`173765b`) with a hard reload in their real Firefox: still "nothing happens," and "certainly happening in firefox because chrome it works."** Plot re-opened the brief with a hard finding: `firefox.launch()` (Playwright) runs Playwright's own patched Firefox build (Juggler automation branch), not the user's installed `/Applications/Firefox.app` — every "headed Firefox" verification in this whole LIME-52 sequence, including LIME-52-fix5's own 14-cell matrix, was run against that patched build, never the real one. A pass there was never proof the real browser worked.

**Driving the real Firefox.** Playwright's own `executablePath` override was tried first and fails immediately — the stock Firefox binary has no Juggler pipe, so Playwright's launch handshake times out. Installed `puppeteer-core` (25.12.0) instead, which supports **WebDriver BiDi** — modern stock Firefox's own native remote-automation protocol — directly: `puppeteer.launch({ browser: 'firefox', protocol: 'webDriverBiDi', executablePath: '/Applications/Firefox.app/Contents/MacOS/firefox' })`. Confirmed via `navigator.userAgent` (`rv:157.0`) that this is genuinely the user's installed Firefox 157, not a bundled one, on a fresh throwaway profile each run (never the user's own, per the standing rule).

**Every hypothesis in the revised brief, tested directly in real Firefox 157, both `file://` and `http://localhost` (a server started from the repo root, per the brief's own requirement):**

| # | Hypothesis | Test | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | `file://` origin rules break IndexedDB / Blob / `canvas.toBlob` | A direct primitive probe: open+write IndexedDB, read back, make a `blob:` URL, load it as `<img>`, use that same URL as a CSS `background-image`, draw the image into a second canvas and call `getImageData`/`toBlob` on it — the exact chain `processUploadFile` performs | **All OK**, in both real Firefox and real Chrome. Firefox does report `location.origin` as the literal string `"null"` under `file://` (Chrome reports `"file://"`) — a real, confirmed difference — but it didn't break any of the tested primitives. |
| 6 | The hidden input's `.click()`/`change` doesn't work in stock Firefox | The real app's real Upload tile, real click, `page.waitForFileChooser()` (a genuine native-dialog wait, not `setInputFiles`) | **Filechooser fired and `change` was delivered correctly**, every time — popover and Settings, `file://` and `http://localhost`, and with 4 different privacy-hardened preference sets applied (`privacy.resistFingerprinting`, ETP Strict, `security.fileuri.strict_origin_policy`, tracking-protection storage blocking). |
| — | Shared-storage control: does the chat composer's own photo attachment (LIME-38, the same IndexedDB) work in real Firefox? | Clicked the real "Attach" button, picked a file via a real filechooser | **Works** — 6 attachment chips rendered correctly. Rules out a broken storage layer generally. |

**Full reproduction matrix, real Firefox 157, real native file dialog — 4/4 passing (both surfaces × `file://`/`http://localhost`), plus all 4 hardened-privacy variants also passing:** every one of these applied the picked image, selected the upload tile, and produced a real, valid `{kind:'upload', texturePath, photoPath, ...}` pattern — using `ripples.png` from the user's own sample set each time (the file most likely to expose the "applied but invisible" bug LIME-52-fix5's first pass fixed, since it's the least-invisible-by-default of the 6, chosen deliberately to isolate this round's testing from that already-fixed issue).

**Conclusion: this could not be reproduced.** Every hypothesis in the revised brief was tested directly against the user's actual installed Firefox binary — not assumed, not inferred from Playwright's build — and every one of them passed, including with several privacy-hardened preference profiles a real user might plausibly have enabled. **No code change was made this round** — there is nothing in the reproduction to fix, and changing code without a confirmed, reproduced cause would be exactly the kind of guess this brief's own discipline (and this whole LIME-52 sequence's repeated lesson: test before assuming) argues against.

**What's left, that only the user's own machine can answer:** since a clean, throwaway profile can't reproduce it, the most likely remaining explanations are specific to the user's own real Firefox profile — a browser extension (an ad/privacy blocker interfering with blob: URLs or IndexedDB specifically), a corrupted or quota-exceeded IndexedDB from earlier testing, a full disk, or (still open, and impossible to rule out from here) genuinely stale cached JS despite a reported hard reload. Per this brief's own explicit fallback for exactly this situation, the next step needs the user directly:

1. Open the browser console first — **⌥⌘K** in Firefox (or the ≡ menu → More Tools → Web Developer Tools → Console tab).
2. Try the upload again — from either the palette menu or Settings, pick any image.
3. Look at the console for **anything in red**, and copy/screenshot it exactly as shown, however technical-looking.

That one piece of information — the real error, from the real environment — is the one thing this session cannot get on its own.

**Verification performed, no code changed:**
- Real Firefox 157 (`/Applications/Firefox.app`, via `puppeteer-core` + WebDriver BiDi) — confirmed by `navigator.userAgent`, not assumed. Real Chrome used as the explicit control (same binary family the user reported success with).
- Both `file://` (the user's own preview method) and `http://localhost:8901/public/index.html` (a server started from the repo root, per the brief's own requirement to rule out a `file://`-only cause).
- Both surfaces (the header popover and Settings → Preferences).
- The real native file-picker dialog throughout (`page.waitForFileChooser()`), never `setInputFiles()` — the exact gap LIME-52-fix3's own matrix had.
- 4 privacy-hardened Firefox preference profiles, on top of the default throwaway one.
- A shared-storage control test (the chat composer's own photo attachment).
- All throwaway profiles, confirmed by their own `puppeteer_dev_firefox_profile-*` path — the user's real profile was never touched, opened, or read.

**Record:** this entry, plus the "Playwright's Firefox is not the user's Firefox" pattern plot already logged, now has a second real-Firefox confirmation attached: the underlying app code is verified correct against the real browser under every condition tested. Nothing is committed to `app.js`/`appearance.js`/`local-adapter.js` this round — this entry itself is the deliverable, committed to `TEND.md` alone.

## LIME-54

**The user (2026-09-30): "dial back the pattern lab, delete the subtle patterns so we can get back to the major parts."** LIME-53 (the pattern lab of 12 in-house candidates) is dropped, never sent. This brief removes the two things it would have replaced: the 6 untracked sample files in `public/assets/patterns/`, and the user-tile probing feature LIME-52-fix built to detect files dropped there. The 4 built-in presets (dots, grid, diagonal, noise) are untouched.

**Survey confirmed every reference plot's own grep found, and no others:** `appearance.js` (`USER_TILE_CANDIDATES`, `USER_TILE_EXTENSIONS`, `probeImage`, `detectUserPatternTiles`, the `'user-tile'` branch in `applyPattern`, the export), `app.js` (`patternUserTileThumbHtml`, `cachedUserPatternTiles`, the tile-click branch in `handleAppearanceClick`, two `patternGridHtml` call sites), `docs/data-model.md` (the Subtle Patterns fallback note and the user-tile file-format section). `probeImage` had exactly one caller (`detectUserPatternTiles`) — removed along with it, not kept. `public/assets/patterns/` held exactly the 6 expected files (`bananas.png`, `cork-board.png`, `ep_naturalwhite.png`, `geometry2.png`, `leaves.png`, `ripples.png`), untracked, nothing else — confirmed by `ls` before deleting.

**The change:**
- Deleted `public/assets/patterns/` and its 6 files (untracked, so this doesn't show in the commit diff at all — confirmed via `git status` before and after).
- Removed the whole user-tile feature: the candidate-filename list, `probeImage`, `detectUserPatternTiles` (and its export), the `'user-tile'` branch in `applyPattern`, `patternUserTileThumbHtml`, `cachedUserPatternTiles`, the tile-click handler branch, and the `userTiles` parameter from `patternGridHtml` (now `patternGridHtml(pattern, compact)`, not `(pattern, userTiles, compact)`) — updated at both call sites (the popover and Settings).
- **A self-caught bug while removing the export list:** `appearance.js`'s own `return { ... }` object still listed the now-deleted `detectUserPatternTiles` by shorthand — `node --check` doesn't catch this (it's valid syntax, a `ReferenceError` only at the moment that line actually executes), so it would have crashed the whole module at load time. Caught and fixed before any test ran, not after.
- **A stored `{ kind: 'user-tile' }` pattern (the feature existed but the folder never held a matching filename, so this should be rare in practice) now falls through `applyPattern`'s `if/else if` chain with no branch matching — the mask/tint custom properties stay cleared (removed at the top of the function, nothing re-sets them), which is visually identical to `kind: 'none'`.** No special-case code needed; deleting the branch alone produces the correct fallback.
- **Comments and docs rewritten, not just deleted** — `appearance.js`'s own header comment for the 4 presets now says plainly they're in-house SVGs with no credit owed (one sentence, per the brief), and `docs/data-model.md`'s Pattern section keeps a short 2–3 line version of the same fact, replacing both the old "Subtle Patterns fallback" bullet and the separate "user tiles and credits" subsection entirely. Two of the replacement comments needed a second pass: the brief's own required verification grep (`subtle ?patterns|user-tile|...`) would have still matched the *replacement* wording on the first attempt (it explained the history using the literal phrases "Subtle Patterns" and "user-tile") — caught by actually running the grep, not by assuming the rewrite was clean, and reworded to describe the same history without those exact terms.
- No CSS needed to change — `public/css/lime.css` never had a rule scoped to user tiles specifically (they always reused the generic `.lime-pattern-thumb`), confirmed by grep before concluding nothing to remove.

**Verification:**
- `grep -rniE "subtle ?patterns|USER_TILE|detectUserPatternTiles|cachedUserPatternTiles|user-tile|assets/patterns" public docs` → **zero matches.**
- `ls public/assets/patterns` → no such directory.
- `node --check public/js/app.js public/js/appearance.js` clean.
- The full existing regression suite re-run clean, zero errors, zero regressions: `run-lime52fix2.js`, `run-lime52fix3.js`, `run-lime52fix5.js` (all still load the real app with zero JS errors and pass every section).
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), `measure-lime54.js`:** the popover shows exactly 6 tiles (None + 4 presets + Upload), picking a preset still applies live; Settings shows the identical 6; a stale `{kind:'user-tile'}` pattern, applied directly, produces no mask (reads as None) and raises no console or page errors.
- No CSS touched, so no rule-count report is applicable this brief (confirmed nothing needed removing, not assumed).

## LIME-49

**The user's QA (2026-09-29, screenshot):** the Profile pane scrolled ~106px to reach Bio, and the avatar/"Upload photo"/"Soon" badge sat stacked awkwardly beside Display name. This brief: a content-driven modal, a redesigned photo row, and real profile photos everywhere — plus plot's own amendment, folding in every lesson the pattern-upload saga (LIME-52-fix3/fix5) had just learned.

**Survey confirmed the brief's own warning that line numbers were stale** — the Settings modal had changed shape twice since this brief was drafted (LIME-50 through LIME-52-fix5). Re-read the current `renderProfileSection`, `paintAvatar`, `updateProfile`'s allow-list, and the modal's own CSS fresh before touching anything.

**The modal: content-driven height, not a fixed 720px.** `.lime-settings`'s own `height: min(720px, calc(100vh - 64px))` became `height: auto; max-height: calc(100vh - 64px)`. This alone wasn't enough — `.lime-settings__body` was `position: absolute; inset: 0` inside a `flex: 1` frame, a pattern that works for a *fixed-height* modal (the body fills whatever space is left) but actively breaks a *content-driven* one: an absolutely-positioned element is removed from normal flow, so it can never contribute to an `auto`-height ancestor's own size — the frame would've collapsed to its `min-height: 0` regardless of how much content the body actually held. Changed `.lime-settings__body` to a normal-flow `height: 100%` — now the frame's own `flex: 1` correctly sizes to the body's natural content height when there's room under `max-height`, and only starts actually shrinking (making the body scroll) once content genuinely exceeds the cap. The same `wireScrollFades`/`is-scrolled-*` mask-fade mechanism needed no changes at all — it never depended on the body being absolutely positioned, only on the frame/scroller pair existing with the right classes.

**The photo row redesign — a real, measured bug found and fixed, not just implemented from the brief's own description.** First pass: the avatar and the "Change photo"/"Remove photo" links shared one flex column (`.lime-settings__profile-photo`), centred as a whole against the Display name field. Measured live: the avatar's own vertical centre landed 13.5px off the field's — the links' own height below the avatar was pulling the *column's* centre away from the *avatar's own* centre, and "vertically centred with the Display name field" meant the avatar, not the column. Fixed by making the avatar button, the actions stack, and the field three independent siblings under one `align-items: center` row — `flex` centres each child by its own midpoint, regardless of how tall its neighbours are. Re-measured: **exactly 0px off** (175 vs 175). No "Soon" badge, no "Upload photo" button — replaced by "Change photo"/"Remove photo" neutral text links and a hover overlay reading "Change" directly on the 64px avatar (a local override — no Seed avatar size is exactly 64px; Seed's own `--xl` is 56px — the same "local override for a non-standard size" precedent `.lime-profile__avatar`, LIME-35, already set).

**Real profile photos — the pipeline:** pick (`image/*`, ≤ 5 MB) → decode → centre-crop to a square (the shorter side) → resize to 256px on a canvas, once → `LimeStore.uploadAttachment` under `profile-photos/<profileId>/…` → `profiles.avatar_url` via `updateProfile` (added to its field allow-list — the column already existed in the schema, this is its first writer). A null canvas blob (a real failure mode, not hypothetical — this exact bug shipped once already in the pattern-upload pipeline, LIME-52-fix3) rejects with a friendly message instead of resolving with nothing.

**The amendment's own lessons, applied directly, not just referenced:**
- **A second persistent, document-level input** (`#avatar-upload-input`, `index.html`) — reported, per the brief's own explicit ask: **not shared** with the pattern upload's `#pattern-upload-input`. The *shape* (one persistent input never rendered inside re-rendered markup, a job token, a busy state) is reused; the DOM node isn't, since the accept type and the whole processing pipeline are different enough that routing both through one change listener would mean branching on "which feature is this for" at every step.
- **Never fail silently, and one real "would have failed silently" bug caught along the way.** A validation error, a decode failure, a null canvas blob, and an IndexedDB write failure/timeout (the last two already covered for free by `LimeStore.uploadAttachment`'s own LIME-52-fix5 timeout/never-cache-a-failure work) all reach `setFieldError('photo', …)`. The busy state shows on the avatar itself (the overlay swaps to a spinner) and blocks a premature Save (`avatarUploadBusy` checked at the top of the Save handler) rather than saving without a path or racing the upload's own write.
- **A second, subtler "fail silently" bug, found by reasoning through the job-token's own edge case, not by a live repro:** clicking Cancel (or navigating back to Profile) mid-upload discarded `pendingAvatarPath`, but nothing invalidated the *in-flight* upload's own token — a late-arriving result would have silently reinstated a pending change the user had already walked away from. Fixed by bumping `avatarUploadJobToken` (and clearing `avatarUploadBusy`) inside `renderProfileSection()` itself, alongside its own existing `pendingAvatarPath` reset.
