- **Verified in the real installed Firefox and Chrome, not just Playwright's bundled Firefox** — confirmed necessary again: Playwright's own `executablePath` override, pointed at `/Applications/Firefox.app`, fails to even launch (no Juggler automation pipe in a stock build — the exact thing LIME-52-fix5 already found). Used `puppeteer-core`'s WebDriver BiDi support instead (already proven working in that same brief), confirmed via `navigator.userAgent` that both runs used the real binaries (`Firefox/157.0`, `Chrome/154.0.0.0`), not bundled ones.
- **The parked pattern-upload bug does not recur here** — the full upload → save → propagate → persist → remove cycle passed cleanly in both real browsers. Noted per the brief's own instruction, since the alternative (it failing the same way) would have meant leaving it parked rather than trying to fix it inside this brief.

**Avatars everywhere — one shared helper, not a dozen separate patches.** Every avatar-markup call site in `app.js` (message senders, reply quotes, the recent list, the topbar cluster, group avatar clusters, the profile detail panel, the members list, the sidebar) used its own inline `data-name="..."` string. Replaced all of them with one `avatarAttrsHtml(person)`, which adds `data-avatar-path` alongside `data-name` whenever `person.avatar_url` is set. `paintAvatar` resolves it the same two-step way every other attachment in this app already does (an `<img>` placeholder, then `LimeStore.getAttachmentUrl` fills in the real `src` once it resolves) — no new resolution mechanism invented. `repaintAvatar` (the live-update path, already wired to `lime:profile-changed` for the sidebar) took a third `avatarPath` argument.

**A second real bug found and fixed mid-verification: the topbar avatar cluster wasn't updating live.** `updateProfileEverywhere()`'s own targeted repaint of `#open-profile-avatars` only reaches an avatar element that's already in the DOM *at the moment a profile changes* — for the app's default (group) conversation, "me" never appears in that cluster at all (only other members do), so the repaint correctly found nothing to update; only once a direct (1:1) conversation was selected — rendering the cluster fresh, for the first time, from the now-already-updated `LimeStore.getCurrentUser()` — did the photo show correctly. Confirmed this is genuinely correct behaviour, not a bug, by checking `getProfile`/`getCurrentUser`'s own implementation: both return a direct reference into the same stored `profiles` Map, not a copy, so `Object.assign(profile, patch, …)` inside `updateProfile` mutates the exact object every later read (including the module-scoped `me` other rendering functions already close over) sees immediately — no separate refresh plumbing was needed once the render path itself was confirmed to read live data.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`, `store.js`.
- The real app loads in jsdom with zero errors (confirmed both standalone and via the full regression pass below).
- **Headless Firefox (Playwright), `measure-lime49.js`:** Profile body doesn't scroll at 1567×905 (`scrollHeight === clientHeight`, 663px); modal height (799px) fits its 841px cap; modal width unchanged (960px); no "Soon" badge, no "Upload photo" button; avatar vertically centred with Display name (0px off, after the fix above); a real photo upload shows an immediate `<img>` preview, "Remove photo" appears; typing into another field survives the photo preview update (confirming the surgical-update design actually protects unrelated edits, not just in theory); Save persists `avatar_url` **and** the other edited field together, confirming "part of the form"; the sidebar, the topbar cluster (on a direct conversation), and at least one thread message avatar all show the real photo; it persists across reload; Remove + Save clears it and every avatar reverts to initials; zero console/page errors the whole way through.
- **Real installed Firefox 157.0 and real Chrome 154.0.0.0 (`measure-lime49-realbrowsers.js`, `puppeteer-core` + WebDriver BiDi, confirmed via `navigator.userAgent`):** the same core cycle (no-scroll, alignment, pick, preview, save, persist, sidebar shows it, remove clears it) passes cleanly in both, zero console/page errors in either.
- Full regression pass, zero new failures: `run-lime52fix2/3/5.js`, `run-lime50.js`, `run-lime51.js` (their own pre-existing, already-documented stale-selector failures from LIME-52-fix2 are unchanged, not new), `run-lime38.js`, `run-lime44.js`, `run-lime41.js`, `run-lime34.js`, `run-lime35.js` all still pass/load clean.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule counts: `lime.css` **647** (up from 638 — net +9 for the modal-height comment and the photo-row redesign rules), `gradients.css` unchanged at **25**.

**Gate:** open Settings → Profile. Everything should fit with no scrolling, and your picture should sit neatly beside your name. Click "Change photo", pick a picture, and Save — your photo should appear across Lime (the sidebar, message avatars, wherever your name shows up with a face).

## LIME-33

**The goal:** `signup.html` creates a real, browser-local account with a profile that appears in the directory; `login.html` signs in with any local account or a seed teacher's email plus the shared demo password; `index.html` requires a session and sign-out works; a second account can be created, signed out of, and signed back into.

**Survey confirmed the brief's own warning that references predate LIME-50–54** — re-read `auth.js`, `store.js`, `local-adapter.js`, `login.html`, `signup.html` and `index.html` fresh. Confirmed: `index.html` had **no session-gate code anywhere** (LIME-05a's original "no gate" decision, still standing); `login.html`/`signup.html` loaded neither `store.js` nor `local-adapter.js` nor `auth.js` — their inline scripts couldn't have referenced `LimeStore`/`LimeAuth` even if written to; `signup.html` still loaded the Supabase CDN script and the unconfigured `supabase.js` placeholder, calling an undefined-until-that-loads global `signUp(...)`; and `local-adapter.js`'s `load()` unconditionally discarded the **entire** persisted snapshot on any `seedVersion` fingerprint mismatch — a real, confirmed data-loss bug the brief itself flagged ("confirm how") that would silently delete every locally-signed-up account the next time `seed-data.js` changed for an unrelated reason.

**The auth API (`auth.js`, rewritten):** `signUp`/`signInWithPassword`/`signOut`/`getSession`, shaped like Supabase's own client (all Promises), plus `changeEmail` (kept, LIME-31) and `changePassword` (now **really** verifies the current password instead of only validating shape). PBKDF2-SHA256 via `crypto.subtle` — `importKey` → `deriveBits`, a random 16-byte salt, 100,000 iterations — under a new `lime-auth-v1` `localStorage` key: `{ [email]: { userId, salt, hash } }`, salt/hash as base64 (JSON has no binary type). **Never the plain password, in any form, anywhere** — verified directly (grepped every `localStorage` key after a real sign-up). Email uniqueness is checked against **both** `lime-auth-v1` credentials and `LimeStore.findProfileByEmail` — a seed teacher's email is taken too, even before they ever sign in locally, so signing up with it can't create a second, disconnected identity for the same person. A seed teacher with no local credential yet still signs in via `window.LIME_DEMO_CREDENTIALS.password`; the "demo credentials aren't configured" message now only ever fires for that seed path, never for a local account. `changePassword` on a seed teacher who's never set their own password verifies against the shared demo password, then stores a real hash — claiming the account from then on.

**Sign-up writes a profile through the store — a new contract method, not a UI-side workaround.** `LimeStore.createProfile({ id, email, display_name })` (documented in `docs/data-model.md`'s "Deviations from the contract (LIME-33)"): every other field defaults to `null`, shaped exactly like `normalizeSeed`'s own profile records so a brand-new account renders identically to a seed teacher everywhere (presence, avatar initials, the directory) — confirmed `app.js` already guards array fields with `|| []` and `presenceFor(null)` already falls back to `'away'`, so no extra defaulting was needed for `grade_levels`/`subjects`/`status`.

**A real bug found and fixed that the brief didn't call out: a debounced-save race that could lose the new account on redirect.** `scheduleSave()` batches writes behind a 100ms `setTimeout`; `signUp` redirects to `index.html` immediately after `createProfile` resolves. A real page navigation can drop a pending timer before it fires, so the newly-created profile could vanish before it ever reached `localStorage`. Fixed by splitting `scheduleSave()`'s save logic into a `persistNow()` helper and having `createProfile` call it directly (clearing any pending debounced timer first) instead of going through the debounce — a synchronous flush, since account creation isn't a hot path where the debounce's batching matters. Documented in the code and in the contract entry, not just fixed silently.

**The `seedVersion` data-loss bug, fixed as the brief asked:** `local-adapter.js`'s `load()` now checks, on a fingerprint mismatch, whether the stale snapshot's own `profiles` array contains any `id` a fresh `normalizeSeed(...)` wouldn't produce (i.e., a locally-created account) — if so, the **whole snapshot** is kept exactly as stored rather than reseeding over it. Deliberately not a partial field-by-field merge (picking up a new seed teacher's added field while keeping user profiles): a merge risks orphaned references across profiles/conversations/messages, and this whole-snapshot preservation is safe by construction. A future seed-data.js edit that genuinely needs old snapshots migrated should bump `PROFILE_SCHEMA_VERSION`/`MEMBER_SCHEMA_VERSION` and handle migration explicitly — noted in the code comment.

**Session shape:** `lime-demo-session` is now `{ userId, email }`. `store.js`'s `resolveCurrentUserId()` resolves by `userId` first, falling back to `email` for an old-shape session (one written before this brief) so an already-signed-in browser isn't silently logged out by the shape change alone. **The `teacher-002` fallback is kept, not removed** — a deliberate reading of the brief's "remove the teacher-002 fallback for the real app": the fallback is made unreachable in real browser use by the new session gate (below), which redirects to `login.html` before `LimeStore.init()` ever runs without a valid session; the fallback code itself stays as a defensive default for jsdom tests that don't bother setting up a session, avoiding a mass rewrite of every existing test harness. Documented in the code comment.

**The session gate (`app.js`):** the first executable logic in the file — with no valid session, redirects to `login.html`; `LimeStore.init()` is skipped in that case too, so a page about to navigate away doesn't waste time loading and rendering data first. Auto-skips whenever `navigator.userAgent` contains `jsdom` (zero configuration needed for every existing or future jsdom harness), with `window.LIME_TEST_FORCE_AUTH_GATE` to exercise the redirect under jsdom anyway and `window.LIME_TEST_SKIP_AUTH_GATE` to force-skip it regardless of environment. Replaces the old LIME-05a "no gate" comment, which is now stale (there's a real backend-equivalent session to check).

**`login.html`/`signup.html` rewritten:** both now load `seed-data.js`/`local-adapter.js`/`store.js`/`auth.js` (neither loaded any of the four before) and call `LimeStore.init()` before wiring their form handlers — required so `findProfileByEmail`/`createProfile` see the real persisted snapshot, not an empty just-booted cache. `login.html`'s inline script now calls `LimeAuth.signInWithPassword`; `signup.html`'s calls `LimeAuth.signUp`. `signup.html`'s Supabase CDN `<script>` and its `<script src="js/supabase.js">` tag are removed (per scope, `supabase.js` itself is untouched and unused — noted in the switch checklist instead).

**Reset demo data (`app.js`):** now also calls `LimeAuth.resetCredentials()` (wipes `lime-auth-v1`) alongside `LimeStore.reset()`, then signs out to `login.html` instead of reloading in place — matching the brief's point 6 exactly. The confirm dialog's message now says accounts are removed too.

**`docs/data-model.md` rewritten:** "The auth seam" now describes the new session shape, the session gate, the `lime-auth-v1` credential store, and the seed-teacher demo-password fallback; the `auth.js` subsection documents all seven functions (`signUp`/`signInWithPassword`/`getSession` added; `changeEmail`/`changePassword`/`signOut` updated; `resetCredentials` added) with their Supabase-equivalent behavior; "Deviations from the contract (LIME-33)" documents `createProfile`; the switch checklist's onboarding-linking step now notes `createProfile` drops out entirely under Supabase (a trigger does that job), the `signInWithPassword`/`changePassword` swap-out list is corrected, and a new step covers `supabase.js`'s now-unused status.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `auth.js`, `store.js`, `local-adapter.js`, `app.js`.
- **jsdom (32 checks, `run-lime33.js`):** sign-up creates a profile and a `lime-auth-v1` credential with salt+hash, never the plain password anywhere in `localStorage` (grepped every key); the write is flushed synchronously into `lime-state-v1`, not lost to the debounce; a duplicate email is rejected (both a local duplicate and a seed-teacher email with no local credential yet); a short password is rejected with no profile created; sign-out then sign-in rejects the wrong password and accepts the right one, writing the `{userId, email}` session shape; the seed email `shem.robinson@ps113.edu` signs in with the demo password and the app resolves to Shem's profile, and a wrong demo password is rejected; the session gate redirects with no session, does not redirect with a valid one, auto-skips under jsdom with no force flag, and `LIME_TEST_SKIP_AUTH_GATE` force-skips regardless of user agent; `changePassword` rejects the wrong current password and accepts the right one, and the old password stops working while the new one works; reset wipes both `lime-auth-v1` and the profile from `lime-state-v1`; a stale `seedVersion` preserves a snapshot holding a user-created profile **and** its seed profiles intact, rather than discarding either.
- **jsdom, real HTML pages (5 checks, `run-lime33-pages.js`):** `login.html`, `signup.html`, and `index.html` (both with a forced-on gate and a valid session, and with no session) all boot with zero errors from a real local static server (docroot at the repo root, so `../vendor/...` links resolve exactly as they do off disk); index.html renders the contacts list.
- **Playwright + Firefox, real `file://` paths (7 checks, `e2e-lime33-playwright.js`):** sign up → lands on `index.html` with an empty conversation list → the new account has no `avatar_url` yet → sign out → `login.html` → opening `index.html` directly with no session redirects to `login.html` → signing back in as the same new account works → zero console/page errors the whole way through.
- **Real installed Firefox 157.0 and real Chrome 154.0.0.0** (`e2e-lime33-realbrowsers.js`, `puppeteer-core` + WebDriver BiDi, confirmed via `navigator.userAgent`, per the brief's own amendment): sign up lands in the app; Settings shows initials only before any upload; uploading a photo and clicking Save changes sets `avatar_url` (LIME-49's own "part of the form" pattern — a photo previews live but only commits on Save, so the test waits for `#settings-save-btn` to enable and clicks it, not just for the upload itself); sign out redirects to `login.html`; signing in as `shem.robinson@ps113.edu` with the demo password renders as Shem; zero console/page errors — all passing in **both** browsers. The pre-flight `crypto.subtle`-on-`file://` check the amendment required ran first and passed in both before any implementation began (`isSecureContext: true` in both; `location.origin` reports `"null"` in Firefox vs `"file://"` in Chrome, neither of which blocked `crypto.subtle`/PBKDF2).
- **A real puppeteer-core/BiDi flakiness found and worked around during this verification, not an app bug:** `page.url()` intermittently read a stale pre-navigation value immediately after a redirect (confirmed by an isolated repro: a plain delay + `page.evaluate(() => location.href)` always reported correctly, while `page.url()` right after `waitForSelector` sometimes didn't) — worked around by reading `location.href` via `page.evaluate` and by combining the URL check with a `window.LimeStore` presence check in one `waitForFunction`, forcing re-evaluation against the live execution context rather than a cached one.

**Gate:** in Firefox — sign up at `file:///Users/shem/Sites/lime/public/signup.html` as a new teacher and confirm you land in Lime with an empty list; sign out; sign in as `shem.robinson@ps113.edu` with the demo password.

## LIME-33-fix

**The user's gate check on LIME-33 (`a398ca3`):** signing up in their real Firefox bounced straight back to the sign-in page instead of landing in Lime, and signing in afterward said "Incorrect email or password." A second sign-up with the same email wasn't refused as a duplicate either.

**Phase 1 — the user approved testing against a private, temporary copy of their real Firefox profile** (`~/Library/Application Support/Firefox/Profiles/5dkc7g2o.default-release`, confirmed as the actual in-use profile via `profiles.ini`'s `Install…: Default=` entry and by `places.sqlite`'s own mtime — the other listed profile, `6bg2njdi.default`, is empty and unused). Copied via `rsync`, excluding `lock`/`.parentlock`/`*.sqlite-wal`/`*.sqlite-shm`, to a scratchpad temp folder; the real profile was never launched or modified (verified afterwards: no `user.js` was ever written there, and its `.parentlock` mtime predates this session's work). Read-only: only `extensions.json` (names/ids) and `prefs.js`/`user.js` (none) were inspected — no history, cookies, saved logins or form data.

**First reproduction attempt (via `puppeteer-core` + WebDriver BiDi, `userDataDir` pointed at the copy): could not reproduce.** Sign-up → redirect → sign-in all worked. Investigating why led to a real methodology finding, not a dead end:

- **`puppeteer-core`'s own `@puppeteer/browsers` package silently writes a `user.js` into *any* Firefox profile it launches — including a real user's own profile copy passed via `userDataDir` — baking in a fixed list of "testing-convenience" preference overrides.** Confirmed directly: the real profile has no `user.js` at all; the copy's `user.js` first appeared the moment `puppeteer.launch()` ran against it. Among the baked-in overrides: **`security.fileuri.strict_origin_policy: false`** and `privacy.trackingprotection.enabled: false`. These are not `extraPrefsFirefox` (an opt-in option) — they're unconditional defaults inside `@puppeteer/browsers`' `defaultProfilePreferences()`, which `options.preferences` (built from `extraPrefsFirefox`) is spread *over*, meaning they can be overridden by passing `extraPrefsFirefox` explicitly, but silently apply otherwise. **This is a stronger version of the "Playwright's bundled Firefox isn't the user's Firefox" lesson from LIME-52-fix5: even the RIGHT binary, pointed at the user's OWN real profile copy, still doesn't behave like the user's real Firefox unless this specific override is corrected.**
- Re-launched with `extraPrefsFirefox: { 'security.fileuri.strict_origin_policy': true, 'privacy.trackingprotection.enabled': true, 'network.cookie.sameSite.laxByDefault': true }` (Firefox's real defaults, confirmed absent from the user's own `prefs.js` — meaning the user never customized this, it's just how Firefox behaves out of the box) — **and the bug reproduced exactly**, on the first try, with the exact symptoms described: sign-up redirected straight to `login.html` (not `index.html`), and `login.html`'s own storage bucket had no `lime-auth-v1` credential and no `lime-state-v1` snapshot at all — both were written to `signup.html`'s bucket instead.

**Named cause: `security.fileuri.strict_origin_policy` at Firefox's real default (`true`) treats every distinct `file://` URL as a separate, storage-isolated origin — including different HTML files in the exact same folder.** `signup.html`, `login.html` and `index.html` are three different files, so under this default, **each one gets its own `localStorage`, with no sharing between them at all** — not "the same directory shares an origin," but "the exact same path is the origin." Confirmed directly:
- Writing `localStorage.setItem` on `signup.html`, then navigating to `index.html` in the *same browser, same tab, same profile*: `index.html` sees **nothing** written by `signup.html` — confirmed by dumping every `localStorage` key on each page.
- The **same test over `http://localhost:8901`** (a server started from the repo root, per the brief's own Phase 1.4) works perfectly — the session and credentials persist across every navigation, because HTTP origins are per-*origin* (scheme+host+port), not per-file-path.
- **Real Chrome, checked directly** (a value written on `signup.html`'s `localStorage` was read back successfully on `index.html`, same directory, same session): Chrome does not implement this per-path isolation for `file://` — confirming exactly why "chrome it works... certainly happening in firefox" (the user's own words from the parked pattern-upload thread) generalizes here too.

**Findings table (Phase 1.1/1.4):**

| Page | Surface | Profile | Session/credential visible from a prior page's write? |
|---|---|---|---|
| signup.html → index.html → login.html | `file://` | profile copy, `strict_origin_policy` at Firefox's real default | **No** — three separate origins, confirmed |
| signup.html → index.html → login.html | `http://localhost:8901` | profile copy | Yes — works correctly |
| signup.html → index.html | `file://` | real Chrome (throwaway profile, unrelated to the user's) | Yes — no per-path isolation in Chrome |

**Phase 1.3 (pattern upload, profile copy, `file://`, `strict_origin_policy` corrected):** the persistent `#pattern-upload-input` element exists and is reachable on `index.html` regardless of this cause — the upload happens entirely within one page load, no navigation involved, so **this specific cause does not explain the parked pattern-upload bug**; they're very likely separate issues. A full interactive upload attempt hit a `puppeteer-core`/BiDi "no such handle" tooling error specific to this real-profile session (a harness limitation, not attempted further — out of scope for this brief per its own "don't fix the upload, just record" instruction) — recorded here for the parked item, not resolved.

**Phase 2 — the cause is a real Firefox security boundary, not something app code can bypass.** There is no way for JavaScript on one `file://` origin to read another `file://` origin's `localStorage` — that's the isolation working as designed, correctly preventing one local HTML file from reading another's data. Per the brief's own branching: this is "a Firefox setting the app can't work around," so the fix is **a clear, cause-specific message plus exact steps for the user**, not a code workaround:

1. **`LimeAuth.checkStorageWorks()`** — a write-then-read-back probe (not just a try/catch around `setItem` alone, since some failure modes let `setItem` "succeed" without actually persisting). Called at the top of both `signUp` and `signInWithPassword`, rejecting immediately with a plain-language message ("Your browser isn't letting Lime save data on this computer right now — private browsing or blocked cookies/site data are the most common causes.") instead of proceeding into a flow that would fail confusingly later.
2. **The session gate (`app.js`) now carries a reason when it redirects:** `describeGateRedirectReason()` returns `'storage'` when the probe itself fails on `index.html`, or `'fileorigin'` when arriving via a `?from=auth` marker (see below) while `location.protocol === 'file:'` — appended to the redirect as `login.html?reason=…`. `login.html` reads it and shows the matching message instead of nothing.
3. **A second real finding while building this: `document.referrer` comes back empty for `file://` navigations in Firefox** (confirmed directly in the real profile copy), so it can't distinguish "just came from signup/login" from "opened index.html completely fresh." Switched to a `?from=auth` query parameter that `signup.html`/`login.html` attach to their own post-auth redirect instead — reliable regardless of referrer policy.
4. **The seed-teacher sign-in hint (Phase 2.3, unconditional — not cause-dependent):** a wrong password for an email matching a seed profile with no local credential now says "Incorrect password. Demo teachers use the shared demo password unless you've changed it in Settings." instead of the generic message. A local account's own wrong password still gets the generic "Incorrect email or password." — confirmed the two paths don't cross (a local-account sign-in never gets the seed hint).

**The exact steps for the user (Phase 2.2, since this can't be fixed in-app):**
- **Recommended:** open Lime through a local web server instead of `file://` (e.g. `python3 -m http.server` from the repo root, then `http://localhost:PORT/public/index.html`) — confirmed working perfectly above, and this is also what a real deployed version would look like (served over `https://`, not `file://`).
- **Advanced, with a real security tradeoff:** flip `security.fileuri.strict_origin_policy` to `false` in Firefox's `about:config`. This is a browser-wide change, not a per-site one — every local HTML file could then read every other local file's storage. Not built or changed automatically; the user's own call.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `auth.js`, `app.js`.
- **jsdom (8 checks, `run-lime33fix.js`):** `checkStorageWorks` returns `true` normally and `false` when storage is broken (simulated via replacing `window.localStorage` entirely — jsdom's Storage doesn't honor a direct `localStorage.setItem = …` override, confirmed by an isolated repro); `signUp`/`signInWithPassword` both reject immediately with the storage message when storage is broken; the seed-teacher wrong-password hint fires only for a seed email with no local credential, never for a local account's own wrong password; the gate still redirects with no session (baseline, unchanged) and correctly attaches `reason=storage` when storage is broken. The `fileorigin` branch itself isn't unit-testable under jsdom (jsdom throws a `SecurityError` for `localStorage` under any `file://` origin at all — a jsdom-specific limitation established earlier this session, not an app concern) — covered instead by the real-Firefox verification below.
- **jsdom, the existing LIME-33 regression suite (`run-lime33.js`, 32 checks) and the real-page-boot suite (`run-lime33-pages.js`, 5 checks): both still pass unchanged** — this fix didn't touch any of the auth/session logic those exercise, only the messaging around failures.
- **Playwright + Firefox, real `file://` paths (`e2e-lime33-playwright.js`, 7 checks, re-run after this fix): still all pass** — confirms the fix doesn't regress the normal, working case (Playwright's bundled Firefox never had `strict_origin_policy` forced back on, so it behaves like Chrome here: one shared origin, normal redirect, no `fileorigin` reason attached). Needed two small fixes to the test itself (its URL assertions didn't tolerate the new `?from=auth` query string) — not an app bug.
- **Real installed Firefox 157.0, the exact reproducing conditions (the user's profile copy, `strict_origin_policy` restored):** sign-up now redirects to `login.html?reason=fileorigin`, and `#login-error` shows "Your account didn't carry over from the last page — Firefox treats each file:// page in this folder as separate. Try opening Lime through a local web address instead of a file:// link, or use Chrome for now." — confirmed **not** showing the old confusing "Incorrect email or password."
- **Real installed Firefox 157.0 (throwaway profile), the seed-teacher hint:** signing in as `shem.robinson@ps113.edu` with a made-up password shows exactly "Incorrect password. Demo teachers use the shared demo password unless you've changed it in Settings."
- **The profile copy was deleted** after all verification (`rm -rf` on the scratchpad copy only — confirmed the real profile's own `.parentlock` mtime predates this session and it has no `user.js`, so it was never touched).

**Gate:** the underlying Firefox behavior is unchanged — signing up over `file://` in real Firefox will still redirect to the sign-in page (that part can't be fixed in-app). What changed: you should now see a plain-language explanation on the sign-in page instead of a silent bounce or "Incorrect email or password," and a wrong password for a seed teacher now explains the shared demo password. If you want the account itself to actually work end-to-end in Firefox, open Lime through a local web server instead of a `file://` link (see the exact steps above) — that surface already works today, confirmed directly.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-49-fix

**The user's gate check on LIME-49 (`912240a`):** "everytime i reply the main chat avatar breaks and duplicates my photo. this only happens if I reply my own messages." A parent message with 4 replies showed 4 side-by-side copies of the sender's photo stacked into one avatar circle.

**Survey confirmed plot's own diagnosis exactly**, against the current code (line numbers had shifted since LIME-33/LIME-33-fix landed, re-read fresh):
- `paintAvatar` (`app.js`, the photo branch) did `el.appendChild(img)` — append, never clear — while the initials branch used `el.textContent = …` (which replaces). Every call before LIME-31 only ever painted a brand-new element, so this was harmless until LIME-49 gave `paintAvatar` a second, *repaintable* caller.
- `refreshReplyIndicator` (called after every reply) rebuilt the message's footer, then repainted **every** `.lime-avatar[data-name]` in the whole message element — including the sender's own avatar higher up, which was already correctly painted. Each reply appended one more `<img>` on top of it.
- Confirmed `.lime-avatar` never holds any other children that need to survive a repaint: the presence badge lives in a sibling `.lime-avatar-frame` wrapper (`lime.css`'s own comment there), never inside `.lime-avatar` itself — every markup call site builds it as a bare `<span class="… lime-avatar" data-name="…"></span>` with nothing else in it. Safe to clear unconditionally, no need to stop and ask.

**The change:**
1. **`paintAvatar` is now idempotent** — the very first thing it does is `el.textContent = ''` and strip any existing `lime-avatar--pN` palette class, before either branch runs. Every call site (a fresh element or a repaint) goes through the same clearing, so there's exactly one code path instead of two (one for "first paint," a different one for "repaint").
2. **`repaintAvatar` dropped its own duplicate clearing** (`el.textContent = ''` and the palette-class strip) — `paintAvatar` does both now, so keeping them in both places would've just been redundant, not wrong.
3. **`refreshReplyIndicator` scoped to the new footer only:** `messageEl.querySelectorAll(...)` → the freshly-inserted `.lime-message__footer`'s own `querySelectorAll(...)`. It no longer touches the sender's own avatar at all — not just "no longer stacks onto it" (idempotency alone would've fixed that too), but genuinely doesn't repaint work it didn't need to redo.

**Other bulk sweeps, left unchanged per the brief's own instruction** ("idempotency already fixes them" — listed here instead of touched): `app.js` lines 1995, 2188, 2398, 2589, 2642, 2914 (the load-time full-document sweep), 3218, 3535, 4701 all call `.querySelectorAll('.lime-avatar[data-name]').forEach(paintAvatar)` or `repaintAvatar` on some scope. None of them needed scoping — a repaint of an already-correct avatar is now a no-op in effect (clear then repaint the same thing), not a stack.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`.
- **jsdom, the real app (`run-lime49fix.js`, 12 checks):** the app boots with zero errors; gave the current user (the jsdom-fallback default, `teacher-002`) a photo and repainted their existing sender-avatar element (exactly 1 `<img>`, matching the real live-update path a profile-photo save triggers); sent 5 replies to their own message via `LimeStore.sendMessage` + `refreshReplyIndicator` (the exact calls the real composer's `onSend`/`afterSend` make) — the parent's own sender avatar still has **exactly 1 `<img>`** afterward, every footer reply-avatar has at most 1 each, and the max `<img>` count across **every** `.lime-avatar` in the whole app is 1; an initials avatar (no photo) repainted 3 times still shows its initials exactly once with exactly one palette class; changing the photo then removing it live (via `lime:profile-changed`, the same event LIME-49's own live-update path uses) leaves the max `<img>` count at 1 throughout, never higher.
- **Real Firefox (Playwright's bundled build — this is plain DOM manipulation, no browser-specific storage/crypto/file-API divergence like LIME-33's own amendment had to account for), hand-driven from the actual UI (`e2e-lime49fix-playwright.js`, 7 checks):** signed in as Shem, gave him a real photo through Settings (the actual upload + Save flow), hovered a message of his and clicked the real "Reply" hover action (not a synthetic call — a message with zero replies yet has no visible `.lime-message__replies` indicator, so the real entry point is the hover toolbar), sent 4 replies through the actual reply composer (typing + clicking Send). The sender's own avatar has exactly 1 `<img>` before any replies and **still exactly 1 `<img>` after** the 4 real replies; the max `<img>` count anywhere in the app is 1; zero console/page errors.
- **Screenshot** (`lime49fix-after-4-replies.png`) taken after the 4 replies: the parent message and all 4 rows in the Thread panel show one clean avatar circle each for "Shem R" — no stacking, no distortion. The circles themselves render as a light, blank-looking disc rather than an obvious photo — confirmed this is the test fixture itself (`everyday-test-photo.png`, a near-white diagonal-stripe pattern reused from the earlier pattern-upload testing this session) blending into the light UI background, not a functional issue — the `<img>` element is genuinely there and singular, confirmed both by the DOM count checks above and by there being no visible duplication or overflow in the circle.

**Gate:** reply to your own message a few times — its avatar should stay a single, normal photo, not stack. Replying to someone else's message should look right too.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-48

**The user's direction (2026-09-29):** "update our sign in and sign up flows to have more of a feeling like [Claude's own sign-in page]" — a two-column layout (logo, headline, card with social buttons and email on the left; a large rounded photo on the right), which plot developed into a full redesign brief: one email-first flow, a Pexels video hero (not a photo, per a later update), and every LIME-33/33-fix behavior preserved underneath.

**Survey confirmed the amendment's own warning that line references predate LIME-33 through 49-fix.** Re-read `login.html`/`signup.html`/`auth.css` fresh, and confirmed `avconvert` (needed for the MP4 fallback) is installed at `/usr/bin/avconvert`, `ffmpeg`/`ffprobe` are not.

**Design decision (the brief left this to tend): consolidated into one `public/auth.html`,** not two pages each rendering the same flow. `login.html` and `signup.html` become thin redirect stubs — `login.html` preserves its full query string (`?reason=storage|fileorigin` from the session gate still reaches `auth.html`); `signup.html` redirects to `auth.html?step=create`, which skips the "does this email exist" branch entirely (arriving here is an explicit "I want to sign up" signal — if the email turns out to already have an account, `LimeAuth.signUp`'s own "already in use" rejection surfaces normally, same as any other duplicate). Both stay real files rather than disappearing, since `LimeAuth.signOut()` and the session gate in `app.js` both still target `login.html` directly, and an old bookmark or link should keep working.

**The email-first flow:** one email field decides everything via a new store-agnostic read, `LimeAuth.accountExists(email)` (checks both `lime-auth-v1` credentials and `LimeStore.findProfileByEmail` — the same definition of "taken" `signUp`'s own uniqueness check already uses). An existing account goes to a password step ("Welcome back, `<first name>`", read via `LimeStore.findProfileByEmail(email).display_name`); a new email goes to a create step (display name, password, the terms checkbox — kept exactly as LIME-33 required). "Use a different email" returns to the email step from either. All three steps live in the page from load (`hidden` toggled by JS, not re-rendered), so `password-toggle.js`'s existing delegated listener and the persistent-markup lessons from earlier briefs both just work without any special handling.

**Every LIME-33/33-fix behavior carried through, confirmed one by one, not assumed:** the storage probe (`LimeAuth.checkStorageWorks()`, called inside `signUp`/`signInWithPassword` exactly as before — no page-level duplicate needed); the `?from=auth` marker on the post-auth redirect to `index.html` (LIME-33-fix's own fix for Firefox's empty `document.referrer` on `file://`); the gate's `login.html?reason=storage|fileorigin` messages, now read by `auth.html` itself (`login.html` just forwards the query string, per the amendment's own instruction) and shown in the same `#auth-error` element on load; the seed-teacher wrong-password hint ("Demo teachers use the shared demo password..."); `signUp`'s synchronous (non-debounced) save. None of this needed changing in `auth.js` beyond the one addition (`accountExists`) — the whole redesign is a UI layer on top of an unchanged write layer, exactly as the brief's own "It depends on LIME-33... this brief is the UI and flow on top of it" framing intended.

**A real layout bug found and fixed, not just implemented from the brief's own description:** the video panel (`.lime-auth__media`) had no definite height, and its `<video>` child's `height: 100%` couldn't resolve against an `auto`-height parent — so the video's own intrinsic portrait aspect ratio (404×720) took over as the sizing driver instead, stretching the WHOLE grid row (both columns, since `.lime-auth` only had `min-height: 100vh`, a floor not a ceiling) to ~1396px against a 905px viewport. This pushed the form card's bottom content (the "Continue with email" button, the legal text) off-screen — confirmed by measuring `getBoundingClientRect()` live before assuming anything was wrong from the screenshot alone (the same "measure, don't eyeball" discipline this session has needed repeatedly). Fixed by changing `.lime-auth` to `height: 100vh` (definite, so a stretched grid item now has something real to resolve percentage heights against) and adding `overflow-y: auto; min-height: 0;` to `.lime-auth__form` so if content ever does exceed the viewport, it scrolls internally rather than clipping. Re-measured after the fix: form content 650px tall, comfortably within the 905px viewport, `document.documentElement.scrollHeight === innerHeight` (no page scroll at all).

**The video: re-encoded and verified, not assumed.** `mdls` confirmed the original `teacher.mp4` is 2160×3840 portrait, 10.01s, 18.9MB. `avconvert`'s fixed-dimension presets are landscape-named (`Preset640x480` etc.), raising the brief's own explicit worry about letterboxing — checked directly (via a real `<video>` element's own `videoWidth`/`videoHeight`, since `mdls`/`sips`/`ffprobe` can't read the re-encoded output's dimensions at all) rather than trusting the preset name: `Preset1280x720` produces 720×1280 (6.57MB), `Preset960x540` produces 540×960 (4.78MB), `Preset640x480` produces 360×640 (2.5MB) — **all three preserve portrait orientation correctly, no letterboxing**, confirming `avconvert` scales by the source's own long/short edge pairing rather than forcing landscape. `Preset640x480` was picked — the only one under the brief's ≤3MB target, and this is a Safari-only fallback behind the much smaller `teacher.webm` (0.87MB, already portrait, used untouched). The poster: captured via a headless browser loading the real video, seeking to `currentTime = 1` (exactly as specified), drawing that frame to a canvas, and exporting JPEG at quality 0.8 — 22.8KB, well under the 200KB target. Committed as `public/assets/auth-hero.webm`/`auth-hero.mp4`/`auth-hero-poster.jpg`; the original 18.9MB `teacher.mp4` and the obsolete `teacher.ogg` were deleted (both untracked, so this doesn't show as a deletion in the diff — confirmed via `git status` before and after), per the amendment's own decision (the user may overrule at the gate; the original is re-downloadable from the Pexels URL, recorded in a comment in `auth.html` and here: <https://www.pexels.com/video/businesswoman-doing-a-presentation-in-an-office-12896417/>, Pexels License, no attribution required).

**The video panel's own behavior, verified against every requirement, not just built:** sources and the poster are only ever attached to the `<video>` element by JS, gated on `window.matchMedia('(min-width: 768px)')` — confirmed via Playwright that at 767px, `video.querySelectorAll('source').length === 0` (no network request at all, matching the brief's own "the poster isn't downloaded either" instruction, not just the CSS `display: none` on the column). `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` is checked once at load: the video's sources and poster still load (so the poster shows, and the pause button can start it), but `video.play()` is never called — confirmed directly (`paused: true`, poster URL set, sources present). The pause/play button reuses the exact pattern the voice-message player already established (`syncAudioPlayerButton` in `app.js`): an inline two-bar SVG for pause (no `dew-pause` icon exists in the Seed icon set — checked the full icon list before improvising one), `dew-play` for play, with `aria-label` flipping between "Pause video"/"Play video".

**"Use phone number instead" removed**, per the amendment — confirmed gone from the new markup (there was never a working phone flow behind it). The old tagline "Connect families and teachers, effortlessly" is fully replaced by the user's chosen copy ("Where teachers connect" / "Messages, groups and communities for educators").

**`docs/data-model.md` updated:** the switch checklist's step 7 (Supabase CDN/`supabase.js`) now references `auth.html` instead of the no-longer-real `signup.html`; a new step 8 covers wiring the two OAuth buttons to `supabase.auth.signInWithOAuth({ provider })`; a new step 9 documents the account-enumeration tradeoff `accountExists(email)` introduces (acceptable for the local demo, since there's no real security boundary to protect locally; production should use a magic-link/OTP flow or a uniform response instead, and `accountExists` itself has no Supabase equivalent — remove it, don't reimplement it).

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `auth.js`, `app.js`.
- **jsdom, over a real local static server (docroot at the repo root, matching prior briefs' own established pattern), `run-lime48.js` (22 checks):** `auth.html` loads with zero errors, the email step shows by default, both OAuth buttons are disabled; an existing seed email reaches the password step with the correct "Welcome back, Shem" text; a brand-new email reaches the create step and successfully creates an account (writes a session); a wrong password on the password step shows the seed-teacher hint inline without navigating away; "Use a different email" returns to the email step; `?reason=storage`/`?reason=fileorigin` show the LIME-33-fix messages on load; `?step=create` forces the create step even for an existing email; `login.html`/`signup.html` each redirect to the right `auth.html` URL, query string preserved (verified by evaluating each page's own redirect script against a minimal fake `window` — jsdom's `Location` object refuses to have `.replace()`/`.href` overridden or read back after a navigation attempt, the same limitation the LIME-33/33-fix harnesses already hit, so this sidesteps it rather than fighting it).
- **The existing LIME-33 (32 checks), LIME-33-fix (8 checks), LIME-49-fix (12 checks) and real-page-boot (5 checks) jsdom suites all still pass unchanged** — re-run after these edits since `app.js`/`auth.js` were touched again.
- **Playwright + Firefox, over `http://localhost` (the brief's own explicit instruction — not `file://`, since LIME-33-fix's own origin-isolation finding would apply to a REAL Firefox, though not to Playwright's bundled build), `e2e-lime48-playwright.js` (19 checks):** the headline/subline match the copy exactly; the media panel is visible at 1567px and hidden at 767px (with its sources genuinely unloaded, not just visually hidden); the video plays and progresses; the pause button pauses it and flips its label; the full existing-account flow (email → password → wrong-password hint → correct password → lands in `index.html`) and the full new-account flow (email → create → lands in `index.html`) both work end to end; zero console/page errors.
- **Screenshots** (`lime48-desktop-fixed.png` 1567×905, `lime48-mobile-767.png`, `lime48-password-step.png`, `lime48-create-step.png`, `lime48-dark.png`): all match the intended design — the two-column layout, the card, the video with its pause control, the "Welcome back, Shem" and "Create your account" steps, and correct dark-mode contrast (the token system already handles this — no auth-specific dark-mode CSS was needed). One pre-existing, out-of-scope observation: the brand wordmark SVG (`logo-wordmark.svg`) has its color baked into the file with no dark-mode override anywhere in the codebase (confirmed via grep — this isn't new to this brief), so the "lime" wordmark text is dim against a dark canvas; only the mark icon reads clearly. Not fixed here — it's a pre-existing asset limitation shared by every page that uses `[data-brand-wordmark]`, not something LIME-48 introduced.
- **Real installed Firefox and real Chrome** (`e2e-lime48-realbrowsers.js`, `puppeteer-core` + WebDriver BiDi, over `http://localhost`, Firefox launched with `extraPrefsFirefox` restoring `security.fileuri.strict_origin_policy`/`privacy.trackingprotection.enabled` per the amendment's own instruction and TEND.md's LIME-33-fix entry): the full existing-account and new-account flows both complete and land in `index.html` in both browsers; the video is confirmed portrait (`videoHeight > videoWidth`) and autoplays in both; zero console/page errors in either.

**Gate:** open `http://localhost:8000/public/login.html` in Firefox (start a server from the repo root: `python3 -m http.server 8000`). It should feel like the reference — headline, a clean card, the teacher video with a pause button — and the email-first flow should work for both an existing account (try `shem.robinson@ps113.edu` with the demo password) and a brand-new one.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-48-fix

**The user (2026-09-30):** refined `auth.html` live in Firefox's Inspector (not saved) and shared a screenshot: a bigger logo, a smaller bold headline, tighter spacing. Asked for exact values, they said to use plot's own judgement — "these values are plot's estimates from the screenshot... match the look; don't treat the numbers as sacred."

**The change, in `public/css/auth.css` (and the headline text in `public/auth.html`):**
- Logo mark `32px` → `40px`; wordmark image height `16px` → `27px` (computed from the SVG's own `288.06:113.56` viewBox to hit the brief's ≈69px rendered-width target: `69 × 113.56 / 288.06 ≈ 27.2`, then confirmed live, not just trusted — measured `68.48px`).
- Headline `--seed-text-3xl` (48px) semibold → `28px`, line-height 1.2, margin-bottom `--seed-space-2` (was `--seed-space-3`). Added the trailing full stop the screenshot showed: "Where teachers connect."
- Subline `--seed-text-lg` (20px) → `22px`, line-height 1.4.

**A real gap in the brief, caught before writing any CSS:** it names `--seed-weight-bold` for the headline, but that token doesn't exist — `tokens.css`'s own weight scale stops at `--seed-weight-semibold` (600), confirmed by grepping the file directly rather than assuming the brief's own reference was accurate. Used a raw `700` instead (matching the brief's own explicit fallback: "if a value doesn't match a Seed token, a raw px value is fine here"), with a comment explaining why. Montserrat's 700 weight was already loaded (the `Montserrat:wght@400;500;600;700` Google Fonts link from LIME-48 itself already included it), so nothing else needed adding.

**Verification (measured live via Playwright, over `http://localhost:8931`, 1567×905), not assumed:**
- Mark: 40×40px (target 40px — exact).
- Wordmark: 68.48px rendered width (target ≈69px — within a fraction of a pixel).
- Headline: 28px, weight 700 (both exact), text "Where teachers connect." — its own glyph width (measured via a `Range`, not the block element's box, which is always 400px regardless of text length) is 369.5px against the brief's own ≈335px±10 estimate, about 34px over. Given the brief's own explicit "don't treat the numbers as sacred, match the look" — left at 28px rather than chasing the exact figure; the rendered result visually matches the "bigger logo, smaller bold headline, tighter spacing" description the user asked for (screenshotted below).
- Subline: 22px, wraps as "Messages, groups and communities for educators" on one line at 1567px (the brief's own two-line wrap estimate was for a narrower calibration; at this actual viewport width it fits on one line — reported as measured, not forced to match a guess).
- Gaps: logo→headline 32px (target ≈32–36px — in range), headline→subline 8px, subline→card 32px.
- The form column fits at 1567×905 with **no scroll** on the email, password, and create steps alike — confirmed via `document.documentElement.scrollHeight <= window.innerHeight` on all three, not just the default step.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule counts: `auth.css` **39** (up from the LIME-48 baseline), `lime.css` unchanged at **647**.
- Screenshots: `lime48fix-desktop.png` (1567×905), `lime48fix-mobile.png` (767px), `lime48fix-dark.png` — all match the Inspector-refined look: the bigger logo, the tighter bold headline with its full stop, and the closer spacing underneath.

**Gate:** open `http://localhost:8000/public/auth.html`. It should look like your Inspector version — the bigger logo, the smaller bold "Where teachers connect.", and the tighter spacing under it.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-29

**Goal:** a New message button opens a people picker (the search-modal look) to find any teacher by name, email, phone or school — including one who just signed up — and start a DM or group with them.

**Survey confirmed the amendment's own warning that line references predate LIME-48/33-fix/49-fix.** Re-read `index.html`'s tabs row, `createModal`, `selectConversation`/`initMessagesList`, `syncSection`, and `createConversation` fresh. Confirmed `profiles.phone` exists in `schema.sql` (`text`) — but no seed teacher actually has one set (every seeded row normalizes `phone` to `null`, confirmed by grep), so phone search was verified against a value set directly on a test profile, the same way a real Settings edit would set it.

**The button and the picker's own home:** a `dew-plus` icon button (`#new-message-btn`, `title`/`aria-label` "New message") sits beside the Messages/Communities tabs, in a new `.lime-list-col__tabs-row` flex wrapper — not added as a child of `.seed-tabs` itself, since that component's own `--active-index` pill math is keyed to its own width/children, and a plain sibling row alongside it doesn't touch that at all. The picker (`#picker-modal`) reuses `.lime-search-modal`'s own classes directly on the same element (same field/results/backdrop/blur/Escape/focus-trap shell, via `createModal`) plus new `.lime-picker__*` classes for the parts that differ: a chip row, an optional group-name field (shown only at 2+ chips), and a Start footer.

**Where the logic lives:** inside `initMessagesList()` (the function `selectConversation`/`currentUserId`/`messageConversations` already live in), not a new top-level IIFE — the picker needs all three, and every other list-col/thread interaction in this file is already organized there.

**Search:** case- **and whitespace-insensitive** against `display_name`, `email` and `school` (whitespace-insensitivity was a real requirement caught by the brief's own worked example — "ps113" has to find the seed school "PS 113", which a plain case-insensitive substring check would miss, since there's a space in the real value). Phone matches separately: digits-only on both sides, only once the query has ≥4 digits. An empty query shows everyone (sorted by name, excluding the current user); a valid-email or ≥7-digit query with no match shows "No teacher found with `<query>`" and a disabled Invite button with a "Soon" pill; anything else with no match shows "No teachers match `<query>`".

**Two real bugs found in verification, not assumed from reading the code:**
1. **Picker avatars never painted — confirmed by a live screenshot showing blank circles, not initials.** `renderResults()` built the row markup with `avatarAttrsHtml(person)` (setting `data-name`) but never ran the `paintAvatar` sweep every other avatar-inserting call site in this file already does after its own `innerHTML` write. One missing line; fixed.
2. **A real data-loss race, found by the brief's own required real-browser verification, not the structural jsdom/Playwright passes (both of which stayed green throughout — the bug only ever showed up against real Firefox and real Chrome).** `scheduleSave()`'s 100ms debounce can lose a write to a real page navigation — confirmed directly: create a DM via the picker, send a message, sign out quickly, and both the conversation and the message were silently gone on the next sign-in, in **both** real Firefox and real Chrome (Playwright's bundled Firefox never reproduced it — plausibly faster/slower relative event timing than a real browser, the same category of gap LIME-33-fix's own investigation already established about automated testing not perfectly matching real conditions). This is the *general* form of the exact race LIME-33 already fixed *once*, narrowly, for `createProfile` alone (a synchronous `persistNow()` call before `signUp`'s own redirect) — every other debounced write (`sendMessage`, `createConversation`, `toggleReaction`, …) was still exposed. Fixed generally: a new `LimeStore.flush()` (clears any pending debounce timer and runs the save immediately, a no-op when nothing's pending), called unconditionally from `LimeAuth.signOut()` before it navigates — the one place in the app that navigates away on demand, so flushing there covers every debounced write, not just the picker's own.

**The rest of the change, verified against the brief's own literal requirements, not just built:**
- **`LimeStore.listProfiles()`** — a new read (documented in "Deviations from the contract (LIME-29)"), a plain `[...profiles.values()]`; excluding the current user and sorting are the picker's own concern.
- **One chip → DM** via `createConversation({type:'direct', memberIds})`, which already reused an existing DM via its own `dm_key` check (confirmed, not assumed — no code change needed there). **2+ chips → group**, with the group-name field appearing only then; a blank name stays `null` and `getConversationTitle` derives the auto-title from the members' first names, exactly as it already did for every other unnamed group.
- **A real bug in the group-name field itself, caught by a jsdom test, not read from the code:** the field's value survived a close/reopen, so a name typed for one group silently carried into the next session's own (possibly blank-intended) group. Fixed in the picker's own `onOpen` reset, alongside the existing `selectedIds`/`activeIndex`/search-input resets.
- **The empty state:** `syncSection`'s own `emptyMessage` param only ever escapes plain text (every existing caller passes a static string) — the "No conversations yet" state needed a real button, so it's handled as a separate step right after `syncSection` leaves the list empty, not by extending that helper for one caller's richer markup. Both the header button and the empty-state's own text button open the same picker via one shared `[data-open-picker]` attribute and a single delegated listener.
- **Arrow keys + Enter:** Enter with a highlighted result toggles its selection (clearing the search so the next person can be typed); Enter with the query empty and at least one chip already selected triggers Start directly — the brief's own second trigger for it, alongside clicking the button.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`, `store.js`, `auth.js`.
- **jsdom, the real app (`run-lime29.js`, 32 checks):** boots with zero errors; the picker opens from both triggers; "valene" finds Valene and "ps113" finds PS 113 staff; an empty query shows everyone sorted by name, excluding the current user; the no-match/Invite/empty states all render correctly for their respective query shapes; phone search is digits-only and gated at ≥4 digits (confirmed a <4-digit query does *not* match on phone alone, and that punctuation in the query is ignored); a newly `createProfile`'d account appears in search results with no extra plumbing; picking Alexi reuses the existing DM (no duplicate — `dm_key` confirmed); picking a new person creates a DM and sending a message puts it at the top of All; a 2-person group gets a name and the current user is owner; a blank group name auto-titles correctly; the group-name field resets between sessions; a brand-new account's empty state shows the New message button and it opens the picker.
- **The existing LIME-33 (32), LIME-33-fix (8), LIME-49-fix (12), LIME-48 (22) and real-page-boot (5) jsdom suites all still pass unchanged** — re-run after these edits since `app.js`/`store.js`/`auth.js` were all touched again.
- **Playwright + Firefox, over `http://localhost` (`e2e-lime29-playwright.js`, 14 checks):** the full milestone scenario end to end — sign up Test Teacher, sign out, sign in as Shem, New message, search "Test", start a DM, send "Welcome!", sign out, sign in as Test Teacher, see the DM with "Welcome!" — plus the visual checks: the picker is horizontally centered with the blur backdrop visible, selecting 6 people wraps the chips without horizontal overflow, and the New message button stays within its own row (no overlap with the tabs) at both 1567px and 767px. Zero console/page errors.
- **Real installed Firefox and real Chrome** (`e2e-lime29-realbrowsers.js`, `puppeteer-core` + WebDriver BiDi, Firefox launched with `extraPrefsFirefox` restoring `security.fileuri.strict_origin_policy`/`privacy.trackingprotection.enabled` per the amendment and TEND.md's LIME-33-fix entry): the same milestone scenario, both browsers — this is the run that originally caught the `flush()` bug above (failed at "Test Teacher sees the DM" in both browsers before the fix, passed cleanly after it); also confirms picker avatars are painted, not blank. Zero console/page errors in either browser.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule counts: `lime.css` **667** (up from 647 — net +20 for the tabs row, the picker's chips/group-name/footer/no-match/empty states, and the shared `.lime-text-btn`), `auth.css` unchanged at 39.

**`docs/data-model.md` updated:** `listProfiles()` and `flush()` documented in a new "Deviations from the contract (LIME-29)" section (with `flush()` flagged as local-only, dropped from the switch checklist — Supabase writes don't go through a client-side debounce at all); a new "Finding people and starting conversations (LIME-29)" section documents the search-matching rules, the invites production design (an `invites` table, a server-side send via a Supabase Edge Function or SMS provider, accepting one links the new `auth.users` row to the waiting conversation — documented only, not built, per the brief's own explicit instruction), and the "both accounts share one browser's local data" note the amendment asked for (with the actual production equivalent: Supabase membership rows plus RLS land on the same visible-to-members-only result through a different mechanism, not something that needs building to match).

**Gate:** the user runs the milestone in Firefox at `http://localhost:8000/public/auth.html`: sign up a new teacher, sign out, sign in as yourself, click New message, search their name, start a chat, send a message, sign out, sign in as the new teacher, and see your message.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-55

**The user (2026-09-30, screenshot of the app after LIME-29):** "I would like to move the add button to the nav with a multi action [ + Add | v ]. When you press Add it adds a new chat; if you press the arrow it should open a dropdown for Link, Jam (soon). When the menu is toggled to collapsed you only see the plus sign."

**Plot's placement/style decisions, taken as given (the user could overrule at the gate — nothing here conflicted with what shipped):** first in `.lime-nav`, above Search, matching the Search field's height/radius/fill (neutral, not lime), two halves in one pill with a 1px divider.

**A real gap surfaced by survey, resolved narrowly rather than stopped on:** the brief asks for the ⌄ menu to follow "the LIME-21b menu pattern... arrow keys" as if that's an already-established behavior. Checked directly — **no other dropdown menu in this app has custom arrow-key navigation** (confirmed by both reading every `wireDropdownToggle` call site and re-reading LIME-21b's own `TEND.md` record, which only ever describes a shared *visual* style: rounded rows, grey hover — never a keyboard behavior). Rather than treating "arrow keys" as a systemic redesign of every menu in the app (out of scope, and not what a 2-item menu needs), added one small, self-contained arrow-key handler scoped to just this new dropdown — it doesn't touch or extend to the notifications/user/more-menu dropdowns, which still behave exactly as they did before this brief.

**The change:**
- **Markup:** `.lime-nav-add` — `#nav-add-btn` ("+  Add", `[data-open-picker]`, the exact same delegated-listener mechanism LIME-29's own picker already responds to — no new opening logic needed for the "Add" half at all) and `#nav-add-toggle` (⌄), each a real sibling `<button>` so they get independent hover/focus, with a `.lime-menu`-styled dropdown (`#nav-add-dropdown`) holding "New message" (`dew-chat`, a muted "Link" hint) and a disabled "New jam" (`dew-pencil`, the LIME-23 "Soon" pill, `aria-label="New jam (coming soon)"`).
- **Wiring:** `wireDropdownToggle('nav-add-toggle', 'nav-add-dropdown', { fixed: true })` — the same helper every other dropdown in the app already uses, so Escape, outside-click, and "opening this closes any other open menu" all come for free from the existing shared registry/listeners, no new code needed for any of those three.
- **The collapsed rail:** `.seed-layout--collapsed-left .lime-nav-add` shrinks to 40×40, hides `__toggle` entirely and centers `__main`'s icon — matching `.lime-nav__btn`'s own collapsed treatment (confirmed by measurement: the collapsed "+" sits at the exact same horizontal center as the other rail icons, `#notif-btn` included).
- **The mobile drawer:** per the `PLOT.md` pattern note ("any change to a sidebar row's styling must also be checked in the collapsed rail *and* the `.seed-layout--mobile-open.seed-layout--collapsed-left` `!important` overrides"), added the matching override block, mirroring `.lime-nav-search`'s own existing one exactly — confirmed live by forcing *both* classes onto the layout at once (collapse the sidebar on desktop, then resize to 767px and open the drawer), not just by testing the drawer alone (which, on a fresh 767px load, never actually carries `collapsed-left` at all, so a test that only covered that path would have silently never exercised the override block it was meant to check).
- **The tabs row reverted:** removed `#new-message-btn` and the `.lime-list-col__tabs-row` wrapper LIME-29 added around it, restoring the tabs to their original unwrapped markup and their row's full width. LIME-29's own empty-state "New message" text button is untouched — it already worked through the same shared `[data-open-picker]` delegated listener, which doesn't care how many matching elements exist on the page.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`; `lime.css` braces balanced (743/743); `git diff public/index.html` shows only the two intended changes (the new nav markup, and the tabs-row revert) — reviewed directly, not assumed.
- **jsdom, the real app (`run-lime55.js`, 20 checks):** boots with zero errors; `#new-message-btn` is gone; the split button is the nav's first child; "+ Add" opens the picker directly; ⌄ opens the menu; "New jam" is disabled (a native `disabled` button, so it's unfocusable and unclickable — confirmed clicking it does nothing and the menu stays open); "New message" in the menu opens the picker *and* closes the menu; the "Link" hint renders; Escape closes the menu; opening this menu closes an already-open user menu (the shared registry); ArrowDown moves focus to the one enabled item.
- **Playwright Firefox, measured at 1567×905, the collapsed rail, and 767px (drawer open) — `measure-lime55.js`:** the split button's height (36px) and border-radius (`9999px`, a pill) exactly match the Search field's, background color exactly matches too; the tabs row's own `#new-message-btn` is confirmed absent; collapsed, the button is exactly 40×40 with the ⌄ half hidden, centered at the same x-coordinate as `#notif-btn`; the collapsed "+" still opens the picker; the mobile drawer (both alone, and with `collapsed-left` forced on alongside it) shows the full 216px-wide split button with its label visible, not the collapsed rail look.
- Screenshots: `lime55-desktop-light.png`, `lime55-menu-open.png`, `lime55-collapsed-rail.png`, `lime55-dark.png`, `lime55-mobile-drawer.png`, `lime55-mobile-collapsed-edge-case.png` — all match the intended design.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule count: `lime.css` **677** (up from 667 — net +10: ~25 new rules for the split button/collapsed-rail/mobile-drawer-override/item-hint, minus the 3 removed `.lime-list-col__tabs-row` rules from LIME-29). `gradients.css` untouched (confirmed via `git diff --stat`, zero lines changed — not worth a live re-measurement of a file with no diff).
- **The existing LIME-33 (32), LIME-33-fix (8), LIME-49-fix (12), LIME-48 (22) and real-page-boot (5) jsdom suites all still pass unchanged.** LIME-29's own suite (`run-lime29.js`) needed its trigger selector updated from the now-removed `#new-message-btn` to `#nav-add-btn` — a one-line test fixture update, not a regression in the underlying picker logic, which every other check in that suite (32 of them) confirmed still works exactly as before.

**Gate:** at `http://localhost:8000/public/index.html`: the new **+ Add | ⌄** button sits at the top of the left nav. **Add** starts a new message; the **arrow** shows New message and New jam (Soon). Collapse the sidebar and you only see **+**. The + beside the tabs is gone.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-27

**Goal:** Share moves out of the menus into its own header button, opening a Claude-style popover (add people by email for groups, who has access, member list, Copy link); deep links (`#c=<id>`) open a conversation directly, gated by the same membership rule the RLS draft already specifies; toasts for both.

**The amendment settled every conflict with the original (superseded) brief before this ran** — only the header button + popover exists; the title ⌄ menu's Star/Rename/Archive/Delete order is untouched (confirmed live, not assumed — see verification below); "Share link" comes out of the "…" menu; Copy link is the popover's own footer button, not a separate keyboard-shortcut menu item.

**Survey confirmed the amendment's own warning that line references predate LIME-29/33.** Re-read the header markup, `CONVERSATION_ACTIONS`, `can`/`canReason`, `wireDropdownToggle`, and the drafted RLS comments in `schema.sql` fresh. Two things worth recording from that survey, not assumed:
- **`messages.type` is constrained to `('text', 'voice', 'location', 'image', 'file')`** (read directly off the column definition) — no `'system'` value exists, confirming the brief's own conditional instruction ("a system line... only if a system-message type already exists") resolves to **skip it**, documented as a deliberate gap rather than worked around.
- **`addMembers`'s own owner-only write maps directly onto the already-drafted `conversation_members_insert_creator` RLS policy** (commented out in `schema.sql`, not yet enabled) — its `or is_owner(conversation_id)` branch is exactly this write. No new policy needed when that checklist item is enabled for real.

**The change:**
- **Toasts:** Seed's own `toast.css`, linked for the first time; one shared `bottom-center` container (`role="status"`, `aria-live="polite"`); `showToast(message, { tone, duration })` in `app.js`, adapted from `toast.html`'s own pattern to this app's single-line usage (`.seed-toast__title` alone, not Seed's title+message two-line shape, since every call site here — "Link copied", "That chat isn't available to you." — is one short line). Neutral tone only, neutral Seed tokens — no lime, per the standing lime rule.
- **Deep links:** `conversationLink(id)` builds `#c=<id>` from `location.href` with any existing hash stripped first. On load, after `LimeStore.init()`, a `#c=<id>` hash that names a conversation which exists, isn't soft-deleted, and the current user can read (a member, or any conversation with `type === 'community'`) opens it; otherwise the default opens and a toast explains why. `selectConversation` now ends with `history.replaceState` (not `pushState`) — a reload keeps your place, browsing doesn't grow `history.length`.
- **The header:** avatars · Share (`dew-share`) · palette (LIME-50) · "…", exactly the amendment's own order. The Share popover (`.lime-share-popover`, positioned via the same `wireDropdownToggle` fixed-positioning path every other panel in this app already uses, just with much richer content than a `.lime-menu`) renders per conversation type: title `Share "<name>"`, a subline (private for DM/group, "Anyone in Lime" for a community), an "Add people by email" form for groups only (a muted "start a group from New message" line for a DM instead), "Who has access" with a lock or globe icon, the member list (avatar, name, "(you)" for the current user, email, role), the students-privacy note, and a footer with a read-only link field plus the primary "Copy link" button.
- **`dew` has no lock or globe icon** (checked against the full icon set, same as LIME-50's own palette icon before this) — drawn inline to match `dew`'s own stroke style (24px viewBox, stroke-width 2, round caps/joins, currentColor), the same precedent LIME-50 already established for "dew doesn't have this one."
- **`LimeStore.addMembers(conversationId, profileIds)`:** a new store write, owner-only and groups-only (`can('addMembers', conversation)` — a DM's stored `role: 'owner'` on whoever created it is never treated as real ownership anywhere else in the app, per LIME-34's own decision, so this explicitly excludes `type !== 'group'` rather than relying on that flag alone). Adding an already-existing member is a silent no-op, not an error.
- **Copy link's fallback chain**, exactly as specified: the Clipboard API, then a hidden `<textarea>` + `document.execCommand('copy')`, then — last resort — focusing and selecting the popover's own read-only link field so the user can copy it by hand.
- **`pagehide` flush:** LIME-29's own `LimeStore.flush()` (which `signOut()` already called) is now also called on `window`'s `pagehide` event — the general fix for the `scheduleSave()` debounce race, covering "closed the tab" as well as "signed out," which `signOut()`'s own call can't reach.

**A real gap found in verification, not assumed from reading the code, and fixed before it shipped:** the deep-link check only ran once, at initial page load. Navigating to a `#c=<id>` link that differs from the **current** page only by its hash fragment is a same-document navigation in every real browser — no reload, no script re-run — so a link to a conversation the user isn't in, clicked while already sitting on `index.html` in that same tab, silently did nothing at all instead of showing the "not available" toast. Caught by a real Playwright run (not the jsdom suite, which always does a fresh load) landing on an unexpected hash with no toast. Fixed by promoting the one-shot IIFE to a named function, calling it once on load **and** on every `hashchange`. Building that fix introduced its own regression on the first pass (an early `return` after the toast skipped the "select a default conversation" fallback on a fresh load with a bad hash, leaving nothing open at all) — caught immediately by the jsdom suite going from 30/30 to 28/30, fixed before moving on, not shipped and found later.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`, `store.js`; `lime.css` braces balanced (770/770); `git diff public/index.html` reviewed directly.
- **jsdom, the real app (`run-lime27.js`, 30 checks):** boots with zero errors; `conversationLink` format; "Share link" genuinely gone from the rendered "…" menu items (not just absent from a substring search, which the removal comment's own text would have false-matched); header order; `#c=conv-004` (a DM the user is in) opens it; `#c=conv-008` ("STEM Squad," confirmed via the seed data directly — the user isn't a participant) and a nonexistent id both fall back to the default and show the toast; a plain load with no hash shows no toast; `history.length` unchanged across conversation switches; `showToast` renders correctly with the right `aria-live` container; the popover renders correctly for a group (title, private subline, the email form, every member row, the correct link) and for a DM (the muted line, no email form); an unknown-email invite shows the right message; a real invite adds the member and it shows up in `getMembers` immediately; `addMembers` rejects a non-owner and no-ops on an already-existing member.
- **The existing LIME-33 (32), LIME-33-fix (8 + 1 new — `login.html` also forwarding a deep-link hash), LIME-49-fix (12), LIME-48 (23), LIME-29 (32), LIME-55 (20) and real-page-boot (5) jsdom suites all still pass.**
- **Playwright + Firefox, over `http://localhost` (`e2e-lime27-playwright.js`, 13 checks):** the conversation ⌄ menu order is confirmed unchanged (Star/Unstar · Rename · Archive/Unarchive · Delete); header order; the popover stays within the viewport with no field overflow; Copy link shows the toast; the toast is horizontally centered and its rect doesn't intersect the composer Send button's rect (both reported); a real deep-link navigation opens the right chat (confirmed by comparing the rendered crumb title against `getConversationTitle`, not assumed); a reload keeps the same chat open; opening a link to a conversation the user isn't in shows the "not available" toast — **this exact check is what caught the same-document-hash-navigation gap above**, after `page.goto()` to a URL differing only by hash turned out to be a same-document navigation, not a fresh load, exposing the real bug rather than just retesting what the jsdom suite already covered differently. Zero console/page errors.
- **Real installed Firefox 157.0 and real Chrome 154.0.0.0** (`e2e-lime27-realbrowsers.js`, `puppeteer-core` + WebDriver BiDi, Firefox launched with `extraPrefsFirefox` restoring `security.fileuri.strict_origin_policy`/`privacy.trackingprotection.enabled` per the amendment and TEND.md's own LIME-33-fix entry): Copy link's toast shows in both; **the real OS clipboard was read back and confirmed to actually contain the link — in Firefox.** In Chrome, `navigator.clipboard.readText()` itself was blocked without an explicit user-gesture grant (confirmed the write still succeeded via the same "Link copied" toast) — **exactly the per-browser clipboard difference the amendment called out**, not a bug, reported as found rather than worked around. The full deep-link-through-sign-out-and-back-in flow (hash carried from `index.html` → the gate → `login.html` → `auth.html` → back to `index.html?from=auth#c=<id>` after a real sign-in) was confirmed end to end in both browsers. Zero console/page errors in either.

**Gate:** in Firefox, at `http://localhost:8000/public/index.html` — Share → Copy link shows "Link copied," and pasting it into a new tab opens straight to that chat; ⌄ → Share… is gone (folded into the header popover); reloading keeps you on the same chat; opening a link to `conv-008` ("STEM Squad," a group the demo account isn't in) says it isn't available.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-55-fix

**Goal:** the user's own screenshot of LIME-55 (`0f504fb`) plus two reference crops of a split mic ⌄ button: "make the New button the color of the active nav item and then make the nav items follow the same pattern as the other dropdowns etc. that way the lime green can be on the primary action" — the lime rule flips. Lime moves off the nav entirely and onto the Add button (and, by the user's own follow-up, onto Send too, sharing one source of truth so the two can never drift).

**Survey confirmed the brief's own line-number caveat** (current numbers ran ~150–200 lines past the brief's estimates, expected drift since LIME-27 landed in between) but every fact it asserted held: `.lime-nav__btn:hover`/`--active` read `--selected-bg-subtle-default/-hover/-active` (the lime scale); `.lime-menu__item:hover` reads `--calm-bg-subtle-hover`; the split button is `.lime-nav-add`/`__main`/`__toggle`. Beyond what the brief asserted, survey also found:
- **`.lime-composer__return.is-active` never set its own `color`** — it only toggles `opacity`/`background`, inheriting the base rule's `color: var(--soil-text-muted)`. So "match the icon and text colour to the return button's active state" resolved to: the Add button's ink, already `--soil-text-muted` since LIME-55, needed **no change at all** — it was already the right value, confirmed rather than assumed.
- **The selected list row** (`.lime-contact--active`) uses `--calm-bg-subtle-hover` — the *same* token as its own `:hover`, by design (a code comment there says the default-selected row should read as "currently hovered," not a separate flatter shade). The "one step darker" partner in that family is `--calm-bg-subtle-active` (confirmed via Seed's own button/dropdown/layout components all stepping subtle→hover→active in that exact three-token sequence) — used for the nav's active-hover and for `:active` (press).
- **The "divider hover" the brief asked to check (from an older `PLOT.md` note about `--lime-nav-hover` users) turned out not to exist** — `.lime-center-handle` (the list↔chat resize handle) already reads `--calm-border-normal-default`/`--calm-border-bold-default`, already neutral, never lime. Noted rather than silently "fixed" (nothing to fix).
