- **`--selected-text-normal-default`/`--selected-icon-normal-default`** (lime-700/lime-600 ink-on-lime tokens) already exist in `lime.css`'s token block but are unused by anything this brief touches — not read by the return button or the nav, so left alone; flagging so a future brief doesn't assume they're wired to something.

**The change (`public/css/lime.css` only — no markup needed):**
1. **One shared set of primary-action custom properties**, added to the top-level `:root` block: `--lime-primary-bg`/`-hover`/`-active`, mapped straight to `--selected-bg-subtle-default/-hover/-active`. `.lime-composer__return.is-active` and `.lime-nav-add`'s three lime rules (expanded split button, the collapsed rail's single 40px button, and the mobile-drawer `!important` override) all read these instead of `--selected-*` directly, so the two consumers can't drift apart by editing one and forgetting the other.
2. **Nav items go neutral:** `.lime-nav__btn:hover`/`--active` now read `--calm-bg-subtle-hover` (same as a menu item, same as the selected list row); `--active:hover` and `:active` read `--calm-bg-subtle-active`. Same change for `.lime-sidebar__user-trigger:hover`/`:active`. `#notif-btn` already shares the `.lime-nav__btn` class, so it needed no separate rule. No collapsed-rail- or mobile-drawer-specific override existed for `.lime-nav__btn`'s *colour* (only geometry), so this one edit covers the rail and the drawer automatically — confirmed by reading every existing override, not assumed.
3. **The split button becomes the lime primary action:** the container (`.lime-nav-add`) now carries `--lime-primary-bg` as its own fill, full stop — no border, no divider (`.lime-nav-add__toggle`'s old persistent `border-left` is removed). Each half (`__main`, `__toggle`) is transparent at rest, inset by a 3px margin on **every** side (including the shared inner edge between them, not just the container's outer edges) with its own `border-radius: var(--seed-radius-full)`, so hovering one half shows a fully-separated rounded pill — confirmed against the user's reference crops, not just the text description. Hover fills `--lime-primary-bg-hover`; press (`:active`) fills `--lime-primary-bg-active`. Rest height drops from the container's 36px to 30px to make room for the 3px top/bottom inset; the container height itself (36px, matching Search) is unchanged. A visible `2px solid var(--calm-border-bold-default)` focus-visible ring (the same neutral pattern `.lime-lightbox__close` already uses for "a lime/dark fill needs a real ring, not just a background swap") replaces relying on the background change alone. The collapsed rail's single 40px "+" gets the same three lime steps directly on `.lime-nav-add__main` (margin reset to 0, since there's no second half to inset from); the mobile-drawer `!important` override block got the same treatment (lime background, 30px height, the 3px margin, full-pill radius) so it doesn't silently fall back to the old neutral/divided look now that the rail's own override would otherwise win the specificity fight.

**Verification (Playwright + Firefox, `http://localhost:8931/public`, `measure-lime55fix.js`, 29 checks, `shots-lime55fix.js` for two more screenshots):**
- Container height/radius still match Search exactly; the halves are transparent at rest (container alone carries the fill); the old divider (`border-left`) is gone (0px).
- Hovering the main half fills only that half (toggle background stays transparent) and vice versa — confirmed both directions, not just one.
- Focus-visible produces a real non-zero outline.
- **Nav active (Link) background is measured equal, pixel-for-pixel (same computed `backgroundColor` string), to the selected list row's background** — the brief's own acceptance test, done as a live comparison rather than eyeballing two screenshots. Notifications-hover measured equal to nav-active too.
- **Send (return) and Add are measured equal at both rest and hover** — same computed `backgroundColor` for both controls at both states, proving the shared-token refactor produced pixel-identical results, not just visually-similar ones.
- **Collapsed rail stays exactly 40×40** and now carries the lime fill.
- **Contrast, ink-on-lime, swept across all 8 shipped canvas tones (light) plus dark**, both the rest step and the (harder) hover step, reported exactly:

  | Tone | Rest | Hover |
  |---|---|---|
  | Warm | 7.94:1 | 7.00:1 |
  | Cool gray | 6.60:1 | 5.82:1 |
  | Warm cream | 6.80:1 | 5.99:1 |
  | Blue tint | 5.81:1 | 5.12:1 |
  | Pure white | 6.60:1 | 5.82:1 |
  | Lemon | 5.02:1 | **4.43:1** |
  | Sage | 5.96:1 | 5.25:1 |
  | Lilac | 5.14:1 | 4.53:1 |
  | Dark (Seed's stock dark-green `--selected-*`, unretuned by `lime.css`) | 5.98:1 | 5.32:1 |

  The sweep found `--soil-text-muted` (`--seed-soil-600`) is itself tone-relative — `appearance.js`'s `applyCanvas()` retunes the whole raw ramp, this stop included, not just `--soil-bg-canvas` as an earlier comment in `lime.css` implied for a *different* token (`--seed-soil-900`). Measured, not assumed, after a first draft of this check wrongly asserted the ink colour would be identical across tones and failed.

**A real conflict inside the brief's own two requirements, found and reported, not silently resolved:** on the **Lemon** canvas tone, the hover step comes in at **4.43:1** — under the brief's own 4.5:1 floor, by 0.07. This is **not a regression this brief introduced**: `.lime-composer__return.is-active` never set its own ink colour (see survey, above), so `--soil-text-muted`-on-lime-200 already had this exact gap before LIME-55-fix touched anything — it simply had never been measured against the Lemon tone specifically, because the Send button previously only ever sat on a background nobody had run a per-tone contrast sweep against. The brief's own two instructions collide here: "ink ≥ 4.5:1 in every canvas tone" vs. "the return button's look must not change: its computed colours before and after are identical." Changing the ink to fix Lemon's hover step would change the return button's computed colour, which the brief explicitly forbids; leaving it matches the return button exactly, as instructed, but doesn't clear the bar on this one tone/state combination. **Not fixed — flagged for the gate.** If the user wants it closed, the narrowest fix is a small follow-up brief giving the hover step (only) its own slightly-darkened ink specifically for lime backgrees — which would, by the sharing this brief just built, move the Send button's hover ink too, so it needs the user's sign-off rather than a unilateral call.

**Lime consumers left unchanged** (per the brief's "list every remaining lime consumer without changing them"): `.lime-appearance-swatch.is-selected`/`.lime-pattern-thumb.is-selected`/`.lime-pattern-tile.is-selected` and `.lime-pattern-tile__delete:focus-visible` (both use `--selected-border-subtle-default` for an outline, unrelated to the nav); `.lime-recent__item--unread .seed-avatar`'s unread ring (`--selected-border-bold-default`); `.lime-audio-attachment__play` and `.lime-voice__play` (`--selected-bg-bold-default`, the voice play buttons) — the unread ring and the voice play button both stay lime by the user's own earlier choice (LIME-21b gate).

**Gate:** at `http://localhost:8000/public/index.html` — **+ Add** is now the green button (both halves, and the collapsed rail's "+"), the same green as the chat box's Send button once you start typing. Hovering the **Add** half or the **⌄** half highlights just that one half with a slightly deeper green, inset from the edge — like your two mic-button examples. **Link** and the other nav items (Notifications, your name at the bottom) now use the same soft grey as the selected chat in your list, not green. **One open item:** on the "Lemon" canvas colour specifically, the green Add button's text is very slightly below our usual contrast bar while you're hovering it (still easily readable — this is a technical measurement, not a visual complaint) — flagged above for you to decide whether it's worth a follow-up.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-48-fix2

**Goal:** swap the sign-in page's hero video for the one the user supplied — `public/assets/signin-teachers.mp4` — replacing the old Pexels stock clip from LIME-48.

**Video source:** the user's own prompt for this brief didn't name where the clip came from (the tend prompt's "Video source:" field was left as the unfilled placeholder). Per the brief's own instruction for exactly this case: **recorded as "supplied by the user, source not recorded."** Flagging it here rather than guessing — if the user knows where it's from (stock library, a real school, an internal shoot), worth adding once, since `auth.html`'s comment block currently carries the same "not recorded" note LIME-48's own Pexels credit used to occupy.

**Survey confirmed the brief's own inspection:** `signin-teachers.mp4` is H.264 + AAC, 4096×2160 landscape, 12s, 11.49MB (`mdls`, matches the brief's figures exactly). `ffmpeg` is still not installed on this machine (confirmed via `which`), so the brief's own fallback assumption held: `avconvert` only, MP4-only output, no custom bitrate or crop filter available — `avconvert --help` lists trim (`--start`/`--duration`) but no spatial crop and no audio-disable flag on any preset.

**The change:**
- **Re-encode attempts, all from the untouched 11.49MB/4096×2160 source, reported in full (not just the winner):**

  | Preset | Output size | Dimensions | Video bitrate | Audio |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `Preset1920x1080` | 13.03 MB | 1920×1012 | 8681 kbps | AAC, 2ch (kept) |
  | `Preset1280x720` | 8.97 MB | 1280×676 | 5971 kbps | AAC, 2ch (kept) |
  | **`Preset960x540` (chosen)** | **5.68 MB** | **960×508** | **3779 kbps** | **AAC, 2ch (kept)** |
  | `PresetMediumQuality` | 1.09 MB | 480×252 | 719 kbps | AAC, 2ch (kept) |
  | `PresetLowQuality` | 0.20 MB | 192×100 | 130 kbps | AAC, 1ch (kept) |

  **None of the five fixed presets avconvert offers hit the brief's own ≤3MB target while still looking sharp** — the two below the target (`MediumQuality`, `LowQuality`) are visibly soft once rendered at the actual panel size (confirmed by loading each candidate in a real browser inside a 740×880 CSS-px box at the sign-in page's own scale and screenshotting — not judged from the raw frame alone, where softness is far less obvious). `Preset960x540` was the smallest of the three sharp-looking options, so it's the one installed as `public/assets/auth-hero.mp4` (5.68MB) — **just under 2× the target, reported plainly rather than silently accepted as a pass.** AVFoundation's presets are fixed quality/resolution tiers with no bitrate dial and no crop filter, so getting closer to 3MB without ffmpeg (not installed, per LIME-48's own finding, reconfirmed here) isn't possible with the tools this brief authorized. **Not a blocker, flagged for the gate** — same "report a real limitation rather than force a number" move as LIME-55-fix's contrast finding.
- **Audio:** every preset kept the AAC track; `avconvert` exposes no per-preset audio-disable switch. Reported as the brief asked ("if it can't [drop the audio], report it") rather than worked around — the `<video>` element stays `muted` regardless, so the extra bytes cost download size only, never an actual audible track.
- **Poster:** `public/assets/auth-hero-poster.jpg`, a frame captured at t=1s (same method as LIME-48: a headless browser canvas draw from the chosen `auth-hero.mp4`, not the raw source), JPEG quality 0.85, **60.1KB** (well under the 200KB cap; 0.75 and 0.65 were also tried — 45.5KB and 38.4KB — but 0.85 was kept since size was never the binding constraint here).
- **Framing:** sampled frames at t = 0s, 2s, 4s, 6s, 8s, 10s, 11s through the panel's own `object-fit: cover` crop at 1x. **Both people stay fully in frame at every sampled timestamp with the default `object-position: 50% 50%`** — the shot is fairly static (two people seated together, centred), so **no `object-position` override was needed; `auth.css` was left untouched.** Reported as the brief asked ("report the value chosen") even though the value chosen was "no change."
- **Sources:** `auth-hero.webm` (the old Pexels clip) deleted. The `<video>` element's `loadVideo()` (`auth.html`'s inline script) now appends a single `video/mp4` `<source>` — the `webm` source-creation block is gone, not just emptied. The provenance comment above the media panel (`auth.html` ~114–135) rewritten to describe the new file, the encode method, the ≤3MB shortfall and why, and the audio-track situation — replacing the old Pexels/Pexels-License paragraph entirely, not appending to it.
- **The original:** `signin-teachers.mp4` (11.49MB) is untouched and still untracked — confirmed via `git status` before and after every step of this brief. Not committed, not deleted, not moved.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on the inline script (extracted and checked separately, since `auth.html` itself isn't a `.js` file).
- **Real installed Firefox 157.0 and real Chrome 154.0.0.0** (`puppeteer-core` + WebDriver BiDi, Firefox with the same `extraPrefsFirefox` restore this session always uses) at `http://localhost:8000/public/auth.html`, 1567×905: video is muted, loops, autoplays; exactly one `<source>` (`video/mp4`); poster is set; **no `.webm` request was made at all** (checked the full request log, not just the DOM); pause button pauses, play button resumes; zero console/page errors. All of the above passed identically in both browsers.
- **Playwright + Firefox, `reducedMotion: 'reduce'`:** the video does not autoplay (stays paused) while the poster and sources still load — matching the existing `loadVideo()` behaviour, which this brief didn't touch (only gated on viewport width, never on motion preference, by design from LIME-48).
- **767px, both real browsers:** no `auth-hero*`/`auth-hero-poster*` request of any kind — confirmed from the actual network log, not inferred from the CSS `display: none`.
- **Framing screenshots** at 1567×905 and 1024×768, both real browsers: both people fully visible, sharp, well-composed at every size checked.
- `git status` before commit: `auth-hero.mp4`/`auth-hero-poster.jpg` modified, `auth-hero.webm` deleted, `auth.html` modified, `signin-teachers.mp4` still untracked. `public/assets/Logomark-outline.svg` also shows untracked — **pre-existing, not this brief's, left alone.**

**Gate:** open the sign-in page. The new video plays on the right — two people reading a magazine together — loops cleanly, and both stay in frame the whole time. **One open item:** the file is 5.68MB, not the ≤3MB target — the tools available on this machine (macOS's `avconvert`, no `ffmpeg`) only offer fixed quality presets, and every preset small enough to hit 3MB looked noticeably soft once actually sized into the panel. 960×540 was the smallest one that still looked sharp. If you want it smaller, the real fix is installing `ffmpeg` (custom bitrate + a crop pass, since most of the frame width is cropped away by the portrait panel anyway) — happy to do that on your say-so, since installing new tools wasn't something this brief authorized on its own.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-56

**Goal:** tune the 8 canvas tones to the brand palette the user shared — keep all 8 (their decision after seeing options A/B/C), refine Warm's surface toward the brand's `#f0eee6`, and retune Sage so the green Add/Send buttons don't blend into it.

**Survey confirmed the brief's own token map** (`--seed-lime-100/200/300` = the three `--lime-primary-bg*` steps; `--seed-lime-500` = `--selected-bg-bold-default`, the solid primary-button green; `#131b17`/`--seed-soil-900` = the pinned ink) and found two things the brief didn't anticipate, both changing how "surface" ended up being implemented:
- **`--lime-layer-surface` (bubbles, the composer box, Search, the segmented toggle) and `--lime-layer-hover` are genuinely different tokens** — but **the selected list row and the nav active item deliberately read the *hover* token, not surface**, by LIME-50-fix's and LIME-55-fix's own prior design decisions ("the default-selected row should read as if it's currently hovered"). The brief's item 1 grouped all five under one "the surface" label; in the real code only three of them (bubbles, composer box, Search) are literal `--lime-layer-surface` consumers. Confirmed by canvas-pixel readback, not assumed: on Warm, Search/composer render the brand hex exactly (`#F0EEE6`); the selected row and nav-active render the *hover* step instead (`#DCDBD3`, now itself anchored on the new brand surface rather than the old canvas, since hover's own formula was updated too) — visibly part of the same shift, just one step darker, not pixel-identical to the panels. **Not fixed** — reversing either LIME-50-fix's or LIME-55-fix's own explicit prior choice wasn't in this brief's scope, and the gate's own wording only asks about "the panels and bubbles," which do match exactly.
- **A pre-existing, out-of-scope contrast gap, found and reported, not touched:** `.seed-button--primary`'s "white" text (`--selected-text-bold-default`) reads `--seed-soil-0`, which `applyCanvas()` retunes to the canvas tone's own colour — so the primary button's text is always *the same hue as the canvas it's sitting away from*, not true white. Measured against the solid green fill (`#09a950`) on all 8 tones: **2.37–3.09:1, below 4.5:1 everywhere, on every tone including Warm — a Seed-stock behaviour that predates this brief and canvas tones generally**, not something LIME-56 introduced or worsened. Out of scope to fix here: it's a `vendor/seed` token mapping (`vendor/` can't be edited, per `appearance.js`'s own header comment), and this brief's "May touch" list doesn't cover it. Flagged for the gate and for a future brief.

**The change:**
1. **Warm's surface → the brand's `#f0eee6`.** Mechanism: a class (`lime-canvas-warm`), not an inline custom-property override — `applyCanvas()` (`appearance.js`) toggles it on `<html>` alongside the existing ramp retune. A plain `root.style.setProperty` override would win over `[data-theme="dark"]` too (inline `:root` styles beat any attribute selector regardless of specificity), silently leaking Warm's light surface into dark mode; the class, scoped as `[data-theme="light"].lime-canvas-warm` in `lime.css`, naturally has no effect there. Hover/active are recomputed with the *same* 92%/8% and 88%/12% OKLab mix the other tones use, just anchored on the new `#F0EEE6` instead of canvas — not hand-picked, so they stay genuinely "one/two steps darker," not just visually close. Canvas itself (`--soil-bg-canvas`, the page background outside every panel) is untouched — still `#F9F8F4`.
2. **Sage retuned greyer:** base hex `#D8E6D0` → **`#D9E1D5`** (fed into the existing `makeCanvasRamp`, same as LIME-50/50-fix's own two prior retunes of this same value). Found by search, not eyeballed: desaturating `#D8E6D0`'s HSL in 5% steps until Sage's OKLab distance from `--lime-primary-bg` (`#e4f9be`), measured on **both** its canvas and its surface, is at least as large as Warm's own (`#F9F8F4`→lime: ΔE 0.0797; the new `#F0EEE6`→lime: ΔE 0.0705) — the smallest desaturation that clears both, at `#D9E1D5` (canvas ΔE 0.0808, surface ΔE 0.0966), confirmed against an OKLab `color-mix` implementation cross-checked pixel-for-pixel against a real Firefox canvas readback before it was trusted for any tuning decision.
3. **Every tone re-verified, full table** (ink/muted contrast in `--seed-soil-600`, the same target LIME-50 established; ΔE is OKLab distance from `--lime-primary-bg`):

   | Tone | Canvas | Surface | Ink/canvas | Ink/surface | Muted/canvas | Muted/surface | ΔE canvas | ΔE surface |
   |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
   | Warm | #F9F8F4 | **#F0EEE6** | 16.51 | 15.10 | 8.44 | 7.72 | 0.0797 | 0.0705 |
   | Cool gray | #F8F8F8 | #F0F0F0 | 16.52 | 15.40 | 7.02 | 6.54 | 0.0842 | 0.0798 |
   | Warm cream | #FDF8F0 | #F5F0E9 | 16.60 | 15.48 | 7.26 | 6.77 | 0.0772 | 0.0731 |
   | Blue tint | #F0F4F8 | #E8EDF0 | 15.88 | 14.88 | 5.94 | 5.57 | 0.0848 | 0.0828 |
   | Pure white | #FFFFFF | #F7F7F7 | 17.55 | 16.38 | 7.46 | 6.96 | 0.0930 | 0.0833 |
   | Lemon | #FBF3D0 | #F3ECCA | 15.74 | 14.76 | 5.09 | 4.77 | 0.0450 | 0.0458 |
   | **Sage** | **#D9E1D5** | **#D2DACF** | 13.10 | 12.26 | 5.17 | 4.83 | 0.0808 | 0.0966 |
   | Lilac | #F1ECF6 | #E9E5EE | 15.10 | 14.13 | 4.99 | 4.67 | 0.0941 | 0.0957 |

   All 8 clear ≥4.5:1 for both ink and muted, on both canvas and surface. **Every tone but Warm and Sage is unchanged** (reported, not just implied, since the table needed computing fresh regardless to confirm nothing shifted).
4. **The primary-button-state check (item 3's own gate) found more than the brief's own "known gap" note anticipated**, and its prescribed remediation was applied: measured `--soil-text-muted`-on-`--lime-primary-bg*` fresh across all 8 tones × 3 states (rest/hover/**pressed** — LIME-55-fix had only checked rest/hover). Failures: Lemon hover 4.43:1 (the known gap) *and* pressed 3.70:1; Lilac hover 4.53:1 (passes, barely) and pressed 3.78:1 (fails); Blue tint pressed 4.28:1 (fails); Sage pressed 4.39:1 (fails). Per the brief's own instruction ("if any primary-button state is below 4.5:1, switch `--lime-primary-*`'s icon/text colour to full ink"), added **`--lime-primary-ink`**, applied to `.lime-nav-add__main`/`__toggle` and `.lime-composer__return.is-active` (replacing their `--soil-text-muted`). **First attempt used `--seed-soil-900` directly (the pinned light-mode ink) — measured 1.03:1 in dark mode** (dark's own lime steps are a near-black green, not pale, so a fixed dark ink washed out completely) **— caught before it shipped, by checking dark mode explicitly rather than assuming light-mode math would carry over.** Fixed by pointing `--lime-primary-ink` at **`--soil-text`** instead (Seed's own full-ink token, which already resolves per theme — `--seed-soil-900` in light, `--seed-soil-50` in dark — and is never touched by canvas tone retuning, unlike `--soil-text-muted`). Result: **15.53/13.69/11.44:1 in light, 15.32/13.63/11.63:1 in dark — every tone, every state, both themes, one token, no per-tone exceptions.** This also closes LIME-55-fix's own previously-flagged Lemon-hover gap.

**Scope check:** touched `CANVAS_P.sage` and `applyCanvas()` in `appearance.js`; the `:root` block (`--lime-primary-ink`) and a new Warm-only override block in `lime.css`, plus the two `--lime-primary-ink` consumers. Dark mode's own layer formulas, the patterns/swatch UI, and every other tone's `CANVAS_P` entry are byte-identical to before (diffed directly, not assumed).

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `appearance.js`; `lime.css` braces balanced (775/775).
- **The OKLab `color-mix` + contrast math was cross-checked against a real Firefox canvas pixel readback** for 6 representative mixes before being trusted for any Sage search or table entry — all 6 matched exactly.
- **Real Firefox, `http://localhost:8000/public/index.html`, live tone-switching via `LimeAppearance.applyCanvas()`:** all 8 tones swept — `lime-canvas-warm` class present only on Warm; Add-button ink-on-lime ≥4.5:1 on every tone (measured 15.53:1 uniformly, confirming the fixed-ink switch); Warm's surface confirmed `#F0EEE6` exactly via canvas-pixel readback on `.lime-nav-search` and `.lime-composer__box` (two genuine `--lime-layer-surface` consumers); dark mode + Warm confirmed the override has no effect there (surface stays a dark oklab value, nothing near `#F0EEE6`); **dark-mode Add-button ink re-measured after the `--soil-text` fix: 15.32:1** (screenshot cropped to the button, legible). Zero console errors across the sweep.
- Screenshots captured and viewed for all 8 tones plus dark+Warm: Warm and Sage viewed directly — Warm's shift is subtle and correct (panels/composer/Search all the new brand tone); **Sage's green Add button now visibly separates from the grey-green canvas**, confirming the ΔE-based tuning produced a real, visible effect, not just a passing number.
- **The existing LIME-33, LIME-33-fix, LIME-49-fix, LIME-48, LIME-29, LIME-55, LIME-27 jsdom suites all still pass** — this brief never touches `app.js`/`store.js`, so this is a sanity check that `appearance.js`'s changes didn't break anything those suites boot through.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule count: `lime.css` **709** (up from 704 after LIME-55-fix — the new Warm-only override block). `gradients.css` confirmed untouched (`git diff --stat` empty; it isn't even linked from `index.html`, so it never appears in the live `styleSheets` list at all, unrelated to this brief).

**Gate:** open the palette menu (☺/palette icon) and click through all 8 tones. On **Warm**, the panels and bubbles now use the brand's `#f0eee6` warm grey (a subtle shift — look at the composer box or Search field, not the selected chat row in the list, which intentionally reads one shade deeper by design from an earlier brief). On **Sage**, the green Add and Send buttons stand out clearly against the greyer canvas. Everywhere, "+ Add"'s text and icon are a clean dark ink, easy to read, including in dark mode. **Two open items, not fixed:** (1) the primary "Continue with email"/"Create account" button's white text measures under our usual contrast bar on every tone — a pre-existing gap in Seed's own token, not something this brief touched or could fix without editing `vendor/`; (2) the selected chat row and the active "Link" nav item read one shade darker than the plain panels on Warm, by an earlier brief's own design — not a bug, flagged so it doesn't look like a miss.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-57

**Goal:** avatars ≥28px use a lime-shaped silhouette (the user's own brand mark, `Logomark-outline.svg`, masked onto existing avatars) instead of a plain circle; smaller avatars stay round. Two stops: a preview (Phase 1), then app-wide (Phase 2), landed in separate turns — this entry covers both.

**Phase 1 (survey + extraction + preview, no repo changes committed at the time):**
- `Logomark-outline.svg` confirmed as a Penpot export with heavy wrapper cruft; the outer silhouette is its first `<path>` (byte-identical to the second, one an invisible fill-shape copy, one the visible stroke) — a hand-drawn, slightly wobbly circle with a small nub at the lower-left, confirmed by `getBBox()` (241.35×241.55, not hand-measured off the viewBox).
- Extracted to `public/assets/lime-silhouette.svg`: the single path, coordinates rounded to 2 decimals (the Penpot export carried ~14 decimal digits of meaningless precision — 6410 chars down to 2452, verified the rounding didn't move the bounding box at all, same 4 significant figures before and after), a square `viewBox` centred on the shape's own bounding box (not the original canvas's, which had ~2px of uneven padding), `fill="#000"`, no ids/defs/cruft. **2552 bytes, under the 3KB cap.**
- A scratch preview (outside the repo) applied the silhouette via `mask-image`/`-webkit-mask-image`/`mask-size:100% 100%` to initials and photo avatars at 20/24/28/32/40/56/64px, light and dark, with a prototype unread ring and presence dot. Zoomed crops confirmed the nub reads clearly (not as a glitch) at 28px and up, and cleanly disappears below it.
- **Stopped and shown to the user:** the preview screenshot plus the question "is 28px the right cutoff, and does the nub read well?" **The user, via plot: confirmed 28px.** Nothing was committed at this stop — `lime-silhouette.svg` stayed untracked, per the brief's own instruction to land it with Phase 2.

**Phase 2 survey (fresh, not reused from Phase 1's read):**
- Every avatar in the app is one of Seed's five `seed-avatar--{xs,sm,md,lg,xl}` classes (20/24/32/40/56px) — confirmed by tally, not assumed: `seed-avatar--lg` ×7, `--md` ×1, `--xl` ×2, `--sm` ×14, `--xs` ×1 call sites in `app.js`. **There are no separately-sized avatar classes at all** — the brief's own "custom-sized avatars (profile/details, Settings 64px)" turned out to both still carry `seed-avatar--xl`, just with a CSS width/height override on top (`.lime-settings__profile-avatar-btn .lime-avatar` → 64px; `.lime-profile__avatar` → 85%/max 220px). Since `mask-size: 100% 100%` is resolution-independent, masking `--xl` alone covers both automatically — simpler than the brief anticipated.
- `.lime-avatar-cluster__member` (the stacked-avatar tiles) never carries any `seed-avatar--XX` class at all — it sets its own `width`/`height` directly (20–26px, always below the cutoff) — so it was never going to be touched by a size-class-scoped rule regardless of the brief's own "stays as they are" instruction being followed.
- **Two real, measured findings, not assumed from the brief's own warnings:**
  - `border-radius: 50%` (inherited from `.seed-avatar`'s base rule) clips the mask's nub almost entirely when both apply to the same element — the circle-clip and the mask-clip intersect, and the circle wins at the nub's exact location. Confirmed side-by-side in a real browser (`border-radius:50%`+mask vs `border-radius:0`+mask — the first showed barely a bump, the second the full nub). This directly affects how "border-radius stays as a fallback where masks are unsupported" (the brief's own item 4) had to be built: a bare fallback declaration would have silently neutered the mask everywhere it *was* supported, not just where it wasn't. Fixed with `@supports (mask-image: url(...)) or (-webkit-mask-image: url(...))`, so `border-radius: 0` only applies where the mask that's supposed to replace it is actually active.
  - `box-shadow` on a masked element is not just "clipped to a rough edge" as the brief put it — it's **invisible**, confirmed with a direct side-by-side render (a 2px green box-shadow ring on a masked avatar rendered nothing at all). Confirms the ring needed a full rebuild, not a tweak.

**The change:**
- **`@supports`-gated masking** on `.seed-avatar--md/--lg/--xl` (`lime.css`): `border-radius: 0` + `mask-image`/`-webkit-mask-image: url('../assets/lime-silhouette.svg')` + `mask-size: 100% 100%`. `--xs`/`--sm` untouched (stay round). `.lime-avatar-cluster__member` untouched (never matched the selector to begin with, confirmed above).
- **The unread ring, rebuilt as a wrapper**, `.lime-avatar-ring`: always present in the Recent row's markup now (`app.js`, wrapping the existing `.lime-avatar-frame`), but `padding: 0` and `background: transparent` by default — a read row's rendered box is unchanged from before (verified: zero layout shift). `.lime-recent__item--unread .lime-avatar-ring` gives it `padding: 2px` and `background: var(--selected-border-bold-default)`; the SAME `@supports`-gated mask is applied to the wrapper itself, so the ring's outer edge is the identical normalized silhouette, just scaled to the wrapper's slightly larger box — the ring follows the nub, not a plain circle (screenshot: the ring's own notch lines up with the avatar's). The old box-shadow rule (`.lime-recent__item--unread .seed-avatar`) is removed, not left dead.
- **Presence dots:** untouched, and needed no change — `.lime-presence` already lived as a sibling of `.seed-avatar` inside `.lime-avatar-frame` (a pre-existing structural choice, confirmed by reading the markup, not the brief's own claim alone), so it was never inside the masked element to begin with.
- **Focus rings:** `.lime-settings__profile-avatar-btn:focus-visible`'s `outline` sits on the *button*, not the masked avatar span inside it — `outline` also isn't subject to `mask`/`overflow` the way `box-shadow` is, on top of being on a different element entirely. Checked, not touched, no clipping.
- **Group avatar clusters:** confirmed untouched (see survey above) — not a case of "leave alone," but "was never going to be touched."

**Scope check:** `public/css/lime.css`, `public/js/app.js` (the one wrapper span, Recent row only — no other avatar markup needed a wrapper), `public/assets/lime-silhouette.svg` (new, committed with this entry per the brief's own instruction), `TEND.md`. Icon-button hovers, avatar colours and presence logic untouched.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`; `lime.css` braces balanced (780/780).
- **jsdom:** the existing LIME-33, LIME-49-fix, LIME-48, LIME-29, LIME-55, LIME-27 suites all still pass (this brief never touches `store.js`/anything those suites boot through besides `app.js`, sanity-checked anyway).
- **Playwright + Firefox, the real app:** every `seed-avatar--{xs,sm,md,lg,xl}` instance on screen tallied and checked live — `--sm` ×10 round (unmasked), `--lg` ×25 masked, `--md` ×10 masked (all `md`/`lg`/`xl` instances found were masked; none of `xs`/`sm` were). An unread Recent item's ring: real `2px` padding, a real fill colour, masked. A read Recent item's ring: `0px` padding (no layout shift), transparent. Screenshots: the list + Recent row, a thread, the members/details panel, Settings Profile (the 64px avatar), the New-message picker, light and dark. Zero console errors.
- **Real installed Firefox and Chrome** (`puppeteer-core`/WebDriver BiDi, the same `extraPrefsFirefox` restore this session always uses): signed in, confirmed a real `--lg` avatar is masked and a real `--sm` avatar isn't, confirmed the unread ring's padding/fill are real (not just correct in Playwright's engine), zero console errors — identical results in both.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule count: `lime.css` **712** (up from 709 after LIME-56). `gradients.css` untouched (empty diff, still not linked from `index.html`).

**Gate:** across the app, avatars 32px and up (the Recent row, thread headers, Settings Profile, contact details) are little limes with a small notch at the lower-left; anything smaller (message-row senders, the members panel, search/picker results) stays a plain circle. The Recent row's green unread ring follows the lime shape, not a circle. Presence dots and focus rings are unaffected.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-58

**Goal:** the solid-green buttons (`seed-button--primary` — "Continue with email," "Create account," Settings "Save changes," the picker's "Start," Share's "Copy link," the confirm-dialog button, and the voice/audio play buttons) get dark ink text instead of white, since LIME-56's own contrast sweep found white-on-`#09a950` measures ~3.1:1, under 4.5:1, on every canvas tone.

**Survey confirmed the brief's own figures exactly:** `.seed-button--primary` (`vendor/seed/components/button/button.css`) reads `--selected-bg-bold-default/-hover/-active` and `--selected-text-bold-default`. Light theme's stock values (`tokens.css`): bg lime-500 → hover lime-600 → active lime-700 (darker each step), text `--seed-soil-0` (white). Dark theme already uses ink text (`--seed-soil-950`) with **lighter** hover/active (lime-400, lime-300) — confirmed as the working precedent to mirror, not just assumed from the brief's description. Computed directly (not copied from the brief): ink on lime-500 = 5.68:1 ✓, ink on lime-600 = would be ~3.5:1 ✗ (the brief's own reason hover/active must go lighter, not darker, confirmed independently). Two other consumers of the same token pair found: `.lime-audio-attachment__play` and `.lime-voice__play` (voice-note play buttons) — no `:hover`/`:active` rules of their own, so they inherit the fix automatically with zero extra CSS.

**The change** (`public/css/lime.css`, inside the existing `[data-theme="light"]` block only — `vendor/seed/` untouched, `[data-theme="dark"]` untouched):
- `--selected-text-bold-default` → `var(--seed-soil-900)` (Lime's pinned ink, the same token `--lime-primary-ink` and the nav's own ink already use).
- `--selected-icon-bold-default` → the same ink, so an icon paired with bold-green text (none currently render both together, but kept in step per the brief's own item 3).
- `--selected-bg-bold-hover` → `--seed-lime-400`; `--selected-bg-bold-active` → `--seed-lime-300` — both now lighter than the lime-500 rest state, reversing the stock darker-on-hover direction. `--selected-bg-bold-default` (the rest fill) is untouched.

**Verification, contrast table (measured live in a real browser, not computed offline and assumed to match):**

| Consumer | Rest | Hover | Direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| Auth "Continue with email" | 5.68:1 | 8.32:1 | lighter ✓ |
| Auth "Create account" | 5.68:1 | 8.23:1 | lighter ✓ |
| Settings "Save changes" | 5.68:1 | 8.22:1 | lighter ✓ |
| Confirm dialog "Confirm" | 5.68:1 | 7.15:1 | lighter ✓ |
| Picker "Start" | 5.68:1 | 8.23:1 | lighter ✓ |
| Share "Copy link" | 5.68:1 | 8.32:1 | lighter ✓ |
| Dark mode (unchanged, re-verified) | 5.58:1 | 8.17:1 | lighter (already was) |

Every rest/hover pair measured in Firefox via `getComputedStyle`, not assumed from the token math alone (the token math matched exactly: rest 5.68:1 on every light-mode consumer, since they all resolve the identical two custom properties). The voice/audio play buttons weren't screenshotted live — **no seed conversation has a voice message** (checked `seed-data.js` directly, none exist) — but they read the exact same `--selected-bg-bold-default`/`--selected-text-bold-default` pair every other passing consumer above does, confirmed by reading the CSS rule directly rather than assumed to "probably be fine."
- `node --check` n/a (CSS-only change); braces balanced (780/780).
- The existing LIME-33, LIME-49-fix, LIME-48, LIME-29, LIME-55, LIME-27 jsdom suites all still pass.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule count: `lime.css` **712** (unchanged from LIME-57 — new custom-property declarations inside an existing rule block don't add a new CSSOM rule).
- Screenshots: the auth "Continue with email" button at rest (clean green, dark legible text) and in dark mode (unchanged, already correct). Both viewed directly.

**Upstream candidate for Seed:** the same fix (ink text + lighter hover/press on `seed-button--primary`'s light theme) is a genuine Seed-level bug, not Lime-specific — Seed's own dark theme already avoids it. Noted here per the brief; not actioned (Seed is a separate repo).

**Gate:** the green buttons across the app (sign-in page, Settings Save, New message Start, Copy link) now have dark, easy-to-read text, and hovering makes each one lighter, not darker.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-59

**Goal:** soften the list's hover/selected fills and the header icon hovers (the user: "heavy"); make the header (Share, Appearance, "…", right-panel toggle) and composer (+ attach, mic, ⌄, ↵ return) icons one consistent visible size; point chat bubbles at the same colour as a hovered list row, since the user perceived them as darker.

**Survey confirmed the brief's own token map exactly** (`--lime-layer-hover`/`-active` at the percentages named, Warm's own override, dark's own override, `--calm-bg-subtle-hover/-active` feeding list rows/menus/icon buttons/the nav active item). Two things the brief's own text didn't establish, measured fresh:
- **Item 2's real root cause.** Every header/composer icon's *actual rendered ink* was measured (screenshot each glyph, scan for non-background pixels — font-size/em alone doesn't tell you this, exactly per the brief's own "dew glyphs vary optically" warning): Share 24.0px tall, right-panel-toggle 20.3px, mic 19.7px — all real outliers — against a ~15.3px standard every other icon in both groups already shares (Appearance's hand-drawn SVG, dew-plus, the composer chevron and header ellipsis both naturally shorter by shape, not by mismatch). `dew`'s own icons render inside a `1.5em` mask box (`vendor/seed/icons/dew/dist/dew.css`'s `.dew` base rule) — not a plain font-size box — so "font-size 18px" across different `.dew` glyphs doesn't produce equal ink even before accounting for each SVG's own internal padding; confirmed directly from the icon source, not inferred.
- **Share couldn't be fixed by resizing alone.** Tested a range of font-sizes on `dew-share`: 11.5px matched the target height but read visibly thinner-stroked than its neighbours; 13px was a closer compromise but still slightly heavier. No single font-size matched both height and stroke weight at once (the underlying SVG's own proportions don't scale onto the 15.3px target cleanly) — exactly the brief's own anticipated case, redrawn as a hand-drawn inline SVG instead (LIME-50's own palette-icon precedent), landing on the target height exactly (15.3px, matched to Appearance's own) with a matching stroke-width.

**The change:**
1. **Softer layers, measured not guessed.** Computed OKLab ΔE between neighbouring layer steps (surface→hover, hover→active) across all 8 tones + dark at several candidate percentage pairs, cross-checked the OKLab math against a real browser's `color-mix()` output via canvas pixel readback before trusting any of it (6 test mixes, all matched exactly). 6%/9% (light) and 6%/9% (dark) both produced a **byte-identical hex** between surface and hover on at least one tone (Sage in light; the fixed dark canvas in dark) — a real zero, not almost-zero. **5%/8% (light) and 7%/10% (dark)** are the lowest percentages that stay genuinely nonzero everywhere (Sage's own tightest light-mode gap: ΔE 0.0127; dark's tightest: ΔE 0.0083) — both the brief's own suggested starting numbers, now confirmed rather than assumed. Applied to the default light block, Warm's own override (anchored on `#F0EEE6` since LIME-56, same formula), and dark. `--lime-border-subtle`/`--lime-layer-raised` untouched — outside the brief's own item 1 scope.
2. **Icon sizing, all measured before AND after** (not just adjusted and assumed fixed):
   - `dew-share` → a new hand-drawn `.lime-share-icon` inline SVG (`index.html`, 18×18 direct size, `viewBox 0 0 24 24`, `stroke-width 2`, round caps/joins — the exact `.lime-appearance-icon` pattern), rendering at 15.3px tall, matching Appearance exactly.
   - `#right-panel-toggle .dew` font-size 18px → 13.5px (20.3px → 15.3px tall).
   - `.lime-composer__voice .dew-microphone` font-size 14px → 10.5px (19.7px → 15.3px tall).
   - `.lime-composer__return` (Send, a literal `↵` text glyph, not a `dew`/SVG icon — kept as text, just resized rather than redrawn, since a plain font-size change matched the target within the brief's own ±1px tolerance with no weight mismatch) font-size 18px → 23px (11.7px → 15.0px tall).
   - `dew-plus` (composer +) and `dew-chevron-down`/`dew-ellipsis-menu`: **left untouched** — already within ±1px of the standard (attach) or naturally short by shape, not by mismatch (chevron, ellipsis) — confirmed by measurement, not assumed fine because the user didn't name them.
3. **Header hovers soften automatically** — they already read `--calm-bg-subtle-hover`, so item 1's change alone covers them; no separate rule needed (confirmed by reading `.lime-icon-btn:hover`, unchanged).
4. **Bubbles → `--calm-bg-subtle-hover`** (`.lime-message__content`, was `--soil-bg-surface`) — the exact token `.lime-contact:hover` already uses, so the two can never drift again. One shared rule covers the reply panel too: `.lime-message__content` turned out to be the same class in both contexts (confirmed by reading `app.js`'s own render functions — no separate reply-bubble rule existed to begin with, simpler than the brief anticipated). Checked for a message-row hover state that might now collide with the new bubble colour: `.lime-message:hover` only reveals `.lime-message__actions` (the react/reply hover buttons), never touches the bubble's own background — no collision exists.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`; `lime.css` braces balanced (782/782); `index.html`'s 3 `<svg>` tags all close.
- **The OKLab math was cross-checked against a real Firefox `color-mix()` canvas readback** before being trusted (same method as LIME-56), not assumed correct from the formula alone.
- **Colour verification, measured live (canvas pixel readback, not just computed offline):**

  | | Bubble | Hovered list row | Selected list row | Composer box (surface, untouched) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Warm | `#E3E2DA` | `#E3E2DA` | `#E3E2DA` | `#F0EEE6` |
  | Dark | `#202824` | `#202824` | — | — |

  Bubble and list-hover are **identical** in both themes, confirming the user's own perception was correct (they genuinely were two different colours before — Warm's old surface `#F1F0EC`/`#F0EEE6` vs. hover `#E3E2DA`, a real, measurable gap, not just a screenshot artifact) and that the fix actually closed it, not just brought them closer.
- **Icon bounding boxes, before → after** (real pixels, `deviceScaleFactor: 3`, corner-pixel-diff scan):

  | Icon | Before | After |
  |---|---|---|
  | Header: Share | 17.0×24.0 | 12.0×**15.3** |
  | Header: Appearance (reference) | 14.7×15.3 | unchanged |
  | Header: "…" | 17.0×3.7 | unchanged (naturally short) |
  | Header: right-panel toggle | 22.3×20.3 | 16.7×**15.3** |

  | Composer: mic | 12.3×19.7 | 9.0×**15.3** |
  | Composer: chevron | 12.7×7.0 | unchanged (naturally short) |
  | Composer: Send (↵) | 14.3×11.7 | 14.3×**15.0** |

- **Real installed Firefox and Chrome** (`puppeteer-core`/WebDriver BiDi): signed in, confirmed the bubble background resolves to a real colour, confirmed Share renders as the new 18px inline SVG, confirmed Send's font-size is 23px, zero console/page errors — identical in both.
- **Screenshots**, light and dark: the header icon row at rest (Share/Appearance/"…" now visually even), the composer icon row at rest (+/mic/⌄ even), Send active (lime-filled, legible), the list with a hovered and a selected row next to a message bubble (visibly the same shade). All viewed directly, not just measured.
- The existing LIME-33, LIME-49-fix, LIME-48, LIME-29, LIME-55, LIME-27 jsdom suites all still pass.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule count: `lime.css` **714** (up from 712 — two new selectors, `#right-panel-toggle .dew` and `.lime-share-icon`). `gradients.css` untouched.

The Share, palette and "…" icons at the top are the same visible size; the composer's +, mic, ⌄ and ↵ match each other too. "Chat box link button" was read as *every* composer icon, per plot's own interpretation — flagged in case that's not what was meant.

## LIME-57-fixb

**Goal:** supersedes the never-sent LIME-57-fix. Its root cause and fix (no masked element may be an ancestor of a presence dot) still apply; this brief adds a Slack-style polish on top — a real cut-out notch in the avatar where the status icon sits (not a painted ring), four hand-drawn state icons (active/busy/away/DND), sized ~28% of the avatar, at the edge at ~45°.

**Bug confirmed live before any code changed** (not assumed from the brief's own description): a screenshot of a Recent row's presence dot showed a green "leaf" shape, not a circle — `.lime-avatar-ring` (LIME-57 Phase 2's own wrapper around `.lime-avatar-frame`) carried the silhouette mask *unconditionally*, and being an ancestor of `.lime-presence`, clipped every presence dot into the lime shape regardless of unread state, not just when the ring itself was visible.

**The structural fix (LIME-57-fix's own items 1–3):** `.lime-avatar-ring` is gone. The unread ring is now `.lime-avatar-frame::after` — a layer *behind* the avatar (`inset: -2px`, `z-index: -1`, filled only for `.lime-recent__item--unread`), the same technique the frame's own dormant "story ring" (`::before`) already used, never a wrapper. `.lime-presence` was already a sibling of `.seed-avatar` inside the frame (confirmed by reading the markup, not assumed from an old comment) — it just needed the wrapping ring gone, not a structural change of its own. **Audit of every masked avatar for a non-sibling presence/badge/outline relationship:** `.lime-avatar-frame` is used in exactly 3 places in `app.js` (message sender, thread-header DM avatar, the Recent row), all `--lg`, all already siblings — confirmed by grep, not sampled. `.lime-settings__profile-avatar-btn:focus-visible`'s outline sits on the *button*, a different element from the masked avatar span inside it, and `outline` isn't subject to `mask`/`overflow` the way `box-shadow` is — checked, not an ancestor-masking case at all.

**The notch (new in this brief):** a second `radial-gradient` mask layer, `mask-composite: subtract` (`-webkit-mask-composite: source-out`, the brief's own specified fallback keyword), cut where the presence icon sits. Scoped to `.lime-avatar-frame--{size} .seed-avatar--{size}` — i.e. only avatars that actually pair with a presence icon (every other masked avatar app-wide — contact lists, the picker, Settings, contact details — never uses `.lime-avatar-frame`, confirmed above, so none of them are touched). `xs`/`sm` get an explicit circle as their own base mask layer (border-radius alone can't participate in a two-layer composite); `md`/`lg`/`xl` reuse the existing silhouette. The unread-ring layer gets the identical two-layer treatment, so its own bite lines up with the avatar's (the brief's own item 1: "the ring never runs into the icon").

**Geometry, computed and reported (not eyeballed):** the icon's centre sits on the avatar's own edge at 45° toward the bottom-right — a proportional, scale-invariant point (50% + 50%·cos45° = **85.4%** in both X and Y, the same fraction at every size) shared by the avatar's mask, the ring's mask and `.lime-presence`'s own `top`/`left` position via three CSS custom properties on `.lime-avatar-frame` (`--notch-cx`, `--notch-cy`, `--notch-r`), so all three can never drift out of alignment with each other. Icon size ≈28% of the avatar at every step (target was "~28%"; actual: xs 7/20=35%, sm 8/24=33%, md 10/32=31%, lg 12/40=**30%**, xl 16/56=28.6% — lg is the only one with a live consumer, measured directly: **12px icon on a 40px avatar, 30.0%**, notch radius 8px).

**A real bug found in verification, chased at length, and resolved as a verification-methodology lesson rather than a CSS fix:** a Playwright screenshot with `clip` set to *exactly* an element's own bounding box (zero margin) rendered the notch as uncut in Firefox — solid silhouette, no bite — even though `getComputedStyle` reported every `mask-*` property correct. It looked exactly like a real Firefox compositing bug: isolating the identical declarations on a bare element outside any cascade worked perfectly; reintroducing the override (this rule winning over the single-layer rule above it via ordinary specificity) reproduced the failure every time; adding `!important` to every `mask-*` declaration "fixed" it. Before committing to `!important` as a real fix, a **full, unclipped page screenshot of the same live page** was checked — and the notch rendered perfectly, with or without `!important`. The tight-clip screenshot itself was the bug (a Playwright/Firefox screenshot-capture quirk, not a real rendering issue — confirmed by removing `!important` entirely and re-checking against a full-page screenshot, which still rendered correctly). `!important` was removed; the shipped CSS has none. **Lesson recorded directly in `lime.css`'s own comment**, so a future session chasing a "notch looks uncut" report checks a full-page screenshot before trusting a tightly-clipped one.

**The four states, drawn as inline SVGs** (`app.js`'s `presenceIconSvg`, one 24×24 viewBox design per state, scaled entirely by `.lime-presence`'s own CSS `width`/`height` — no per-size redrawing):
- **Active:** a solid circle, `--good-bg-bold-default`.
- **Busy:** the same solid circle, with an SVG `<mask>` (a white rect minus a black circle at top-right) cutting its own small notch, plus a "z" `<text>` sitting in that notch — the `<mask>` id is generated per-instance (`lime-presence-mask-N`, a module-level counter in `app.js`), not a hardcoded/duplicated id, since multiple presence icons render on the same page at once.
- **Away:** a hollow ring, `--soil-text-muted`, no notch.
- **DND:** a hollow muted ring with the same top-right notch-and-mask technique as Busy, plus a "z" — replaces the old solid-red DND (the brief's own explicit call; the user can ask for red back at the gate). `PRESENCE_LABEL.dnd` added (`'Do not disturb'`) for completeness; `presenceFor()` itself is untouched and still never returns `'dnd'` (no data status maps to it, same as before this brief — confirmed by reading `PRESENCE`'s own mapping, not assumed).
- **The "z" lives inside each state's own SVG**, never inside anything the avatar's mask touches — it was never at risk of the ancestor-masking bug to begin with, since `.lime-presence` itself is never masked (only the avatar and the ring are).
- At `xs`/`sm` (7–8px) the "z" is dropped (`showZ` in `presenceHtml`) — unreadable at that size — while the notch/gap alone still shows the state differs; `md`/`lg`/`xl` and the inline variant always show it.

**Fallback:** `.lime-presence` keeps its old `box-shadow: 0 0 0 2px var(--soil-bg-canvas)` ring as the *default*; a `@supports ((mask-composite: subtract) or (-webkit-mask-composite: source-out))` block removes it. Tested by simulating an unsupporting browser directly (stripping the notch layer and re-forcing the box-shadow via an injected override) — the ring reappeared correctly, confirmed live, not just read from the CSS.

**Scope check:** `public/css/lime.css` (the frame/ring/presence/notch rules), `public/js/app.js` (`presenceIconSvg`, `presenceHtml`, and the four render call sites), `TEND.md`. Presence data logic (`PRESENCE`, `presenceFor`), avatar colours and the silhouette itself are untouched.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`; `lime.css` braces balanced (783/783).
- The existing LIME-33, LIME-49-fix, LIME-48, LIME-29, LIME-55, LIME-27 jsdom suites all still pass.
- **Full-page (never tightly-clipped) screenshots**, light and dark: the Recent row showing all three real seed states (active/busy/away — no seed person has `dnd`, confirmed via `seed-data.js`, so DND's screenshot is from a live but synthetic state swap, noted as such) with correct notches and rings; a hovered Recent item; a message-sender avatar; the Settings profile avatar (confirmed *unmasked-by-the-notch*, since it's outside any `.lime-avatar-frame` — the plain silhouette, uncut, exactly as before this brief). All viewed directly.
- **Real installed Firefox 157.0 and Chrome 154.0.0.0** (`puppeteer-core`/WebDriver BiDi): `mask-composite` supported natively in both (Chrome also supports the `-webkit-` form); full-page screenshots confirm clean notches and correct state icons in both, zero console/page errors in either.
- **The fallback path**, tested directly (not assumed from the CSS alone): with the notch layer and `.lime-presence`'s `box-shadow: none` override both disabled, the ring returns and every avatar reverts to its plain (uncut) shape — confirmed live.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule count: `lime.css` **711** (down from 714 — the old `.lime-avatar-ring` block removed outweighed the new rules added). `gradients.css` untouched.

**Gate:** in the Recent row, status icons sit in a real cut-out notch — active is a solid green dot, busy is green with a tiny "z," away is a hollow ring, DND is a broken ring with a "z." Nothing is clipped, on hovered or selected rows, light or dark. The green unread ring still follows the lime shape and stops short of the icon.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-59-fix

**Goal:** LIME-59 normalised every header/composer icon down to a ~15.3px standard it derived from `.lime-appearance-icon`/`dew-ellipsis-menu` — but the user's own reference for "the right size" is `#left-panel-toggle`, which LIME-59 never touched. Bring every icon LIME-59 touched back up to match it.

**Survey confirmed the brief's own instruction exactly:** `#left-panel-toggle` (`dew-sidebar-left-open`, inside `.lime-icon-btn`) was never touched by LIME-59 or any prior brief — its icon still reads the base `.lime-icon-btn .dew { font-size: 18px; }` rule, untouched since before this whole sequence started. Measured directly (real pixels, not font-size): **20.5px tall** — the real target, replacing LIME-59's own 15.3px one.

**The change, each measured against the 20.5px reference individually, not assumed to need the same delta:**
- `#right-panel-toggle .dew`: LIME-59's own override (13.5px) **removed entirely** — it's the same glyph as the left toggle, mirrored, so falling back to the shared base `.lime-icon-btn .dew` 18px rule matches the reference exactly (measured: 20.5px, identical).
- `.lime-appearance-icon` / `.lime-share-icon`: both direct-pixel SVGs, 18px → **24px** (measures 20.0px / 20.25px — these two were always meant to match each other, not dew's own convention, so one shared new size for both).
- `.lime-composer__aux .dew` (the + attach icon): 14px → **19px** (measures 20.0px). This one was never touched by LIME-59 at all — it was already under the real reference before LIME-59 ever ran, confirming the user's complaint ("the + icon... look smaller than they were") predates LIME-59 for this specific icon.
- `.lime-composer__voice .dew-microphone`: LIME-59's 10.5px → **14px** — simply reverting LIME-59's own change. 14px (its value immediately before LIME-59 touched it) already measures 20.0px against the real reference, so no new number was needed, just undoing the wrong one.
- `.lime-composer__return` (Send, a text glyph): LIME-59's 23px → **32px** (measures 20.25px), still comfortably inside the unchanged 32px button — the glyph's own ink doesn't fill its full em-square.
- **`.lime-composer__voice .dew-chevron-down` (⌄) and `dew-ellipsis-menu` ("…") are left unchanged** (12px and the base 18px respectively) — both are inherently short, wide marks (a caret, three dots), incapable of ever measuring 20.5px tall without the glyph itself growing absurdly wide to compensate. "Match the left toggle's height" has no meaningful literal answer for a shape that's never tall to begin with — flagged at the gate rather than forced to a number that would look wrong.

**Verification, every value measured before and after (not assumed from the new font-size alone — the lesson every one of this brief's own predecessors already learned):**

| Icon | Before (LIME-59) | After |
|---|---|---|
| Left toggle (reference, unchanged) | 20.5px | 20.5px |
| Right-panel toggle | 15.3px | **20.5px** |
| Appearance | 15.3px | **20.0px** |
| Share | 15.3px | **20.25px** |
| Composer + | 15.0px | **20.0px** |
| Composer mic | 15.3px | **20.0px** |
| Composer Send | 15.0px | **20.25px** |
| Composer chevron (unchanged) | 7.0px | 7.0px |
| "…" (unchanged) | 3.5px | 3.5px |

- `node --check` n/a (CSS-only); braces balanced (782/782 — one whole rule, `#right-panel-toggle`'s override, removed cleanly).
- The existing LIME-33, LIME-49-fix, LIME-48, LIME-29, LIME-55, LIME-27 jsdom suites all still pass.
- Screenshots: the left toggle next to the lime logo (the reference, unchanged); the header row (Share/Appearance/"…") now visibly bigger and matching each other; the composer row (+/mic/⌄); the right-panel toggle alone; Send in its active (lime-filled) state — all viewed directly, no clipping or overflow in the unchanged button boxes.
- **Real installed Firefox and Chrome** (`puppeteer-core`/WebDriver BiDi): every computed font-size/width confirmed directly (not just visually) — `right-panel-toggle` matches `left-panel-toggle`'s own font-size exactly, Appearance/Share both 24px, attach 19px, mic 14px, Send 32px. Zero console/page errors in either.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule count: `lime.css` **710** (down from 711 — removing `#right-panel-toggle .dew`'s own rule outweighed the comment-only edits elsewhere). `gradients.css` untouched.

**Gate:** the right-panel toggle, the palette, Share, and the chat box's + and mic are all back to matching the left sidebar toggle's size. The composer's ⌄ and the header's "…" are left at their own smaller, naturally-flat size — flagged in case that's not what was meant.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-57-fixc

**Goal:** redraw the presence/status icons to the user's own Penpot mockup geometry (bigger icon, corner-mounted cut-away, a legible "Z"), and report what caused the green band/square the user saw around status icons on fixb.

**Two independent bugs were found and fixed, not one — the brief's own "report what caused the band" required separating them:**

**Bug 1 — the "Z" path's own stroke geometry rendered as a solid blob, not a Z.** The first redraw attempt (`M 72,-8 L 86,-8 L 72,14 L 86,14`, stroke-width 8) put bars only 14 units long under an 8-unit-thick stroke. Rendered in total isolation (no avatar, no mask, just the bare path), the three strokes' own caps and miter joins swallowed the gaps between them — one solid blocky corner, not a recognizable "Z". Fixed by spanning the mockup's full 68–90 box (22 units) at stroke-width 5 (a 4.4:1 length:thickness ratio instead of 1.75:1) — confirmed legible by the same isolated-render technique. This also moved the Z's own farthest painted point: measured precisely by rendering the path alone and scanning every pixel for the farthest green point from the icon's centre (not assumed from the bare path coordinates — stroke-width and the square cap/miter both push past them), it now sits 76.0 icon-local units out (icon diameter = 100 units), i.e. 76.0% × 31% ≈ **23.56%** of the avatar's width — just over the 23% `--notch-r` already in use, so that was bumped to **24%** (`--notch-r`: xs 4.8px, sm 5.76px, md 7.68px, lg 9.6px, xl 13.44px) as a single value for every state, same reasoning as before (a slightly bigger gap for active/away than their own bare formula, so the Z never crosses the avatar's edge on busy/DND).

**Bug 2 — the real cause of the band: a cross-browser `mask-composite` rendering bug, pre-existing since fixb.** Reproduced in total isolation — plain `<div>`s, no app code, no screenshot-clip artifact (confirmed via genuine full-page screenshots, both a Playwright full-page capture and canvas-cropped versions of it): whenever a `mask-composite: subtract` layer's own circle extends past the edge of the FIRST mask layer's own shape, the overflow paints **solid** instead of vanishing. A corner-mounted badge notch almost always crosses the base shape's own curved edge by design, so this isn't a rare edge case — recomputing fixb's own geometry (85.4%/85.4% centre, ~21% radius) shows it already crossed the base shape's edge by a smaller margin, which is almost certainly the original "thin band" complaint; fixc's mockup-accurate corner position and larger radius (for the Z's reach) pushed the same bug far enough to look like an obvious solid square. Confirmed identical in Firefox 157.0 and Chrome 154.0.8037.92 (real installed binaries), with a plain `<div>`, the real `lime-silhouette.svg`, and a pure two-gradient version — all three reproduced it. A first fix attempt (swap the second layer's operator to `intersect`/`source-in`, which should avoid the overflow mathematically) instead made the browser disregard the FIRST layer's own shape entirely (confirmed with an explicit base radius and with the real silhouette — both just filled the whole box). Two failed compensating patches is tend's own stop condition — this went back to plot (PLOT.md's LIME-57-fixc amendment) rather than a third attempt.

**The fix: no `mask-composite` anywhere.** The notch is a fixed ratio of the avatar (centre 90%/86%, radius 24% of the width), so it's baked directly into a plain SVG's own internal `<mask>` — one shape (silhouette or circle) minus a `<circle>` painted over it, one `mask-image` layer per element, no cross-layer compositing left for a browser to get wrong. Four new files in `public/assets/`:
- `lime-silhouette-notched.svg` / `circle-notched.svg` — the avatar's own cut-away (md/lg/xl and xs/sm respectively). Pure ratio geometry, so one file each serves every size in its group.
- `lime-silhouette-ring-notched.svg` / `circle-ring-notched.svg` — the unread-ring layer (`::after`). Its box is 4px bigger than the avatar's (`inset: -2px` both sides), so the avatar's own ratio would land the notch ~1.5px off; computed a **minimax-fit ratio** per size-group instead (solved so the worst-case size has the smallest possible error, not just picked the middle size and hoped): lime ring centre 86.67%/83%, radius 22% (max error across md/lg/xl: 0.4px, 0.36px, 0.24px respectively — all comfortably inside the ±0.5px bar); circle ring centre 83.85%/80.46%, radius 20.31% (max error across xs/sm: 0.12px, 0.11px, 0.07px). One file per group, not five.

All four isolated as plain CSS masks on bare `<div>`s before touching the app — clean circular cut, no overflow, in both Firefox and Chrome. Verified again live afterward (see Verification).

**Scope check on "only avatars that actually show a presence badge get the notched mask":** reading every call site of `.lime-avatar-frame` in `app.js` (3 total: message sender, thread-header DM avatar, the Recent row — all `--lg`) found none without a paired `.lime-presence` today, same finding fixb already made. Scoped the new rules with `.lime-avatar-frame:has(.lime-presence)` anyway rather than relying on that staying true — `:has()` support confirmed in both real browsers (`CSS.supports('selector(:has(a))')` → `true`), and the masked (notched) rendering only appears where `.lime-presence` is actually present, confirmed live.

**Also removed as dead weight:** the per-size `.lime-avatar-frame--{size}::after { --notch-cx/-cy }` overrides added earlier this session to compensate the ring's own position mismatch under the old technique — no longer needed now that the ring's notch position is baked into its own SVG; and the `--notch-r` custom property, which nothing reads anymore (the notch's radius is baked into the SVGs too). `--notch-cx`/`--notch-cy` are kept on `.lime-avatar-frame` since `.lime-presence` still reads them for its own position.

**Other mockup geometry (unchanged from the original fixc plan, not re-litigated here):** icon diameter 31% of the avatar width (xs 6.2px, sm 7.44px, md 9.92px, lg 12.4px, xl 17.36px); icon centre 90%/86%; busy/DND's bite is an SVG `<mask>` with a per-instance id (`lime-presence-mask-N`, a module-level counter), unrelated to the avatar's own mask; `.lime-presence` carries no background/border-radius/shadow of its own in the working case (`overflow: visible` only, for the Z's own overflow); the Z shows at md/lg/xl only, per the brief.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`; `lime.css` braces balanced (782/782).
- **A 4-state × 5-size synthetic grid** (the real `presenceIconSvg`/`presenceHtml` markup, reproduced in a test harness since those functions are closed over an IIFE and not exposed on `window` — not a reimplementation of the design, the literal same strings), with the ring forced on for the "busy" row to exercise both masked layers together: every one of the 20 combinations renders a clean notch, correct Z-or-no-Z by size, in both Firefox and Chrome. No square/band artifact anywhere.
- **A mockup-comparison sheet**: all 4 states at 400px on the `#451a49` purple background, matching the mockup's own presentation (white silhouette, nothing drawn around the icon) — directly comparable, and matching.
- **The live app**, Recent row (Jean C, busy, unread — the exact spot the bug showed before this fix): clean in light, dark, and Sage themes, hovered, and selected. Full-page screenshots throughout, never tightly clipped (TEND.md's own standing lesson from fixb).
- **Real installed Firefox 157.0 and Chrome 154.0.8037.92** (`puppeteer-core`/WebDriver BiDi, not just Playwright's bundled browsers): `mask-image` SVG `url()` support confirmed, `:has()` support confirmed, zero console/page errors in either, full-page and zoomed screenshots both clean.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule count: `lime.css` **710** — unchanged from LIME-59-fix's own 710 (this brief removed about as many rules — the old multi-layer notch block, the per-size `--notch-r` and ring-compensation declarations — as it added, the four new single-layer mask rules and the `:has()` scoping).

**Gate:** status icons look like the Penpot mockup — a bigger icon on the lower-right edge, the avatar cut away around it with nothing drawn around the icon; solid green for active, a bitten green circle with a legible green "Z" for busy, a grey ring for away, an open grey ring with a "Z" for do-not-disturb; no green band or square anywhere, on any row, theme, or state.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-57-fixd

**Goal:** per the user's check on fixc (`d50d85a`), "closer" — a bigger "Z" and a notch that hugs the icon instead of leaving a loose gap.

**Survey (confirmed, then corrected) against fixc's own assumptions:** fixc's own numbers (`--notch-r` 24%, the brief's own "icon radius 15.5%") treated the icon's nominal `.lime-presence` box (31% of the avatar) as the icon's own *visible* size. **Live DOM measurement found this wrong**: the icon's SVG `viewBox` is padded to `-10 -20 130 130` (130 units) so the "Z" has room to overflow, and it's the full *padded* viewBox that fills the 31% box — the icon's own painted circle (100 of those 130 units) is only 0.31×(100/130) ≈ **23.85%** of the avatar, confirmed by measuring a live circle's own `getBoundingClientRect()` (9.55px of a 40px avatar = 23.88%, matching to within rounding). This brief's own first-pass numbers (written from the same "15.5%" assumption, before this was caught) computed a notch radius and a "Z"-cutout rect that were correspondingly ~20–30% too generous. **Caught and corrected before shipping**, by working backward from the brief's own explicit target ("≈4% of the avatar, ≈1.6px at 40px"): true icon radius (11.92%) + 4% gap = **15.92%** notch radius lands the gap at exactly 1.6px at 40px — matching the brief's stated number precisely, which the uncorrected 19.5% (15.5%+4%, the brief's own literal formula) did not (it gave 3.0px, nearly double). Flagging this for the record since it's a correction to the brief's own survey math, not a new design decision — the stated visual target (≈1.6px, "a thin gap like the mockup") is what was matched.

**The changes:**
1. **"Z" is bigger:** new path `M 68,-8.833 L 95.867,-8.833 L 68,22.833 L 95.867,22.833`, stroke-width 6.333 (was `M 68,-8 L 90,-8 L 68,14 L 90,14` at stroke 5) — same top-left anchor (icon centre + 18%, icon top − 12%), height scaled 30→38 icon-local units (the mockup's own "~38% of the icon diameter," up from ~30%), width scaled proportionally, stroke scaled to keep the same 4.4:1 bar-to-stroke ratio fixc established for legibility. Verified by rendering the path alone and scanning its painted pixels (the same technique that caught fixc's Z-blob bug): farthest point (102.8,-12), tight bounding box x:[61,102.8] y:[-12,25.9] in icon-local units — these are what the new Z-cutout SVGs (next) are sized from.
2. **The plain notch tightened:** `--notch-r`-equivalent (now baked into the SVGs, no CSS custom property) from 24% to **15.92%** of the avatar width (corrected value, see Survey) — regenerated into the same four fixc filenames (`lime-silhouette-notched.svg`, `circle-notched.svg`, `lime-silhouette-ring-notched.svg`, `circle-ring-notched.svg`), same technique (a plain SVG `<mask>`, no `mask-composite`), same ring-vs-avatar minimax position-fit as fixc (max error 0.4px at md/lg/xl, 0.12px at xs/sm) recomputed for the new radius (max error 0.2px/0.05px respectively — smaller, since a smaller radius has a smaller absolute footprint to compensate).
3. **Busy/DND get a second, separate cut-out**, since the now-tight 15.92% circle alone doesn't clear the bigger "Z" (farthest corner of the dilated Z area sits at 25.06% from the icon centre): a rounded rect (the Z's own tight bounding box, dilated by the same 4% gap on every side, corners rounded by that same 4%) unioned into the SAME mask as the tight notch circle — not a wider circle, which would reopen the loose-gap problem everywhere just to clear one corner. **Four new files**, one busy/DND sibling per existing notched file:
   - `lime-silhouette-notched-z.svg` / `circle-notched-z.svg` (avatar, md/lg/xl and xs/sm)
   - `lime-silhouette-ring-notched-z.svg` / `circle-ring-notched-z.svg` (unread ring, same two groups), ring-position-compensated the same minimax way as the plain files (max error 0.39px lime group, 0.12px circle group).
   - **CSS selects the `-z` variant via `data-presence`**, already set by `presenceHtml` (app.js, untouched by this brief) — `.lime-avatar-frame:has(.lime-presence[data-presence="busy"])` / `[data-presence="dnd"]`, no JS or markup change needed, confirmed live in both real browsers (`:has()` support already verified in fixc).
4. Every number above was computed from the same source geometry (icon centre 90%/86%, icon's padded viewBox ratio, the "Z" path's own measured pixels, the 4% gap) via a single derivation, recorded here rather than a separate generator script (none was written — these are one-off static SVGs, regenerated by re-running the same arithmetic, not by a build step).

**Scope check:** the 8 notched SVGs in `public/assets/`, the mask-selection rules in `public/css/lime.css` (`@supports` block gains the four `-z` overrides; the two outdated header comments mentioning the old 24%/formula were corrected in place), `presenceZPath`/`presenceIconSvg` in `public/js/app.js` (new path + stroke-width only — `presenceHtml`, the bite/mask-id logic, and the state mapping are untouched), `TEND.md`. No change to `index.html`, avatar sizes, or presence data.

**Verification:**
- `node --check` clean on `app.js`; `lime.css` braces balanced (786/786).
- The same 4-state × 5-size synthetic grid as fixc (real `presenceIconSvg`/`presenceHtml` markup), ring forced on for **both** busy and dnd rows this time (to exercise both `-z` ring variants): all 20 combinations clean, tighter notches throughout, bigger legible "Z" at md/lg/xl, no square/band artifact, in both Firefox and Chrome.
- The live Recent row (Jean C, busy, unread): clean in light, dark, and Sage, hovered, and selected. Full-page and padded screenshots throughout, never tightly clipped.
- **Real installed Firefox 157.0 and Chrome 154.0.8037.92** (`puppeteer-core`/WebDriver BiDi): icon diameter measured at 23.85%/23.88% of the avatar in both (confirming the survey's corrected number, not the nominal 31%), zero console/page errors in either, full-page and zoomed screenshots both clean.
- **A cross-browser DOM-API inconsistency found and worked around, not chased as a bug:** `getBoundingClientRect()` on the stroked "Z" `<path>` gave 57% of the icon diameter in Firefox and 32% in Chrome, for pixel-identical rendering — a real difference in how the two engines report a stroked SVG element's visual bounding box (miter/cap inclusion), not a rendering difference. Confirmed by a pixel-scan measurement (the same canvas-based technique used throughout this brief's own geometry derivation) on both real browsers: **identical** painted bounding box (10.25 × 9.25px on a 40px avatar) in both. The DOM-API check was dropped in favor of the pixel-scan one.
- Browser-parsed CSS rule count: `lime.css` **710** — unchanged from fixc (new `-z` selector rules roughly balanced by the two shortened header comments).

**Gate:** the "Z" is easier to see, and the cut-out around each status icon is a thin gap hugging the icon, not a loose one — in every state, size, theme, and row condition tested.

**Record:** this entry.

## LIME-60

**Goal:** per the user's check on the same screenshot as LIME-57-fixd — the composer's "↵" return buttons look too big again, the reply box's mic hover background is cut off, and the mic should split into a mic half + a ⌄ half, like the nav Add button, with its own per-half hover.

**Survey confirmed:**
- `.lime-composer__return`'s glyph: `git show 6db2847^:public/css/lime.css` confirms **18px** before LIME-59 ever touched it (LIME-59 → 23px, LIME-59-fix → 32px to match the left-toggle reference — which the user's own check on *this* brief's screenshot says now reads as too big, so ↵ is excluded from that rule here, as the brief asks).
- The mic was one button (`#voice-mode-toggle` / `#replies-voice-mode-toggle`, `.lime-composer__aux.lime-composer__voice`) holding both `dew-microphone` and `dew-chevron-down`, wired as a single `wireDropdownToggle` target — clicking anywhere on it (mic or caret) opened the device menu.
- **The literal clipping bug could not be reproduced.** Tried: the reply panel at 1567px and at a narrower 1100px viewport (panel floors at 300px regardless), a shortened viewport height, and a composer expanded to multiple lines by typing — in every case, both real browsers rendered the existing combined button's hover as a complete, unclipped rounded pill (screenshots in this session's own scratch output). Read every ancestor's computed `overflow` up to `#right-panel` (the one actual `overflow: hidden auto`, far enough up the tree that nothing between it and the button constrains the button's own box) — none clip. Likely explanation, not confirmed as the literal mechanism: the combined button's single shared hover pill, spanning both icons plus the gap between them, can read as "the wrong shape" / "cut off" against the mic's own circular icon, especially in the reply box's narrower column — which splitting into two independently-hovering, correctly-fitted pills (this brief's own main change) resolves regardless of the exact prior cause. Flagged here rather than silently assumed fixed.

**The changes:**
1. **↵ back to 18px**, both composers (`#composer-send`/`#replies-composer-send` share the one `.lime-composer__return` class, so a single rule change covers both) — confirmed identical pre-LIME-59 value, not a new guess.
2. **The mic split into two buttons**, markup restructured in `index.html` (both composers, identical structure): `.lime-composer__voice-dropdown` now wraps a `.lime-voice-split` container with `.lime-voice-split__mic` (placeholder — recording isn't built, LIME-10, unchanged behavior) and `.lime-voice-split__caret` (the real dropdown trigger). **The "voice-mode-toggle"/"replies-voice-mode-toggle" ids moved to the caret-only button**, so app.js's existing `wireDropdownToggle('voice-mode-toggle', 'voice-mode-dropdown', {fixed:true})` calls needed **zero changes** — confirmed live (menu opens on caret click, not mic click, in both real browsers). The old `.lime-composer__aux.lime-composer__voice` combined class is gone from the markup entirely; its now-dead CSS rules were deleted, not left behind.
3. **CSS**: new `.lime-voice-split`/`__mic`/`__caret` rules, mirroring `.lime-nav-add`'s own inset-hover technique (explicitly referenced in that rule's own comment as the pattern to reuse) but **neutral** — no shared fill or divider at rest (this button isn't a primary action), each half gets its own inset rounded hover (`--calm-bg-subtle-hover`) with a 2px margin on every side including the shared inner edge (scaled down from Add's 3px/36px to this control's 28px), a pressed step (`--calm-bg-subtle-active`), and its own `:focus-visible` ring (Add's same treatment, needed for the same reason — no fill at rest means a background-only hover wouldn't read as a focus indicator). Icon sizes carried over unchanged from the old combined button (mic 14px, caret 12px — both already correct per LIME-59-fix, TEND.md).

**Scope check:** `public/index.html` (the two `lime-voice-split` markup blocks, identical in both composers — reviewed via `git diff`, shows only the intended lines), `public/css/lime.css` (`.lime-composer__return`'s font-size, the old voice-button rules replaced by the new split ones, `.lime-composer__aux` simplified now that nothing else shares it with `.lime-composer__voice`), `TEND.md`. **No change to `public/js/app.js`** — the existing `wireDropdownToggle` calls needed no edits, since the id move was enough (see item 2). Not recording, the device list's own contents, or any other composer icon's size.

**Verification:**
